VISION Being the Continuation of "REALITY" AND "DEDICATION"

HARVARD UNIVERSITY 

LIBRARY 

Oct4, 1943 

D. B. Updike, The Merrymount Press, Boston

TH 

FOREWORD 

HESE letters, begun in 1931 by one who in the midst of a busy life had doubted the existence of anything beyond this world, were continued until April, 1937, when death took Mrs. Soule, the well-known and trusted medium. Thus the long pilgrimage in search of the Truth has fallen back on the three printed records, and on the casual communications from such mediums as have offered themselves; These, while obviously genuine and coming from the right source, do not show the long dictations, the unbroken outpouring of joy and sacred purpose coming week after week.

The later communications give tidings of a great movement to combat the evil threatening this world. In this the spirit world is enlisted and the writer of the letters active.

EDITOR

VISION LETTER I 

June, 1935 OU and I have found beauty in storms over seas con- You stantly rushing between the continents-we have found beauty that was itself a perpetual exhibition of the powers beyond our conscious control. It did not always find acceptance by others in the realm of majesty and power-the soft rustle of leaves as we moved quietly through some spot secluded and apart gave a thrill that could not be described, but was forever a memory as we went forth again into the activities of business and the association of minds ignorant of all except those troublesome affairs of interest and per cents. It is transferred at times to the pleasurable and almost unmarked interest in art and science, all that makes men willing to accept poverty in a particular desire. It seems possible to find a way through the supersensations that knowledge gives, but only momentarily, the real and definite understanding is that it is but a snatch of song that is sung by voices far away from the turmoil of life, only to be caught in a moment of ecstasy, and then the dip downward into the every-day common business life. Is business transferred to a higher understanding of its proper place in the minds of the world? And if so, where do we begin the onward march toward the Eternal? I was ever keen and alert in business, for it held the key to a fine perception of the need of brotherly love. One almost forgets in the pressure of times of need that there is something divine in the acceptance of the call for broth-

VISION 

erly love in business. Thank God for some ideals that I held as the better tribute to the needs and aid of those about me; there came so great a desire that I had not time to give to those fluttering passionate moments of high exaltitude of soul, yet through darkness the light was not dimmed and the song was singing into the requiem for the dead, not solemn but stilled into silence, and the music of the stars that swing in space might find me lifting my heart to the splendid harmonies that the law of the universe adds to our small efforts toward the acknowledgment of faith in something beyond our conception. All this I was feeling as the truth I should depend upon, the law of the universe, that we should know about and find safe anchorage for our ships at sea. 

Now when I remember the disaster and despair that I saw in the lives of the men and women about me I wonder if I could have done any better in my gift of life to those in need if I had been assured of the future that brings compensation through knowledge. Yes, I think

I could have left what you will leave when you are where I am. But to do this my hand would have failed to do some other tasks assigned to me by association and the business of life, for time is a taskmaster and demands energy and strength and hours that the completed duty that we accept and assume may be carried out to the letter. Is it not the most stupendous and important truth that we acknowledge these obligations to life?

Yet we may pay a price that leaves us little to give and too often we rely on our preachers or religious teachers to give with Sunday School piety the little they know of immortality. Such immortality as they feel competent to explain is to my mind unworthy and might be

VISION 

3 cast aside first as last if it is only sounding praises for activity over death. Why victory over death when death is that door that opens on the highway of new realms of understanding our bit of service to others?

This must not mean to you that I consider what little I was able to give, even though I felt it a joy, quite equal to the obligation and desire, but if through my repeated purpose to know and understand all that was so disconcerting I find my desire the very same urge as soon as death gave me the gleam that shot over the highway, I am glad to know that the emphasis on the pursuits of my mind is now revealed, and you have given the words I give in the form for which I am able to feel gratitude. Does the mother holding the still form of her dead child know less of immortality than the trained teacher of truth knows? Is there more demonstration of faith in his accepted desire to give comfort to those who mourn? There may come to him a consecration of mind and interest which sets him apart, and the same repetition of words that heal and comfort because of his position in the world which hushes into silence the crying soul, and the answer in its own way to the problems that are seldom answered. 

To-day as you sit with your friends or look into the sweet faces of those you love or speak in no hesitant tone of a life that is stretching out into worlds we know not of, you—yes, you are that ministering angel who wakes the consciousness of life that is definitely realpart of the atmosphere that I am so eager to give to those in need; there is the warm glow of life that flushes the cheek and brightens the eye that must come eventually over the pale face of the inanimate form, and if

VISION 

we, you and I, may add one atom of the knowledge that is ours of the continued life of the infant, of youth, of old age—that will give us the reasonable position of asserting that death is an incident and not an end, that the centurion's child is in the same precious unfolding on the plane where birth gave him a right to longer existence, and that only ignorance has killed the life in some mistaken understanding of the laws. That is the human experience, but why linger in the shadow of a loss caused by ignorance of the law and carry it on into the next step? The child that is accepted as the offspring of love should be the giver of knowledge that we may add to the world. Before, when I tried to give some comfort with my age-old proposition that there was the Father in Heaven who would take that little one into His care, I tried to accept that faith which to-day I refute, so impossible was the belief that there was a care which we did not even receive. All that I see of those who have passed into the life where I am I find normal; -teachers and friends carefully prepared who are ever as in life helping in unfoldment-much less to overcome in unfoldment-much less to overcome in the infant mind that finds the truth before falsity has been into the environment. That gift to others, making the empire of truth about life, flows on and on, the ceaseless gift of energy from the Creator; and the babes whose infant eyes close on the motherhood crushed by grief become bearers of a message from God that the old have never yet accepted. There are many who do not accept these truths even after years in the spirit life; they just hold the young in their keeping, very much as the mother restricts that child's unfolding knowledge

VISION 

5 because she has an unaccepted philosophy of her surrounding group of influences. If you can understand how the union of purpose accepted through our love gloriously unfolding in service recreates me and adds to my felicity—so to speak-you will know that my heart still beats with an intensity of love that has grown overwhelmingly delightful and is the fulfillment of a companionship quite out of proportion to any companionship known in the past. I feel as if you were on one side and I on the other side of the portal that leads into the power and light and knowledge of life and death. I know that you are ready and eager to go forward with it. I sometimes hold my breath wondering if 

I am taking too much from you. But when the day dawns and face to face we look into each other's eyes, there will be needed no word of regret or apology or of desire that we had left unsaid one of these messages. 

LETTER II 

June 27, 1935 

HE VOICE. He was just saying softly to himself, This our time and hour. TH THE LETTER.-When memory does not hold us to the past, giving almost the sense of fading flowers that will never be the same again, but an hour of "forward" and beautiful hopes that glorify what is to be. There is often a tender sweetness in roses as they drop leaf by leaf when day is done and breezes blow. It is pure sentimentality to feel sad, for we realize that summer comes again and gives the fresh joyous knowledge that we

VISION 

have lost nothing and are still in the gardens of the world.

I have been very much aware of the preciousness of what we are giving to those who are studying with some hope that we are right; we may open the book of knowledge for them; the world glows with the experience and 

I count it our choicest blessing, this lesson-consciousness-whatever we may wish to call it. I think of the parting that frequently cuts in two the consciousness of a loved presence; now, the note of unending friendship, the continuance of experience and the summing up of my experience is "there is no parting." Oh, I see very definitely when there comes a time of readjustment, and on the blank page is written the memory of what has been. That is not parting, it is progress far-reaching into the promise of reunion. Is it only that? No, no; superior to all that is the fact that there has never been any parting through the years and experience of death and physical separation. Even that must be reconsidered. To wives who have for years had the hand of love ever near, in the tragic moment of physical death, there is the way to ease pain by voicing a faith that is reiterated day after day that the silent one has entered into silence and is at peace with God. What a picture! 

Fifty years or more without a momentary consciousness of the companionship which marriage brings. Death is but the feeling that an end has come to that dear.companionship and common burden and joy of life together. 

Thrice welcome is the response you have made to my desire to give you this new and wondrous knowledge that there has been no parting. That is the reason for my eagerness to carry this message to a world that is

VISION 

7 moving on with new power to express itself in the shadow of indifference or sorrow or hopelessness. My own desire is to give you the consciousness that all through the experiences of these later years we are as true in our affection, without a voice, but with a purpose in the messages that give the understanding of life marching into the higher realms. Mary of Bethany discovered this when thinking she saw the gardener; suddenly she cried, "Master," and was blest by the fulfilled promise, “I will return again." This stupendous truth has been placed in a treasure house of hope, but often sealed up as something too sacred to accept as a daily revelation. 

How wonderful it is when a baby after the slight sounds before words come breaks into language and becomes a part of the speaking world! That joy was mine when I heard your prayers, some unuttered, but ready to break into song when the realization of my presence found its expression. It has never returned to the silence of the sealed box, never to be understood until the same plane of life should hold both. More than three-quarters of.. our speech is not language but the soul shining through in such a way that one is never deceived. The truth that animals and children catch what we do not utter is evident every day of our lives; they catch the truth. This must give us confidence that the power we have recognized of an understanding mind and a living faith gives us contacts that are god-like in power, with an upstanding soul-constancy which is never silent, but like the tones of the universe, which are rising and falling that he who heeds may interpret the language of the planets and those who live upon them. This is to some the evidence of the unity of world races, and when we

VISION 

are able to learn this truth we become part of the unfolding life.

Now this is what we might call a parting hour, only a clasp of hand and the thought that I step down the steps for a short time, but the absolute contact is as definite a presence as roses that grow in your garden or the touch of a hand that carries peace, or the stars at night that bring consciousness. Last year when you went away I felt like a boy on the last day of school saying good-bye, or sadly taking leave of the university.

All this I felt and I had contact several ways. There was the little lad transmitter who was so glad to take a message to his mother for you from me just to let you know I was thinking of you. To-day I feel we have come a long way on the journey of understanding and when sometimes you sit with the wind and the shadows I know you will say, "I hope you are here." Instantly the tone changes-a consciousness that we are one, that there is no parting of our minds, our hearts, our high hopes, desires to carry the truth into a world aghast at misery and pain revealed and needless. I feel that my doubts that kept me forever in the desire to know yet more, yet never urged me enough to try and find the answer, brought me to a point of purpose that with your help I found what I wanted to know, and you have made it possible to tell everybody who wants to know that heaven is a state of knowledge, not merely a state of mind. A state of knowledge. We must come up through scorn, indecision, doubt, and know exactly as we watch wild flowers pierce the earth and rise in beauty at the signal from the Lord. I must explain that a bit, too.

VISION 

9 I have often seen a woman bending over a few vegetables to feed her family. I have been aware of the lord of gardens blessing her as she worked with him. So I came to a sort of investigating process and discovered millions of gardens on the other side of life lived by the poor and needy. The one who worked with the lord of gardens found ample growth. Care can be given only by coöperative influence the spirit people on the other side. Understanding is needed to coöperate, and in that way I insist that not only fathers and mothers, but friends and all who can, must add their power to those who need it, and the work goes on. ess.

I have heard talk by a sect I did not know much about -Swedenborgians, who have a belief that everything is moved by saints of the Swedenborgian religion-that babies, for instance, are divinely protected. Anyway, I find your life and mine enriched by this coöperative proc- 

Sitting the other day thinking it all over, I said, What could I have done without you? That thought came first when I wondered where I could find a door to some place of understanding. Even had I found it in some prayerful moment, what would have happened there if there was no one to take the message, I talking to you, your response? I made myself free from sorrow or pain or deferred hopes when our hopes made true a heaven made for us. This is heaven for me, and from that you came, and I reasoned out that added joy for which I am so grateful to you, the feeling that I am some use after death. A man or woman who has wanted to do much, and finds life falling through listless fingers and knows he has not accomplished the full measure of his.

VISION 

desires for usefulness and finds he is not dead at allmust find, as I have done, new names, new paths to peace, and may be able at least to hush the sobs of separation and add to the courage that makes the world different. Idleness and stupidity enforced through leaders who are not blind but do not see, all these are in my word coöperation. Without you nothing. The gift of power is mine only to speak to you the truths that lift us toward the stars. And we are not anchored at death -we are set free to go sailing new seas and find new pilots and new power. 

Now when you come to me, don't think you are going to land from some ship watched for. I think one day I shall say to myself, "This is the day when she is coming." I shall walk out of the house I have planned, and shall come down some steps-easy steps. I shall come down and say to the flowers softly, "She's coming home to-day," and shall go to where so many arrive at once. 

Oh, the greetings are so wonderful! To see old men, young wives, and a man like me when at my best, erect, and strong, happy, walking swiftly to your place of arrival, you coming to meet me. We shall meet and look into each other's eyes. I shall touch your hand. In the completeness of our work so new to you and to me you will come laden with the love you have created, strong in the truth you have expressed. Tears for those you have left, but we have left the messages, the book, which would be the comforter for all who need comfort. 

I think the words I leave you of sending the comforter are the truth of that latest work for us, the best message, as a letter left in a book means so much to all that sorrow. Think about that.

VISION 

I am going with you now. I don't know what comforter I can leave, but it will be an illuminating for all who need love. Well, I have to go now.

LETTER III 

October 10, 1935 [After summer absence] [THE Voice began to eulogize my part in the letters. 

He stopped her, saying, "Just thank her, she will understand."]

THE 

LETTER. Ah, again we come to our altar and record our joy that our words may find the soul of some other two, blessing them with assurance of con-tinuous life. I have felt that I would use that word continuous because it is unbroken even by the shadow and silence of night before the opening day. If I may tell you what you have made it possible to give to the world —that is our world—it would seem like a miracle after the doubts and wonderings of the life I had lived. Now 

I am prepared to go on with any of those whose minds are made clear about the advantage of the continuous contacts with those who still linger in the shadow of sorrow. Not only is it the comfort and assuaging of grief and of the torment of doubt, but it is, I believe, one of the ways of service to the world. I feel I may give to the religious world a new hope that there is a Father in heaven whose will is ever for the care and provision for the children of earth. This may seem a confession of faith on my part, and it is a new light on the ways those long gone. Often I have wondered if the Creator of

VISION 

had any desire or purpose in the usefulness of the spirits who had passed out of our vision when we have left them in the bliss of new-found joys that must not be destroyed by the sin and tumult and agonies of those left. Sometimes long ago I thought of the sorrow that did not touch the dead, and with something like a sense of their safety from the wounding spears of conflict, the broken situations in the family life that would bring such pain to mother and father. I thought that death was really a relief from obligations and the knowledge of broken faith and sin. That is a dream that does not come true when death breaks the family circle, or when the horrors of war and desolation sweep into the civilized world and destroy our structure of peaceful life. 

This I have seen in these days of conflict and the disasters that come from accident and all the despair that is definitely known—and I believe seen-by the nearest and dearest. That, humanly speaking, would be the very opposite situation that we have been obliged to accept as the feeling of those who pass into the kingdom of the glorified ones. All changed; and in so many understandable ways that I now see, the dead are of service to the living in almost all the ways of life. Hindered, limited, and often not of use in the way they desire to be, but always with high hope and firm faith that whatever they may do must be the way to greater and nobler existence than they had dreamed of in the peaceful rest of a life they had earned and accepted as the future after death. Thinking of the noble men, lovely women, little children—well, what are you going to do about it -you who know as I do that the finest, best experiences are those that have given opportunity to serve our fellow

VISION 

13 men? My joy has been in the knowledge that this experience with you has made possible. Quite true it is that the personal comradeship and the definite realization that you and I have will go forward forever without loss of identity and the idealizing purposes within us. I say quite earnestly "forever," not that I am able to prove that, but because I am constantly meeting historical characters who lived long, long ago, and I find them aware of all the efforts that are made to create new ideals and bring to the world the philosophies and truths that shine in the darkness of undeveloped men. 

They have been of the greatest help to me as I sought to prove that my bliss is not a temporary heaven created by my desire to give you the same joy that is mine. I began to question all this when all these months I have just passed gave so much of terror and danger and conflict between the fine joy of the finest and the careless merriment and adventure that has given wings to death or the deep waters that swallow the victims of the "speed" life. That is the reaction to all these wonderful mechanical advances in saying and knowing all that happens ten minutes after it happens. Talking with a man who was ever in my thought as one who loved the silence of his library and sat quietly with his books, his mind attuned to dreams of high and beautiful lives of those who had filled his hours among his books with sane and delightful feeling of the good and true, he going out from Elmwood with the equipment that peace gave him, travelling out to duties that needed just that type of man-I found him regretting the hours that had been lost to the common family of neighbor and friend. I wondered about this, having some thought of

VISION 

the feeling when no longer the newspaper could be tossed aside and left for another hour as now the news of the day is heralded from every open window and through every room in the house, from the last battle to the last gasp of the criminal intruding on all the sacred silence of one's quiet domain and quiet observation of the truths of life. Well, he surprised me as he answered, smiling, "Yes, yes, but if confusion reigns and war is continued, can we close our doors of consciousness to the need of our service?" Then I knew, as I knew before, that as war in Jerusalem or Germany or New York or Italy may be very diffused by the press which keeps the open page for the latest bulletin, and if we have influence as I know we have, we may add to the consciousness that the news carrier gives to our friends the higher consciousness that there is much to be done to destroy the confidence that war is important and may add to a country's influence the wealth and possessions that victory gives. High time that such nonsense should be given its congé. Men are not and may not be possessions to be traded for the acquisition of land and what it contains. Sending the youth of America to give victory to a world that cried for peace does not mean that one may lightly consider the children of Italy, trained to warfare before they have found the higher wisdom that is so important in the world. This is not new to you or to me, but the new and lovely thing about it is that we have power, influence, and something creative of public opinion, and public opinion is the judge in all these disputations and quarrels between the nations of the earth. Young mothers, looking into blossoming faces turned to them with ardent desire to be-

VISION 

15 come of use in the world, will not feel the eagerness of patriotism, giving back smiles that may fight and die for a cause. There are masses of spirit people getting together for a definite effort to give back to the adventurous and loyal the faith that it is better to serve in some specific way. Something of this influence is felt and I am thankful to say that many respond to the ideals and to the disbelief in the barbarity of adding to power through war. Enough for that. I give it as one of the unknown powers that reside in the hearts and minds of those who have died; and I think personally that saints singing before the throne of the Eternal and Infinite Creator are far more useless to the Infinite Father than a man who rises through his freedom from personal responsibility for daily bread to add that power to the world and inspiration to step up out of slavery and customs to freedom of the idea. Romeo, before the balcony of his beloved, singing in the starlight, is a lovely picture where all is love. Romeo still standing below the beautiful and radiant Juliet while the storm of war rages just beyond his step, holding him away from service, is a pitiful sight. I leave that just as it is -poetry, emotion, and desire fighting against the call to a duty imposed by citizenship.

Well, I keep on hand the purpose to aid in whatever way I may the child growing into a life that must be sacrificed or redeemed. It is not as teacher, preacher, or politician that I take this to be the work of my life, but because I love the child of to-day and would give him the freedom and understanding that life is the most precious endowment and the most beautiful experience that leads us on and on and on. Death never destroys

VISION 

life, it may destroy opportunity, and the experience which is so important as we move forward. 

I have been with you all through these unusual days of a very wonderful season. Sometimes I have been so near that the touch of my hand on yours was as definite as if the physical hand had touched yours, and I have seen you look from side to side listening to some lovely tribute at which you smiled and wanted to share it with me; or in the quiet room when you sat by yourself, asking almost aloud, “Are you here?” I wanted to say, "Am I here? Where else would I be?" But instead of that there is vision. I am able to see you in the lovely scenery of your abiding place, and never feel that I am going away from the place you see or the England you do not see, but that I am able to be part of each at some moment just as you hear sounds far beyond you and hear the voices at your side. We have that elongated vision and long-distance hearing-it's ours-we have that and become a citizen of the world just as of 

New York, Boston, and Washington, and make our contacts by some physical power. It grows in each period of time, it's a cultivated power increasing with use. We are so limited in our earthly conditions we can hardly see the paper but only our own writing on it. It is not the suburban life of Paradise, but citizenry of worldlife. We no longer assert, "I'm an Episcopalian" or any other sect—I am interested in any form of religion that leads to truth. You may see behind the rows of consecrated faces, angel faces, literally. It is time to come into the lesser lights and in all these favored forms of religion, a chapel by the roadside, shrine in a corner, all have a light shining in them; it would be good for us

VISION 

17 to look into them and not feel ourselves separate. Mohammedan and Christian are far apart but the purpose is to get into the realm of the Eternal one. Entirely different nomenclature may form an attitude of belief, but I doubt if all come together in the same form of attitude, of prayer; each individual is teaching, but what a difference! Out of any of them may come a great soul of use to the world. Out of these one rises and lifts the others. Personally you accept much that we have to prove, but we accept because it is good and true. It may bring our friends by these letters from me to you when I tell you that I can see you, hear you, while something I tell you is just inside your mind, and it becomes a part of your happiness-you speak of it to some little woman whose heart is crushed by the loss of the only man she has loved and trusted, through my words to you and your mind, to where there is a vision of her beloved one, our faith becomes a staff for her to lean on and little by little what we have known and accepted comes to her as an accepted truth. The truth encircles her prayer and answers to her the question of life after death. I mean that when we have been blest, happy, confident, the hand I hold to you is nearer than only when going forward with new knowledge, there we shall be forever. I don't know that we shall go off and take a trip around through all the lovely things, but we shall go, see and know, and come back again, to the home. How could anyone come to a reasonable belief that there are no homes, as if people just flew about! 

We enjoy everything, all glorious art, literature, nature —we take it all home with us, and sit with them all as our companions.

VISION 

I'll see you again (his rare smile) and I'll go down the steps with you and drive home with you.

LETTER IV 

October 24, 1935

HE LETTER. Here again, and so wonderful to find 

THE 

the same quiet peace that is the abiding influence of one consecrated corner of a turbulent world. I have been thinking of the various shrines that one finds in various places throughout the continent, and it seemed something like that feeling, only this is surrounded by the invisible influence which leads up to the place of prayer, and we go down again to take our way wherever it may lead us. Rather better, I think, than to pass it by on the highway, bless oneself and move on, as a common attitude in the presence of the seen. 

Yes, Faith, I have been quieted and comforted by the knowledge that we have added to the new consciousness of the Divine in the world, and yet I missed the morning that always gave me the hand-in-hand confidence and spoken word. I never feel that we are separated by distance, for our hearts beat in tune with the beautiful realization that the Eternal life is already our inheritance, and that we have begun what is almost a new experience of the constant understanding of the immortal life.

Some radiant experiences have come to me from the life beyond, where my feet are wandering among those who have gone behind the curtain that parts only when the fingers of love open it. They stand shining like

VISION bright and glorified friends who will never be subdued in belongings and desired even by the hand of death. 

There are the words that come to me through the silence and stillness to our dear ones. Some of that joy sifts down into the lives as the sun shines through trees made lifeless by the autumn winds and rains. Destruction, separation, that makes possible a view of far-off beauty and indestructible life springing again into the life of another period? It may not mean to any of our dear ones just what it means to us, as they stand in strength and youth and constant endeavor, but there it is, the cup of water that they may drink and find it is from a fountain of life flowing forever from the Creator and all who gave it to the world for future need when fever first adds to the conquest which life is ever seeking. So much of the inspiring radiant beauty that is ever before my eyes I would give you, my comrade in the highway between the lives that have been given to us. I am sometimes thrilled with the purpose to give names of those long gone and carved in history's memorials to the great and good-then I hesitate, and my mind finds the little heroes of everyday experiences, and I give to them something far more important than a name in granite or a portrait hanging in a great hall of fame, a name I have ever held sacred, the soldiers of love; they are the mothers of men, the fathers of daughters, whose lives must be brought out of the shadow of pain which they bear that love must have its way. You know how I treasured all the men and women whose names were only remembered in the hearts of those who loved them and perhaps carved on the marble in the family lot (he smiles), carved and left and the mosses

VISION 

grow above and around it, then in sweet modesty hide it again. But I have found many of them almost abashed by the radiant company that gives them a new light and beauty for the faithfulness that ennobled the everyday experiences. We-you and I (making a gesture to each of us) who have been lifted from that security of names that gave us a place among our fellow men, lifted as those who stand out for the new knowledge (to us anyway) and a supreme victory over something that may never tyrannize us more, have been given what I like to name as the gift from one to another. If you were to stay where you are for twenty years and find an increasing number of little folks growing into the family life, with love and tenderness, and had not the knowledge that I still wait at the open door for your coming, and you had no way to make sure that I was always there it would have been a bit wearisome for poor me and a bit lonely for you. This constant communion is not a feast to be despised, even though it may mean a little less than the preacher has desired to give us. To me it is the fellowship of the high life of IMMORTALITY, and I am aware that what one may miss of deific

Presence is not a loss but is the presence of life and love and duty and beauty that companions us all the way to the life we might not even desire to have if we must surrender the precious associations that have made us what we are and wish to be. I find God in the sparkling waters that come flying down the hillside that we love, murmuring, singing at the feet of noble pines, go in and out to the ceaseless tides of the ocean that binds together the continents, and may be used in a service for life and union in the world we learn to understand. I

VISION find God in the smile of an infant child at the mother's breast, that binds us in the holy rite of marriage. I find evidence of the power of God in the mightiness of Nature. I do not find evidence of His power in chapel or temple, but the beginnings of an understanding of something beyond mere existence. The widow who finds her place in Old Trinity in New York may not feel that she is especially cared for by the Eternal, but quietly she finds herself in a place where praises are sounded that bring all together with one thought of Something that must be at the centre of worship, and it falls like dew on a withered rose, becoming officially the presence of God in the heart of Idolism. And I mean New York with idols that are cherished by money often given out of the emptiness of life and the unspeakable desire to be a part of all that is holy. I only mention New York as I might mention London, or any other official head of certain privileges that come to the world through the effort to find God-God in His temple. There may be no better evidence of God in the world than the abolishment of death in the meaning that it holds for those who have not found the cup of water in the wilderness of sorrow. Whatever brings sorrow to man is bewilderment, until he finds the answer to his prayer. Death, disaster, continued physical illness, desertion by a friend, the terrible slaughter of confidence, and the whirl and uncertainty of the bewildering world, cause sorrow to be attributed either to the fault of man or the punishment by something outside the life we know. Let us say that it is the misunderstanding of the meaning of life and its opportunities. Shall I say the amassing of fortune at the expense of honor? That is always an answer,

VISION but fortune does not always depend on loss of honor. So we go through the catalogued sins, and we must find some answer for the crimes that bring nothing of fortune or honor and we know that the loss is because there is no one to say that life is not ended by death, but is a pursuit after knowledge of its own meaning and opportunity.

Well, that's all the preachment I have, yet I sometimes think it is the thread of gold woven into the warp of knowledge, and even if I could, I would not leave out these blazonings on the trees that we may leave something for those who follow to realize the way we are going.

I feel that you should now have your hour of answers and opinions that may bring more light to you and your followers. Do you know that there are hundreds of people who will take what we have been able to inscribe in our work as the testimony of a man who died and a woman who found his desirable effort to tell her all about it. It is because I desire that following group to know that we are not content to leave the matter just where it was before we began to realize the significance of our findings. Like a cool wind on a day of parching heat comes the significant truth that there is a hillside from which the wind has swept the leaves from forests on its summit, and left the sun still shining in the world above.

I have seen men with gray face and discouraged heart threaten to throw themselves into the water and end it all. Somehow I had a quality of faith, not knowledge, that it would not end all, and I could not even say, "What about her you love?" or, "What will become of

VISION 

23 your daughter?” but made my appeal to the sense of honor to fight it out. Now, I would answer differently.

The river never swallows a man's soul-it may release his memory, and make a hell for him that he never dreamed of. 

Now, did I hear a whisper? something about a desire to know and to have made plainer just the best way to proceed? Did I feel a still, small regret that there was so much to be said, and how may I give it out? I think so.

I have spoken of the following that the work (let's not call it work, the joy) we have been able to give will be increased until there will be a cry for more and more.

I think it is so unusual to have the direct message without those ever busy stenographers and secretaries and scientific busy-bodies trying to measure emotions as they would measure the stars in the heavens. With this whole matter of getting truth from one man who knows what he is talking about and wishes to have the loveliness and happiness as well as the parson's godliness expressed to have it bear weight on a mind not broad enough to hold anything but a contribution, I think we may get somewhere. We must try overweight and underweight, but try and have it hit the need of the hour and triumphantly insist that one man at least survived death; one man may bring back the evidence of a new continent-whoever travels far must bring as his trophy of success something that the world will solemnly accept as evidence. So I who travelled from New 

York to the other life have found a terminal-not the terminal on a little side railway on which you have to retrace your steps, but with trains going both ways laden with love and understanding.

VISION 

THE LETTER V 

November 7, 1935

HE VOICE. He had already put down some words in a very careful and thoughtful way. 

THE LETTER. Again we meet at the little altar where our prayers are said and our words reveal our longings and our dreams. With no purpose at all, I sent a word of the queer feeling I had in your absence, and it occurred to me afterwards that it might have seemed as if I called you a truant, but not so, not so, for we each know that the time is longer now between the precious interviews. There have been many doubts in my mind about my persistence. In having so little to do, myself, I may have been too eager to impose on you the effort and time and energy in the undertaking. You will remember for me that I am still in that ardent state of joy that makes me always eager to speak my messages and I am also conscious that we are not separated even though voices are unheard and messages unwritten, and beyond all that the knowledge that this is a continuous and unfolding companionship that finds us ever on the way to the glorification of the love that is eternal. 

The Dedication book is wonderfully beautiful in the way it is given, as an experience that forever blesses love that is spiritualized by dedication to service for truth. Strangely enough I have been sought by interviewers on my side of life where are many true and adoring lovers who would give much for such opportunities as you have given me. I might mention many who did not know that the love they held as a divine ex-

VISION perience could be enshrined in the life after death as a precious pearl that lightened the dark when it came. 

These I call the Immortals; not those who found the pleasure of dreams and hopes, not those who wrote sonnets that give grace and beauty to sentiment and desire, but souls illumed by the divine fires that are only lighted when two are one in all the purposes of the great Forever. I know you, as the treasure that has always been mine, and I know that while the book lay uncompleted it was almost like a duty imposed upon us that the full meaning should be revealed. Often I used that word as I dictated a message at this table, and now that it is like a ship on the ways, and only waits for the word to be given to launch it and see it sail out on the sea of life, we may rest and be quite certain that the future holds for us that victory of the design and building of a cargo-carrier to a world that needs the evidence of the eternal values we hold in our hearts. Now as we come in our vision of further usefulness we do so with the inner sense of further light shining from stars that we have never dreamed of. Too big to be comprehended is the knowledge that is hourly expanding our joy; yes, yes, it gives me no concern now whether the next step may be into some rarified atmosphere that gives vision of faroff worlds, or whether we stay dreaming a while over the splendor of our constant life together in the realm we still love; all these sunrises and sunsets to bless us in our morning and our evening hours. Are there greater glories to give us than the joyous recognition of place and purpose in the lives of men? That may be, but we are exalted by what we have to-day, we may rise on wings-wings of faith that all is increasingly wonderful.

VISION 

I found some of our poets, who again and again questioned me about you, and I waved my hand as notice that the lady who gave me light in all my earthly light did not put me away and gather sackcloth and ashes to mark the passing of my spirit. “Oh,” said Browning, "that is the secret, that we mourn not but lift our souls into the Beyond life and follow with the desire to hold fast to the hand that was momentarily released, finding then that we should be able to make ourselves still a part of the love-life, and not trace and re-trace through dearest memories all the days we had been together, trying to pick out the gold of the past and pin it on our breasts as the light that still shines." I said, "Is it not always finer to move upward than to sit in silence and brood on lost love? And did you not find your power as wonderful after Elizabeth's death as before? Not bereavement but acceptance of a diviner love is my answer to the question (he smiles). This is a tribute to you, Faith—yes, you made it possible for me to tell you these things that poets rave about, and seem to lose consciousness when the door swings wide open. Not that one may go, but that both may enter into that new contract [THE VOICE. “Not contact, contract,” he is used to contracts] and that broader conception of the life of both wherever love leads.

This seems almost a holy hour. To-day all over again, with the first day, I find myself able to use language to give you the meaning of the life I found after death. It was the most tremendous influence that I could ever believe would come with Immortality. It had ever been a question in my mind about life after death-just how there could be communication between the spheres and

VISION those in them that I knew nothing of and they might have forgotten. The first words that came as the breathing of my desire were merely words I had known and gave heed to. Then they grew, our words that my lisping lips would not utter came singing through the silence with perfect freedom, and I then expressed what I had not even uttered in letters or conversation. The knowledge itself pushed away the stone at the sepulchre. The knowledge brought freedom and the full meaning only came with the power of opening the door of death and the sepulchre; there was no longer hesitation or fear of misunderstanding, nothing but the Absolute. 

I did not elect to say them nor you to receive them; they were already built into the body of expression to await the time when they became words with a new meaning, not the language of youth but of the spirit. We rest on that—there they are, the evidence of our birthright and our own future. Now I build every day something new in my mind for us to have, to live with, and to expand in our spiritual lives. I sit down beside you when you are alone and close your eyes and wait almost as if you expected to have me, and you find a new sweetness, a spiritual sensation, as we feel it through the body. Joy, hope, courage, confidence-dislike-all that goes on on the other side of the story; how they affect those living by our acceptance of them! Why, when I touch your hand as you sit there with that feeling, that sensation that I am giving you, something of my joy, my courage, my knowledge, it is as real as if I had opened every door to the Elysian Fields of life and re-objectized the reality of life with the dear one- -one on earth, the other on earth as long as the earth-life bound them together

VISION 

in a far more definite way than ever before. Suppose I had written you a letter just like these and you had received it, what would have been your reaction to it? I think it would have been a shock. "Has the man gone crazy? Nothing like it before. It was a friendship held above all others but to drop quite out of that relationship-" I think it would have been a shock, it would have been to me, to see such words written with new meaning. But here we are, spiritually renewed in our knowledge and vision of what is to be. I am not praying or hoping for this now nor to acknowledge it by desire because everything about me is evidence of continued life and companionship. If you were the only one to find the great secret it would have been less helpful to us, for it would have involved some great mystery of some fatality when we had no will about it. Now on every side 

I find through these interviews from men of ancient races there is the desire to have what we have found. 

I think it is because we accepted as true what was but on our communion table (he smiles). Just as it is true for us, so it is for those younger ones. The most profitable life is true in acceptance not denial; they live up to this that has come to you and me. 

THE VOICE. The young ones here come into groups; like boy scouts they are just apparent in their purpose. 

When they go out they become radiant, understanding what they see, no questioning about it; they have not had it taken out of them by doubt of others. It is wonderful for the boy you know to have found your man and he is as dear as if he were his son. Your friend helps the father and mother to see what he is doing. Your friend is like a prophet, he has it from the spiritual soli-

VISION tude of his life on earth which sent him back into contacts with those whom he had never seen or known. 

Just as he is able to give you something in your spiritual solitude those old spiritual men take little folks and they become leaders. G. P. is glad that your friend is interested in these boys. They go on their feet (I have never seen any with wings) and see the building and falling of empires.

THE LETTER continues. It is something to see an empire fall after reaching the height of power and mechanical success.

THE VOICE. He loves this boy. He feels that these boys, having seen nothing of the less noble perceptions of life, begin to throw into the world's activities the spirit of love that is like redemption by great souls; like Jesus, or some Eastern saintly man. The empire of love is established by those who are willing to give alllife, body, energy, that the spiritual inflow may be increased and bring new life. 

THE LETTER Continues. I feel like this daily—that even if the hierarch of men fails over and over there is always a standard which clarifies in the time of need. I don't apologize for any mistaken views I may have expressed about the errors of church or parson or preacher, I run to help any standard bearer give a shadow of truth. Love is found the saving grace.

Now, before I get preaching I say one word about my over-enthusiasm; it carried me through many experiences, it has brought me where I am. [The Voice tells about his reconciling opinions in the world. He was a balance wheel for conflicting people, he could get them together and bring them to harmony.]

VISION 

THE LETTER continues. I'll try to keep balances and 

I'll try to add knowledge and to bring peace between people who have somehow lost patience with life. 

THE VOICE. He is a boy with the boys. If you can reach the little lad, ask him to thank his mother for the message she has taken to you. You will love it when you get it.

LETTER VI 

November 14, 1935 

HE VOICE. He is already here.

THE 

THE LETTER. I Would be a light on the path that leads to knowledge and the glory of what is to be. The moment is to me a blinding glory all the way, and I realized when I felt the thrill of writing a letter to you to-day that there is life in the contacts we make with those who respond to our love and companionship, for often I go to those who would be useful to me and I to them, but it is like sitting by a still pool with sunlight falling on it with beauty, there is no vigor, no activity, only the peace and beauty of something that is part of love, but never the full flow of energy that adds to joy and the desire to do more than ever for the knowledge that gives us the sense of immortality of the soul, and energizes one to be busy with the work of our knowledge.

I am so happy-just that full glad happiness that is ours after sleep has given refreshment and complete sense of the reality we have gained. All the small matters of ways and means of impressing our knowledge on the world would have been of little moment to us, for

VISION immediately comes the thrill of everlasting life; we have been hand in hand, and all the evidence has come (he smiles) trailing along in the wonderful light transforming everything for us.

I have been importuned many times by groups of men who wished to use what they have found through our companionship and expression, for some of their friends to be helped to understand. I say, "Oh, you can't do that, you must have the right one." I am impressed most deeply by your acceptance of my faith that you are leading me quite as definitely as I am leading you. Once in a while I think how you always led me, and our comradeship of the past now becomes the breaking of the bread of life together and finding our days of strength and impassioned desire for the noblest use of our gifts. I know that I quite amiably gave you the change of the separated weeks, but I found now how homesick I had been when I said this morning,"Our day, our hour, that we may be fed as we have been sometimes." 

I say over and over again "feed my lambs,” and as I look out toward the seats of the wicked-mind you I said "toward the seats of the wicked”—I feel that our influence may be the protective power of truth, and to feed my lambs is as definitely a command as to take care of the affairs of men. There is now, I think, a steady stream of influence flowing into the life about us which is revivifying and giving the light that we are helping to understand to the young and little ones who had not yet caught the gleam of opportunity to save the world. Even the outlawed world of cruelty has stepped into the light through atrocious and vicious attacks on those who must defend themselves or sink back into

VISION obscurity. Even that outlawed spirit is finding it difficult to overcome the enemies who do not wish to give power or expression of interest in the deplorable condition of the world. So even a great fire may be kindled by a combined desire to have justice rule and love conquer. To us there has always been the purpose to right wrongs and to stand as an avenger against the littleness of great people, and now that is added to by our faith that work begins with death in a new understanding of the law and order of the universe. Ships go down and are lost between the waters of treachery and the loss of faith in orderly procedure; but from distant shores there are new ships arising every moment with the news that knowledge shows an equation of life and property and honor that is now visible to me and to you. Well-well! 

It seems to me after I have been sermonizing with all the desires of our hearts bursting for the light to be given to others as to us, I fall back in the very confidence of conquering love, and I know that the years we gave to what was ours to realize have brought fruit to our own and given them the new faith in God after the body has been deprived of its impetus and the soul has found its wings. Had we been willing to compromise with our own purposes in life, had we given to ourselves reasons for full-fledged effort to find the joys of the temporal world, as many have done feeling that life had its gifts that might be taken as the gift of love, of power, of wealth, of a thousand things that men cherish, there would have been no insight, no glory of the revelation which now makes us confident, walking with sure steps up the heights of the life clearly seen in the magnificent

VISION vista of worlds countless, waiting to be given in triumph to our work.

THE VOICE. He shows some papers, apparently letters; one of them seems to be a transferred letter to you, rather blurred. Everything he has written here to you he has kept as he wished it to appear to you; there is so little lost in his book that he is amazed and feels that it is because he has always given what he believed. 

There is very little of the dear ones in the same life, only here and there is the additional word from father, husband, brothers, or near relatives in the transcript of the messages he had thought out carefully before coming here to what he calls the "rose-covered table" [a tablecover of rose-colored velvet]. The carelessness and hideousness of ordinary messages would probably have scared him away had he tried to do what you are doing -only as he has used this way and caught the meaning you transcribed has be found that he must have a good receiver of communications. 

THE LETTER continues. Now your father, wise, good, steady, and true, could send a very good message to those in his family and they would accept it or no, as if those to whom he may send a word of love and care could in the least understand that he had grown into the new beautiful understanding of progressive life. 

You must trace my way through my love, in the growth

I have made since death opened my eyes and revealed the reality of life of the spirit and all that it means to find oneself able to seek a picture of the hours lost in days of high living, in honor and truth, but dull perceptions of the vitality of the soul in its new relation to

VISION 

life. To feel that we must don a gleaming garment and take a place in an angelic choir, in George Eliot's conception that may have been a noble aspiration of hers, self-effacement, quietly "sitting in" with angels to add a little note to the good and noble memories of the dead. But the truth is, I want no gleaming garment,

I want no hymnal from which to bring majectic strains of love. I say it with all my soul quivering to the handclasp that gives me free step to go on, and not stand still like a Fra Angelico picture; all very well to lift our eyes to painted beauty when the sky is hidden by the cloister wall. But for me, with love illuminating all that we attempt to do, that brings out the reality of love from romance the mightiest force in the universe. To go from one group of planets to another upper world until all systems of the starry heavens are working in unison, magnifying the glory of eternal life-that is the aim and desire as we go forward, with one bound to the other, in the purposes and flights of the planets. And what do we wish for most? Not pliant fingers that slip into mine with ardor, saying, "Let us go forward," but the steady clasp of the firm hand. There has been borne in upon my desire something that may give a still better understanding of our companionship; it is of the soul. 

When I say I want your strong hand of faith, I mean not a picture, not poetry taken from a book like a valentine. That is too much like a child's picture, with colors, and imagination of interest: it makes mistakes, the boy falls in love with the wrong person; he is a voice singing in the dark about the tumult of life, garments, food, all that hides and binds the soul and keeps the glory of immortality as a surprise awaiting us at the

VISION end, lengthened vision, deeper notes, firmer consciousness of the body becoming a body of the soul. 

A few days ago, I caught the sound of rapid breathing-and then silence. I traced it to our altar with the rose-colored cover. I came and asked if I might understand the picture I saw. The lady [Mrs. S.] who has given herself to service for truth, I thought had been lost in the hour of some writing, not like ours, but the body submerged and the spirit almost pushed out of its orbit of expression. I immediately, as you know I would, asked the reason for that: "Is it necessary to cause such depletion in the desire to have opinions expressed?" There was a group of men, none gone very many years—ten, two, fifteen or so-standing to answer the call of one who asked that political leaders might come to her and tell her what could be done and give her opinions to be expressed in book form. I was amazed. 

The death-like stupor gave me a voice, and with others who resented this sort of adventure I prayed for a release from such an intrusion; that first the rights of the men themselves should not be forced into expressed opinions that had no reason to be called. "Abominable stuff,” I said, “enough to counteract the influence of good people in an effort to have things wrongly represented." Since then I have thought much, have had conflicting statements of men and women. One of the men chosen to be interviewed was the father-in-law of Lindbergh. "Is Hauptmann guilty?" and "How do you know?” These foolish questions found denunciation in the old groups of teachers, but damage was done. 

I have felt so gratified to have prevented automatic writing in our case. You will be asked, "Why don't you

VISION have a hand write for you?" Because this is methodical and protective. We seek to protect every message. I am a thousand times grateful, and am calling upon the star of truth to go into the camps of those who are using the gift for ignoble and selfish ends. 

Now perhaps some day you will be able to give me some way to condense my ever increased desire to bring so much; then I may give you some time for your own. 

I'll try to give you an answer to questions.

LETTER VII 

November 21, 1935 

HE VOICE. He turned on the stairs and was looking back.

THE 

THE LETTER. As I came up the stairs I became aware of tones; something like a group of people who were talking in low, well-modulated voices, and I turned to see if they were words used by people we knew, or friendly tones of those who might join with us in these most wonderful hours. But no one was near, and no one appeared, and as it was a new experience I remembered that at times when I had been sitting with you a tone would come like the note struck on a bell, and that was all. Now I am quite sure that we are coming into new experiences that may add to our understanding of some of these new sounds; that may help us to come out of the confusion of tongues and bring the high note of the harmonies of another realm. Love is always binding me to you with the sense of something divine, something out of the realm of consciousness which we carry with

VISION us like a bundle of memories and associations that make for us a world of our own. So far I have been content with that consciousness as the gift of understanding of the beauty and companionship of memory as a part of the spiritual life. [THE VOICE. Just then he stopped and waited as if with the movement of his fingers he accompanied his thought.] Yes, when I am alone with you find words coming into the atmosphere that must be heard somewhere, just as my words now appear and carry ideas that I want you to have from me. So far we have been teaching each other a new method of expressing our desires; old plans fade and new ones appear, yet in the fading they are retained as a book of experience that adds to the breadth and beauty of our lives. I am not an imaginative person, yet easily I could drop into the imaginings that bring so much into the confusing world. Knowing this, I hold myself in check, just as I always tried to do, and I find that there falls on me a silence, and a new comprehension of what the babel of tongues may mean, and I am grateful that our messages have been kept from intruding ideas, and that it is our desire-yours and mine—to keep them so, for it brings purity of thought and clarity of vision. With my hand in yours, there is always the movement that has been referred to. I have smiled sometimes as if 

I found myself using deaf and dumb language without realizing it, and I have said, "At least no sound disturbs their conversation," and it seemed that there were compensations for those who did not use the voice to express what was in the heart. I have always—

I think been aware of all that is in your daily life, where you are, with whom you are talking, all the affairs

VISION 

that call for attention and little journeys to and fro, and I love it and want to go right straight on through eternity with just that dual consciousness of where you are and with whom, and what is done; the vision of your life of beauty and sincerity that is always on the path on which you go. Very rarely is there anything like curiosity about it never as if you were another (he smiles) party of the first part, but always it is really a part of my own knowledge that just is mine. No father and child, no brother or friend, can have that completeness of knowledge except when there is this spiritual bond binding eternally each to the other and to the Creator of all life. There is much to be said about these notes that echo through the world, instantly responded to, as some bells respond to other bells when the note is struck on only one; a symphony of sound that moves on through the world to waken the minds of those who catch the note and reverberate through lands where attainments are perfected and carry to listeners the quality and power of awakening emotions, martial, doleful, triumphant-or fearful, like guns for funerals. There is something quite new to me in the sounds of bugles as the call to battle, notes sounding through the air of command and arrangement for procedure through tune, and I am alarmed when I realize how quickly the young take up sounds as if they were called to some experience -to dance, to pray, to sing hymns blindly to some unknown god-all this penetrates into the activities of life, and gives anything but harmony; rather discord, which is disconcerting. I wonder if having ears that do not respond as we wish to sounds in the world is not after all an advantage? The ears that have listened to

VISION the splash of the waterfall over the mossy rocks may be useless when the note of triumph does not reach the point of contact. So I would, if possible, shut away much that is not of any use or need to-day; all these new inventions that keep us from knowledge-make-believes, some of them, keep us from knowing what is real and what not. We let it rest and half the world is betrayed by sound into belief in something that is not there. In these times are terrible days of unrest, so much poured into the world of despair, unrest, and sorrow. A man now wishes he were a whole regiment when the need of power is so important. Sense is the greatest need in the world to-day.

This is not a religious movement. Neither of us is preaching that a new sect be given to the world, but that light on problems at our feet may shine out to give the vision of truth, right, and power to understand the way we may attain it.

There is a man here whose loved one is distressed over the knowledge that death' does not silence the tongues of men after death. I talked with him because of the betrayal of her faith in a system that denies the truth of communication through all the realms of conscious life. He finds the truth mightier than the errors that shut each experience of life into a closed book when there is a life waiting to reveal what is so evident to us. This man has come again and again to ask me if 

I were confident that you were accepting this communication. Such nonsense! As if I did not know that every word I give you I make quite sure goes down in the repetition I make where she may read again and again the message; and I said to him, "Keep right on-you

VISION 

will find that the revelation is more than a disturbing element laid aside for later understanding." The dead are more alive after the passing of the body than before. 

Every motion is reflected in the heart of love, and is never disturbing, but gives back faith and love and constancy. [THE VOICE. He puts his hand on a large 

Bible and again on a little pocket Bible.] Why, we are constantly giving out the ideals and knowledge that make ideal life; we are not deceived by statements that may be turned over and read in entirely different chapters on a new leaf. Not for me to argue about the truth contained in apostolic stories-all true in some part of its usefulness for the world-that I leave for those who wish to hold, keep, and set aside, but the world loses the especially true message such as our message. 

I have often wondered at the uselessness of arguments between people already set for combat. I accept what 

I have proved. Did it take me long to know that you were standing just where I expected, ready to accept any evidence of my continued-not new-life? That first gleam of light when I could see you and felt as if I could lean on your consciousness as if it were my own. 

Sometimes I wonder why I keep on with this knowledge as the one persistent and predominant note of my soul; why do I not whisper to you the beauty of all I seethe mountains that I love did not find themselves belittled by my new consciousness of still higher alps to bless my present day. Streams and woods, always a part of my gratitude, did not lose interest because I found seas and ships of no use to me—but I have the joy of knowing that they, too, are in this realm-even that city of vast interests where I worked so long, I have not

VISION 

41 lost that, nor any loved spot. But there was no doubt left when I found the wonderful experience of woods, of life, all that gives the desire to live on and on and see them again. I always haunt a place that has charmed me; I do not go on a picnic to look at great pines, or the coast line, but wander off alone and sit drinking in the splendor and freshness of seeing it again. And when I am not too busy with you I think I might wander off to the sea to find the same thrill I had long ago. This is not a protest that I am too busy with you, it is a joy, for we shall witness together the places we have known, at some sylvan spot. There I fancy I shall say, "It looks just as it did, doesn't it?"

This awful state, that calls out to stop the sorrow of the world! If I were in highest heaven these sounds would alarm me-not for my danger, my loss, but loss of civilization in a high and mighty use tending toward perfection. When I see this I share your detestation, but I also mark with the same feeling the loss of honor -not war, but honor in daily life-that is what the men here like me strive ever for, before all else—to find some who feel our feeling and add to the joy in the world through purity and power—the finest and the best. A little boy from your or my family or taught by some of yours or mine catches aspiration and becomes one in the line of march toward the divine life. 

When you came here and I looked at you-I suppose

I have had that feeling before-I recall it perfectly at this moment-you looked so young, there was a radiance in your heart and mind that far exceeds a youthful beauty that starts in victories but does not even appear at a later day. I am not saying this as a truth that

VISION 

may please, but one that is proved every step of the way when we look at one we know and love. It is not a case of silver hair and wrinkles, it is the radiance of spirit that is never lost when one still retains interest in all that is given to the world. That you have. I might touch your hair and say how lovely it is since years have brought a new aspect-not "How well you hold your age"-but I see the lustre in the whitened hair, more beautiful than the early color. It is true of your smile, not held in bondage to memories of birthdays, it is the spirit of one into whose body has been brought the new light of immortality. I think I might say "She brought the new immortality to those who lived with her." This is my joy message, and as you have brought the thought of immortality to so many, many times, it will carry into a world burdened by lack of knowledge the new immortality in church and society. I want to release you, but my heart goes with you. I thank you for taking this day. [I had an appointment and he wished not to detain me.]

LETTER VIII 

November 26, 1935 [Day before Thanksgiving Day]

HE LETTER. The beauty of the morning, the rose- THE 

cover of the table, the silence of the little sanctuary, all give me the blessed assurance of my power to give you the prayer of my heart. When I saw everything ready for my coming I stopped not nor lingered with the knowledge which I wished you to realize, the thought I wish to give for the season, the hour; and the desire

VISION 

43 suggested the question whether I had been too insistent about keeping my tryst with you. It is not, I believe, selfishness in the accepted term used when one asks for privileges that count so much in our companionship, for always before me is the significance of the created holidays made by men in the desire to say something for the time that is fitting and right. Thanksgiving for us is so wonderfully true that I am thinking of those whose holiday is marred by loss and pain and need. To lift thoughts above material needs and to be thankful that we may have more than ample food, more than joyous greetings from those we love still in our midst, more than the joy and privilege of sending our love into the midst of merriment, we have found the very essence of thanksgiving to the Power that is ever evident to us. But to those whose eyes are not able to catch the gleam of stars moving in order and precision and bringing into consciousness beyond comprehension the love and care that is somewhere the creative principleon my knees I lift my heart with head bowed before the new knowledge and add my tribute to the Power that is and binds me to you in all the words flowing into my already filled goblet of life, the sparkle and effervescence of the flowing life of the Eternal, as my share of the gifts of God.

When I considered the wooden table by which we sit, the pencils on the cloth, the hand ready to write the words my mind is able to hold long enough for transmission, I thought how little dependence there was on the wood itself, the color, the calm and quiet house, even the pencils that make visible the thoughts I express. I was almost baffled by the wonder of the mind

4.4. 

VISION 

that flows into the usual mode of expression, and I almost cried out "To God our thanks are due!" I again felt the wonder of our illuminating hours that have been so great a service to those who have never known the answer to prayers such as we have had given us, my Faith, my Faith. I am very serious about this, for there are minds that with more patience than good sense stand beside the open door and never look beyond, but have the idea of the importance of leaving everything to God for a later revelation. There is one power lost in that patience which surpasses my idea of unfolding life. 

If unfolding, why not unfold, and receive the divine understanding that becomes ours as life moves on? This is not selfishness, just to have these days of memory given back to us as the gift of time; there is something deeper, the hidden mystery of the ages, which is revealed by the opportunity that life gives us. Why do we feel that the great silence comes, falling at our feet, and hides away all that we loved most, to be ours only at the resurrection? Why do we feel that it is a gift of death to rewrite what was so closely bound in every experience? 

Why break the cord that held sacred every pure and holy association? That is the question before me as I know that every one of our dear ones will hold the pleasure and joy which family gatherings always bring. 

Thanks that we are all together, and then a tear that does not fall, with the thought that before another 

Thanksgiving Day one may pass on and leave an empty chair at the family board. All this could be a table with no shadow if there were the consciousness of continued faith. Faith has guided unerringly in many instances to the desire to feel the hand and hear the word; why not

VISION 

45 make it universal, since this is the universal experience, whether or no the truth of life beyond will become a united experience and life begin where it was broken by the spear of death. I cannot for one moment accept the old idea that years will give us the thread of memory after death and set us weaving into the fabric of life with the stitches that bound us together in the past. 

This day, I know, of all days, will be the repeated and oft-expressed desire that we may all be as we expect to be together—with that superior consciousness of the presence of those we love. Nothing, nothing, can hold together even an ordinary family association unless it be love; family may be in many cases a kind of respectable distance which keeps one and another out of the group, each in his particular relationship to other situations in life, but love binds together in whatever place our wandering feet may find a step; it is the sometimes invisible mark of memory that we dwell upon and set our happy thoughts flying over the world, resting a moment in the heart that loves. So to-morrow we may be assured that our love will find its way to old happy companionships in that invisible but most powerful influence that united us as one. I know you will feel with those dear ones—those who, each with the perfect faith in the reality of love, will be in the midst of the joy and laughter that springs unbidden at the festal board. I do not dare to make my Thanksgiving prayer to God as if it were a personal father presiding over his dear ones, for I know I am part of the expression of laws that eternally bind themselves into the years and make for the order and love that is for every soul winding its way up the avenues of unfolding life and coming at last to

VISION 

you the same consciousness of order, law, and love that and I now feel is made evident by our acceptanceacceptance of all that we find each hour. If I were penalized to stay in one state of consciousness of the knowledge I had gained in my experience, that would be purgatory for me, and I am not a Catholic, but I do know that there are many purgatorial influences at work before death comes.

Well, to-morrow I wish to spend the entire day within the consciousness of my life with you and yours with me, extending our personalities from the tables in various places where our dear ones are carrying on with memory and the ever present joy of all that has been. 

Turkey-which may not be as essential as it once wasstill spreads its wings over the feast, and binds us in joy of the past. I do believe that I may sit with my family and you with yours and find interacting memories and present joys a part of each word that is said and each joy that we recall. It is something in my mind like a small elm tree whose roots have gone deep underground carrying the beauty and shade of an ever increasing acreage of interest which may feel the throbbing life of the young tree, in a city apartment, or a table set for two that are left in a little home kept intact by love's dear memory. If you were to come to me (not a prophecy) before another Thanksgiving I know we should find our greatest joy in the knowledge we accepted the spiritual significance of the day, and that the sun would rise and set for us with the beneficent knowledge that we were a year beyond the day, not staying to recount the blessings we had received, but gladly giving voice to our joy that we knew so much

VISION 

47 more before death overtook us in the shadow of twilight to shut away everything till everyone was united once more-never divided. What good is our message for us and the world unless it finds the heart of acceptance? There are millions of men who go their way thankful only for what is the physical blessing of goodsstocks, bonds, and all the things so seemingly important. What did I find when I came here? What was the dearest experience that came to me without any waiting years to help me to grow into my own desire and the light? You know, I know, it was the knowledge that death could not stop anything but the physical heartbeat, a physical help or hindrance to life. Everything else, wondering, hope, desire for knowledge, even houses for the poor, that often appealed to my imagination, even the desire to have light shine in on their lives and give opportunity; and my thirsty soul, striving to drink of the life eternal-all, all found waiting, ready to my use, all still with me, a part of my desire. I put out my hand and felt your hand in my own; I looked into your thought-life and I was beyond words in the ecstasy of the opportunity to take life up where I left it at that moment—no return to childhood that had given its experience, but a desire to walk forth with one who understood. Strange how you did understand me, never doubted or questioned; your own high experience of the spiritual adventure of faith gave you a contact that only began in its fullness when I threw down my armor of fight, for I knew that life was the precious heritage of all that had been. I suppose I may have listened to your thought as you were still talking happily in that old sort of happiness; my prayer for thanksgiving is this

VISION
