truth-not adding to blessings that are possible coming through silence, but what you have given to those we know and love who are more or less sensible to high ideals and busy with plans for carrying on our desires. 

With all that, have you not sent out rays of light into the world shut into an ark that might be able to carry on through stormy waves until the next step is revealed? 

Our ark is the far-away ship-consciousness, responding to all of understanding that life is not for a shipwrecked crew but mariners who steer by the stars-nothing hindering the voyage of discovery that they have made with nothing to fear but ignorance. I would like to say many things that are always before me-praying for rain; for one's salvation when there is much to be explained before the realization of salvation. I did not feel my work done my last thought was not acceptance but what I desired to accept. And here we are. I have all the adventures, you so true and fearless, all the glory of stars, sunsets, all that is clasping hands with those long known to you before I was; think of that! before I knew or realized the beauty of this dual life of knowledge and ignorance-knowledge always on the wing, always just opening a door here and there that ignorance may fly away, no more binding of mind to the new and old in sudden twist of life itself winding and unwinding until the whole plan of life is as simple as a baby on the knee, and a man full grown, with capacity to right wrongs and bring peace where no peace is but only power.

LETTER IX 

December 15, 1935

HE VOICE. He was standing here. I don't know when 

THE VOICE. He w look so straight and sure and eager. 

THE LETTER. I am finding so many and beautiful ex periences in this work; and without the slightest idea of forming a circle of people who might find the light through our communications; for the enduring power of light is that it has formed itself; the truth that binds us together like a vision of power, beauty, and unfolding life, illuminates and gives desire to follow our-our footsteps out of the shadow of sorrow and pain into the light of eternal life. 

Here, with you, bound in this deathless love, I am this hour, and soon the little lad will be here again, not with any planned effort made by us but by the events and circumstances of the days which brought us together in the series; and again the girl so lovely and so much beloved comes following this with you and me.

In all this I find the broadened circle of active love in communication with dear ones who are finding that the key is love. All types of people come knocking at the

VISION 

door, some so eager to know that communication is possible-ambitious and making great efforts to spread a new belief and make it a stepping-stone in any way possible for their own adornment in their hunger and thirst for recognition; some merely wishing to establish themselves as an activity of purely scientific expression. 

You know as well as if I had told you before I came into this contact that I had no curiosity to attain anything like that. I hungered for the bread of life and to make use of the faith that I felt one must find in the solution of life itself, brought from idle dreaming of what might be an earnest prayer for knowledge of God. [THE VOICE. He spoke it so softly.] Then came the truth, shining in our very first interview, a sense that was not quite sustained until later, but something intangible and so real, like an atmosphere that held us together in the old question-and-answer periods of our lives; and then the consciousness and reality. Love is the mysterious power that guides us onward. I have written for you to read some very strong sentences about the unreality-so much that seems to have caught the fancy of some who feel great importance in their finding -proving the confidence they hold in the mysticism of a creed or a book and losing all understanding of love. I speak now of the love of love, [THE VOICE. He hesitates, not sure about this. He says, "All right, go on."] and I am still of the desire to lead away from text and creed and to hold fast to the great reality of life-the love that illuminates, that lifts, that gives wings to the soul. I do not wish to build an empire, I wish to so love the people of an empire that they may know what we know, not what we pray for, not what we have felt must be pos-

VISION sible, but the enduring knowledge and love that never fails nor falters but grows into the fullness of its capacity and works on into the fair vision of heaven that lies about us every hour; I am now thinking of the love that is not held by duty but by the knowledge of the gift of the Creator. If I could give to those who are worrying about how we grow into the knowledge of the gift and its acceptance I would put before the world the truth that all jewels are not to be reclaimed from the depths of ocean, but are in the hearts where love is enthroned, and those who would find what we have found must feel the full understandable truth of the difference between love of power, love of kingdom, love of form, and the building up of truth. The Adler who tears from the 

Scripture a text or two with the solemn avowal that the dead know not anything might as well let it stay out of his mind as a possibility, for there would be no argument when once the truth of a message has given light to his mind. The very many absurd statements that are falling to pieces like a dying rose may not add to the power to understand, but one word of love shines over every grave. We-I-(I mean I especially by this word) have discovered that the pathway through the various forms of life is never lighted by unbelief; it is the acceptance of life and it is the opening door that love gives us. I sometimes wish that only those who are guided by it could come to this house [Mrs. Soule's], but they who come without that enfolding love will never get beyond the telepathic theory, and will waste much energy in the constant rehearsal of experiment and doubt. “Let us try this new theory"—they go on and on with "This is a rock,” “This is a crystal,” “This is a shell,”—that's

VISION 

about all it amounts to. When they get through, they find, if they follow to the end of that trail, that they are engaged in something deeper, that is as yet unanswered. 

Who carries the message from one to another?—another dead scientist who is only aware that some idea has been given and received. But who-who, WHO carried from one mind to another anything beyond the fact that there was a repetition of something said at another point?—Now this is where I believe we add to the knowledge of the power of communication that is at once an answer to the question,"Who is the bearer of the good news?" and "Who gives the communicating definite and helpful conquest of doubt?" Here you have transcribed so literally the way I have expressed what I believe that there is almost a clamor from those who wish the same type and power of mind to mind and heart to heart and soul to soul. First get love, first have love, first express love. I would not give a snap of my fingers to tell how soon the war will begin or end, how soon we may expect a turn in the affairs of the political world, how soon my children may inherit the fortune long set aside for them. No, but I would build into the thought-life of those I love just how dear they are to me since the old life has been lightened by my knowledge of life after death. For you I would not lift a finger to add to your life any unworthy ambition for place or name or fame, but I would stand hand in hand with you to add to the positiveness, the knowledge we have gained, that love is the victor in all the experiences of life here and hereafter. I think the deeper the love, the finer its purpose; the sweeter the song, the sooner the melody of the eternal life will be falling hourly into

VISION 

53 the essence of every experience. Love places no diadem on the brow of the beloved, but deep in the heart lies a peace that like a deepened river flows onward to the open sea of eternal experiences. You see I do not say eternal life-that goes on anyway with the gift of God; we may not hold it in our power to increase ages, but our experiences deepen and strengthen our understanding of the life that goes on and on and on. Love is such a trifling name to use when I think of our experiences that I would add much to my idea of what it should be. 

Love is all, the centre and circumference; Love is all, we may not take it into the business office and continually write love, love, love, speaking of money, servants, collaborators, but the love we are experiencing is ours because-because we are one in our ideals, our joys, our understanding. At once when I named you as my faithful, rose of the dawn, if you please, you understood, and you stood answering me in terms that were like; the words may never have been formed by your lips, but I found them lying in that limpid pool of beauty, the heart. Now if we may find in the hours we are together in expression that vital power binding us to each other through absent days, quiet nights, active expression of the thought-world, are we not blest beyond the comprehension of the man who wears crape on his hat and follows the custom of his countrymen in the expression of his grief? Are we not blest by the consciousness of something that is like a crown on our heads? And if we had come to the communion table of our friends who seek to be fed, have we not brought some new light on the means of bringing the peace that passes understanding to a world that mourns but does

VISION 

not know how to find communication and inspiration by the simplest means? Let us tell those who are eager to know the truth-let us say, "Have you anyone gone whom you love very dearly and do not put away in a costly marble tomb to wait Resurrection day, to make this the one important thing and most effective in the search for truth about the next step in life?" We have never yet, I believe, except incidentally, brought forward those wondrous pictures in the hearts of those who live a continued home life where the sun shines and the mind moves forward after death just as before death in its grasp of knowledge, that threefold is the growing life after death-the soul, mind, spirit. We may stay dead a thousand years and yet be alive to a certain consciousness of existence, but when we awake, a thousand years past, we are still sitting in the same place where we found ourselves when the body died; we have not gone very far into consciousness of the thought-world, which is active when there is the reality of life after death.

There is safety in recognizing the truth I have just expressed: it is the one-third person communicating only what he is able to perceive. He sees the helter-skelter form of living that gets nowhere to-day and to-morrow is no farther ahead. How long he stays in that half-awakened half-life depends entirely upon contacts after death. These business-like people who have drawn power for their existence from those who are in the same state of mind are almost unbelievable in their influence on their own kind. No unworthy spirit could come into the atmosphere you bring of love, confidence, and faith, to which I respond as to an angel who gives

VISION me what seems protection, helpful and healthy. In the soul lies the way of peace and power. No spirit inclined to drive the great Italian leader into a distressful and unreasonable situation for himself and his people could ever reach him and give him ambitious destruction for himself and the land if he set aside the kingdoms of earth as unworthy of being fought for by those who must suffer and die. So true, it should be emblazoned on the dark war-clouds with such devastation and pain. 

But we wait quite confidently for the better and finer desires to come to those whom we may reach, friends, children, citizens. Shall we not keep on with the effort to bring realities into the foreground instead of pictures futile and disastrous? 

That's why, standing in your own place, as strong and steady as if accompanied by angels of another kingdom, your own soul marching on in stateliness and peace, never turned aside by any disharmony, your own body pure in tone and love the captain of your soul, I am thrilled that we made the contact at an early age, and with all our aspiration, I am sure, for the betterment of human affairs. I was not always able to win in ideals, nor were you, but you never faltered. And in this present experience with each other we are guided and bound because each day you are stronger and steadier.

The years built into our stature the strength and power that love bequeaths. If one, two, three, or five of our own are standing stronger for what we give them I would not build my future on the power I might attain to give someone undeserved credit, but I would stand beside the weakest one and give all that love may give. So there is division between two types of people, those who

VISION 

use and those who abuse. We must use to add our strength as power is needed.

THE VOICE. He stands up as if to speak to a group. 

THE LETTER continues. Many voices call to me as I start hereward. "Find for me the best way to join your glorious comrades." Each one becomes a new comrade if there is assurance that he belongs to the "passionate pilgrims" seeking truth. They must show somethinga step toward the threefold life in soul, spirit, and body; the soul finding the highest, the spirit trying to express the best, and the body responding in service; this trinity moving forward in the body which is the gift of God. 

LETTER X 

December 13, 1935 

HE LETTER. As I came up the little winding way, TI caught a glimpse of the blue sky, and I said, "Blessed are the pure in heart for they shall see God." 

Not death, but the pure heart sees God every day and hour, and the revelations that are ours, coming from the desire to know Him, make for some new evidence of love and care.

Often, as I find the words I have given you, when I read them over with you I am surprised that there have been so few that I would change, except to broaden or emphasize some particular word of truth that shines in the language we know. How imperious are words until we have a new command of some great vision that is beyond words! and we stand in need of a finer and more divine way to give full meaning to the pictures we wish

VISION 

57 to convey. I try always to be easy in mind, strong in the knowledge that has given me a new and constant unfolding of life after the body is less demanding and time less limited. One of the most impressive experiences I have had has been like one swimming in a sea of ideas that come and go and reverse decisions that were formerly as steady as the growth of a tree or the formation of walls piled up against a sea-wall preventing the changing waters from entering. That gave me a quite new idea of the desire to be stable and to be depended upon; and suddenly I realized that the whole world of mental associations and forming of opinions, made up of limited experiences, should be less influenced by the elemental will urging us to be steadfast and true; that is, the world of opinions made up of limited experiences, and new vision, higher unfolding, will melt them like candle grease in the sunlight revealing all the physical limitations, new worlds, and new understanding of the world in which we have been experiencing agitation and the dreams that encompass us. I feel that to speak of these experiences which come before the walls are broken down is to liken them to the reality of protection and shelter in homes and institutions of study and refining of knowledge into viewpoints somewhat hampered by maps and laboratory tables—when it might be a day out of doors with sunshine giving glimpses of fish in the sea, the feeling of warmth under sheltering trees, the freedom of little things that make up the world's animated life—so inferior in our use and beauty that we march forward as lords of the lands and waters of the world until a strong pull at the line brings us a wonderful exhibition of a water power; this, I always felt as a

VISION 

tussle of one mind against another-and the power of will in the fish to free himself from hook or line or the fight of the man to gain his point is precisely like the way some scientific minds fight to carry their ideas out in the contests of the world. That is and has been a good suggestion to me as I have been engaged with you in our desire to know some of the reasons why the gates are locked against the inflow of the waters of truth. One might think of the world growing harder in the damming up of the waters of truth lest they become softened and easily dissipated in a fluidic life. Now I am inclined to think that we may become hardened by lack of that easy power, thought, to be weakening by those who feel the importance of being a rocky shore rather than a flowing river. You will, I know, understand, and feel that though there is nothing that will help in the complexities of life except to harden into the walls that hinder, many now find that God is in the air, the waters, the atmosphere that rises; Law is a perfect accommodation to the higher and loftier spheres, and year by year we must accept the truth and realize that we rise in these spheres of activity until we might very well feel that it is important (he smiles) to cast an eye out to see what is going on in our change of view and knowledge of the spheres before we reach heaven. We know, you and I, that there have been heavenly spheres of knowledge from the first moment of our acceptance that I am I, and you are you, just as in those days of our joyous acceptance of what life has brought us. Life and joy are not synonymous terms, for life may be low and miserable, a prison house of pain, a citadel, that we may not break into or even understand, or it may be a

VISION 

59 wonderfully illuminated palace of love and tenderness and dear associations that lead to service and divine exaltations. We all reason about these things, grateful for the freedom that money or time gives as we are moving forward with the world on our backs in our separate spheres of activity that we may share whatever it brings. 

Sometimes I feel as if I were an Atlantis dweller, with this great secret gradually unrolling before my vision and my desire to do what I may for the world, shivering in the sunless spaces and needing more than all else to humanize and bring to life the divine sense and the presence of the godliness that is one step in advance of our own. 

That is a point, as I put my hand literally into yours and my heart feels the throbbing of yours, as our minds are clarified by the vision of a life that somewhere finds the answer to the appeal. I have not often tried to find an answer to the psychological problems from those who have been famous as psychologists, for it is a jumbled mass of knowledge which they may scrap like old iron as far as it relates to the psychology of the soul. 

I do not say this with bitterness for I know that the pure mind and the criminal mind are not related to the same experiences, and one may not harmonize the two classifications now hailed as a key to all our problems in some of the most dastardly schemes to rob or to kill persons in the path of the ill-doer. These methods have been outcroppings of something that did not belong to them, like intertwining influences of fury and devotion, and it is now what I think may be intrusions of other minds that in some queer way may fall into the contact. The only way is to have these matters taken

VISION 

up by those who may later understand what they cannot understand to-day. I believe we have never yet had any confusion about those who might try to send a message from one point to another. Let us find a way to the cry that hope gives through our experience in this spot where at least we understand each other and know that language serves its purpose in expression and words to each other. 

That “little lad" who probably would have followed the ways of his ancestors, passed through collegiate examinations that gave him the right to express opinions and become a part of a cultured community and university life, has done as much for his own loved ones and the continued life of effort to bring knowledge to anyone who may accept it-as much and more for the promise of the revealed truth than if he had lived on in the happiness, beautiful and satisfying, of the world he knew. There are ways in which the compensation is evident, the young who become early a part of the immortal life begin the service to the world without any rock-bound fences of safety and freely bring about what they see and know, and we take note of; this is a point of the life after death, no hiding behind indistinct memories, nothing but the light and love and joy. He carried a slight message as we planned under the directive influence of a group of earnest investigators among which were some well-known friends-and enemies, too -of this manifestation of life and love-mother-love, if you will-joy and all the succeeding influences. They now are free, without any sense of obligation to society and Alma Mater. They may find that the year or two of reality may be worth much more than a century or

VISION 

61 two of the effort to psychologize the world with a new sense of the solemnity of the truth—quite unusual but with due respect to the daring mind who makes at least a gesture of interest. I say we may be quite as useful in our experience to university or theology, to society or to the mourners of the world as they are—or were. It was the questioning mind that I carried deep in my heart with desire to know-if we lived after death, what kind of life would it be?—that brought me strictly to the point of telling my own experience to one who always gave me a listening ear and a seeing eye. I must combine them because your eyes read what no other eyes saw, and your ears heard my appeal and you gave answer when the time came. So I value every hour we have added to the knowledge which we pass on to a heart-broken and heartless world, whichever way it is connected with your consciousness or mine. 

Sometimes I think-oh, if I only could give to my children in words that they would comprehend the story as I give it to you-but it cannot be. Their love is tender and true, but it does not lead them to an understanding of my heart-interest in you, for your wonderful mind that without knowing the truth still gave it a resting place until it should come flowing like light into our lives. 

I've often wondered what my next letter would be like, but I never rehearse it or make notes which I surreptitiously look at that I may not lose the word or theme that I am trying to express. No one comes to dictate or prompt. I walk into this room practically beside you, perhaps drop a flower on the table for love's message; often as you enter I follow, no shadow on the

VISION 

floor, no sound of footsteps, yet I am with you, and before I take my place by the side of my Indian interpreter I find the words spoken. She seems to me to see not only the words but my thoughts as they begin to shape into letters. Anyway, there are we two, alone, with no other mind to hinder my thoughts or to hold you in abeyance of acceptance, but just as if we had taken a small compartment where we could be free to speak as we wish. 

I come, and I have never felt any constraint or hindrance, but just the precious living contact of two who will go on forever. What is it to us whether we are under a sheltering roof or tree, or in Egypt looking at the battered old Sphinx, or at some wonderful concert where your eyes brighten and the music sounds dearer through memory? We are together. I have an idea that we shall not select a crystal palace in the distant future; it will be a violet-crowned hill where we may see the skimming birds reminding us that we are still in a nature world; no palace could be built that would hold our souls. I may look up and say, "How would you like to visit Rome? 

How would you like to go back to America with its sacred ideals?" You will say, "Let us go and see our children." That's what we may say, in a room sheltered from intrusion but always some desire will prompt us to go on. How many planets are occupied by people?

How many occupied in the distinctive work of building another world? I do not know, I do not care. I know we are always together, on the way, Faith, and so long as your hand is in mine I am satisfied. up 

I was asked by your father if I could not say all I wish in a little shorter time to give you less fatigue. I want you to have all if it takes all day or one moment, but if

VISION 

63 you need more time to rest and less time to give your dear hand less effort, just tell me! 

THE VOICE. He steps out, bows very low, touches the flower, a great golden flower fringed and beautiful, lifts it to his lips and says, "We will take it home." He writes a great big R in the middle of his letter, and says, "I'll give you more of this name another time.”

LETTER XI 

December 19, 1935

HE LETTER. With all the loveliness of the life around 

TH us, I find my thought going to one of these hours when you walked into the room with your beautiful head uncovered [it was the previous time when I had taken off my hat for the warm day] and in the joy of looking at you I said, “She wears her beauty like a crown of shining stars." And the immortality of that beauty dwells with me, as a gift of God that keeps the beauty of the body of the world and is always expressive of it in every direction. It is divinity of soul that moves one on through the ages with a message of tender memories of days that have built into our lives years of unfolding understanding of the meaning of experiences which come only to the anointed. And yet the message is not new, and it gleams in and it gleams in every way and every vision of our union. If I were to put into words the knowledge that your life has given me they would seem extravagant, but they are as simple as the sun itself that reveals what the words may never fully express. I have found the many unfulfilled promises that are ever in

VISION 

the possibility of your future. I think it is one of the experiences we may well reckon on as unfulfilled even when we feel at times that it is a lost power that youth bestowed and then stole away; but to me it is like the learning of a power that binds itself into the soul-life and is once more ours as we come to some new great experiences, when we may find it like a language we had used and had command of when occasion arose for its application to a new era of our ascending consciousness. I am aware that often I used to take down some book that had been a part of my university training and again go over words and sentences that gave me the needed expression that had become part of my equipment. If I know myself at all it was as one who caught readily the ideas I had formed about this or that, and I seldom failed to see all I had in my repertoire, but once in a while after I had used an old form of sion I went diligently to work to be sure of my rightful use of it. expres- All this I find very useful to me now. The systematizing of knowledge that had become a part of me, and the adding of new material to bring it up to the hour that is immortality of the gifts we acquire, and the delight of finding that nothing is ever lost or wasted if we find that we may depend on and use it in our way to the higher spheres of life-all that we have made use of through the years (years are so slender) on which we place our hopes for the finer, diviner life and are full of the possibilities of rounding out experiences until they are like cables binding us to the Eternal-God, if you please.

I am now in a heavenly state of mind because the

VISION 

65 heavenly virtues of all our attainments are before me and I see no end but a constant growing into the image of our divinity and the love that binds us together in our soul-life. All these powers, the song that rises into glorious tone, the mind that gleams with the jewels of literature, the constant and urgent desire to find no idle moment, no careless performance of tasks that are before us-all that is a life not separate or apart from the household gods that must be dusted off and brought to view that we may share everything with those about us-not one finger of the daily life may be found useless in the life where we are now. I am not waiting for you, but I wait with you for the life that will still be ours and find us exultant, triumphant over the wonders that seem almost beyond belief when we are just a little farther in the journey that leads to the acceptance of all we find. It has become quite beyond my belief of the actual life where I am. It gives me the sense of having the thing I always depended upon-I mean literally the articles, books, houses, lands, the chair that I find fits my mood and need, the window that reveals the distant view, the stars that come out through the shadow to reveal far-distant scenes, all the little paths that are so convenient to find a spot where are flowers lifting their heads to the light of day. I had, ever since I began these letters to you, begun to wonder if there would come a time when bodily needs would cease and forms become incarnate with Deity and no longer be individualized in the dear and precious companionship of things of no more relative value than stars and seas and mountains that are the footsteps of the Eternal. One may never call reason to prove what has never been ex-

VISION 

perienced, but as far as I am able to think out the answer to my question, I think that the individualized life continues in an individualized body that is always a part of the desire of expression in a much larger way than we may ever dream of, and I am forced to that much acknowledgment of the individual immortality by the people embodied as I came to know them from a long life that has thousands of years to add as testimony. That has so much appeal to me that I take it as a truth that we are all needed in the worlds that are in the making. I have seen so many people who have been so busily engaged in finding the crown they expected for their service in some particular form of life that they failed to see what we have found-that service is its own reward. What I find, that I believe would agitate the soul of a heathen (?) who worships his ancestors, is that he will have no difficulty whatever in finding them; they are ever in the background of his story. All this gives you and me the right to expect the violets that grow at our feet-the children who slipped away into silence when death opened the door and they were out of our vision; the mother and father of our earthly lives waiting to bless us with the memory of our love for them and theirs for us. I have never lost sight of those who played their part in our development and guidance, they are ever within the gates of remembrance and they have a care for everything that gives us the unfolding power of vision and will find the door of opportunity if we knock that they may open it for us. All so human, all so divine.

All these ideas come floating into my mind at this time when here and there the name of Jesus is con-

VISION 

67 stantly repeated as the one name above these multitudes who are never in the ranks of the disciples who bear the burdens and carry the joys of humanity to their days of life on earth. These groups feel that special temples and mansions belong to them by virtue of services they have performed on earth, and in the name of Jesus carry on the song and story, but forget the multitudes who have been blest by a cup of cold water given in His name. It is time for us to know that there is no mansion in heaven for us or anybody else not earned by the desire to give all we may of truth as we find it. To dream on about saving of virtue by hymns is such a stupid way to feel that Paradise belongs to us. I want no other paradise than you may enjoy and occupy with me. If I must withhold my open door for you to walk with me because I am not able to accept creeds and forms for worship by which I find my place, I am too fearful to accept Paradise at that price. It is the universal salvation of souls that come step by step up the highways of eternity and find there the love that is triumphant over sins and brings by faith in the individual the soul in full stature and individuality into this human understanding of what unfolding life may mean. I have found an effort we all have made to keep standards of truth as we find, not as we wish, if we have built into our lives the joy and understanding of life—not temporary experience of a few years, but life eternal.

VISION 

THE LETTER XII 

December 26, 1935 HE VOICE. He was already here. I saw a sort of trail of light to the dear ones who are always in your atmosphere-husband, mother, father, all so happy; there seems to be never a shadow on their faces, only joy. 

THE LETTER. Certainly, I may have failed to tell you of the light of peace that is always in my thought as I leave the group of our friends, helpers, guides, and come to you with the fullness of my peace to-day. 

Another year of our morning hours is past, for when you come again the new season begins for you and me.

To me that means nothing: no end of days or months of winter whiteness nor June roses means an end of anything that we have known, for there is the sweet consciousness of the full life we have each lived and bound together with the unfolding of experience and knowledge. It is as if I came out of the days of dreams and desires to find myself arrayed in the beauty of these days and able to say to you that most of these desires have been given life through these communings with you and the light falling on every experience that revivifies and relates itself to the future.

Without pretensions I have enough sense and judgment to know that the avenues of interest to us that I have seen lead us on toward the experiences that must be ours if we do not stand still and strive to tear apart all the roses of yesterday, to-day, and forever to find the heart of the unfolding loveliness of life. It is that quality in your mind that is always mine as I come into the

VISION 

69 spiritual contacts and find in the higher minds the purpose and desire to be sure of my authority in these truths. I give you a vision that also includes a mind that is, or should be, able to distinguish between the hopes we hold for the future and the realities we have made sure of as we move onward-mark that word "onward," not upward, forward, backward, but onward as the worlds that move constantly in the order of life among other worlds we do not yet name. So we—you and I—in our persistent powers and proper placing of our experiences move forward right into the orderly orbit and feel no confusion, no dreaminess, no vain imaginings of what may be; and there has never been any question in my mind about the right attitude for me to take; that I am still in a world of active life, that I would tell the world that fastens itself to an untruth-an error-that the ceasing of bodily activities in the world we have been familiar with is true, but that we find another body that we are certain of, without any realization of changes, but precisely as we are able to use hands and feet and larger body of physical life, we are able to see that (he smiles) we are just grown up. 

I like to think that I have grown taller, stronger, broader in every scope of seeing, understanding, and using the power I did not know I could havé. Perhaps we cling to the physical life because we are not able to discover that we have potential powers to be used only when we have dropped creeping on the floor of life and walk like superior beings in our added powers. 

To have you ask questions about God, and Jesus, as you would about Socrates, Confucius, all those people who have left a record that we have been interested in

VISION 

because of the wisdom they possessed, would mean that you might quite as well question me about the celebrated and wonderful people of all the ancient kingdoms of which there is no definite story yet given to the world. They had their little day; they sat under palm and olive, they gathered their bits of the world into small places and built them anew that they might give their definite understanding of what it means to have their materials at hand. One cited an experience on 

Mount Olympus, one stood and swept his audience into a thrilling moment of oratory. They pass, but their experiences have given us the idea that I hold this hour, that each one gathers something that he places on the altar of his day and hour, and begins to understand the processes and power and possessions that belong to the intellectual world, and are never lost as time reveals the treasure that mind had given. Do we see Caesar and 

Napoleon and that type of hero move across the book of ages and do we measure that influence that flows through the story of ambition and worldly offerings that fail in the last hour? It is as true as of political and unworthy ambitions that worldly power gives, and only as we move onward and find that the world has a circumference, and in another widening circle we add the spiritual gifts of a diviner life, are we assured that all may be made a stepping-stone without divisions that blind and hide all efforts made by mankind. This I accept. I cannot hide the effects in the world beyond and around us, and I will not and may not be a judge of the awards or virtues of the races that are bound together by their acceptance or rejection of what comes into the knowledge that we all desire to know-where we are

VISION 

71 bound and when we may arrive. Perhaps our experiences in the life we were most familiar with gave us something that we prized and held as sacred truth which will not die and we can never lose; yet death has given us a glimpse of what we may leave as the gift of our day. I think I have always from the first felt what might be our message to a world that treasures love, honesty, and truth. I am always aware, when I stand here blinking my eyes in the dazzling light that we have found, that I may be giving to our own the blessed fact of a message from those who have apparently passed away. To leave to our dear ones, old and young and yet unborn, a word that may help them to the understanding which we have come to realize is the key to the unfolding life of high and noble companionship in our years of continued activity is to feel that our opportunity gave us the words to say and the spirit to commend them to those we would satisfy in the search for an answer to the prayers of those who would know God. This is not the way we would choose to give the message, word by word picked out in a moment of purpose to know for ourselves and yet how better could we do it than by the positive statement of what has happened to us? The dearest possession of life is the existence of those we love preserved in faith; goodness or evil is often a matter of little consequence at the moment but it becomes a part of the records of life and must stand the test of their use and power. A man in the position of having sinned in the blackest of crimes who will not confess or feel any stress of effort to overcome the result of his sin is in the same state as if holding to a peculiar sense of innocence that will not release him from the destiny of

VISION 

sin itself. The destiny of sin is death—always remember that, and remember it for the message it gives to those who are eagerly seeking to have ignorance accepted that they may be free from death. With the honest confession of a sin that has brought its own destructive element into the life of a person of either sex there begins the real death to the sin that has been held in bondage for some time, and the restoration of mind and body with the redeeming power of truth gives back the maturity and understanding that moves onward toward realms of unfolding. Dare we say that sin is ever a power to hold ignorance as a factor realized and part of the performance of restoration? As I understand it, ignorance does not excuse the breaking of the law. We do not like to feel that there is no way to protect one from the ignorance that kills, and here we who know the law and repeat it over and over again are yet so sympathetically aroused by the pain and sorrow we wish to pardon and relieve that you and many of your wise and noble leaders in the affairs of life are ever pleading for some way to bring consciousness of sin in the same degree of power that gives the desire to restore that which has been destroyed and give the peace of forgiveness for sin. Do you know that it has been a stumbling-block all my days. how to protect those who were sinning, not through any definite idea of bringing to the world any hardness, but a bit selfish, a little way on the wrong road, and we know such a case and cannot lift a restraining hand? I always had a feeling that if I had sinned through ignorance I would prefer to pay the price and begin againthat was a personal feeling. I have shed tears over one who had through bad influences and companions

VISION 

73 brought definite sorrow to those about him. I have stood by now and then with the feeling that I had left undone something that might have saved the lad; and in any way

I think the truth about life after death is a very good start to make toward redemption from selfishness and sin. At times my anger rises with unspeakable horror, when so many of those in a life that has held no small opportunity for honor and joy and love are in the world cornered by the revelation of unknown sin and hoping to be pardoned. Those are debts to the world only to be paid by acceptance of the law that binds us all together.

This serious message comes from seeing in the nations the desire to escape the justice of their fate, with the thought, "If I may stay undiscovered I may do all 

I wish for myself and my own." For our children, yours and mine, and the friends who have had sorrow through their children, I look to the simple words, "There is no death"-there is the hour when one stands before the world. Every hour is the end of a period of life. With all the books on crime, sin, theology, all the illuminating studies, there is but one word that brings an answer to what comes, and in having that word distinctly and bravely expressed is the hope I hold for the step toward taking responsibility for deeds-and I have near me the step of causing others to be happy by bringing to them the joy of goodness.

LETTER XIII 

January 2, 1936 

HE LETTER. They who are always measuring life

TH by time and its associations are never free to catch the radiant gleam of a life that is in the realms surrounding the earth and presumably all the other planets that are observed by those who measure stars. That was my thought as I came into the path that leads to the little shrine and I had a feeling that one has often while on the paths of a daily walk in woodland or on hillsides, that the feet pick out the footpath, feel the pebbles, the little growing trees, all the impeding conditions that hinder progress or bring a stumble now and then on the way to the heights. This gives us the self-reliance that we need in our journeys to and fro, not dependent on the paved paths and bushes trimmed that nothing may deter our passage. And that is precisely the way I am sure of my right and beaten path as I come to meet you in this precious hour.

The exultant note of a new year binds me to the old memories, and there is the sound in my ears of foolishness and laughter and gayety of the silly activities of those who would spend their energy and make business good; again I am hearing the bells at midnight, and the solemnity of the new year stills all that beating noise of the lower world, and I dream my dreams of what the years will hold, with you in that same responsive temper of the coming of a new opportunity which may be ours. 

I sat long, silently thinking of what the last year has left for our joyous expectation of what is to be. There

VISION 

75 is never a moment that stands still for our coming but we hasten to greet it as the next step in experience which will bind us more firmly to the eternal and everlasting life. To me it would be a tremendous blow to all 

I hold dear to turn backward the steps and find the old doubts, old questions standing at the milestones of the past. With you to take my hand and move through the quiet shadows of the night toward the glory of the morning I am as one who has conquered all doubts and come into the places where are the realities that we love. Yes, we love them for they give us some understanding of the super-consciousness which comes now and again to those who have been made aware of another existence coöperative with the life which I call the normal bodily life of an unfolding soul. As I know you-and truly I think I always knew you in the superior quality of superconsciousness and it always gave me something which I did not possess in the degree and certainty of your faith, which became knowledge almost before experience gave you the truth-it seemed to me that it was a quality of leadership that was unnamed and not recognized as a probability that now is ours. I felt it was inheritance, or a gift that has come through the ages to you. Now I see you as like a heavenly child whose eyes catch something beyond the small litters of life and finds in every shadow that it brings a new light of beauty to roses growing in a garden or the wonder of night whose beauty only stars reveal. I never attempted to give you any distinctive place, as I might some friend or acquaintance or relative, by making distinctions between this one and that, one having charity, another a different kind of virtue-you were just you-

VISION 

no one in the world to approach you in any thought I gave you. That is the understanding to-day that I have of our wonderful relationship—or experience. No comparisons, no thought even of any classifications of your qualities; they were distinctive; and the fact that I always knew that and always kept you in my thought as the one who expressed a perfect personality individualized and unafraid, and responsive to a higher life, makes me aware to-day that I was on the way to that perfect affection and devotion and love that comes but once in the lives that are practically dual. I have once referred to those soul-mating beliefs that some men have absorbed and believed in and yet are not able to separate them from the affinitizing influences of body and soul. 

The body has its place as the temple of the soul, but very often there is apparently no soul within when the temple door is open; we see that everywhere, at all times and places; the temple is wonderfully built and fashioned and may be at any hour revealing the spirit that actuates certain very lovely traits of character-then there is the desolation when it is discovered that the soul has not yet entered into the temple. Most of the difficulties come from the insistence by one or the other that the body is the domicile of the soul, and then the wise man or woman makes the best of the situation and turns to the finer and diviner gifts that are only dimly perceived, seeking to endow his life with new perspectives, and he is often able to move forward without the divine afflatus which is the ennobling gift of the love we knew and, feeling it is his to be somewhere, sometime, he finds it sitting in his own soul waiting for the revealed truth of his companion. Does that seem to you a

VISION 

77 strange and unrecognized flame of eternal love? I think not. I think you will see just what I see in our hopes and treasured experiences: our desire for the immortality of the divine within and the shedding of the feathers of the life of flight and clothing our superior bodies in the glory and majesty of immortality.

There came to you the first word I have used about the super-consciousness that I believe has always been the gift of some people in this way; one may be aware of the dust and annoyance of the railway or steamer that takes us from this continent to another. Lindbergh alone between sea and sky and sea, steering himself and his craft to the desired destiny of his journey, came nearer to the flight of a soul than one realizes unless taking into the mind the picture of a boy with the superconsciousness of a power that would give him victory over what had seemed unconquerable space, alone and without the usual chart and compass. That is but a thought of mine, but it represents to me some people like you who do, though not wildly, take chances, risks, and adventures, and either come safely back or are lost on the way, but without apparent calculation, only with that inspired consciousness that fits into courage and the purposes of life, making for that one who accepts super-consciousness a very individualized soul. I always felt that about you. When I, without thinking at all of giving you my word of trust in you and your conception of life, did nevertheless give you all; nothing was hidden and I never found you lacking in appreciation of that trust I had in you. 

Words were not necessary. We say to a friend, "This is my private opinion; please accept it as private." You say to your son, "This is my

VISION 

desire for the fulfillment of your best opportunities in life”—it is accepted as a confidence and becomes a silent monitor of every deed that is done. To you my thoughts and my prayers were given as if I were talking to God. I gave them to you with the desire to have the clear light of your mind and spirit given me to help me to understand. I was not such a bad pupil, after all, for when the effort to be sure that the indestructible desire to make no mistakes was mine, immediately when the door opened I saw within the temple the soul that binds me to you forever. That the other bindings of other lives are still ours gives us still the exclusiveness of a far-distant and wonderful pilgrimage where nothing is lost, nothing fails, and we are still grateful for all that has been given us by those who have walked with usno, not grateful; but happily conscious of their dear companionship and their interests that are unfolding and will always be a part of our unending journey through endless life.

That is all I wished to say about this; but through this super-consciousness which has been your endowment and through my recognition of it and our interests in those who are a part of our companions in life and death and eternity, I have lately been made aware that some of that quality that holds in the exultant state of the knowledge of truth is transferable-understand this! When your children are against a problem of the physical life that is like a fighting corps of soldiers of the merely physical world, they do not surrender. If your daughter must decide what is the best school for her child, she is able to receive from the same sources that you and I receive these days, to get some direct,

VISION 

79 definite answer to her need. True of all the children; it is the treasury from which they draw on the account left to them and make use of it even when not realizing just how. Your son-I like to say mine, too—he stands on a plane a little distant from the "hoi polloi" of politics; he is able even in the midst of relationships so dear and fine to confront these perplexities as well as high problems of statesmanship. He stands alone. He reads; hoping to find some solution of the problem before himthe book falls from his hand, the papers are laid aside. 

He asks no help from those gone, his mind is in poise; and down from the sources of light and power-name it God, He works through minds-down from the sources of light and wisdom, comes streaming the thought of what may be breaking out of darkness, showing the evil that has gone to schemes in the dark; there comes a new light, something we never can explain, strength for right and joy and peace and a world of unfolding influences. When there comes a hostile blow by some disreputable circumstance in the management of affairs the whole world hears of the ignorance, superstition, and greed. Superstition works in the super-sensitive mind, not the super-consciousness. I recall great rulers with. superstition: the German emperor and Napoleon. Some of our own politicians and statesmen have been superstitious; they pass away with some super-consciousness above the superstitious feeling of those who expect to wrest from the influences piled against them a success that will carry them through the world that knows its fallacy. Before you slip away to me in our day or mine there will come such an overwhelming turn of affairs that these will find themselves prohibited in the effort to

VISION 

rule your city, your homes, your bank account, and your friendships. I give you this as prophecy of the coming year. Some day I hope to have some evidence that I can give of the reason for failures in this announced importation of the prophecies of world affairs-so many to die by flood, so many to be lifted from poverty to wealth, all these wild statements that are part true, part imagination, part wrong interpretation of the signs they read from astrologers, witches, prophets of old days, and the ones who read as if they were posted for a bulletin for their use. Seriously, I never took much stock in such amusements; good judgment may bring something like a definite statement of certain conditions, but the flexibility with which they are transferred to make the prophecy quite unusual did not make for me faith in them. It is better so, for if they did not fail so often we might find ourselves surrounded by people who would rather have the statement given than judgment reckoned for and with. I find this the day for my beginning this new year because I am beginning my year with you. 

Some people are thrilled by a china cup, others by the stars. My love includes everything; it seems to be all-yet I remember those at home. It seems to include everything; yet so much is excluded, for they have not yet felt what we feel. Suppose I looked out to-morrow and saw you walking toward me. I should be filled with a joy I had never known. I might stand still to take in the reality of our meeting. That won't be so you can't leave your orbit and come into a lighter sphere, but I shall have your hand in mine and say, "I am here,” and those other dear ones coming close will know that for a moment we must be alone-and all this beauty of life

VISION 

81 where we are will be full because our own. Nothing like a dark river with a boatman here when I came-no darkness—the "fog in the throat" is just poetry—a bodily expression; it was like opening a door that had been closed to keep silence around the moment of death, and when the door opened there were the dearest of our lives, the one dearest looking into a surprised face aware of strength and power never known before. I speak for their place when you come.

LETTER XIV 

January 9, 1936 

HE LETTER. I could wait only with impatience to

THE 

greet you this day, so much has come to me of light and life and beauty in these thrilling moments of consciousness of the way the spirit-people have been able to add to the lustre of life and reveal the work I might well have expected would be done if life continued after death. The feeling that held me in suspense when I began to realize that work had its part in the life where I am gave me something beside the desire to know what the end would be to our careers. There is evidently no end, and I have been wondering why this side of the question has not been stressed by those who desire to see life go forward instead of looking for eternal peace in a workless paradise. As I have been brought into the realities of life in the world where you are so alive to the need for some inflowing power of the divine and understanding, I have wished that I, too, might have been less eager to have some answer to my prayer, and taken in

VISION 

faith what was given you-not only an answer, but a part of the problem that faces the world at this moment. I think that the very definite help that I see given to those we love for redemption from fearful calamities facing the future of all the world we have known and loved is one of the miracles of life which I missed by the work which was so appealing to all of us-I mean yours and mine as each took a place in civic affairs and became more interested in government and the officialdom of the world about them; there was always in my mind a purpose to clarify and give each the opportunity to work out his own destiny. Now as I see what a terrible strain is put upon a few who hold the standards of life high, I see that there is no lessening of interest and no bowing to the gods of chance, but a desire for right and justice and high statesmanship that runs political influence into the ground, to fade away in the uselessness of desires that are only to rule; ruin follows the rule in the cities and countries we are interested in; we fall into the past as into the worn-out citadels of a lost world. I have been made aware of the rising group of younger men, finding that there is nothing to give in the way of sanction for misrule and dishonor, but all—all for the high honor and distinction of truth that binds us into the universe of service for mankind. I found that the people, those who have been awakened to the danger of the world that they have never before found in such a dimension of evil-must be strengthened by us as we see the need. Personally, I retain all the hope and joy that service brings. First I am made aware of the definite need of the contacts sustained by us through you and these young men and women who rise to higher powers

VISION 

83 than they ever could have reached if the danger had not been discovered. 

THE VOICE. Your husband is looking at you and the communicator; he is just like a rock; he stands as he always did for the thing he believes useful and right; not at all afraid of what may come; he has his arm around our boy, all using their full intelligence, not just praying; and going from one to another, your husband in contact between you and your boy. He's never deceived, always deciding things for himself in his moments, days, and hours of concentration to see what is needed, as if anointed to do a great work. His father stands beside him and me. These people on this side choose those standing like Lincoln for one immortal expression changing the colors of the world. Your boy is particularly endowed, a child of Right anointed to do a great work. All these are bound together in a wonderful group of power. Your wise man is eager to join in this work for the world and for God. This boy of yours listens for the voice of the Right as from a window whence light falls. I saw that his father inspires him. When I said so to the father, he answered, "He goes me one better." He puts his arm around you and whispers, "We are all working together that we may give to one who is able to stand before the world and stay calm, as seeing beyond the small things of life and smiling back at duty, giving strength to those who would falter if he were not so firm! [He moves away that you may go on.]

THE LETTER continues. I find that the sustained influences give to me a confidence that must not be betrayed by the promises that are mere statements of

VISION 

the truth that there are no separations after death, but go beyond this fact that we-you and I-have demonstrated through our communications that there is life, consciousness, and understanding, and come to the conclusion that if this is so, and nothing is done to aid in this service that we have held dear, we must either have been degenerate after death or not progressive in our opportunities. I am not less a man because I am no longer concerned about my personal place in the universe, and I am very confident that no one stands still after death. It's a queer thing twanging on the old strings forever. There is possibly a purgatorial peace that comes to those who have been taught to believe that the day of judgment is deferred, and that defects may be repaired by virtue of prayer and penance—but as far as I have it that only lasts a little while, for the desire to get out into a new life is ever the whip that leaves purgatory as a covered bridge through which men cross the river for the new and better life. Purgatory is a mental state and one does not stand idle in its shadow before glimpsing that life that waits, and the feet soon find the path that all come to eventually. If there are lost souls, I have not seen or known that there is a place for intolerable torture—it is an intolerable idea, and the safety of the world is to rid it of the idea of eternal punishment for sins they perhaps did not recognize. Now for some of the anathema of these political sinners, and I say that with the full meaning of sinner -the crushing, crashing blow that comes to them when there is revelation of their selfishness, their posing, their selfish announcement of the power they possess; anger and defeat follow; it is the revelation the sinner makes

VISION 

85 of his importance and assurance, self-inflation that gives the picture to the world, enlarged and emphasized. It is a symptom of insanity which does not easily surrender to the captivity of asylums but goes on with its own destruction in the field of its plans for the power to rule empires or states, or society. The calm and intrepid and useful mind that holds its gift for the service of the world is in our thought as we break the ideals of the thoughtless or selfish or ignorant followers of power to hold in subjection anything or everything that stands in the way of will. I could very well follow on to the national capital for the explanation of much that has brought the loss and the pain of losing integrity and justice; yet so near our own hearthstone stands the same demon of desire, and I find my watchful eyes are very useful to me now. I shall not rave over the creeds and beliefs of people near at hand, for beyond and above the personal tastes in worship and performance of the rights of each to accept and perform religious obligations as he sees it for its use, there is the honor of truth and standards of morality untouched by belief, but belonging to the mind trained in honorable pursuit of right and honor for the nation to which he belongs, Jew or orthodox may he be, catholic or heretic in the beliefs of religion; he may climb from the forms of his performance in the sacred rites of worship to the nobleness of world brotherhood and its protection from inroads of poor ideals and become the light of the world in the high sense of the light of reason and reasonable contacts creative of good for all. This, too, is an accomplishment of unfolding life; one does not have to die to gain it but one may die to an issue or grow to a new

VISION 

height that reveals all below in this day when boldly, defiantly, the seat of service is usurped by one who has no more desire to be useful than what moneys he may find lying in his desk or quietly slipped into his pocket unobserved and unquestioned. In the jump from one place to another, like a man caught in a river filled with the debris of past errors, there is the slipping footstep which brings his downfall soon or late. 

Is it not strange that now when I find my past a help to my vision of the present through your dear and useful companionship in the world about you, I am now and again aware of that solvent power which communication gives me, and am ever and always conscious of what you are to me. I intended sometime to tell you this in the words of a citizen in another sphere of life, but always I needed to say over and over again how you are a part of every hour, every day, every joy, and every hope to me. I often find that I neglect to give you all that I feel. I have said sometimes that I could write and talk continuously and still leave so much unsaid. The limitation of communication is not the limitation of time and energy. I would like to say just now that I have never felt any physical limitations in these hours; 

I did not always feel that I was in the presence of a physical body that might resent the long intrusion from the normal experiences of life; it never occurred to me that it was an intrusion, it was so normal and so easy that I did not think of a trance as anything abnormal any more than or as much as-sleep when one lies down to unconsciousness and rest for the body, and only when I heard words that were spoken in regard to the trance induced for the better inflow of ideas from the

VISION 

87 dead, did I take seriously the idea that there was a change of identity which gave me the freedom to speak of all the intimate details of my love for you as a person that responded in joy to our opportunity. And I believe that I have never left in the mind or heart feeling of the entranced one anything that could be harmful or deleterious to the person, the room, the house, or the work. 

I believe that love is creative, and the love with which I bind up my little bunch of ideas gives back something that is happiness and comfort without any explanation of why this is so. But happiness is like any power in the world, it creates all that we need to make us strong and useful, and those ideas that come to some after the experience of destructive talk come from some deep unhappiness of unsolved problems or unholy desires. Now none of that for us. I bathe in the atmosphere of the true and everlasting love that binds us together and to God. And if there is anything harmful in the contacts between you and me and the power of a spirit group trained for this service, I have yet to discover it. It is like every gift of life-if love is at the heart of the experience life becomes love itself and gives more than is taken away by action. You will find many people who worry about what may be happening to one who is shut away from society or worthy pursuits by this service. 

Smile, and reply that there has been no thought of sorrow or loss in our communications to any of us, and the desire to find some reason why the matter should not be considered a normal and natural expression between people in all spheres of life in society work is that there is nothing in society work unless it is a spiritual society; there is nothing to take away from opportunities that

VISION 

are useful because one does not make bread or jackets for the poor when the neediest are those who have never known the glory of love everlasting, and so on through all that seems denied, to ride or go sight-seeing, mingling with the ordinary or other engaged world. But this is the eternal joy of union with the spiritual and tender and purifying love of the eternal life.

All this could be added to, but I only give it to you as an answer to those who feel that there is a robbery committed when one's mind is borrowed or lent for the better understanding of life in higher spheres. When it comes to much that is really in the world one would be glad to come away for an hour that life may be broadened for some other.

Now you see how impossible it is to separate our interests. I look at “our boy” and stand smiling beside him; there is a rare gift of love between you and him. 

The love that his father has for him is reflected in your life and mine and I lift my voice and say, "This is your task" he says, "I leave my precious one to you to give what you can of the eternal truth.”

THE VOICE. A great big R comes on the table-oh, it's W, the "little lad." Your friend says, "The W's are very much blest by this son, who has become so useful to many of us, a kind of universal spirit, he comes to his mother and also to his 'dad.' He is quite wise; many people come to consult him." Your man puts down a large R. There is more but I can't read the rest.
