LETTER XV 

January 16, 1936 

HE LETTER. Again we are at our trysting place, and

THE 

all the hours between are fragrant with the flowers of beauty that bloom in these moments so sanctified by our own joy and hope. Hope is still a part of all my thinking in the new combination of life and its possibilities after the day is done and the morning of the next day brings light on the processes of the life continued. I am always aware of so much that is in the world around you, its opportunities for larger and finer service to those about you make me feel the importance of every bit of knowledge of my life as it now appears, for it is our treasury of all that we may possess of power. 

Even if there are seasons of less perspective and more of the present entanglements with the burdens and trials that beset in the world of uncertainties, there is always memory of the part we have had in making light where darkness had settled down. Then it is my constant joy to know that with your hand in mine, your thought like a shining thread of gold woven into every desire we hold as one-your mind clear as the sunset bell ringing out over the world—I know as a call to prayer and thanksgiving to God that we are still one, not separated by any event. Though you were in the trials of situations that come to many we have known, it should not separate but bind us together as one always clings to the hallowed hand and thought in hours of stress. All this 

I repeat in one form or another in my letters to you, for it is the most precious and helpful expression that I

VISION 

leave for those who have not yet become aware of the fidelity of love in the future life. I do not take it too lightly nor, I hope, with too much solemnity. A marriage is a festal occasion and a funeral may be the promise of a revival of old dreams, some vision of new joy, some realization of the completion of one form of conscious love and its constantly unfolding influence in life. I have longed-yes, longed-for your presence with me where I now abide, for there is always in the heart a feeling of desire for actual contact that makes the relationship wonderfully illuminating in the spoken word. I sometimes—not like a romantic boy or a man grown old in memory-but just as I would have been cheered by our years of faith and fellowship-had the satisfaction of looking into your eyes instead of depending on the correspondence that made all the difference in my life. It may be that those years gave us insight into that marvellous power of expression without bodily presence-I sometimes think so that it was a training in expressing one's deepest feelings as if there were no finer diviner experiences that might come in the nearer neighborhood of the body we love.

This is the way I have a little satisfaction through an imaginative process. I draw to my side a chair, and without any feeling of the uselessness of such a mood I begin to think of you as actually in that chair beside me, that I may touch now and again the hand that has been such a minister of love and service to me. Just think of what that hand has been, through the years, to me, and now after the change is still my faithful friend, giving me the result of these hours in a form I may hold forever as a specially bound book, my treasure of all

VISION the life we are living. In that room where I feel most at liberty to think out through the power of prophecy which love always uses, the life we are to have together, there is a picture of you-all the beauty and fine power that comes out in your many, many experiences. I imagine a smile now and then illumines it, or a strong and emphatic expression of yourself, full of the independent spirit that rises to a height which makes it an imperative power—all these, the presiding genius of my heart's desire I find. I would not have you one bit different. I love you just as you are, and I am thinking of the soul and the body that holds it. And as I turn to the portrait -not merely a picture that charms and holds one in a kind of rapture over its loveliness, but a portrait of a lady I love (he smiles)—“the portrait of a lady”—I have written in letters of gold-it is enough, though I often add in my mind,"The lady"; there is no other to make a comparison. [THE VOICE. He throws his head back and laughs.] Is that enough of a confession? Where shall I find a place for the friendliness of others? In an outer gallery where they may come out and be admired when the day arrives for that experience? 

This is so personal, this glimpse I give you of my days between these messages of the spiritual life that crowns the life of the earth-experiences.

I could rise in wrath in denunciation of the situations that are constantly arising to stir our spirits in defiance of laws, in the insolence of the wicked, but that would be not more than I find many others doing now; so for me there is this sweet silence against the open defiance and it is peace in our heart's joy to add to the world's cup of sorrow and not fill life with too much pain. What

VISION 

we may do is far better than for me to point out errors, sins, blasphemies, and the whole tirade which some men spend their lives in making. Here in the peace of our knowledge, not merely hope, not the inner consciousness of trust in the Creator of all that is, but in knowledge, the door opens for our knowledge that we may speak mostly of that, and that will give us new continents with the rapturous beauty of knowing that they, too, belong to the Infinite one. Right here I have been thinking much of the tyrannical belief in tortures of the future to punish sin. I thought one day when I looked over at you in my happy fancying of your presence, and without any particular reason except to have you verify my conclusions-"What," I asked quite softly, perhaps without much reverence, "do you think of hell?" Then

I waited, and I knew that you had the belief that from some delusions that were held hell was born. Without carrying the idea too far, we must hold the Infinite one lacking as a judge if he could not hold his seat in a world where justice is important. I have never seen any light of a fire that burns and does not destroy but only intensifies the sense of injustice. One of the observations that gave me a feeling quite undesirable yet quite human was when a great preacher was asked, "Do you believe in hell-fire?" He replied, "I have seen some very good material to make one. "That's about it—we see these terrible people who have no sense of their own sin but would escape the result of it, and we are wondering what may become of them after death.

This was the result of my thinking. There is law in the world after death-law that has not lawyers, but judges, who have been following the laws of the earthly

VISION life, and have become wise and as far as possible helpful in adjusting those whose lives have been lawless. You will know why some of your dearest ones who are interested, or would be ready to tell of their desire to move forward after death in as orderly a world as the earthly one, do not for a moment believe that a judge who has been led by the love of law and order would feel very much at home if all the sinners they have seen and tried to help slipped on their wings and became the fearless propagators of their régime. That is not just a humorous line in my message, but it is that curious method of adjusting everything by what one wishes may come after death. If there are streets and gardens, and stars that move in the order of the infinite wisdom, if there are saintly people who come into the new life as a pathway to further saintliness, if there are little children growing to maturity and fullness of life, is it not possible to believe that those who are sent out into the deathless life are to be able to find death a redeemer? I think that wise men like your own group find a place to minister to those whom they would help, and methods to protect their sinless ones from invasion of the mob and unworthy ones into the lives that they find. So they are not left without the protection of law. I think that it is through my own effort to know the truth that I bring this word to you to think of sudden death and endless power for the instant salvation of one who has given nothing to life but its joy—that is impossible. Prayers said as one approaches the punishment inflicted for crimes that are unspeakable make no difference in the end that gives the vision of one's own sin, and I know that the law-makers who seek the better way to real

VISION 

repentance are able to find the soul aware of its own outrages, but not of belief in any prophet of Christ-like spirit even if said to have died for them. 

This is a very solemn yet significant statement of the way those who are below the standards of morality come into the understanding that God is law-unchangeable, everlasting, and brings the orderliness of life as a precious heritage.

Now that I have brought this to you, I am relieved myself. I have always, since I began to write to you, seen that artists, if true, painters, if lovely, singers who might bring the joy of music-all those whose gifts lifted from the common and unimportant matters of life, might very well help create a heavenly atmosphere. I have wondered sometimes about book-keepers, servants, those who sought place for the money or opportunity that they lacked, and in an indefinite manner I wondered what they would eventually find in the world where I have found beauty and love and trust and all the lovely things of life-high mountains, their summits touching the stars gleaming in glory above the world of penury; rivers bounding to the open sea, a bright, exquisite sense of beauty in its flow between banks buried in beauty; little paths leading to little shrines in woodlands, and the forests of buildings that make up big cities, violets brightening the ground, and all the precious things in sea and on earth-I found all these, they were what I had loved. You'll find them there, and kind hands, friendly voices, and all that will speak on the problems about them. All this I expected if there were such means of expression as life had had before death, and I did not think so much about what might become of the man

VISION 

95 who met death as a punishment. Now I feel he does not go into a lawless kingdom, for often to the side of one who has been in the dark there comes one of love and life in a revelation that we know will eventually bring all souls home to the infinite life where love is the light that shines forever.

To be able to say this at the time when children are victims, old men and old women are victims, also young, helpful and needed ones gives me something to add to the virtue of the message that is constantly revealed as the way, the light, and the end. We come to an end of sin eventually through service-to the end of the life of sin through sympathy and understanding of the ignorance—not a mental process that books and study may help only by power, but to the souls of the nations that march when the infinite wisdom fails us in our understanding of the need. I will try to take up again some definite proof of the power we have as individuals to express ourselves on the same tone at some other time. 

I have this to be a proved fact, as I have tried to express my own identity and its new approaches to truth, and I find men of ability and of acquired gifts playing with pebbles on the beach as if to prove that pebbles and rocky heights all belonged to the world of experiment, to prove that granite grew in the world about them. They exclaim over a small gift with evidence that in the mind that relates them is a responding quality, another mind relates them to a power recently discovered. It is queer that they do not take findings of the old oracles, old seers, and the old prophets and finding their likeness to the modern psychics, mediums, sensitives in the world, find their task has been per-

VISION 

formed long ago and they are only re-systematizing the same gifts that have always been in the world. They are spending precious hours and opportunities to tell the world that there is a power. I think that to have a definite person writing you a letter is extremely important. 

It is like the old message moving through the world delivered after months compared with the event coming across the world that Jerusalem was installing a water system brought from a long distance, and at the moment when the word came to New York the water was gurgling in the pipes. That is what surprises me—that all the scientific men get down on the ground together and take years to give the world the news that the dead are alive. When I came to you and found you thinking and adding to your knowledge of this, did I not know it to be a far superior method? Why now consider a demonstration the salient motion? But if we find a way to speak back in one experience there will be a thousand hands outstretched to catch that little motion from spirit-land to those in the world of the post-office. 

If I had not been able to write a full and complete letter I suppose I would have grasped at sending a thought, but here we are in a world binding itself together, no oceans, no seas, all these things free for service of the world—where a man sent a message lately that he was caught in the ice in a dangerous position, his experience thrilling beyond words-without wires or poles or a post-office, his message accepted by the world and his family. In a line with that experience is ours that I am giving and receiving from you, every thought as if you sat here. I know all your thoughts. You are not obliged to tell me. I look, I see, and am blest.

HE VOICE. I felt somehow humiliated when you went away last time. You see I have a friendship you in my own self. He was so earnest at the time that I did not notice your going. Now he puts his hand down to begin. for

THE LETTER. Remember that there are some moments in life when ideas are absorbing even to forgetfulness of the place and methods. So long I have felt this definite and sure sense of my life and the gift of these experiences-yours and mine-as if there were no limitations.

I think I should perhaps look about me more carefully and give you the knowledge as it comes, without too much precipitation of the usefulness of my observations to the world. There is just so much desire on my side to help in the old ways of adding to my purpose to redeem and save from confusion and the prospects of deeper sorrow that so often bound us to the effort like a professional matter; but deeper than all that is the love that gives us joy. I am not cast down by the furies of life of sin, of pain or sorrow; that is always an illumined faith which leads to our hours together. We know, and always did know, that there were terrible conditions around us; we were always ready to recognize that, but nevertheless the ideals we held gave us the joy of giving them out as possibly helpful and instruc-

VISION 

tive. Still, when I think sometimes-when I see even more plainly, as from the atmosphere like a mirror reflected, the conditions around those in the world who disturb the peace of my friends, that there are too many destructive relationships between the good and the bad, and I grow eager to give them the best I have of leadership in my own way, and find my constant companionship with you the most wonderful help and the heavenly joy in our constancy to the truth that these communications give us. Just now I feel the thrill of all the beauty and help that our interviews may give to those who are without the consciousness of the side-by-side, hand-in-hand life which may go on after death has come with the separation and its union of the old and the new.

Seriously, I am not given to the methods of giving testimony as one would give court evidence of the reality of life after death. It annoys me-just why I am not able to say-but it is at least an inconvenience to stop every little while to see if in some way I may add to the evidence of my identity. There is so much wonder and peace, and that ecstatic state of mind in having your bodily presence, so real to me, and your mind attuned to my own thought just as it was through the years more explicitly given in our life and friendship, that I am holding that experience as the dearest and best for us. I know quite well the desire of some of my friends to turn these hours into the same effort to prove the truth that the dead return, as they would try to prove a document for a will in a contest in court. Very well for them, and perhaps less difficult than to accept the finding of our court of love and appreciation and use of the knowl-

VISION 

99 edge that we are each living and moving forward to higher regions of life. 

I use the word "regions” as a new one in our words to each other. It seems rather indefinite to speak of realms as if they were cloud-like worlds without the definite divisions of power and expression. So I may give the word "region" a new meaning as we place a new directory of places and call it a new continent or land of strange customs or whatever terms we use as we think now of that South country not as undiscovered, but one whose goods have been stored and a new land opened up for us for knowledge and protection of things left that others may use. That is just what we are doing; we have discovered, not a realm, but many new communities formed by those who have passed on, made discoveries, and left for us something we may use—and take advantage of their discoveries to give our time and thought to the use of what we find. I find that the best and most helpful stock left for my use is love. I mean personal and individual love that may proclaim itself as a new-found material-yes, material-for better living, as any discovered quantity of something in the earth, the air, the sea may be taken into the medical world to give health and strength as the result of knowledge. We all have something so mysterious within us, something that finds only a few words in the language of the races to give it a body of power within-some call it God, others think of it in words they may not understand as they speak them: Principle of Life, Creator, and all the words that mean Supremacy, the Supreme Power. I think of it as the divine quality which springs into our days like the vividness of lightning which illumines the

VISION 

world about us, the darkest places growing bright in that flash of a second and then the shadows darkening again. To keep that illumination of the moment of a flash of lightning in the darkness of a storm is to find that it has been both terrifying in the knowledge given us and the most beautiful proof of a faith that out of darkness comes the light. My first acceptance after death when I saw you-yes, just as I saw your face, your form, your movements in that abstracted way when you tried to take up the thought of where I might be if I were conscious of the life I had found-I saw words as if they were spoken, words that you thought but without any determination to do so; they formed themselves into a prayer and then a desire, and then the activity of making an effort to find out about me—and such a delight; a new delight, not that I was conscious of you, but that I was conscious of your thought and your desire and your plan to see what you might see. I did not fall to my knees and thank God for the revelation; I might well have done so it never occurred to me. That must not be taken-my treasure of the treasures of life—as an act of infidelity to the years of lifeno-no-it was the absolute knowledge that we were bound together in a relationship superior to anything in our lives. That touched me with the beauty of all you held as a sacred faith and acceptance of our experience which I found so acceptable to me-the supreme moment of the acceptance of our lives in the loveliness of emotions that led us forward to lead those we love into the definiteness of the life after death. Everything else was incidental, nothing could be left out, but much could be added. It has not been a duty to announce my

101 findings in this court of love. I could not hinder the flow of my perfected faith. It was like a little stream far up on a mountain that flowed on and on down into that valley of pleasantness and peace, and carried with it all the power and added precipitant wealth of the high power of its beginnings into the physical life that is definite to touch, responsive to thought, and beneficent in its contacts, pure and unsullied by doubt. 

Of course I put my hand on yours in the hours when you are giving the body its rest. I touch your hair, I touch my fingers to your closed eyes and whisper to you because a whisper is the tenderness of speech, and I get so much of that fine sense of bodily contact that helps to the understanding of a dear companionship. I feel the graciousness of your presence, of which you are in a way unaware, but later in your slumber you find the same sense of presence that is mine. We are not like angels sitting in the sunlight of a higher and more elevated position-mark that word "position"-but there is the same feeling of restfulness after a walk through some scene enchanted because we are enchanted-by the flash of a winged creature of the air perhaps within a few steps to trace the sound of a little waterfall made by the same natural response that we find in walks anywhere. We do not look up every moment to find the source of our delight; I think we somehow understand it; it makes us part of all this life around us, wind in the face, tides home-turned, night in the sky-one can move out of the shadow. To be warmed by the sun is as precious as a band of diamonds that gives back the scintillating light till the sunlight finds its way to a crown. All this we are enjoying in all (he smiles) noc-

VISION 

turnal journeys, we come back refreshed—vigorous with spiritual energy that keeps us in harmony as we move on. Just to throw one's body on a bed that weariness may have its way and bring bodily strength, not at all like throwing oneself in the grass under a tree and blue sky-that is exhilaration that one does not need to find. 

And our little adventures-they are little, a few steps into the silence with the blessed light of love shining from your eyes into mine, and my whole soul depending on the exquisite joy that does not need sleep or memory or even consciousness of having walked into a new experience it is energizing and never depleted-the power that keeps alive a spiritual contact is the most useful in the world. Music lifts you away from the buzzing of people who clap hands as they may respond to a big band in the street. But you find something better, something like a star shining on a little lake-it grows down into the lake, and one might feel that one could walk out to it; it quivers in the clear water. So is it with your sense of music; you go away, sit down and find the star still shining. That's what I mean by attempting to remember experiences—they are like the stars shining forever for us.

Men of supposed science take incidental things which memory bears through the years of life and use them to prove immortal life. “I am of small stature”—“My name is--and all that is not real life but one seen by others, given to track down the life of the dead; nothing to interest except that a man proved that he was in the Bronx and had just come from headquarters. 

If they must have their heads opened up to memory and make that a proof of return, well and good, ours is in

VISION 

103 another stream of memory that must find its inspiration in present experience.

Now I speak of "regions" again, for it is a new thought with me. There is so much feeling about the relationship in viciousness and sin and its neighborhood of virtue, sorrow for the loss of it, that I began to look into the matter; it is as dangerous to the sense of presence to live in a city of bandits and murderers as if one were under the shadow of pestilence. How it is that many of our younger ones may be able to keep on in pursuit of knowledge and joy in the midst of such scenes as we are constantly aware of is very remarkable; but there is a vesture of virtue which is protective, and there is a regional process that is also protective, and the people over here with whom I am fast getting acquainted have certain powers by which they seek to impress upon those they love the finest virtues which clothe them. I don't want to stay too long, but I have been looking at the young king of England as he walked alone behind the body of his father, strong, tender, and true. Great companies of people who were royal in the best sense accompany him and help him; people look to him to balance some of the terrible things even over here, and help to balance affairs in your own town. 

Now for a word I must tell you before I let you go.

I was amused when you walked out on me last time. I understood, but I had to finish my sentence.

Sometimes I want to tell the world about right and wrong—but we live our faith into them when they can receive it, and my faith rests on pillars of love. I am always longing, but patiently, to have our words become useful to those who need them. I've never seen a hermit

VISION 

living alone with joy and peace where there is no love. 

I have seen Thoreau and have admired him much. If you would go on a journey by yourself, start now-you will arrive. A short journey with a path of love is better than mountain-tops without the joy that must be ours as we go on. 

THE VOICE. I talked with him one day. A lot of people were thrilled by your book, but still feel doubt. They are poisoned. We know the evidence that makes us strong is to give the helping hand in sorrow. Your husband came the last time. They both love you, but differently.

LETTER XVII 

February 6, 1936 [A VERY slippery day, but as I received none of the usual warning, I went in my car.]

THE 

HE VOICE. First I must give you a message from a lady—it is small writing-she very strong in purpose working for many things, always writing notes; a fine spirit, rather small, who went rather unexpectedly not so very long ago; very much interested in these letters, very great love for you. "To one who is dear to me 

I send my love and would that I might be able to do more." He stands beside her, very polite, but says, "Don't spend too much time." 

THE LETTER. To me these hours are so divinely interesting and inspiring that every time I leave this house with you and think of what has happened in this little room, it is almost unbelievable that you have been able to hold the understanding of the wishes and hopes I

VISION 

105 entertain for the greater light to dawn for us, and that you may have the awareness of my hand in yours-quite literally this is true-and often I am thrilled to think that the literal reception which I find so useful is just what it is. Mind, there is never a feeling that I have come down to you; there is never a sense of diving below to find the sweet atmosphere that gives me peace and joy and strength; the old idea that heaven was above the stars, above this atmosphere of the earth is so nonsensical that it takes me some time to realize that it was always taken to be a place above the ills and cares of life and only to be attained by the upward flight of the soul. Why—it is not at all like that to me. I find an open door at the end of a passage that leads from one room to another and walk toward it, and am with you; there's no idea in my mind of up or down, but only across a little threshold—there the light gleams, and I am with you and those I have loved, and I still desire to know what is their particular need of me. I find you beyond the threshold of your house, your work, your friends, and we are quite aware of each other—a sudden shaft of light, a swift feeling of having all-all -that I wish for-you with your mind awakened to the wonder of these experiences and this ever forward movement toward the real, tangible life—that is, it is a little more tangible to me than to you, but you have so much of the tangible life of the body that you do not miss anything that I have found, and it will become the same property of your new life over here with me as it is with me now. 

I decided that I had been saying much that might need illumination by picture or imagery which would

VISION 

give you much more than the outline of the definite experience of mine after death and add to your consciousness of the very definite life that I live now. But you had it almost before the thought was mine to give to you. You always were two steps ahead of me (he smiles), but I think that virtue that falls into the life of truth does not always add its gifts to those who are working to make everything fit into a groove that may help to measure the loss and experiences that come to us. Somehow, when I look into your mind and find there the same faith that kept me from falling into doubt too many days at a time, I suddenly realize that you catch the meaning of what I say almost before I know that I have expressed knowledge of my experience. It is one of the beauties of imagination. 

There may be similitude of pearly gates and amethystine light of the stars within the shadows of trees revealing forms of beauty that the average person never sees-hours of sorrow and deepening shadows may be revealed about us when the burdens of life are laid aside for a time and the new joys of creative experience come to us. That will be ours, when one day you find yourself with me, looking at some spot of beauty that I have held as a surprise for you-you who find in fluttering leaf or starry light the beauty that comes after the dark has brought the background, and I know all this will be ours, not because of the background, but of a clearer vision. I have found that the world around me is a printed page on which many messages appear. I am sure that it is my lengthened vision, and that somehow may be able to add to the vision you have of what is real where I am. There are, I believe, all types of won-

VISION 107 drous beauty and the power of fine, almost undecipherable, and minute beauties that grow under your observation, always there, I believe, always seen by someone, but created for those who find them first. 

Now about human experience. That is always a matter of individual growth and unfolding knowledge. Some experiences that come to little children are remembered forever as first received, and must eventually slip into the past and its new definite life of the adult, and come to be that acceptable evidence of eyes that have begun to see and observe the difference from childish impressions and the wisdom and value of the reality about them, all in the same measure of unfolding life in any sphere. I do not find men growing taller. I myself look to myself very much as I did in my early maturity. I feel much taller, not because I measure more inches, but because I see so much more than ever before. Mountains that were not accessible viewed from the distance of a lower level become visible as if I could look over them and see what I did not know was there. We climb to a height to discover that the mountains stretch along to greater heights, and we are not sure whether they shelve down to a lower level or still move on to greater heights in the Alps of spiritual life. It is a part of my desire to climb to some such heights with and to see if in some unsupportable manner the planets that swing in space may not belong to a chain of worlds that open up for our future lives in a supremacy of unfoldment and power that we dream not of. I have known of some astronomers who have come to believe much more than Lowell did, and yet at the time when he broke forth with his ideas of contact by some etheric methods, it you

VISION 

seemed like a dream of what might be and was accepted in some minds as a nightmare that should be placed among the curious phantasms of a mind carried out of its natural, orderly method in obeying the desire to know what could not be known. You know well this desire, just as you heard and knew many of the theories that have been floated to keep us all on the qui vive to know what might be undiscovered country.

Now you will see that we shall have no lack of interesting search for truth after we have discarded the effort to rally our powers for truth about the situation roundabout us. If the truth were discoverable by the desire to know, it would still be a question as to the validity of the statements. No-you know about as much as I do, and I know about as much as is important for me to know in order to work plans for those whom you and I love that they may discover their duty to the world in which they live.

I give myself to you in dreams. It seems that they are the very strength and power of my life, not only now but for all the days to come. Not idly do I say that I will love you forever. One may make that statement in youth before the truth is discovered that youth does not always hold to the dreams that bring the life of reality after a time. Changing, ever widening and deepening is life, and the effect of those changes is to produce something quite separate and apart from the early dream and the expected unfolding. So one must seek for deeper confidence of unfolding into the divine things that whispered belief to the dear ideal of youth. I am sure as far as I have been able to observe that very few of those hold to that sense of loving forever; the one

VISION 109 who lives finds that to be the realization of companionship, there are matings of a sort that hold one or the other which they hold as the precious possession of love, but not when there are such experiences as we have had and are having, and the soul becomes the wonder and the builder of the future. I do not think it would ever be abandoned or outgrown, for every experience that is true and spiritually accepted as a part of the unfolding life, binds together in beautiful and expanded life and moves forward toward mutual understanding and acceptance of the new dimension of love that is divine. It does not mean that two so bound in interest of purpose and life would have no other to share the joy they create in the same spirit as married people love to share the responsibility of children born to them through generations of those yet to be, so two in the high altitude of spiritual life wish to share their joy and knowledge and become of wider influence and love without releasing the splendid life that adds to the community of souls and never separates. Two young lovers who fly away to a distant home there to bill and coo in the delight of no one near with rights to any of their preferred bliss, miss in the days of that selfish heaven the heavenly joy of sharing with others what is important to be shared, adding that much to the heavenly joy eternal.

This is my acceptance of the life of two who are rightly united in the long years that bring their treasure to the treasury of life. [Sunbeam spoke of the love given to two in the same way and separated by death, the old question of which will be preferred.]

Yes-yes-I find them sometimes seeking one who

died in youth, and I have been glad that they could meet and understand and make no demands on the rights of a wedding certificate long ago. This comes to a very modern interpretation of one of the laws after death that should be taken if needed as a question to debate when they meet; it has nothing to do with the very common experience of finding that the physical life is impossible together under some circumstances with one who after a time of temporary exhilaration found that it was not the real companionship.

LETTER XVIII 

February 13, 1936 

HE LETTER. Nothing will give me greater confidence

TH in you and the work we are going forward with than to know that the human and desirable contact will be sustained by those you love and who love you. Not for the whole planetary system would I try to usurp the privilege of expressing that love that has been so marvellous for us. I do not see my way clearly in the purpose 

I hold to advance the kingdom of God in a world that is losing interest in the realities of life as we find them unless you and I make a trial of perhaps one in three or more as you wish, and divide our time and efforts in two types of communication. There may be such a thing as use for the evidential work even on our days of writing, and you will add to the spirit of personal identification just as you have done in the super-personal, and added to the educational and beautiful experiences of those who have never believed that the higher realms sent messages to the world. Perhaps these people were never more eager to receive advice about the life beyond them, and in such a case we will not set them aside but hope the evidence will give faith in the laws of life and the wisdom of finding a way to add to the joy of communication. I love thee no less if I try to give the personal message that love desires. All I may add of my own knowledge I leave as something to guide in the love of

VISION 

friends who pass through the shadow and leave open a door to return. There should be some way to help them in the effort to communicate. It is not easy to hold to some identifying experiences and give them accurately, and that is why I put up my hand to have the few that made themselves visible to me while working, and with an unspoken thought I felt they might come again after seeing what I was about, and catch the manner of the work by observation. So if it is right for me to plan ahead and give "Father" an hour I will do so one at a time without the fear of destroying anything I might wish to say; this will be, I believe, the best method for us—at least we may try. [Here I interpolated, saying, "I don't want that-I want to go on in the usual way."] 

Well, here I am, then; I'll be right back, because I believe that the feeling I hold for you and you for me is the power that gives knowledge of things that would not be known if someone did not take a stand and express this love, constant and everlasting. Nothing gives me that speed in the higher, finer, and diviner life like the consciousness of you. All nonsense that I could come to anyone else in the universe, for I have no love that is acceptable to any of them. The perfect joy of union is what I am interested in, which binds me to you night and day and all through time and eternity. I know this better than anyone perhaps who thought that the life lost at death was the greatest loss that would come, but found out that the days gave new experiences that filled their need as they built themselves into new situations. Not for us! I have been thinking much about the conditions in the world, dangerous, uncertain, unwished-for, and have been amazed that it was under-

VISION 

113 stood that evil has power over those who are in need of care and protection. Evil is powerful, but vulnerable.

Weakness is the weakness of selfishness, which tries to bring to its use the power that the good have, but never in the smallest way seeking to increase the good. That is only a temporary hold on the emotion of those who are themselves untrained. Safety lies not always in the purity of a life used or unused in some form of experience but in the protection of love which stands like an angel of light and keeps away the vicious ignorant and untrue. I have heard discussions by men who make for themselves a business of taking away evil spirits that there may be no more insanity and the loss of integrity that befall people in the world. Now that there are doctors, psychiatrists, who place the burden of crime on the shoulders of the dead, it is time that we who love each other—and God-should take a stand against the outrageous doctrine of picking off evil influences as if they were bugs on a rosebush; and I shall find a way to say this in terms that may be understood before the psychiatrists are the judges of the land as they are fast becoming. They do not always agree any better than any other followers of professions that should be helpful. Ministers make up forms of worship to fit their congregations and their habits and manners; doctors assume responsibilities that they are not fitted for, and lose now and then a celebrated case where life is important; and so on through all the professional lives of men and women. Astrologers give out, to those who would seek fortune, little maps of days and hours that become a compendium of the ills that may befall on Thursday at six or Sunday at ten, and the poor little victim is con-

VISION stantly fingering the map, and sits through hours of agonizing fear before he dips his pen and writes the letter that should be on its way. People like you and me may not realize to what an extent lives are held in the vise of customs that do not make them responsible for what happens, but bring the stars into court and add the dead ancestor to the list of evils. It has been one of my late experiences to find some of those who have been in the new type of hospitals for mental diseases. A good clergyman who has the divine inclinations to give opportunity for the better ordered lives of those who have either studied too much or too little may give relief by one's own acceptance and faith in the unfolding power of personal responsibility, and yet the task is so hazardous, the need so great, the number in need increasing as the wild rumors of the air, that the psychopathic hospitals receive any type of disordered mind and turn it away no better because the truth is that personal responsibility is still a law, it is the law of life every where whether before physical or after, and the order and power of overflowing love that will not cease and does not go away-that is the regeneration of soul and body. When one dances through the streets of Italy, a song on her lips, without the furnishings of wealth, and bursts into triumphant music as Pippa did with the note of "God's in His heaven: all's right with the world" -I adore that creation of a little fountain of light and joy: again and again since I have come into the glory of our unfolding possibilities in the years to come I have repeated those words, and to-day with my eyes open to the need of joy that laughs out the foolhardiness of so many who take chances with the laws of life,

VISION 115 

I put my hand in yours as if I were holding Divinity within my grasp. You, too, with your faith which has been an abiding power for me, must let me say to you that in my experience in this room I find answer to the wail of sorrows that would not be stilled, and the answer to the question, "What good are these spirit-people?" Certainly, if they have the power to bring joy at the very thought of love they are able to add a circle of light to heads that rest easily in the darkness of night with the consciousness of protection and helpful companionship.

I never leave you-never. When I am unable to trace my way back to you, as occasionally I go where I may see in operation some of the efforts made, I am able to turn and say (like leaving a little handkerchief where it may catch the light), "There she is," and then I rush later to your side, and feel that we are still one and that your letters, your reading, your companionships are shared by me, just as I try to have you share my knowledge. I just accept a part of all your experiences. 66 "Any questions?" So much is brought out in questions that the instructor himself overlooks-it makes a period. And let me tell you that not all the professors of psychology have the slightest idea what they are doing when they find that there are psychological effects but not all from the dead. A tower rising to the sky in a great city where people by thousands walk by through day and night would attract attention from one philosophical man who looks at the tower and sees a star always there, always a reminder of life beyond the city and the houses men live in, just as would put a magnifying glass over a finely printed book and suddenly the

VISION print becomes visible and intelligible—that is the way that when I see you reading some of the messages we have had together, it is suddenly enlarged by the inflowing thought of its power and beauty; and I say "Amen; my prayers have a point of expression through experience which leads us to the light of life.”

HE LETTER. You will know how some of the sweet

est messages may lie hidden in the thought and wait for the right moment. Now that I am sure it is your desire to let me have the perfect pleasure of going into the deep and blessed experience of feeling that we are part of a great movement in the world we both love and would serve, I am on the qui vive to add more to the evidence that the world is but an unfolding process of the individual and its relationships to all who are in the realm of its experiences. That means both good and bad, for we each have an influence far-reaching in that realm of which I write-influence for good on those who are less good-that is like the sun on a garden that has been neglected-the fallow field that lies as pasture land and suddenly knows that new day has brought the sun and dew and growing power, and the scene may any day spring into the influences that set growing weeds and shrubs and all that changes the face of the universe through fructification.

VISION 

117 Now that I have said that, I must explain that the good we may find somewhere when least expected, as any result of our lives-the good we take with us after death, that which has been essential to our comfort and our unfolding knowledge, is continuous in the lives of those we love;—no doubt about it,—it is not the heavenly treasure of a lovely dream of future inheritance of life in a heavenly kingdom, but the plain and ever ready power constantly revealing itself as we find our own left without words or messages still feeling the influence of life that was enjoyed together as it brings back a desire to find the better way to reach the Heavenly Father in the life of service. To me again and again has come the knowledge of that extension of power that is beyond words but is nevertheless a wonderful influence and blessing. There is an increase in my own power over my own people as I grow into the intimate and perfected experiences with you; you do not yet realize how much I depend on these literal experiences with you for my nearer approach to those, and through you I am eager that they should have that same conception of the life after death as we have; and I see especially in the days of question or of doubt, that they each, without the sense of having imbibed the reality of the messages, do turn literally toward me, and there is a prayer in hearts that feel the desire to have me become a part of the light they need on a problem. 

Do I forget that you have been my message-bearer to them far deeper in that meaning than counsel or good wishes? It gives some depth to their acceptance of God in the world; and with your dear ones that sense of obligation is theirs to us as we sit in the sunlight of

VISION 

both the sun itself and the light of the new day that is ours. They have something more than a question as to the fitness and proper place of a communication like these, for we have kept strictly to the truth as revealed to us through experience. Experience is its own reward for going after it wherever a door opens that we did not know existed in a wall that hid the gardens of the Eternal from those who gathered in a fashion to seek the faith which they could not sustain when death closed all the lovely reality of life. 

The ministers are walking toward the light now just as fast as they can, and one steps on the heel of his leader, stumbling into a place a step ahead of those who never stumble but walk humbly in the paths of the church; and very good for them—it is not leadership, it is faithful discipleship in the way they find to walk until revelation adds its light for the future. I would not dare to pray or desire the clergymen who are so important in the lives of their parishioners suddenly all together to find a demonstration never before given of the entrance into life as was that of the risen Jesus, which was a demonstration of victory over death and set the whole world into a commotion that has brought much of the autocracy into dispute. 

Now that may sound as if I did not wish this truth made evident as on that morning in Judea long ago. 

Well, I may as well confess that I think it would now be a demonstration confusing, conflicting, and not convincing, for the individual acceptance of a great truth is the better way; there could no doubt be found a place where such a demonstration might be given, but just as these wonders of communication by telephone,

VISION 119 radio, or wireless transmission of England's affairs, ceremonies, and the pictures accompanying them; all that when it is arranged, planned, and proven, is accepted with a nod of the head, a glance at the transmitter, and becomes as a stalk in the wheat field, just something more to add to the truth, and no thanks to God.

Now that the book has found its way, not by the sponsorship of your words or mine, in the conversations that are held about it, but with something of reverence, a feeling inspired by the truth somehow stripped of trappings, and said in words-words are so faithful and yet so fitful, yet they have power; and now the book is having its day; when read and favorable attention given to it we may be happy indeed. If a professor in 

Harvard University had taken just the same sort of interest-same sort, I said—we could have had messages as well phrased and as clear in the effort to clarify their meanings, and no one would have dared to dispute his findings, but the friends who carried the message of Jesus were humble folk who had nothing to lose-remember that—everything to gain by calling attention to the happenings in Jerusalem.

There have been ministers in the Episcopalian pulpits who have taken our book and had no difficulty whatever in finding a hope that it might be truth expressed by us your reception, my expression; they wait for further demonstration, which I say with all my belief energized by our experiences-they may get just as they are able to receive as you do. That will come.

Good will survives-all will be made clear; we must take what we find to be true. That prophetic influence was about me in my life-then the jewel was revealed.

VISION 

One day I was allowed to hear a very useful message given by a wife to her husband. I was not present, but it was a message that one of this group thought I would appreciate. I give it to you. The thought of where the foundations rested which supported the house in the after life had been in this man's mind continuously as something unthinkable, and the message was about this-it troubled him—and she replied to this thought expressed in words. "I found the home of Father and

Mother as real as any home we have ever had. I took it as I took Grandfather's barn when I was a little girl. 

It was there when I was a baby; I did not know who built it or owned it, but it was Grandfather's barn, and I never thought about it except as it was a part of the life I was born to.” That is it. I saw houses and all kinds of constructed buildings. I did not find an unclothed child; I saw them not in the leaves that fluttered before them. I saw them not as reflected pictures that memory gives to one-they were there; not mine, not my people always, but among my people where houses were suited to my needs; you know very well-a big table, a chair, my books, windows that gave sunlight shimmering through tall trees, and the waters that were flowing from somewhere. I was not surprised, although I had not dreamed of the life after death as peopled, forested, and with houses, and all that we work so hard to establish on earth. Strange that I did not at once question on what the foundations rested. I am still thinking of the reality of the things we use after death, and dare not express the opinion that there are strata of seemingly perfectly useful lands that hold their buildings as they hold us. I walk, mostly to some place I want to

VISION 

121 reach; no wings yet for me; thought is the wing on which we wend our upward flight. There may be something that binds us to the land of the heavenly kingdom, like the power we have to balance our bodies as they carry us forward to office or church-that will be a further revelation. This I do know-that love is the foundation for my strength. I think that hate falls away as love crowns purpose and aspiration, and that is such a definite experience in life that we may well trust it as the foundation of all that love desires; if it desires perfect freedom from house and lands and the care they impose, and we desire only beauty and light and the joy of perfect freedom, it may be then that the power of motion is like the "wings of a dove." It may be that the very expressions of angels wing-equipped, coming back and forth through realms of light, were first formed in the mind of one who saw them moving without that enervating loss of power in the avenues between spheres. 

I know, as I know I am dictating partly words, partly understanding, to you at a table, and not a scroll somewhere put up for my use like a blackboard. I know that my thought is on that table transmitted to you by a voice of one who I know is able to speak to you. I know that the appurtenances are all as real and necessary for the transmission of my thought as if I were in a former life where pencils, secretaries, papers, and all needed were placed for my use in the business I had to do. Tell them to wait, the climbing will not be so difficult for the love that binds us together-the search, the revealing of the Eternal One.

LETTER XX 

February 27, 1936 [A very dark day] 

HE LETTER. This is a day that gives inspiration

THE 

only by the expectancy of what is to be. Nothing less inspiring could be imagined in the streets of the cityno sun, nor moon, nor stars to bless us, but the dearest wish of my heart is answered by the darling of all my inspiration and activity opposite me waiting for whatever I may give. The new wondrous influence which comes hourly to us is so beautiful to share with you, and I say briefly but truly it would not be so wondrous were it not ours—not yours, not mine, but ours.

I have been thinking of the way knowledge comes to us from those who are unaware of the way they bless us by their questions; it gives the desire to answer. No one likes to confess ignorance when asked a question, and so our dear ones on the side of life where I am have begun a series of conferences asking each other, "Why this? Why that?"-and going forth to find out, they bring each other to a point of knowledge which is useful in our contacts with those we love. For instance, Why Indians? Why so few who bring the answer to prayer? Why so much confusion about the ways and efforts used? 

I bring my own faith as my answer to everything—I mean you, dear Faith, for without you I might have blundered along in the same uncertain ways that have now brought us light where light was needed. Almost everything else in science and knowledge is put under

VISION 

the scrutiny of glass or comparison in the effort to squeeze out all that is in the fruit of knowledge and yet leave the truth free from the squeezing process. I think these efforts to prove what is not commonly perceived would be far more productive of results that add to beauty and consciousness of the divine in the world and would be given freely, if the manner of seeking were less aggressive and more faithfully received as you have received from me. I feel something like those who walked in the Garden of Eden without any previous knowledge that there was anything evil in the garden until they met the old He-one, who told them to eat and know-which they promptly did, and found the garden had been only a place of temptation. That is the way so many lovely, beautiful experiences in life have been but a place of temptation destroying peace, the only pleasure of the tempter; and I am not thinking of the temptations in the physical world when one is aware of them stalking through the gates of the city, but the garden where the tree of life grows and its fruit is the apple of Paradise, the eating of which gives knowledge of a life supreme and, as far as I realize, eternal in the heavens.

It has been one of the questions I have held during my experience in this little room where the transmitter is a young and apparently an Indian girl-I don't know why. [The VOICE. I can tell him why!] I have been told that with the undeveloped mind, child-like and responsive to what is given as a task that leads to larger life, the Indian comes into the life of universal and growing consciousness of the realities of life after death more quickly than those whose trained minds-question, question,

VISION 

125 interest, interest, unfolding daily by the study and research of many peoples in many lands-do not at once understand that there are truths which are unfolded only by a spiritual consciousness that is able to accept and prove afterwards by experience. We come as part of that group of people who have found many truths that are indubitably true to us but are left waiting for our experience right here until we feel ourselves sometimes miles ahead of many spiritual leaders and great numbers of those impressive people who lead in art and science and have no conception of accepting anything till they have dismembered the body and left arms, legs, and the differing parts of a truth to be joined together sometime, somehow, somewhere, to a body of belief that adds to life. You and I know that their efforts are quite useless in many departments of human consciousness. 

Shut up under a microscope it is no actual experience, but a "may be Truth" attitude that is very disheartening. You and I have come to the very essence of eternal life when we meet and find the truth that we are people —not dismembered bodies of the past, with memories we may give of the days we have known and left there as evidence that they were experiences that prove the identity of one who had lived and died. What about the present and the future? Is there nothing to be added to memory, making it the fore-runner of a future that may find the truth through prophecy and the answer bestowed by the passing of days showing that past, present, and future are all of one piece in the life of the individual? That, to me, binds us in the progressive life of those who are able to see something besides the memory of a past, left-as some of those Eastern students

VISION 

assert-as a shadowy, dim, and unreal expression, hung somewhere in the life after death as a phantom-mind that is repeating what it knew. 

Now with this present life of ours we are having as our response to the truth, which I assert and you accept, a definite and glorious experience, perhaps more so than anything in the past, and possibly matched only by having no break in the transmission of our thought and constant knowledge of presence, which we may not demonstrate as definitely now as we shall in the loss of the limited expression and gain of unlimited experience. 

Another question which troubled me somewhat was:

Where is the line drawn in communication? and if the good and the bad and indifferent, the holy and the unholy are able to communicate, where are those saints who were saintly in aspiration and in beauty of expression for light and God? Where are they? They would, I believe, remember the needs of those who walk without the light, and I have thought it strange that there was silence about them and that they were not manifest in the world we know. And when I asked that question a white hand waved back to me the answer.

Always in any phase of life one stands to take the hand outstretched in time of need, without any demonstration of presence, but adding to the grace and power of one who calls to God under whatever name He is known; it is the divinity in the saintly person that is God and answers prayer, through mother, father, husband, infant child whose precious body is the gift of love-any and all who find joy to serve in the hour of distress are the angels of the kingdom of life.

For a moment I was abashed before the evidence we

VISION 

127 have had of care given to us in our moments of contact in the midst of those who sin against the laws they hold as privilege to do the will of a distorted mind. Right in the buildings where our dear ones are housed, where the thrust by the bayonet of sin may be a danger signal to them, they are preserved and we are able to go forward. Something better than the methods that would destroy us or others like us, holds the world as by the fingers of strong and mighty ones who are able to add to our other blessings the definite care and provision for our safety and our power to add to their help of those others that makes life livable. I speak now of the love-life you and I know, of those who, passing on into the higher life, found love the finest and most wonderful help in coming to those they left. The beloved mother of a rector whom you know-still living-gone on in her life beyond the limitations of the existence here, approached me with a smile and a bit of reproach for me that I had thought her son had not lived up to the faith he preached; not as personal reproach, but as general for clergymen, bishops, and all who make up the ritualistic life, and that I had apparently believed that they should do more to bring this consecration of power and communication to be met and accepted, if true. I think I may have been in a state of enthusiasm for what we had found-the vision beatific of life after death. 

I suppose I did say something of that kind; it was not so much a reproof of one's demeanor, not at all a shaft against those who wear the cloth, but a desire to ask for some effort to bring comfort to the suffering. And what do you think she said to me? "Remember that you did not make a step toward the understanding of this

VISION 

power-not because you did not wish to know, but because you did not feel able to accept what might come to you through any source you knew, and only this devoted Christian lady with faith to find the truth made it possible for you to speak almost as if you were embodied again in the land of the living. If I might send a word to my son and find the same help which you took so naturally as if it were your own idea and effort, perhaps there might be a heart healed of sorrow by a mother's love. At all events, and through the beautiful truth you two have given to the world, there may be a light in every home-the blood on the lintel." 

THE VOICE. He looks at her; she is not young, not immune from care and responsibilities; her love was intensified by these.

THE LETTER Continues. Well, if we have done this, I 

give you all the glory there is, for I know better than I can say how you sat silently without murmur or question and took what I was able to get through to you. 

Your faith and care and the spiritual purpose of those who came into this room-the Indian, your motherall breathe a blessing for the work to go forward. Now 

I see that beyond us—you and me—this care we feel around us is definitely a gift from some we have never known, and it has not detracted from the independent expression of truth as we find it, nor has it left us without purpose to carry forward forever the work of lifting out of sorrow, ignorance, and physical pain whoever might need any help from us. It becomes a universal power, and I am quite sure that thoughts go out as light into the world and carry the light of truth where it becomes useful for growth and progress. Perhaps we

VISION 

129 built better than we knew in years of doing things important in life, perhaps even we may have carried forward the expression of truth as we find it. Thoughts are creative-even the hoodlum who sits dreaming over his power to do some daring deed in its wickedness may not recognize the sudden dropping of his plan as the wind off a truth swept into the world as the gift of power. 

Preaching again! But I don't preach all the time. I found myself the other day mingling in happiness and joy with the young-the dancing young who find that an avenue of expression as I have sought it in prayer. 

I love the little things of life; I love youth-it is light, goodness, and beauty.

Now as usual the minutes have slipped away, but I want you to know that I am definitely in a place filled with all sorts of people and protectors. You are protected. Now I feel not reluctant to go, but happy to know we go together. 

THE VOICE. That lady who was there last time seemed to know all about this. Her love is for you and she does not intend to do anything that would interfere with the letters. He says, Everybody is asking who I am. 

LETTER XXI 

March 5, 1936 

HE LETTER. Blessed hour, that gives me the joy of 

Tbringing some small bit of bringing some small bit of my thought for you to be as conscious of me as I am of you. 

Because I do not send words to all those dear ones

VISION 

left must not militate against me for absence of love, outgrown affection, or loss of memory of the years I lived and loved. It is very often in my purpose to do something that may help them to realize that the man who was to them father, and all the other relationships to many friends and dear ones, is just the same man in mind and desire as if he walked among them clothed with the joy of their relationships. It is because I have so many experiences each day that give me knowledge of the dual life becoming so natural that I feel it important to stress that point and enable the world to come into this wonderful accomplishment of living in both time and eternity without loss to others of any precious and glorious expressions. Quite irreverent seems the claim to immortality unless one has some definite idea of what immortality means. Certainly you, Faith, and I, have some comprehension of the immortality of the dearest and finest accomplishment of our earthly career. Love that is immortalized in song, poetry, literature, and the confidence that it will never die, may also be proclaimed as the essence of eternal life; for without it there is nothing that may be as useful as the heavenly manna in the desert ways of perishable things. 

I claim as a vitalizing truth that love is the power that makes possible the evidence of life after death;—not that divine quality that illumines our work at this time and place, but love that is ever habited in a body that becomes the vehicle of its life wherever it may be. Sometimes there is a breathless moment when the body I am using seems definitely transparent as if it reflected everything in the world about me-like looking from a car window when one is moving onward through spaces

VISION 

131 that make up countries, formations of communities, and all that is in the world below us and above; across a bridge we move and see the swirling waters at the buttresses and above we turn our eyes and catch the gleam of winds flying higher above our bodies, and over and above and beyond mountains piled up against the sky become to us as real as if we were able to touch them with fingers pointing them out as something to discern so far away. It is a breathless moment for me when I catch the meaning of the transparency of a body that gives at one moment consciousness of all that is in the line of vision, yet after what we have known as I have just described it to you, it is a tangible and beautiful truth revealed in larger measure to me or you or anyone who stops a moment, meditates on the new joy, and then comes to reveal the reality of experience after death to any who will receive the word of life. 

I am not aware of sin or sorrow in the sense of being related to it, but with that undertone of a great sea that is ringing in the ears even when one has passed away from the shore and moved into a noiseless and grand forest where every sound is of winds that softly blow, or the noise of small things working in the grasscrickets in the evening and grasses moving as if stirred by something beneath the clover at our feet—that is often a call to the danger zones of life, the call of the sinner, the sorrowing one, the undeveloped one, as if from a quiet retreat we heard a cry for help, a moan of distress or a threat of destruction by evil ones in the world. You know that such a call may come to you or anyone whose mind has been troubled by the useless and worthless experiences of those who still persist in

VISION 

the agonizing ways of death and sorrow and sin. You know that there are times when you would stand near to offer all you have to bring back joy and goodness and the love of God to the world. You are doing this; you are doing it, as I wish to remember that there are lives to be vitalized with all this experience of the truth forever as far as I am able to judge-forever in an endless desire to bring sunshine and peace to those in need. If one is quite satisfied to let the future take care of itself, to leave the darlings of our love and care to the after life for a continuance of our care, and there is no eagerness to know, we will not strive to revolutionize the faith and patience that all may be well in heaven.

There is often a meeting of many wise spirit-people, and ever a desire on my part that ways and means may be found to bring to the attention of those in need the evident truth that is ours, and I wonder why in all the years that they have known what we know there has been no evidence forthcoming to such as might carry the truth in a form in which it could be received. One great teacher replied, "They stand in their own shadow." 

What a charge against those who should be able to stand aside that the revelation might be seen in the day of need! In a building filled with people respectable and 

God-loving, the edifice was crowded with spirit-friends, at the end of the pews where families have been sitting Sunday after Sunday with now and again an absent one, his book of prayer untouched, the one gone sitting still in the midst of associations endeared by worship, praise, and prayer. One could not tell the difference between the body that had been often in its place and the one that was used as the means of presence at that altar.

VISION 

133 

Oh, I prayed aloud that the eyes of those who loved might be opened to see a transfigured heart and soul that waited in the old habitat hoping to be recognized. 

Is not Easter the day of transfiguration from the dead body hanging on the cross, to the glorified beauty of the 

Risen One? Our day? We rejoice that to us Easter is all that we need to express the knowledge of the arisen friends. [THE VOICE. He speaks of the alarming state of our situation to-day.] Justice must be our purpose through all. If it is to forgive again and again, we must rise on the wings of forgiveness and carry with us the one forgiven. 

I want you to realize that your father and a company of men like him are using the power of thought and justice as a pry to get in under the secret movements and reveal them to a world that is not so sure of justice as he is. I was given that message by your father that you might know him to be still a part of the fine sensible movements of courts and the law-not only to those we love, but those who follow in the line of descent and may inherit the spirit of justice by acknowledging truth, and no namby-pambying with the law to let a criminal escape conviction.

Now I am so sure that our thoughts are powerful and useful—they become a part of the universal thoughtlife, and the stronger our grasp on them for the best, the more definitely the best comes into the lives of our own circle of loved ones, then down through the ages. 

I have been with our sons, yours and mine . [A long digression here, about general affairs.] ...

We will not go back on our decision in life—we made our choice. But if we had left it as it was before I came

VISION here it would have been robbery for me, and you would have missed what I have tried to give you. As one who knows in the more transparent body I have told you of, you and I are a part of the love-life that has been so beautiful in other instances of our love for our own. Every night as I meet you after sleep has set you free, your spirit radiant with beauty and love, as I take your hand, we do not wander far away from the life you live -I am not sure whether that would release you entirely from the body, but I have no right to absorb much of the gift of movement toward higher and diviner life until the body is absolutely of no use to you as a vehicle of thought. But we walk to my far-off hide-away where we may speak soul to soul (usually I have taken you, for you have not expressed any special desire) to a garden that I love. We stand hand in hand taking in the beauty of that garden, silvery with the moonlight shining on water near by, and when you are free from body and loving friends, we shall, I know, have there the first few moments together. I wait until night brings shadows and stars and then there we are, talking of many things, joy, no separation, plans, and thoughts. I never yet have shared our garden, with the view of the silvery moonlight on water, with any of your friends or mine—that belongs to us—our little garden that leads on and on, but as sure as night comes we are there together. 

Remember this-no sleep at night will lead you anywhere except to me; any prophecy, warning, or suggestion will come in some other way, for we need our nightly talk. And now good-bye for this time.

LETTER XXII 

March 14, 1936 

HE LETTER. With the assumption that we realize by 

Ta known pressing upon our consciousness, we are made aware of the blending of all life in all experiences for the better understanding of the pain and sorrow that is a process in the unfolding of Eternity. Its probably constant and determining forces we could not accept earlier without these wondrous experiences.

I am to-day finding the evidence that brought us to the very gate of the kingdom of life. Those letters and our clasping hands in these experiences gave us the wisdom that we each brought to the other until this experience of death gave wisdom, vision, and enlightenment; and we may now find revelations coming each hour to ourselves and the world around us. I never strive to forget any of the doubts and troublesome queries to which in all my life I tried to find an answer, for I am held to the belief that these friends and companions of life may be just as eager to know as I was, but the little things of life—the many diversions that come with responsibilities and cares hold them in the state of doubt without the unrest until the breaking of the casket brings the heavens into view-then, to me, revelation is startling—yes, startling it is and was and continues to be. Our own, those we chose to be our own in all the fullness of responsibilities that we assume, will always-Isay that without hesitation-always be a magical link with us as they find the light and the associations that make

VISION 

for more vivid and illustrative beginnings of the future, as those we have had. I do not wish to set aside but always to find the place and power that our lives have found, and the helpfulness to those who are a part of our minds as we move forward. Talking to some of our people long since away from the shadow of life and the mists that surround so much that we only see through after time has lost its significance, I found that there is a very clear conception of our dependence on those who have done for each of us what was an important help in our unfolding life of the body—and they recognized that they could not have done for you or me what we have done for each other. Without conceit or adding a thought of superior help to each other I still find that my dependence on you and yours on me are what no one else could give us; these others also know that, and accord what we have learned and gained of the divine afflatus which flows to us as an additional gift to them. 

It must always be like that—not a selfish possession of the wonderful experience we are having, but the knowledge that they are blest by the blessing of this opportunity to express and give out the abundance of our love to them, with them, never withdrawing any of the beauty and active service through the ages to come. Of course (he smiles), there are moments when one is so filled with the desires of this high and exotic love that a land, an island, a quiet place where there might be the completeness of expression comes over one. It must be that there is a time far off in the realms of life when we are no longer thinking of houses and lands and the laws that must be observed to keep the machinery of civilization moving forward. It does seem almost too much

VISION 137 to comprehend what will come after we have taken our hands off the wheels of civilization, but there must be something higher yet-perhaps some new soul-purposes that will give us the same personal contact with other forms of life as definite as growing from babyhood to grandfather life on earth (he smiles). 

Now for a moment let me say that through the raging storms of physical life there is a sympathetic chord of spiritual life that attaches itself to experiences that are terrible in the conflict but reflect the care and responsibility of those who are not completely deluged by the bitter waters of experience when floods come, and one feels the safety of an abode which does not meet destruction through the devastating waters. There is never in a well-poised and sympathetic person that sense of being apart from what goes on-it is a sort of vibratory influence which almost feels the desperate situation which other humans face. War in Germany or Ethiopia gives pain to those who are safely ensconced in a position not included in the calling to arms of dear ones who are able and willing to go if needed. Always in our hearts, yours and mine, when danger was known to be in any part of the world response came from heart and mind and as far as possible we took a place, in thought at least, with the need of those in conflict. One almost prays for death when such scenes demand our sympathies as well asthoughtful consideration of what we may do. Yet death does not release us from that sympathetic feeling of kinship-all one in the world of affairs; and often I find myself longing for power to-day to demonstrate what I think might be something like the use of spiritual liberties to add to rights in the justice of life when dishonor

VISION and sin make something to be dreaded. Do you think 

I am able to stand still and leave it all to God? I never did; it often seemed that mine was such a small service, yet such as it was it had to be. And instead of waving a good-bye hand to the old world that had taught me so many things, I wave it again in greeting to the opportunities that may be ours in a spiritual way, and find that such service lifts the world itself, the part where we are known, and may add beauty, joy, and love, through the very influence of the words I write on this little light-stand, as I call it, where a candle burns constantly to give what would not be given without its precious opportunity for us.
