I said give joy and love, and I add peace, not from warring arms may we give it-that comes from understanding of the uselessness of much that goes under the name of patriotism. I know that the peace of soul important in protection of bodily life may be found in many small groups here and there through the world, but we must find our lamp lighted and giving the new light after death as a sequence and not as something to be decided or avoided if it comes without the fearful price that accident, carelessness, and pain exact. I find it very important to understand that to go away with the light out into a new experience of life is to find that everyone taking such a step is a very important factor in the world that weeps over graves that hold no body and never had any soul; there is the fear that oppresses, binds down to the fact of the dead body and stays right there at the very door where the light might shine out and illumine the darkest moment.

You know how hard sorrow is to bear. The separation seems so real—and it is; changes even at the place at

VISION 139 the table, the family with the absent one always in mind, missed in the dear ways that have grown into a manner of living-that is a private and individual grief. 

Let us take it away from the hearts and minds of mankind. All right for prayer and the solace of hopeful words, but truth swings open the doors of life after death with joy and acclamation that it is mysterious but not mystifying to-day. I know, I think I am sure, that the words with which I try to make a clear statement of life without the old implications of bodily illness or ability to give all that heart and mind could give, these words that I always wish to give that doubt may be forever set aside about loss and separation, may find their way to those who have not had time or opportunity to get the glad expression of one who knows that he is in constant companionship with all those who are dear, and in perfect responsive companionship with one who sits with him for the experience that adds to knowledge. That we give not as a sentiment that may bring momentary joy, nor as a wiping of tears from eyes that need comfort, not as something that binds one to give up sin that he may have heaven-all good, all right as far as they go-but as those who are partakers in the Kingdom of God in reality, in power, and in that divine love that would give the life held dear for those who need demonstration of truth.

This sounds not so much like a sermon as a call to the service of mankind. I am—I want to say-beholden, grateful-any word that will express my glad joy for your help in this service. Without you I might stand weeks at a time beside one to whom I would gladly give that same evidence of my love and proximity, but have

VISION 

no chance at all even to give a word. With you it will go with power to them and to the world. Personally, I could rest now that you and I are so aware of each other, finding that delightful companionship so unusual in life before death and so brilliant in diffusion of light after death, and wait for the others to discover for themselves what we know. But I could not do that. We know that spirit-bodies (I think I have said this before) are as definite and useful after death as physical bodies before death. We grow in our bodies-do not hold them to animalistic needs, but they hold our thought-life in an avenue of digesting truth, which does not depend on meat or bones or oatmeal—one forgets about it. I often had to be reminded of the hour, and that I must get to dinner. I did not feel the prey of hunger because my mind was on another matter, but when I came to take it, I did so with the relish of a hungry man. I have not thought about this until this moment. The body I use is efficient and meets my need. I think in the home place (where I always plan to have you keep your contacts after death as I do with you), in that place full of sunshine I have outside the loveliest garden. The flowers do not depend on my care, they are a spontaneous growth that comes up like the little fronds of ferns that appear under the great trees, and look like children of the palms. I would not have them taken away. When I see the Indian Pipes I smile as to old friends. I never planted Indian Pipes, they were there-natural children of the soil. Beyond, looking through the trees I see waters that flow and flow and are beautiful to look at. I know you will love that, too-I can almost see your hand as you point to all the beauty-the sunlight sparkling

VISION 

141 through trees on the river. Some friends I still have who hanker for the juice of a peach; they can have it, it is a part of the thought-life. 

And here we are. I can touch your hand, I can see your form as you come here. I was so grateful when word came for you to come to-day and not the regular day. I know about these floods, but this day brings sunshine. 

THE VOICE. He touched your shoulder, he hopes to make it felt. He says, “I'll do it yet." (He spoke of the companionable association of his mother and mine. “Good-bye” was said by the Voice.)

LETTER XXIII 

March 21, 1936 

[The floods] 

HE LETTER. Never before have I been more eager to tell you of the experience that I found in the torrents, not only of waters but emotions that almost brought tears to my eyes, as the terrors were revealed to my sight. I, who have been aware of desolation in many lands, and had my own opinion about shipwrecks and fires and the devastating influence that pestilence brings to many communities, had never been aware of the terror that accompanies such scenes. I rushed to you with the impetuosity that made me long to speak—yes, speak to you, and I found much comfort in the knowledge that we shared this experience in a very real and vital understanding of what comes after the flood. I found by many signs and tokens of the hours that there

VISION 

were few who had any idea of the reality of life after death which has come to us. They hugged their trinkets under wet and awful conditions of body and mind. 

Prayers were offered in a way that did not seem like an appeal to God but a desire to save the treasures of the past. Pathetic indeed to see the extremity and the need of one word to give them the understanding that there was life and love after the sorrow and pain. It occurred to me about homes, that the thoughts had made them precious possessions that should keep them from harm, sheltered among the possessions they had earned or saved or inherited, and without the sense of walls to protect, of chairs in which to rest, there was desolation in the attitude which they took. Then, and this is what 

I wish you to remember forever with me as a sanctification of a service which we now know is a part of the heavenly life-there they stood, those who had long been on this side of life, quite well accustomed to scenes of pain, separation, sorrow, and disaster; they created little places of rest for those who came up out of the terror and found an abiding place with the touch of home life—a light burning in a room exactly as if someone were waiting to receive them. All was order and the happy passivity of those who knew that there was life still waiting for their acceptance of its part after the pain and sorrow. I did not attempt to find a place to offer assistance, for all was as well managed as if a school of training had been used by them for such circumstances. The worst terror was not among those who were swept to death, but in your world and mine, as we have seen it in confusion and without thought of all that it meant, but asking for some clue as to the place

VISION 143 where they might go to. I will not dwell on it, but the superb picture of those who were supposed to be in a world of peace and beyond the contacts with such conditions as existed for hours-still do exist-to find the atmosphere of trust and courage and faithful attendance to the needs of their brothers in the destitution of life-that gave me that courage that time brings into the consciousness of eternity as an unfolding confidence in the Heavenly Father. 

Then I turned toward you, and with you, your mind taking on the sense of dangers that might attend those we love, and even there I found that others before me had been working to keep poise and peace and protection, taking means to perform miracles if need be. That word "miracle" I now believe is made a part of the plan of care and protection for those who are held in the hearts of love. It gives quick response from those in trouble to see what should be done, precisely as if one who loved and was wise could at once penetrate the mind of peace and poise and say, "Move," "Jump," "Stand still!"-like commands coming through the atmosphere that lies between the dead and the living, to speak as one at a safe distance looking across the perilous stream or broken bridge or awful staggering burdens which the waters carried, and save from the disastrous situation by steady and wise movements. 99 A very wonderful experience for me to have and you to know in time of a house fire, not so dreadful that life is endangered, yet if a false move is made, a step taken into a danger spot, and there is no real contact such as you and I know, there may come a sudden overwhelming smoke, a broken casement or stairway with no es-

VISION 

cape. Our own have been a part of the protective power of love. It is a part of that confidence which in time of trouble our friends in the spirit hold in God. It is a part of Christian wisdom and acceptance of the care of the 

Heavenly Father. I find that this spirit animates those who die with faith that God is a part of the universe of life-a living God, and I also know that that faith in God makes itself felt through the care one may take for the dear ones who are in need-transferred from the eternal one to the help and comfort one may give to those, just as you and I accept responsibilities for those we have brought into the consciousness of the world through the love we have found in our hearts by virtue of our care. We bestow that same responsibility which we may assume after death if it be our desire, not as an obligation like providing for one's family because it is our family-no-not an obligation, but a desire that lifts us away from the purpose to go on and on and on and see all there is to see without regard to those we have left. For one I do not desire to thrust my responsibilities on anyone. I love to have them, and it seems that before I (he smiles) graduated into this new conception of life others before me had found the truth and joy of the possession of power. I think we may add to our responsibilities after death just as we add to them in community life when we have a need that we may help to overcome with our directive power. There will be sorrow, I fear, for days and days, and if out of it we may without any idea of superior knowledge or power add to that faith in those who have gone on beforeif we may give our testimony and belief that there is guidance and care-we shall add to the definite accept-

VISION 

145 ance of the messages that are flowing every moment from hearts that love and minds that guide, and guide to them in need.

It makes me feel greatly indebted to you, for I see that all the years before we came to this point of contact you had the same confidence, almost beyond belief to me because I was of another type of mind. I wanted to feel the consciousness that could be proven through experience. I had to die to find that, but I had been so helped by your faith that it was not difficult to accept the knowledge which you had found through faith. 

When you were here that last hour before this one, I had some feeling that there might come something that I could give you that would add to our understanding and strength for others. This may be it, but I know that the words I give you do not need translation—they are as plain and as well worth while for others as for us. 

It is your philosophy, as I well know, to make the best of whatever comes your way. It is not that you do not realize how tough the game may be; you do you do-you see perhaps more clearly beyond a present emergency than anyone I ever knew, but you do not bend under it, you lift up your head and pick out your word of confidence, in manner, voice, and message—“We must go through it"—and through it we do go; there is always a bridge somewhere that leads across the sea of sorrow. You say, "We'll do the best we can under the circumstances." This is particularly your work; you are an inspiration. I'd deepen the word to a finer meaning and acceptance of a divine law, the love that binds me to you. I use it because it is the word of life and destiny. 

I use it because I must use a term that expresses in-

VISION 

finite love and power. It becomes strength, which I may use in a new capacity. [A digression here which I did not get very well. It had to do with the prevalence of cut-and-dried opinions held by some public men.]

Now about my personal interest in some of the people who have been taking me as an authority in these messages. Always glad I am to help any who are climbing up a hill. I may call back, "Look out, a loose stone up here," or "Make that little turn as I make it,” but I never say, "Better go back, it's a hard tramp," for I know it is worth the climb, and the height reveals the glory of Paradise. I think the well-informed communicator may help much by advising common-sense. Sometimes I say a doctor does not say, "You can take or leave your medicine, it is all the same." He says, "Take it and get well." This is what I try to suggest to some who have never felt the insistent note of truth, but have taken what seemed to be a very good thing to have in times of trouble-a physician, a lawyer, all a part of the desire to select what pleases rather than reveals. 

One dear friend, who has been so much interested in you and me and had caught in almost a brilliant spirit the knowledge that I tried to give you, has gone on in her search for truth as you and she had made some steps toward the advance of the spirit-philosophy, and has found at last the courage to ask me to tell you that sometime she would like to say that "he (meaning me) is very wonderful in his grasp of the need the world has of this truth." And with that smile with which she took the light and walked away with it, she says, “I have been electrified by the ideals that become realities just

VISION 147 as soon as the doors open and you find yourself within." 

She is very independent and very outspoken but careful. Here is something else I can give you, another thought wise and good. She thinks you made the world -you would and could do what you want-"You're going right on, won't we have a good time when you get here, talking it all over."

THE VOICE. She is very literal in her words and careful about appointments. She has found many people here who talk about you. She sends her love to you. 

THE LETTER continues. Now I'm so glad we had this morning. Go home with me, for I shall go with you.

These are really the happiest hours I have had even in Paradise. This does not end, it goes back to all we have been and are and promises all we are to be. THI 

LETTER XXIV 

March 26, 1936 

HE LETTER. Now that you are here there is light beyond stars and suns shining on my path that leads to the divine and never-failing love above us. All the paths of the universe open freely before us in divinely appointed ways, and I stand beside you going forward step by step with you. Even before we have moved out of the distraction of life, our spirits catch the radiance and beauty, and we almost live beyond limitations now recognized and made use of in our understanding of those who have not scaled the heights and caught the glorious dawn of the new day.

I love your spirit of victory-I share it; for it overflows

VISION 

in my life and you feel that it is the source of life. Yes, it is superior, if we admit the view unfolding before us. 

Once in a while I feel the desire to take you far away with me, that on your face you may feel the flush of the glory of the world in its new beauty, where neither storm nor stress may hide it from view if thought keeps pace with the freedom we may find. And I am impatient for the coming of our life over here as a school-boy is impatient for the pavement to dry, that he may play his marbles in the joy of spring. 

This day, so dear, so precious, should hush my longing for the high and wonderful experiences which await our coming, for is it not the heart of gratitude that I give to you and to God? Again and again I stand with the thrill of recognition and cry, "It is God-It is God!"

The law of the universe even in the dark days of despondency makes known the power that is God. Now when we fight against error, knowing that our feet are placed firmly in the paths of righteousness, we know it should give us consciousness of the world's need of standard-bearers for truth and assure us that we may wait for it in the beauty of the life together which is our answer to my cry and granted prayer-in these terrific onslaughts on our civilization we cherish above all else the verification of our purpose to stand forever wherever we breathe the life of God for the service we may render.

This is not another sermonizing letter, it is victory that gives my voice victory over ignorance and pain and death. Think for a moment what it means to one who adores you and has always known your attitude toward life, its experiences, the sudden changes in form and

VISION 

149 power, to be a part of what you are now doing and bearing for the light that shines into the darkest depths that sin creates. Sin has its creative power; it is the creative power of evil that must surrender at last to the higher creative power of good, love, light, glory. Nowhere are we safe from the effect of it on the boundaries of our life among men. Children and all who are voiceless in the chasms of pain and sorrow are ours by right of the knowledge we hold in our keeping for their inheritance in the hours to come. You see that I do not say eternity, or years that come after we are gone from this particular form of power, for their use, but hours-for the days open and close with anguish, and with the very definite form of governments of evil purposes that destroy and build on the bodies of their people—and it does not take a journey to the jungle lands of beasts of prey to find serpents that coil and sting, for the instinct of each type is resident in the master minds of evil intent-and no one knows this better than those who have mingled among the selfish and greedy criminal classes who have absolutely no consciousness of virtue. 

This I tell you as one who has always been aware of the two classes of mankind even in cities where the steeples tower to the skies in an effort to lift the mind to higher and more peaceful life, and chimes sound out over the city at Christmas when Christ was born, and at Easter when He conquered death and brought victorious life to earth as a symbol of His faith. There could be no finer effort in objective form than the towers, and chimes sounding morning and evening giving back the vision of faith to the world. I kneel beside you. I feel your pulse beating high in hope that the messages of life may be

VISION 

heard within the human heart that goes to the temple to pray.

There is something (he smiles) I wish to write to-day. 

I have found another organization quite different from the organized effort of those who are bound together to add to the knowledge we hold and give, that death is but a pause in life, a little space and time for readjustment to the new powers that await the spirit-body. This new (at least to me) organization is made up of people, largely men, who have had experience in legal and political life, now serving the world in a way that you and 

I may and do appreciate. Your father, and many of those jurists who have always been faithful to law and order, they have found the same type of gift that I have found in the groupings of certain other people with the same idea which is strengthened in its influence on the earthly life and producing much that finally comes into activity as a new law or a revivified old law. 

I find several of these groupings, widely separated in their personal and distinctive powers, but all working for the unfolding life—artists in beauty, music by the singers of the world, law by those who were by faith and service a part of its powers and restrictions, ministries where the poor and distressed, for they are here in some parts of the spiritual kingdom, and those who desire to find the hidden boundaries under the earth and what there may be of usefulness to men who strive to pierce the atmosphere and measure the heights that we may reach for knowledge of wind and waves of life above and all the relativity of things that make life better fitted for the mentalities that are now submerged. All these are interesting to me—perhaps not all as interesting as

VISION 

151 some, but they fit, as to a golden dream of life on earth; the poets take up the song and whisper it into the ears of those who hear, and write again in literature that binds us as the finer and diviner romances and the stronger and most patriotic songs to which the world may march forth, singing them into the minds of the nation. I cannot take my precious hour to repeat all these that I have seen, but in an hour of discouragement, when rascals laugh together at the tormenting grievances which they hold over the less able groups when these rascals are uppermost in power and one is compassed about with evil, the word I give you may help you to be confident of their downfall through the power of combined interest and thought in the world affairs; not all in one moment, but one moment hitched to our wagon that is on a voyage to the stars will make an endless chain toward the finer civilization which we yearn for. I believe Byrd in his retreat in the caverns of the 

Southland would have perished there had it not been for the thought of older explorers who had died under the pressure of their explorations, taking strength to him as the ravens fed Elijah. The way these powers of thought and purpose arrange themselves for the needs of those who dare to tread the pathways of the unknown is remarkable. The very cutting off of human life intensifies the power of the spirit help, and in a degree the prodigies that appear now and then-infant musicians, infant wise-heads, or even infant workers in some place where little fingers pick out the bobbins of industryare served by those who had learned the way to save energy and supply it, like oxygen carried in containers for those who would die but for the aid it gives.

VISION 

I think that adding to our messages of the truth, the truth which I give you as an assurance that the angels of God do not fear to walk into the den of lions or the fiery furnaces of life and protect those who are thrust into those difficult situations-this shows a better heaven than the old ideas of golden streets and gates of pearl. Now I feel that to stand a solitary pilgrim in the very midst of the beauties of life on earth, or surrounded by the glories of the life after death, is a sad fate for anyone, and I arrive at that point of experience on both sides that the solitary pilgrim is an unknown figure unless he shut himself away with purpose, like old monks in monasteries or fair maidens in nunneries finding such experience more alluring if alone. Occasionally a Dante finds his Beatrice and lives forever with that dream as real as if in body they walked side by side with undying devotion; that makes the pilgrimage less solitary for the dream he holds of eventually coming into actual companionship. 

Oh, it is so good to tell you that there will never be in my mind a cultivated garden for our use, with roses, orchids, unusual hybrids in perfect order. I like a little natural disorder, a vine running up a wall without any plan. 

Now I don't forget that there is a group on earth like your "little lad's" mother, and the mother of Louise, and several more connected with us in our community interest for building up evidence. [THE VOICE. Your communicator keeps them all at top speed.] I want to have either one of these children or all help to bring to this table as evidence a lady who has lately arrived and is as interested as if she always knew you. She is also

VISION 

153 interested in your minister. It will be irreproachable evidence by young minds.

Now we have almost got in an extra sitting. I go with you, so glad of this sunny day with promise of flowers and happy freedom.

LETTER XXV 

April 3, 1936 [A very rainy day] 

HE LETTER. Ah, Faith, we are together in this intimate and personal touch which binds us in every way together for an expression which we find useful as evidence of our definite consciousness of eternal values in the companionship we hold. Only once in a great while I am aware of the conflicts and burdens of the earthly life that is often so treacherous in its experiences, the world swayed by some wave of determination to lift itself into a supremacy of power to have its own way and dominate all things to its own purposes whether right or wrong. Warfare against law and order, goodness and truth-it sweeps all into the sacred places of justice and hallowed hearts and homes, giving a momentary chance for doubt to enter and destroy peace and confidence in the eternal good. We look down the pages of history side by side with the constant and abiding words of the wise, the good, the true expressed in poems and prayers and appreciations of the Heavenly Father; it is not a fair comparison, but it comes, nevertheless, and strengthens our hearts with the desire to do—yes, do something to relieve the pressure put upon our minds

VISION 

-are and find the Eternal God in a confidence established within our souls during the grinding of the corn that shall feed us until the wave passes. There is no triumph in the error and untruth of those who toss to the winds all sense of right and justice; it fails-but in the triumphant note of confidence we realize the difference between the real and the false disastrous power that after the conflict has raged is swept away by the dear days of the victories won. I have wondered what can possibly be the outcome of many conflicts in this very hour. 

We—some of us who live with the spirit-consciousness developed to a high degree of vision, whether alive in the earthly body or in the spirit-body after death—a one in the purpose to add our voices to the ringing truth that God still reigns and error dies in the making of a power that destroys its own idols. We consider all cases that are momentous to-day among the nations at war either primarily or in the possessions which are only temporal—and in the sins, the dastardly sins that are veiled until the continued cry, "I am innocent”—then evidence and the very acts which portend to the sinner are as definite as if the eyes of the world had witnessed the crime which is written in hidden funds and careful destruction of evidence which is indestructible and will bring about the result all wish for. The result includes deceptions that have been outrageously designed to overcome the rightful punishment of those who planned them. I might go on with far-reaching statements that are plainly discerned as plot and plan to overthrow justice and not with any humane feeling that a man should be spared. Do I believe in the death penalty for sin?

Death is not concerned in this particular case, which I

VISION 155 need not name; it would in reality restore life to the mind and make free a soul that has been all but lost to any human interest except his own.

For us to-day I wished to have you come with me, above the storm, just to feel that we were as we shall forever be, walking forward. Life has two paths which men may follow, one toward the light and beauty that is never poisoned by low lands and the miasmic influences of the darkened path leading to the storms of life, continued and distressing. Once I think I referred to 

Dante's Inferno. I have thought much about it. It is true that death does not come to release one from torture, and the lurid light of the tortuous hell may illuminate the faces of victims until there is an awakened consciousness of the part played to turn the feet toward the upward life which redeems, instead of destroying souls whom we are not able to help until time brings reason to the bar of repentance. I am not downcast; never will I submit to the fear of that state of mind, for on every side, even in these very disastrous times, I find voices that have been used only for the definite purpose of protection of right and the joyous acceptance of life, but with a new note ringing throughout the world for something to be done that wars may cease and laws find an obedient company to move swiftly through the world with strong united efforts for right and light.

I feel somewhat like a man who may yet find his words bringing to the world some sense of responsibility in all the activities around and about him. We, all of our age and time, have heard the bolts click in the locks of homes where cowardly and selfish people might hide away until danger is over; we know them, some of us, and we may

VISION 

have found the disregard of the rights of all about them, only coming for safety in the realm of danger and holding themselves from the real fight that comes to souls. 

That is a stimulating thought for us to hold, because we have always done what we wished in expressing loyalty to the world about us, and have not failed to approve of methods that would give us a part to play in life conflicts. It does not give us an old-age pension to live upon in security and freedom from a new day when we may persist in the same old way asking for our flag and our place in the procession of events. 

See how I find the place I must fill with my determination to help in giving God back to His children and find the evidence of the glory of our faith in Him as a power if not a person with whom we might feel on intimate terms as of the personality of power at least as an evidence of something we do not see in form but know exists at the beginning of the experiences of soul life. 

Love is always the first expression I feel in any hour, not only in the dismal day of uncertainty which is always somewhere in the life we live, but in the high, exultant day when joy comes to the world through some victory over worry, some recognition of the song that never ceases, some knowledge of the new powers which have been bestowed on some of the quickened souls over here. In all these days I add my understanding to what has come, and reach for your hand to take mine in the time of our rejoicing, and hear me say, "This is our happy day." Happiness is to me a constructive power in the world; I mean the world of art, of music, of science, and the discovery of upper spheres of life that yield the golden key of truth. Happiness for our

VISION 

157 children gives them the gift of power and adds a constructive ability in home, society, and education that is often submerged by opinions in waters of discontent. 

No child ever responds to the suggestion of doubt in his own ability or ever lifts a smiling face to those who are taller and bigger and have no conception of what real happiness is. No powerful and constructive influence of those who determine without any conception of what a child should have for his future equipment for life and its possibilities is worth while in activities assumed for children without their acceptance and delight in those activities. Schools and churches insist that young, fashionable men and women under advantages that place letters before or after their names have many defects. I do not always wish to add to the supremacy of their findings and desires, but when I see a bit of a lad springing up through all the traditional losses of family and home through poverty and hardship but with fine sense and desire going to the peak of success in the desired forms of life, I know he is on the way to universities and the like; and if you and I add the knowledge we have found so helpful to us to those who are busy with problems of business and learning, we may find one in a group taking seriously these messages and others coming from other sources adding the evidence of continued life, and we may feel that we have done something toward the establishing of the Kingdom of Heaven in the world. I know what that Kingdom of Heaven is-so do you; we have felt its tender influence in the world around us as we watched our little children, with the contented mind that was like a lovely song lingering long after the infant feet have

VISION 

walked out into places of service, still giving us the sense of the Kingdom of Heaven in the life of earthly experience. And instantly when they are transplanted from our homes they are giving the same feeling of heaven in the world that they carry with them, remembering the love that blessed them. This is sometimes accompanied by failures in the too great regard of those who attach themselves to us just as we have found that which (he smiles) is after all a selective appointment that we ourselves make with those about us. 

Here, too, comes help from the unfolding spiritual vision. 

Not for grace of manner, beauty of face and form, intelligence which inspires ideals to life, its influence and word. No-no-no-these fail, beauty fades, forms change by habits and objective purposes which thwart the very hope of our own lives at times. But the seed remains in the fertile soil of the mind, and sooner or later the mistakes in judgment and wrong understanding that impedes spiritual progress will be healed by the hand of wisdom doing its part to make right what was wrong. When we assume personal responsibility for our own, we know that we must leave the rest to experience which gives light. Since I am where I have a wider knowledge and extended view beyond the tombs where bodies are left to await the awakening of life, I take your children and grandchildren as a responsibility—and you take mine. In that same way a part of the love is pursuing its way in the resistless current of life. 

Now as I have been in this theorizing life for many years, working out my theories of many ways to improve conditions-Conditions-and never accept them-for to accept is to approve-that is one of the basic princi-

VISION 

159 ples of my life-we may try to reform a man who acts in a stupid or wicked way, and we try to make him see as we see. No good-his conditions fashion his actions; he may be untrue to his legal spouse and even his intimates cannot know the conditions he is under in seeking a false heaven on earth. I know about more such discussions than I would ever wish to prolong by my ideas of what happens but with purpose and power to end in the house a division in interests and produce something that leads to mind-companionship and contributes to the heaven on earth. One may ask "Does death bring freedom?" Not death, but vision, the vision that abides forever after death and leads to mind-companionship and finer understanding of the reality of love-love and all that is creative may give us the right to speak of things that are understandable only in freedom. Love is creative and instructive and indestructible and forever unfolding only in freedom. Love would control the elements that create loveliness in our enemies. That only love I recognize is that which is creative and constructive, which binds others to oneself by the desire to make them lovable. To be loved they must be lovable.

One man going forward in the divine conception of Jesus Christ, and another accepting brotherhood as the revelation of duty to his neighbor are each claiming for his acceptance of Jesus the Divine or Jesus the human a reason for having his form enshrined in glorious stained glass through which the same sun carries to each the suggestion pouring through shadow to the Arisen One, each one accepted, perhaps in a demonstration needed for the beauty and truth it embraces.

VISION 

All this is a part of my earthly years, but it has become so personal that I find my mind ready to discard all argument and accept Him for what His life has meant for us. A question often asked of me is, "Have you seen 

Jesus?"-they expect to see Him at once. I have not.

I do not believe that it would strengthen my feeling. I follow after Him-after the crucifixion, the sorrow, the defeat and to the Victory which we accept to-day. And 

I have not seen God as a visible Being, but in the works of the mind of the Eternal that are ever before my eyes, when rain drops softly as to-day, not a great raging storm to bring danger near, but dropping on a spot where violets grow, so small they would not be noticed until in company, making a blanket out of a bit of hillside. I love the rain, and on those days I often wish for you to talk with me (or rather I with you!)—then you are like the spot of sunshine bringing the violets that follow the gift of water. Floods are destructive because misunderstood. 

I am so glad that you did come to-day.

LETTER XXVI 

April 9, 1936 [I HAD found the last period exhausting.] 

HE VOICE. He was already here.

THE THE LETTER. The absence of the pressure of time on my mind may have made me unmindful of the long hour, and I do not desire to give you any concern over what I do. I will strive to remember that I am working with limitations which are of the world of time. I

VISION 

161 measure every word carefully that I may add to the beauty and usefulness of these ministrations which are divinely given to me by wise and kind individuals; and 

I think we may sum it all up in the great joy which exempts us-me especially, I think-from the doubt and wonder that such as this may become a normal and acceptable gift of God to those who take it as we do in its completeness, the threefold power of soul, body, and spirit. It has been often my knowledge that there are varying types of affection which fit the times in different situations of a career, and are as different as the love one may have for nature in its appeal and its joy. Very seldom the same appeal is felt toward all the beautiful expressions of nature; one cares intensely for the sea, another for the stars, and another for the dark forests that people the high places where unfolding life is ever seen as a personal appreciation of all it brings. In a general way these are all a part of the divine love for the unfolding world. Just so, people find it almost impossible to find in one person the completeness that I find in you. Often I have spoken of the Brownings, and yet I am confronted by the evidence of his different attitude toward what was dear and sacred to her. Her intuitive and responsive soul found reality in these experiences that we enjoy, and loving her he did not file exceptions to such a belief and the following of her beliefs to espousal, and I do not yet know whether in the literal sense of walking forward to the new Light he walks with her hand in hand as you and I are bound to do. Our experiences in communication give us the confidence of complete and perfect response all the way up to the throne of eternal truth, for this must have been

VISION 

a part of the Creator's design, to have the continued experiences of communication and love and helpfulness through all changes.

THE VOICE. Suddenly he stops. 

[He resumes.] Oh, dear, I hate to feel so limited! 

THE VOICE. I know that out of every sitting something comes from him after he goes away, like the reflection of sunset. You must not worry about Medie; 

I take care of her. You got tired and were holding on tight to get it just as he said it. 

THE LETTER continues. Now that this is a day of solemn memories for the Christian world I feel the rigidity of the ceremonies which keep us aware of the historical truth as something more beautiful than any other experiences of JESUS OF NAZARETH. To give ourselves up to the acceptance of that truth is to become inheritors of the experiences of those who talked and walked with Him after the crucifixion, and the return to the dear ones and to the faithful who were made aware of the victory after seeming defeat. I have seen thousands and thousands of those who have been through what you and I are experiencing to-day. I do not refer to the 

Christian family, but to those whose eyes were opened to vision after death had given them the same belief in the possibility of a service to the blind and the desire to bring that consciousness and knowledge down to the daily experience of presence. And in my many talks with them there is always one note just a little out of the ordinary of those who are striving to reach a point of contact which I so highly prize in my messages to you. First to find words that bind you to me as nothing else can; then that strength of power which is yours

VISION 

163 to grasp the significance of a love that was never expressed in words that are so much a part of life and its contacts, suddenly finding the soul of love giving the definite form and the joy that words bring; then the desire to share this gift with those who are in need of the knowledge. I do not refer especially to the comfort of mourners, but that is part of the note I referred to in the sorrow felt that the knowledge was not theirs until death opened and light came to the vision of cherished and beloved companionship with those who had seen and believed and did not break for a moment the established lines and love. We have done just that. The moment that I became aware that life in the old way was done for me, I also became aware of you as a living presence in my life just beginning. Think of that-I who had been so eager to know and yet had never been sure just how the life after death might become a part of future unfoldings of the consciousness of God. It was no romantic dream of mine or yours. It was not the awakening to a new bond between us, but just as naturally as you may go to Maine or New York or any of the loved places you continue to care for after the establishment of house and family, so I found the life a continuance of my purposes, desires, strength, and joy going on within me in another town- -one might say if one might be forgiven for speaking of the spirit-land as a township (he smiles). It is made up of such abiding places of the spirit-body, and yet is not so set apart by boundaries as the world is here. How would you, or anyone who is kind and tender, feel related to be ruled over in a small portion of the earth by one who insists that annihilation must come to another group of people that

VISION 

he may have a place of colonization for his growing race? This at Easter when love has conquered death and life broadened by the victory, the outspoken declaration that it is a right belonging to him when there appears an advantageous spot of earth for him to plant his victorious armies and sweep into nothingness the land beloved and ancient with its treasures that are valuable and important. We become aware of these tremendously selfish assertions now, by the grief that swells with the note of despair when the beauty and fidelity of a people are being dealt with as if of no consequence. 

All this comes rising to our atmosphere until I feel the old desire to right a great wrong and see no way to do it except by the constant appeal to the higher, diviner purposes that bring us into contact through unfoldment and not by force of arms. To pile our youth up with a defensive army for the unfortunate is only adding fuel to the fire. So we are working with the right power, which is as if calling to each other from point to point. Open up the gates of the temple of knowledge, O you people of earth, and bind the world together in the understanding of life and power in the future. If death ended all it would still be in the heart of the pure and good to resist all the desire to build on the bodies of mankind the power to work out one's own way to success. 

There have been many revelations within a few weeks. 

All these disasters that come as afflictions to people of every kind of life, good, bad, Christian, heathen, whatever it may be. There has been much to add to the desire to bring comfort to those who sit in the midst of ruin and weep for the lost treasures of life; and out of all the misery there shines a light of human brotherhood

VISION 165 by those who are aware of that brotherhood, oh, so wonderfully expressed by those who are aware that human brotherhood exists even in its pains and its devastations. I have also been much aware of the beauty and solemnity of these memories of past Easters. Every word, every bit of testimony that has been brought to the present hour, is repeated again and again from London to Paris and New York and Boston, and every bit of evidential matter that comes through experiences like ours is treasured as never before, I believe-so it becomes of absorbing interest to us-you and me-to bind our knowledge into the form to be treasured for those who need it-and who does not? With that in mind as an additional reason for my continuance of evidential pur- poses I have caused what seems to be an accepted challenging attitude in a group of youngsters who call me by one name after another as fast as they can think of them: "Philosopher," "Communicator of Frances,❞whatever comes to the mind; they come and talk it over with me. It has been something that I could not very well escape, and I am especially glad to find that it gives them a desire to add their own evidence. I in no way wish to interfere in the effort of George Pellew in his constructive work among the young; he is very earnest about the care they use in making their plans. The "little lad" long ago came to the light that he could give in some problems and lo! here is his mother giving from him to one and another a word, a name, or friendly interest achieved through his earnestness, which seems to hold each of us with some degree of the power we may use. There have been some messages carried from the “little lad” by means of friends, which have given the

VISION mother hope that the relative values may be sustained, so now, if now and then I give to you some part or some name that has been given to her, it makes you partner with me in a work we had never taken up as to-day. In the sitting not long ago another lad whose name begins with R [Roger] came with the desire to have the mother of the "little lad" take to the mother of Roger a word of love and greeting. Roger had been here at this same little altar before, and most naturally found it possible to send the greeting and also to tell, when possible, much that was going on in the material affairs of the father he loves. I speak of it to you that you may see how we find ourselves part of a system to keep fresh in the mind that the love of children remains fresh and tender as if death had never touched them. And the "little lad's" 

Louise came, too; she is a wise and lovely girl, pure in spirit, sweet in her love. Quite ingenuously she asked if she could not come to me with a word to you about her adoption of you as "Aunt Frances" and if you pass the word along at a time when it seems right, it will add to the feeling that we are captains in a little regiment of the "soldiers of Truth." This Louise laughs as she says, "When Aunt Anna was here one day in a storm, the man of this house said, 'You should not have attempted to come out this day,'—he was thinking of the long journey she had to take and wondered if she could get home again, for a blizzard was on. She lifted up her face to him and said, 'I don't believe that Aunt 

Frances ever stayed away for any storm, and if she can come, I guess I can."" Then they both laughed, showing that your intrepidity bears fruit and gives the ex-

VISION 

167 ample that confidence in the spirit message is superior to blizzards and storms.

Now I am to sit beside you in all these hours that are filled with the memories of past Easters when the reality every morning for us had not been established except in the faith you held that all things work out for the understanding of mysteries that may confuse for a time. 

All these are now like a garden of lilies that rise to our memory and we are aware of angel voices and beloved faces and forms that are swept with joy that there are revelations that come every day to lift us into the dispersion of the agonies of doubt and victory of knowledge. I'll go now. You will be happy to know that even if I leave here, I'm with you-you can't escape me. 

Isn't it beautiful to have the sunshine, light, and air that seem to clear away all the clouds of the moment?

LETTER XXVII 

April 23, 1936 

HE VOICE. He was here before, but has stepped out.

THE Voron lerful this morning. The spirit in him is so different at different times, one so exalted, standing here quite separate from everything and everybody except you. I find that consciousness in him like a gleam from an exalted soul as he stands out in the open, breathing in the spirit of eternal life. I love him as one would worship a superior being.

THE LETTER. Like one who comes to a communion table I come to this hour.

I have been thinking deeply

VISION 

of all that has been revealed to us as individuals, quite without the joyous experiences which come as a result of the new-found knowledge of the continued life with possibilities that were unbelievable until the test of death proved that the kingdom of life is open to us if we touch gently the opening; then we find there all that man can desire in the ecstasy of bliss, and the progress of unfolding beauty in all that is created by God. Quite solemnly, yet with the constant unchangeable companionship which I have with you, I accept—I accept in full measure-knowing that you wish me to do so and to share with you all-everything that I find more than a dream, more than a feeling coming to each of us at some times in our human experiences of wonder and joy finding the very presence of God in the world about us. I am so often thrilled by some tender word, some feeble expression of a great poet like Tennyson who held the flower in his hand, like Browning who whispered his faith in simple phrase—all these come to me again and again as in the old days, when a little fern broke ground, unrolled its green verdure, and became a waving plume in the woods. I wanted to tell you all about these inner feelings that held me captive for a moment when I felt sure that I was alive after all the years, after all my love of the beginnings of things that grow under our eyes and come out in fragrant beauty, or the stars that lead us to all the points of the compass as if the directive power were like a finger pointing to all above, below, and in between, where we might find the answer to the soul's prayer for understanding; when 

I was aware that you also had been over that same trail

VISION 

169 up the mountain heights of life, sometimes looking from a window at night when some splendid demonstration of the orderliness of the stars was before you, sometimes in meditation, and often in the very clear and lucid way you expressed yourself to me in those beautiful experiences of soul-exchange in letters to me. Not thoughtexchange, they were beyond that, but soul-exchange. 

Why, Faith, we were then in this same precious relationship that only death made clear to us! After I had given a few of those first messages I said to myself—I will not attempt to bring into the messages I give any advice or any of the many things I might see to, or in any way bring into contact the lower, the needy, the sad, or the terrible things that still go on; although I always desired to have those errors corrected and life made smoother for the erring and the suffering and the poor. "The poor you have always with you" has been so long a command to each one to remember and serve. 

I loved that implied command. I desired more than all else to tell about the release from that as a purpose to go on into the realms of the blest where I found myself hand in hand, heart to heart, soul to soul, made eloquent by my joy. So I did not make very much of an appeal to go on in any of my old services-if I may name them in that way. But by increasing your faith by knowledge and giving you the facts, the truth of what I found, we might spread a veil of happiness over the other situations—that is what they are, situations that must be met and mended in a wise way. This is a consecration of our knowledge that I pass on into the world of truth to become a victorious expression of the almightiness of you

VISION 

life with God, the creator of the worlds that march endlessly, so far as I may understand, onward in the procession of time.

All this has been absorbing, and we—you and Ihave been as active as if it were a planned work, planned only as days came and nights brought us together in a re-consecration to the wonder and beauty of the life we have found. Then came the appeal of the youthful group of those who had never been trained in the ways of wisdom by experience, but entered joyously in juvenile spirit into the wonderful experiences which they had in coming to the dear ones whose lives had been saddened by their death and brightened by their new life. Without any intention of sponsoring them in their efforts or of bearing a part in their personal experiences, I responded to that appeal to youth in my own personal experience where I am. Quite like a child I felt the impulse to be up and doing, as and doing, as they were. The they were. The very attitude of their acceptance of me as one of their group I think gave me a delight that I had thrown aside the wrappings of age and appeared to them as one dear because of a very earnest response to all they cared to invest in me. Not pride—not that—but I had long been part of the older and sometimes sadder men and women, and it was like-shall I say play-day?—yes, although with no feeling at all like a grandfather at a children's tea-party, yet that would make me quite able to stand up and dance a minuet with them if I had still retained old age and snowy hair.

Now I am thinking that I should make more of that first venture in giving to you the evidence of my progressive march toward the divine and more than divine

VISION 

171 companionship with you. First I would not involve these fresh young spirits in all the truths that must come personally through changing scenes, hours, days, and years. 

They will come to you and me in the days that await us with that better understanding of what they have been experiencing than they may do now. The fathers, mothers, brothers, and sisters share some of their duties which prepare them for their future. Just as the little lad, so fresh and sweet and tender, like a young rose in a garden-just as he declares for the better study and better use of opportunities which his brothers do not always accept as opportunities but the drudgery of preparing for larger opportunities-just as he tells his mother in the tones and methods and experiences that she may expect of him-just as the other boys in that group find others still streaming into the new life with pain and sorrow to be set aside, from the home life that is the foundation of all that is glorious in earthly life, they take them in hand and try to give them just what they have received. Why, it becomes a very strenuous and very honorable effort that we oldsters make to add in a way a lesson now and then; and I find that like 

Longfellow in his study I am besieged as they come down the stairs, and begin to add to my happiness and my responsibility. Isn't it the strangest way in which we immediately, from questions of our children, find that we have responsibilities which we may neither leave to society nor to God?-that is something parenthood does to make the life we live right and true and pure. 

If we do not accept it, life becomes somewhere a failure, and neither society and the fathers in heaven, God, nor His angels may pay the price-it is paid by the irre-

VISION 

sponsibility of those who accepted and failed. We know that, and I am inclined to believe that my knowledge of the seriousness of life in the imperfect state of living may have colored my sense of responsibility to these dear children. 

But always, Faith, my first and dearest message of life came through you, and you must as I do feel the honor of sharing with them whatever we may that will keep them in the faith we hold dear. Still I have decided, and I said to their sponsor and helper and teacher that for a time I might stand aside with you in that dear familiar attitude of the spirit-to-spirit conversation which adds to your knowledge as fast as I find it where I am, and let these tests of the interpenetrating knowledge of the conscious life of those they love rest on merits given without my particular care. Do not for a moment feel that I desert them, oh, no. I shall have a children's open door to my abode, entered through a garden where they may linger and talk about the dear old "philosopher" who lives within, but "we must not disturb him, for he is helping the world, including those dear to us, to come to the place where we live and are happy." I suppose those words will float in like a sort of mysterious whispering outside a window, and perhaps I may leave the chair where I sit to think over the next step for us, and perhaps I may look out and say, "Oh, come in!" or "I may come out and play with you!" 

Now as I think of this I want not disappointment or feeling of regret but rather that the "O. P." has started them on an independent venture of experience and service.

I have been amazed—that is the word-to know how

VISION 173 these spiritual faculties have been suppressed or sent out in directions of inquiry that are as futile as if they had been left dormant by curiosity or the perpetual effort of some minds to discover the laws of some things that may happen, like hauntings-mental scientists who are never to be depended upon to stand steady, but run away on a wild flight of fancy about raising the dead, or forever erasing traces of sin which bring death by some supernatural power they have captured and paid a price to obtain wholesale and retail at so much a head. [THE VOICE. Sarcasm.] Not sarcasm, Truth. 

Sarcasm is only a play on words that may bring temper or hatred all the evils that arise from a disappointed appreciation of a virtue not possessed. No, it is truth.

Many of those who claim to heal the sick may believe that the robe of discipleship has fallen on them because they use the name of the Master. There are so many crucifixions of truth-nailing truth to the cross that they see borne by one who established a custom, not a truth. It is our part in our efforts to stand by the truth that is eternally perceived, of the hourly resurrection in the deaths of life and the strength of the spiritual life born, often for the first time, after the body has been laid away.

All this is to me a call to the souls of men. If death is the only recognition they may find possible for that inflowing life that is eternal, then death is an angel releasing from bondage the spirit that perceives its own grave and knows that the path leads forward, not backward, to that strange combination of old philosophies that have been disclosed by truth. How strange to fashion faith on a belief that the working hypothesis

VISION 

for soul growth must be carried on in the same old world with temptations, vices, and foolishness to make it an impossible belief to the wise! I have seen some of the supposed old souls working out salvation in a less feasible and useful plan than the spectacular one of the rising of a spirit into life and God. I did not fancy very much the ideas of changing bodies as tabernacles for old souls-nor will you when you see the disappointments of Cleopatras of the modern world who turn back history and remember all that happened in old Egypt. There are so many sensations aroused by imagination that I wish to keep right on with our useful, helpful knowledge that death is an incident in life, and once passed in the body that has kept the soul safe in transit is not to be endured again even for the sake of an argument. 

Never but once, as I have been made aware, does the soul pass through that experience of infinite changebut once, and that is not to be regarded as a fatality; that is why I desire to say in words-Death is not a fatality when the whole path is clarified and pain and separation and sorrow swept away by the knowledge that one step through an open door brings to each soul life that is persistent and consistent, for the greater the faith and consciousness of God, the wider, the wiser, and finer is the recognition of truth and of Him, still 

Creator of life.

Not too serious. It carries with it that exclamation- 

Light! light! still light! My last word may not be translated into the exclamation of one who found light, but my first word after finding it was, "Light and still more light!” And as so many souls passing out in the quiet of the chamber of death hear music, voices speak words of

VISION 

175 noble truth, it is the entering of a finer sense of hearing and consciousness of a divine truth-a vision of light that never fails. One, two, three instances I may give you, duly authenticated. One of a man who sat beside the great Emerson in his last hours, wishing it had been possible to write the words that came from Emerson in his last moments, in unconsciousness of body. A man in 

Japan sitting beside a beautiful, beloved wife, who never shared with him the hope he had through communication, telling the story of her last hours; she suddenly opened her eyes-"I hear music, beautiful music. I see our son." Evidence? Yes, multiplied by thousands who have had such transmission of word and knowledge in the last hours, and accepted as delusion of the dying. 

Some day I hope to speak about delusion of the dyingthe delusion of the living, and the illusion of those who know not the beauty, joy, pain, and sorrow of death.

THE LETTER XXVIII 

April 30, 1936

HE VOICE. Your splendid man has written on the table.

THE LETTER. Blessed hour of consecration! Now I 

have been thinking of the wonder that never ceases when we consider the seeds of truth that are sowed in the minds of those who need the evidence and joy of the truth that gives to life a new meaning. Tragedies slip into the life as experiences that may be understood when we do not write Finis on the door of the tomb. I have also been thinking much about the possibility of

VISION 

guidance and directive influence from those beloved ones who may not be able to have the blessing bestowed on me by you. Do not forget that you are the light of my life now, and you know I wish it to be so forever, with that dear precious influence of the devotion we have for each other, which in lieu of a better name we call love. It is love. I love every expression which gives me the beauty of your mind and the glory of your spirit. 

I have found that I grow stronger every day. These days are the open-letter days when words fall on the table with some eloquence that I might use in stronger language, but will withhold that we may not be misunderstood, but stand with our faces toward the unfolding of all our propensities for the better and diviner life that is love itself, pure and undying. 

But as I said a moment ago, I grow stronger in my power to help all-yes, all—of our dear ones who must live their lives as better formed and directed by our constant care when care is important. At times I have wondered, after walking down the hill to the car that waits for us, why I did not write some specific word to one or the other of those we hold in our hearts, and yet when I come, the wonder of the eternal life that is ours as we perceive it in its majesty comes to me as if the ministry of the spirit-people is after all the beneficent influence we may give to those we love and to the world, and turn the faces toward the light that has been given to each other—you, my dear one, and I, your devoted one. Neither of us could have imagined a work like this. 

I have reached for your hand so many times in the years that have brought us to this supreme experience -literally reached for the words that never failed me

VISION 

177 in my desire to find answer to my prayers, and I thank

God that you never failed even when I tried to get the very utterance of my soul to you. You must know that no obligation which is mine in any realm of life will ever be forgotten or set aside, but that obligation of a love that is co-existent with my desire to reach the highest estate of life where I am is the very essence of a grasp of the truth that sets us in the realm of life together. 

It would be a divided allegiance to my own soul if I separated our lives with the false idea that there is any type of limitation to the unfolding possibility of a greater service which is inclusive. There is something that comes into my life from you which it would be useless to try to explain. We each find that power, and we do not hesitate to accept it, but joyously add it to our power to bring the highest and best into the lives of others. This is a part of our ministry which we place on an altar, and would have it in its present form go as a message to those who are immersed in riddles of the mercantile life and the demands that keep the door closed, sometimes locked, where even the dearest ones are temporarily set aside against the tender and beautiful lives of those in any sphere. Just as we men do not open our doors for the sympathy or comradeship of those of our household who would like to add to our cares the mind of helpfulness. It can't be done. The doors automatically close as we know that the burden or problem would add to the tremor of the situation, and so we are divided in that hour of encompassing difficulties. All this makes us what we are as providers and care-takers of our family responsibilities. I imagine 

I have held that idea so long that I feel the desire to

VISION give you the full freedom of my hopes and let problems that are best solved by time go on, unless it is possible to find a chink in the wall where we may pass a letter through, or a whispered word find its way to aid. That is not precisely the way, but pretty nearly, as well as I may express it, to give you the understanding of my knowledge that you will carry on the truth and the joy that truth gives.

In all the world of research, and science, study and application of lessons learned, I say I do not know anything that may compare with the purity and loveliness of established communication, and there is nothing that may compare with the certainty of the communications. 

Communications may be very terrible from some sources and may be so out of tune with the heavenly music that comes floating into the world like a Psalm at the end of the day that one reaches to shut it off and find complete silence rather than the words that stain the mind. 

That is one of the increased blessings of communications that reach the listener almost before the words are transmitted, and one of the evils is that we may not make distinctions between the good and the evil until the mind travels back to the sender and finds that the bad and indifferent people who are constantly (he smiles) emitting sounds are foolish, ignorant, or definitely evil. 

Voices may be raised in protest of evil communications (not these, not these)-but until there is the gift of knowledge to find out ways of making distinctions we swallow them down bones and all until we find ourselves with mental indigestion. Truth is always available if we seek to find it, and it is always my desire (that is not a boast, but a real attitude which I hold)—my desire

VISION 

179 to know the truth-anyone seeking from me in any position I held was made aware that I must know the truth-only in that way may an advisor [Voice. He accents the "or" to see that I do it right] know how to bring light out of darkness and help for helplessness. 

That is so true of the life where I am. Dressed-up falsifiers, with smiles and smirks and promises, do not get the shadow of attendance that they do in the earthly world. Two things help-one the insight of the spiritpeople who are able to see motives underneath the uniform of flesh. That is a very great advantage we have over the physical life. Lies of deceptive power to those on earth are swept aside as one might throw up the shades and the lights fall in revealing power. Too busy to do that when the abundance of life in clothes and sheltering homes must be earned, the whole fabric is challenged by appearances and the broken threads are never quite as firm as the garment that covers but does not hide. If I could bend my knee in prayer for one descending gift that would bring life to a better and happier time of the years spent in building up the best, I would pray for the illuminating gift of the soul, which comes as the gift of death. What would ever become of those frightful enemies who neither safeguard themselves nor the institutions that support them?

Now to a happier theme. There have been some very definite helps that these youngsters have been able to give to those they love, and I do not wish to be put out of the ring where I see them like a garland of flowers, with the beauty and lithesome spirit of youth. I am just one with them. And always I know how you will feel the necessity of including the young. Dark indeed would

VISION be the world about me if I had to sit with only old people as companions, and I have been with you in your companies of old, young, and little ones. 

May I speak of a vision I have often had of you? I think I will. Always in a garment like a bit of the skythat wonderfully beautiful cloudless blue that gives one a dream of a fair and lovely flower growing at our feetand there is always above your head something that I call my Faith's crown of asphodel, which is always ashine, for the light from above falls into the flower as if it were a cup full of sunshine to be splashed as from a goblet on all who come within the radius of its overflowing beauty. And I look up and say, "O Goddess!" —and then I find you walking swiftly toward the place of our meeting. And I want to tell you-have often meant to do so—about the approaches to the world where we are both familiar with roads, paths, bridges, taking us into certain limited areas in full contact with each other. There are what may be called cloud-ways, like currents in a river, and sea and land passage-ways that lead from house to house. They come to earth in certain specified directions. I have never bumped into another angel in my passage to earth and you. I fancy that there is a tendency to increase these highways, roads, really currents that we easily find, and use them as we find them in a boat and a river, get out and “don't pull against the stream!"

It was without any idea of using my particular pathway to you, because I could see you. You were just where I could easily do so. Then later I found that great companies of people were surging in one or the other direction; I looked, and there was definite evidence

VISION 

181 of coming to certain stations, London, New York, Boston-wherever people are. Those highways to unknown lines are sometimes found by thought, and like the Amazon or any other great unexplored area come to the mind, and with the thought also the desire. All this is only something that makes possible contact with a world without difficulty. Somewhere in this realm of life are very distinct paths that lead to a church, a school, a monument, or to a house consecrated to a service; not necessarily church forms, but as they might go to a group of Quakers or teachers or Shakers, or Salvation Army or Sisters of the Church-always one sees pathways to these. So I see them moving about. I go sometimes just to see, but always I say, "I wish you were here!" knowing that you will come. The literal life that goes on between those who have reached great knowledge and those who struggle and hide and falter needs something or someone to lead them out, and I say, "Now, my precious Faith, we are establishing a little highway shining with light. It leads to you, not only to me; I don't need to be led-but it leads. You will find spirits coming in numbers to you. To get a word to them will be glory and peace; this pathway to you is overshadowed by love. I walk with you, for I am there to see them as they come. No one of your people is left out. Your father stands there erect and sure that his daughter is part of a great and beautiful revelating power. 

I will take no more time now. I am happy-doubts all cleared, and God, as God, is restorer to me as the beneficent power of life.

VISION LETTER XXIX 

May 7, 1936 [I BEGAN with a few words: I want to be sure that you understand that the delay in giving out in full publicity the two books is that J. asked my promise not to circulate them even in private. This does not entirely preclude some people who have had the first book, and 

Dedication has gone to some of such. 

He replied: "Yes, I do understand, and appreciate all that you have told me, my precious Faith.”] 

HE LETTER. All this that I have had of light, beauty,

Tand loveliness of spiritual companionship is the finest experience of my entire life, past, present, and all that is to be, which will, I know, bring the same rare and vital life we may enjoy forever.

As I came to-day to give myself the happiness of writing my letter to you, I had a few suggestions that may be useful to those who follow our experiences which give in a measure the knowledge we share with each other. Blessed and pure as the morning dew on a rose in June are all these intimations of diviner love than is usual between the most precious relationships. Three things have been revealed to me as I recall these relationships. First, your constant and abiding faith in the possibilities of communion since we began; then my undying devotion to you as a person, combined with that also undying desire to add to the knowledge of those who wish to know that these truths are available and important; and then the guiding and guarding powers which surround the conditions of communication.

VISION 183 I place these factors as largely responsible for our unusual and fruitful experiences in spirit communications. 

I realize that mere curiosity, or lack of desire or care, may work havoc with the perfection of expression and stimulate some idea for use of these opportunities to make gain for personal reward or renown, a fact that does not harmonize in any degree with what we find. 

That is all that need be said to understand why so many occasions that might have brought the glorious truth of life sanctified and made useful by the people on this side of life clarified by death, are not forthcoming. So we have been aware of the ungratified desires of many of our political enemies, and we know better than you now know how much the effort made from our side has brought about changes which may give the world itself knowledge which may wreck the plans of selfish and appropriating influence in the lower order of seeking politicians. Glory be to those who are able to catch the thought of higher and nobler thinkers in and beyond the life where you dwell and bring out of destruction and immoral life the noble, constructive life of unselfish workers in the kingdom where truth abides. Of course, as the world is made up of all kinds of peoplehigh, low, mediocre, we have them all about us after death, if we desire to find they survive as those who even before death are called immortal for the attitude of nobility they claim. They are in the life after death, paralyzing, stultifying, and defiant as if they had the sense of power over any of our plans and pursuits in the search for truth. The truth is that they are apparent the moment they come into a place of light-no one may dress for the part he would desire to play to over-

VISION throw the kingdom of life after his days of conflict and slight powers are ended; like a dark form of cringing effort to hide the real purpose of approach they are as evident as if unclothed, and even through assumed garments there is the clear vision that is ours. I mean ours, as we stand in the light. The flood light of truth illumines all about us, and we are able to look upon the naked soul as it makes attempts to conceal what life after death reveals. I am not writing these words with any sense of superiority over others, for even as I know what goes on through the gift of life after death, again and again the old desire to find some good in the worst offender possesses me momentarily, until I withdraw and remember that light must come from within after all and I may not be able to supply what I might wish to those who have sinned. The day of judgment comes and no man may withhold that first step toward the light which that day may yield. So the old desire to forgive, put forth a hand of help, and give a word of hope is better not used until desire on the part of the one who has sinned is expressed. This does not appear to be in any form a sentiment that is effectively used against the coming light, but rather an opportunity for those who are at last to be saved from even the greatest offenders against God and man to see the helplessness of appealing for forgiveness without the sincere desire to go upward instead of merely onward. There are always some of those who have loved the sinner-I say that just to make distinction between those who are right and true and those who do not wish to be right and true. There must be some effort in such a person to desire truth and goodness and virtue, because it is

VISION 

185 the finer expression that follows the lower desires of the rather animalistic life. Those who have loved these people who have often some fine point of contact with those loved make great efforts to break the connections between the past of memory and the light of hope: mothers, fathers, children, and dear ones who never lose, never desert their own, but even they must give the resolute spirit of confidence and not seek to take up the task of paying the price for the sins of those they love. That is the supreme justice that arrives in time to each soul. The experiences between are, I think, somewhat like that purgatory of the after life where revelation brings results that were otherwise impossible. 

That must keep us to the faith that the Eternal and the Giver of life from beginning to end, if end there be, is wisdom and love and justice and the powerful and blessed Father in Heaven. 

Now that I have swept away in my words to you the thought of eternal punishment and have given a word of confidence in wisdom and love as the aim and end of life in its tendencies without light, we may do away with that terrible belief in unending fires that destroy not but torture through the ages. Unspeakable as that belief has been in many centuries, it still persists among the enlightened people of the world who believe in punishment for sin. As virtue is its own reward, so sin is its own punishment. Results prove what we may not always see at the first glimpse of life.
