Now for my ever recurring expression that love is the only perfect way of life. I am remembering many, many loves that bind to the perfected experiences of the love I personally hold in my heart; love of art, of science, of

VISION 

music, of little children, of nature; ocean depths and starry skies lead us to the divine and personal comprehension of something never to be expressed until the future adds its knowledge and binds all into the oneness of the complete pair. The single soul is a solitary soulthe dual soul comprises all that makes for universal love of everything that is creative of love. Seldom is it possible to find that binding while the mind is growing into the completeness of its knowledge through experience. 

With our darlings round about us holding fast to our guiding hands, experiences of the guiding power of age and wisdom constantly declaring that we are part of a universe of our own creation in family and friendly life, we are not constantly thinking of the supremacy of that complete soul-life, it is not wise or commendable, for our responsibilities demand attention, and if we mingle in the affairs of a neighborhood, a city, a nation, we have other obligations and responsibilities which we would not if we could, and could not if we would, leave if all our efforts and desires corresponding to the finest we know were not given in as full and complete measure as we might give to the upbuilding of each of these situations I have named. Were it otherwise much of what we might find precious as the alchemy of love and service we should lose; and that, too, is something we do not know until we look back and trace the way our feet have found the upper level of duties that are fine and important. That is very useful knowledge for those whose wills are used to obtain what seems to them the most desirable, to those who have attained the divine understanding day by day after death releases from the obligations and freedom comes, sometimes only in bub-

VISION 

187 bles, with the same delight a child may feel in holding a thread on which a scarlet balloon seeks the upper air, holding him to the childish joy until at any moment the thread may snap and leave him instead of the up-winging bit of color, with only a wisp of thread between his little fingers. This is not an imaginary picture; we see those who should be happy drenched in disappointment when the thread snaps and they lie where they are, close to earth without the vision.

All this is not a national disaster, a personal regret over business, religion, politics, or whatever has held them to a hope, but in every department of life there are ideals and ideas, and ideas may bind one to the possibilities of their infallible inflation, and ideals be lost in the fog of deflation, and it is very evident to all who look on the pictures of daily life that to the individual the ideas have their ups and downs, but ideals remain forever far above the doom and conquest of disappointing and broken ideas. We often refer to the ministry of the Gospel, Christian soldiers of the armies of eternal life and the peoples who recite throughout Christendom the prayers, noble and beautiful, led by exalted teachers of those who need leadership, faith, and courage. But if they fail of this wisdom, if they find other realms of sophistry and bring to their people only half-truths, haphazard devotion to ideals, what may we do then?

The crazed and furious men and women who count their blessings by their place in society, bank accounts, and an equality that makes distinction for them and those who are in contact with them, and go to the place of worship only to keep up a form of ceremony as old as time itself when fire-worshippers and other long-lost

VISION races found comfort in offering sacrifices on certain days and altars-that is not of the nature of revealed truth that comes after death; altar fires, communion tables, associations that repeat prayers for this or that, sudden death, disaster, and all the floods of powers that are evident-they are good if taken as a sanctified word to bring to the world belief, faith, confidence of God; all good for the Baptist, the Catholic, the Episcopalian, each in his little realm of baptism, forgiveness of sins, or the place of retirement from the cares of the world.

That is why I add to my devotion and love every time I make any approach in this audible language, at first translated for me, for it has brought to me the understanding of the necessity for demonstration of continued love after death. I must remedy that statement. "After death” may mean to some the regulation of affairs. One says, "I must try to find time to look into this idea of the communion of soul." I say, Through life and death, continuous without rest or change of mind as years may bring the continuation of life from breath to breath-I say very solemnly-one breath merges into the next breath in the moment of the change as naturally as the last breath of slumber merges into the first breath of awakening, without any cessation of activities of the mind-the mind that is normal—when deranged, step by step that, too, is made right.

THE VOICE. He spoke to her of the awful derangement of the man who has taken Ethiopia by force and tyranny, for the gold, the lands, and the wealth that gave the desire to be supreme. He suddenly shows pictures of

VISION 189 a great open book. Across its pages is written a truth for the world to see and understand, the history of the footsteps of truth marching through the ages, always the illuminated text is a call to the highest and divinest lives of men. He turns the book over, folds it back, showing it blank with nothing accepted as final until the conquering races are put aside, and individuals arisen from the slavery of conquerors write on the illuminated pages the truth that life is forever moving forward and upward even through these dark days of depression in our own experiences.

THE LETTER continues. I love you as the sun brings out hidden depths of the winters of life coming into springtime loveliness, and knees are bent in thanks to

God that the sun dies not, but adds to the fullness of life, love, beauty, and faith. I still love all these expressions of beauty and bounty still with me, and with the life that is illumined, glorified, and justified, one may say-by the love that dies not.

Now may I add a word to that friend to whom I have talked, and begged him to say what was in his heart alone with his dear one. He returned to me. We had talked over these truths and he felt that I could help him to the confidence to just say what he would if they were together in familiar surroundings. His joy was great. You have made all this possible.

VISION LETTER XXX 

May 14, 1936

HE LETTER. There are hours when the illuminating 

THE 

power of knowledge comes over me with a tremendous desire to give you all that I am constantly finding as the fundamental experiences far beyond the life that is inconstant and out of the knowledge given by death. 

At first the wonder and delight of hearing and seeing you, and the understanding of your readiness to give others the trial of the apparent and desired knowledge impressed me more as a privilege than as a duty, but in the love of the opportunity and precious privilege of going forward gathering the truth into an understandable language that we take up together quite without effort all these days, we have found the point of contact. I have rarely depended on any mind but my own to give me freedom of speech, and at times I felt almost loquacious, yet never felt that I wandered from the theme of our messages which came so definitely from you to me without words and from me to you with them.

Once I felt the sadness of lost hours which life had not given us to carry into the gracious expression of our devotion to each other. Now I am very glad to acknowledge that those lost years were never lost when I realize that freedom comes as the result of our spiritualized experiences and our desire for the perfection of a friendship as rare as our expressed love is rare now among these around us as we go forward into the Eternal. There has recently come to me a period of balancing the knowledge which comes after death when the

VISION 

191 devaluation of what we felt to be important before death gives one the sense of making plain the account of life, its gifts and its opportunities. There has been a tidal wave of spirit-influence over the nations of the earth, and I have been aware of the unbalanced lives that result from some open avenues where the wave has swept through the world as a malady, catching in its power men and women in the contacts of life, high, low, lofty, mean, scurrilous, and majestic, and there has been a strange unrest as a result of this wave of spiritcontacts unspiritualized by ignorance and used only as a new-found power imperative in its resistance to judgment and the balance which must come. All the unforeseen terrors of an unbalanced world are now a part of the new and wonderful experiences which come to my mind, and I find that there is peace-peace-in the knowledge that we are still able to carry the glory of the divine order to those who may accept. And with that uncompromising acceptance of love as the soul of the universe and the power of humanity to touch fingertips to the robe of the splendid revelations of truth, I hail with joy every experience which leads to light on the darkest problems of the world in its birth-pains and am, oh, so grateful that we each had the eagerness to know about the other, which has given us this knowledge and happiness. Because there is swept into the experiences of life all that drifts and shifts and is never steady or reliable as a test of the values of what may also come as a revelation and an answer, does not mean that the lower is more powerful or more efficient than the higher, the tendency to acclaim oneself as able to change the face of the city, the community, or the indi-

VISION 

vidual lives of others meets a challenge from every awakened mind. I refer especially to the minds on the earthly side of life, where reformers and hard-pressed friends of humanity are eagerly striving to find ways to meet the situation of this hour, not in warfare, not in combat between the souls of the men who make acclaim, but with a tremendous example of the high and glorious power of the lords of the upper realms of life after death. "Lords" is merely a term I use for masters-they become lords as wisdom gives them power to express to those who pray for deliverance from evil, and were once a part of the human life as we are—not an especial creation of the spiritual power of the Almighty to give the world a guidance which they could never have earned the right to use, being celestially born, but as having won through the victory of love and all its earned rights to protect and guide. They are the masters, the lords that I referred to masters in sciences, arts, and all the departments of educational life. They must first find knowledge that fits for the position and power. In these late days of the terrific onslaught on those who have been unable to withstand the struggle either national or domestic in the political or statesmanship life, I felt that I must take a step upward and find an answer to our prayers for a return to decency and truth-and here I come (he smiles) with the knowledge that there is no idleness in the upper realms in the flawless integrity of those who still love the world in its beauty and glory, and those who follow on in the footsteps of the great and good who have gone on. Just as I have been filled with what one might call a glory of the love of all that was good before I came into my work on earth and

VISION 193 tried to give the same illustration of what seemed right and good as those who had passed on before, just as I was serving in my ordinary way the truth as I found it with an almost passionate desire to exalt all service, just as love is continuously a part of the upbuilding of a future we dare not dream of, it is so wonderful—there is a service that begins between two who have seen a light and walked toward it and are always able to find others eager to follow that same gleam that is from the higher life of the spirit. So I put my faith in the same wise and beautiful acceptance of the knowledge which I hold, that the good will bring to all who are slumbering now the dependence they may put in the unfolding knowledge of the laws of the Infinite intelligence of the world in which we grow and find our own intelligence guiding us to the truth. The words that come so quickly to mind when mother and father whisper into the ears of an infant child words that have a sound that remains though understanding does not come, so these highempowered love-crowned spirits speak down through the insolent life of those who assume rights that are never constant but very definitely impossible to quietly dispel, down through all these sins of the world, if we dare call them sins lest we be wrong in our estimates.

But-through confusion, let us say—we are made aware of the breathing of the Infinite One through love, which is the power of redemption from ignorance and all its attendant evils.

In our very personal messages which we hold as consecrate there has grown up in our world about us a new faith in the life that follows step by step into light and knowledge a very important and useful knowledge for

VISION those who have no conception except of the ways of the life about them: no idealism, no great and wonderful acceptance of a spirit of infinite love, no way to think of God except as an unfathomable mystery; these men often just leave the problem of the insoluble mystery to time and without any definite plan move on like actors in a scene with all the formality of dress and manners of a period, the soul set apart for some future that may never arrive. It is one of the very useful plans we have carried out to destroy that lack of knowledge and to add to the desire to have knowledge even if only to drag it into discussions that bring notice to it from our point of view, and give a neat bit of sophistry for their discussions. 

Now in the homes where the death-angel-let us call it by its right name-the death-angel comes into every home with such suddenness that the life is at once thrown out of order and the mind unfitted for life's duties by the sorrow and change, and whenever our words have found a place in the family life, the very desire to speak in a known language that is not mysterious makes an appeal and gives light on steps to be taken. That is the release from the pain of separation, the love that is made evident by the consciousness of life after death, and as a heart is re-established in its rhythmic beat and joy comes walking into life as a companion never lost, and faith is established in the goodness and justice of the heavenly hosts, the world rises from the tortuous doubts and dreadful sins and comes home to God. Every churchman who finds courage to speak out about the life after death is an ambassador and disciple of our teaching from our communications,

VISION 

195 those we have formed into words that may be held forever as a text-book, beautiful in its purpose to bring truth to the universe. Every group of people sitting together for a manifestation is so much salvage from the pessimism of the day; the flippant tone and indifferent is not useful in a world where so much is lost by the lack of faith.

I wish to give you a word about these spirit-folk who have found the use they may make of the responsive people in the world. They come in well-organized companies as if for service in the best possible way. They seem never disorderly nor disorganized, and seldom seem to have any spontaneous expression springing from some new discovery. Each is in an assigned place, and when definite care or bodily help in accidents is needed they are the specific guides; and just as around this little communion table we may occasionally find some of our dear ones who come to the place of appointment with living interest, outside the Indian guides hold off any influence that might confuse-not evil, but the onrushing enthusiast who has just learned that here we come is held off by the strong arm of a kind, courteous Indian who does not allow interruption. They are always here; that is why it is so easy to give this type of message.

Abiding faith, continuous expression of endless life, and all that these words mean to you and me, I give you, and I know you will be blest.

LETTER XXXI 

May 20, 1936

HE VOICE. He did not come with you, but is just coming now.

THE LETTER. Precious hour that gives us the privi lege of all the blessings which make for the happiness and peace in both conditions in which we find ourselves, for there comes into my life the consciousness of all that yours, and I would add to yours the definite understanding of the joy that is mine because of my power to feel that we are forever one in the high endeavor to carry the light of the after life to light the way for those whose feet stumble in the dark. Not just a dream of something we feel to be beautiful, but actual contacts that make and re-make the world for those who are able to find the reality of both worlds. They are so frequently like two worlds that it is almost inconceivable that what we know and find lovely is not always included in the life after death. To us this did not come after death, but some type of beauty and purpose in our own experiences was the stepping-stone to the open door and what we have found in the place of our desire. One very limited experience in most lives apparently separated by death is the absence of the old sense of companionship and love and duty, for the average person must keep on in the ways of life which give sustenance, and it often happens to keep the mind occupied with daily duties hiding away the splendid glory of love, the foundation of all that is to be. Sins do not fall away like a garment when the celestial life is found, but they continue in memory

VISION 

197 as a habit that is outgrown by lack of opportunity to keep on in old ways and methods of life. No day of judgment comes for those who spend time in futile efforts to bring into experiences the joy that is always knocking at the doors of life and giving back nothing that redeems from littleness, and the very power that is expended in the effort to find what the mind conceives as success, and possibilities for the things valued, is ours-I believe I am quite right in my knowledge; we never sought success as the world considers it in the lower, lucrative forms of expression. This is not a matter of pride or exultation over what we have found as the first heavenward steps, but because within our soulseither from the opportunities of both education and a purpose that was revealed to us as individuals intensified by a spiritual desire in me and a spiritual consciousness in you-we have had a wonderful experience of years with the purpose of knowing truth and finding it in many ways before death gave me the opportunity to respond to all that you made possible. If souls are redeemed by the truth there is much for us to do and add to redemption already established in centres our messages have made fertile for further blossomings. It is one of the finest and most nearly perfected powers that come to my knowledge of the way great souls of great men and women rejoice whenever there is a reclamation of faith, the gift of the child or the patriarch is restored by the vision of the definite life I found, when I felt the active desire to make use of the every-day experiences with you. First my own precious privilege of saying just what I wanted to say to you without argument or protest, only as one is free to say precisely,

VISION 

"What is found after death? Is there individual responsibility of care and truth in the purpose of association sustained after the new life is adjusted and accepted by the one who sits with the knowledge deep in the heart and gives it out to those who may receive?" That responsibility I always had as a part of my desire to give to those in need-it seemed as if it belonged to me and to my administration of affairs of many who were unable to keep themselves in line with the needs of their days and hours, and I believe I tried seriously to keep before me the facts in my knowledge of their dependence on me, just as you were ever taking up the duties and the gifts assigned you by birth and inclination and kept your individual responsibility to yourself and those about you as an honest administration of your life in contact with the life about you. How wonderful, how bountiful, how purely spiritual our experiences have been! We are only on the threshold of a higher understanding of these revelations. Religion is a most important factor in them, because the world fancies that the beliefs are respectable and effective. So foolish! Responsibility does not depend on what is preached but on what is practised; that is as true of religion as of art or science, for the arts are often suppressed in the finest, divinest forms and made to pay for all the desires of the body—there is in every art, on whatever platform, what is fine and beautiful, and the debauchery of the ones who may persist in painting a perfectly ravishing beauty in form and color, but without soul or substance; and just as in sculpture, painting, music, dramatics, and also of the highest and greatest beauty in life all this is often side by side with the most disastrous, often disgusting

VISION 199 exhibition of a mind that is dark with passion, soiled with desire, red with the blood of its national disgrace. 

I say this exactly as I would speak of a jurist who sells his gift to protect some monstrous form of the evil one himself (he smiles). I have never seen the evil one myself, but I fancy that he has a finger in many a court dinner, and I would feel about those who are a part of such functions just as your father often expresses his fiery indignation at the way the law is juggled by the wit and indiscretion of those who are not a part of the judicial wisdom which is the saving power in the world about us. 

This I am able to say to you, just as you were the light of my life when without any other idea than friendship we at last found that we had builded into our lives what endures forever when one is able to accept it as a gift of life. In our talks-I guess I had better say my talks-but your mind has been talking back to me all these months, and I have carried into my counsels the very ideas that have grown into your life of possible help to many people, and ever I have found many who rejoice that you have become something which you do not yet realize among those whose friends are not yet receiving the gift of continued companionship as you and I have. Suddenly they will arise and give to you the fine appraisal of your efforts and your work in the world; then there will be an awakened spirit of conviction that they may be able, if you seek to add to the knowledge in the world by going into the secret chambers where love is, still is, a part of life, and adding faith in these of prayers for strength. Some of the chamber council meetings have given the clergy a chance to hit back at me by asking questions, and in a good-natured

VISION 

rally to an appeal for advice, I answered, "When the rectors of a church retire for the virtual acceptance of all that is good, and meditate on plans for the renewal of confidence and faith, stand near, my brothers, stand near, for they are in need of all that you can give, and in that intimate hour of prayer to God, your thought will be a part of the answer to their need." 

They are not faithless nor are they fearful of the result of true communion. They desire to be absolutely true in their efforts as leaders; and you will be glad to know that the messages which come now and then through some word of yours will find acceptance in hearts heavy with the desire to know and give comfort. They have been thinking often of the truth which has come in vision or the wise helpful suggestion from a friend. You will not be surprised that those dear to you have had something of the heavenly tone of inspiration through some of the friends of their own parish. I think that way is very possibly superior to the discussions of right and wrong, but I have now in the church where you are considered a true and useful parishioner, then has come first a wonder, then a question, and then a desire, and to some of them the dear ones who went away into the immortal life have begun to find ways of standing near in the quiet hours of meditation or rest, and dropping into the mind of son or daughter the feeling of trust in the power of love that guides, and is never far in time of need. There are great bishops who taught the first laws of life as the unfolding power of virtue, kindliness, and love, and dared not give too much license and freedom as a general gift of the power to communicate, just as worldly blessings were not to be prayed for by the

VISION 201 children of the church; the great gift was the Sacrament given in the name of Jesus-and just as some devout Catholics find it important to withhold the blessing which a spirit-mother might give of guidance, or which a father-spirit bestowed, they never felt the impulse to be guided but to know when the money to take this place or that will materialize, and subordinate the highest experience of spirit-contact, using the knowledge for personal ambitions and the earthly gifts which they might well strive for and gain in the striving. One might go through all the forms of church-going (not religion) and find the pernicious evil of holding some together for the carrying on of state affairs or rolling into the pathway of knowledge, the stumbling-block of personal desires and giving no opportunity for individual responsibility to make over the embodied spirit that fear has arrested. Phillips Brooks I often see, always ready to give all for the individualizing of responsibility for wealth, position, place, and honor, and leaving no place for those who shift responsibility without the effort to carry out obligations and to hold truth as the most subtle and yet energizing power in the affairs of men in the world. 

You are going away, and you will have words floating into your conversation that will be as simply translated into your belief as they are taken to be a tribute to your sincerity—and there will be something like belief expressed, in what you have found, but not fully and definitely accepted, only "I hope it may be as you say."

Others cannot have what we have until they themselves make possible some experience from the life after death. 

It helps me to know that even a suspicion of the belief

VISION you hold comes into normal conversation, and I shall be with you-life and love and confidence are a part of those to whom you go. Oh, how I would love to give a personal message to him, and he would like to have it, but time ever on the wing carries us forward to acceptance of all truth.

LETTER XXXII 

May 29, 1936

HE LETTER. The personal responsibility persists through all the experiences that have come since I found the way to add to the lives that are near and dear to me, and there are unknown ways to add to the helpful, definite cares that do not exhaust energy but really bring an endowment of joy—it is quite like the resourcefulness of prayer in our hours of extremity in earthly life. Prayer is breathless, yet so evident in its effect on minds that wish for the peace of the Eternal, and ever in my ardent desire to tell you of the power within, I am praying the prayers that fall like dew in a troubled heart and bring love into some new and comprehensive understanding without repetition of the words that are commonly given in form. There is just that same quality of peace which reaches me when in the quiet of an hour your thought comes flowing in with that life-giving courage and confidence that is as tender as it is strong and as beautiful as the glory of the light shining in love's domain. Again and again I could fall on my knees and give thanks to the power definitely expressed in law and order, but the impersonal feeling that I have about

VISION 203 the Eternal is absorbed in the knowledge that we two are as one through that same law, and are already a part of the affluence of eternal life wherever we may be. 

That is a consolation quite within the soul of our experience, and from that search after God, so long the theme and substance of the longing of mankind to realize that we are ever a part of what we seek, we may find the knowledge, the very immanent sense of the soul that accepts and seeks no more. 

In these terrific blasts of the lower mentality of the peoples of the world against the holiness and purity of life there is everything that adds to our incentive to keep on in the day and year that marks time for us with the intrepid spirit of the knowledge that God is within our life radius and that we may use that divinity within to battle with the dark terror of all the defiling influences of ignorance and sin. 

THE VOICE. He lifts his hand, white as snow in the light falling on the spot where he stands, and as he lifts it he turns his face to you. In his look there is something of beauty and glory and unfolding power as he blesses with the power that has come flowing through his new perceptions of God in the world.

THE LETTER Continues. All that one perceives adds to power, the unperceived is the unknowable which waits only for perception to add to the consciousness of the climbing of the nations of earth into the superior life and its wonder-making possibilities of the world in action.

THE VOICE. Here he made a digression at this point, ending, "He sees everything."

VISION THE LETTER continues. And now that the word has been said for me, I add this—I see with you as if I had dual perception-how may I give you that same duality of perception that is now so definitely mine since we are together in these beautiful experiences? I see and know all that comes to you of knowledge in the situations on earth-I prefer to say hidden life in the dark places of the world, not only in Boston where your eyes may see, your ears hear the conflict that is on, and the dreadful calamities that come to those who are never tempted to recede, but on whose homes and friendships, activities and problems, falls the shadow of iniquities constantly working out plans that may be fruitful of war in the ways of life. I call "Up! Up! all you who have seen the monstrosities of crime daily thrust across the pages of news, and the mind assaulted by the unbelievable words that give the meaning no one may deny. Was my cry heard? No, not heard, but found in the new sense of responsibility I just mentioned. The oldest of the pioneers of this new world find that they also have a responsibility that they accept, and go into houses and associations where the good and true citizenry is waiting for the moves of minds in their devilishness, and stand ready to strike back at the poisonous reptiles of society, and take away the rights they assume to use all their power to destroy youth and its courage and its unused gifts. There is crime that is detestable, and yet the sorriest of all the mischief-makers in communities is the one who struts in a garment of words that sound pure and holy, as if a helper of the forward movement of truth; the garment of words falls to pieces like rags on a scarecrow set in a field of corn. In this

VISION 

205 conflict that rages through the courts of the world the fires of hate are burning structure after structure until it seems impossible to withstand the onslaught. 

I still hold to the knowledge that I am given through my contacts as a spirit that the worst is always in the power of the best in the long run. I have no foolish hope that all will be carried into the outposts of the criminal classes and destroyed, but that always one step above the criminal is the saintly, definitely ordained realm of light-light reveals, and revelation is what the world needs to-day. Revelation starts the mind into the battle lines of life and gives strength to those who fight with light as the power of purity to the destruction of hiding places of those who destroy partly their own fortifications and their own regiments of sin. Why, the battle among themselves makes big enough the terror of each to the other and that is self-destruction. 

Protection comes to those who don't run away but stand as lights in the pathway of the world. Could I, knowing the dangers of the gangster and the killer, desert my post of observation? Could I, even though my own body is beyond the destruction of the place it holds could I forget? Never. I am ever solving the problem of the best way to bring the best people into power, that is important. Yes, officers of the national institutions must be fitted by those who are above the desire to bring the disorder and evil of untrained men into the life of the town where it is notably evident because neighbors may at any time turn traitor, pockets inside out, when all the values of life have been superseded by the desire to gain-gain what?—just money.

And now that you and I are in the very heat of the

VISION 

battle by our relationship to statesmen versus politicians, we may feel that we have the upper hand of the argument and give attention to the power that we have from knowledge and publicity to sweep the moneychangers out of the temples of the city. Now that there is an additional danger coming to many through fear, it is our joy to say that fear is a figment of the mind and has no real power of its own until it brings disaster in diseases, nervous disorders that are expressly imported into the city ranks and make it impossible to give the presence when needed at some important place. We defy the same influence which strikes down with ammunition the mental and moral desire by the importation of a mental power which is lost to the pure and noble. In other words, there are the same curious complexes in the attitude the bad seek to hold against the good; we have them in the ordinary life-they do not reach their object, they only make attacks usable for them or for men who are uneducated in ethics. So we go right on adding to those timorous ones. They are not unbrave (coin the word) but timid because of no knowledge of the high and truthful empires and peoples just beyond their vision. That is one of the finer and diviner powers that I find everywhere as I move forward to see if any of my messages may not reach those who have been robbed of the truth now that our words of love and confidence and power are going out like white angels forming themselves in the ranks of the known and tested lawgivers of light from the next step. Judgeships must be aided by juries that are as brave as themselves. 

Buying a juryman is as definitely a crime as to shoot the detective who brings evidence to the court. Yet packed

VISION 

207 by the ignorant in too many cases courts are denied the aid of truth-loving people. One of the most effective uses that may be made of the power which is above the lower orders of manipulation is the jury room, and one of my pet theories is that a jury which is moral and true in daily contacts, however humble, becomes a safeguard for our courts of justice; and one of the most important matters of adjustment is to find the way to obtain such a jury as that one found in a little town in New Jersey, where there was the keenest desire to be fair and always unbargainable and give the answer as it appeared to truth-loving men and women not above the average intelligence but far above standards of court hangers-on. 

I have been greatly encouraged about the work we are doing, for I see that there is so evident a help given to those who had been troubled, set on the path of sorrow through death, loneliness, and the tearful hours spent in longing for the companionship that was wrecked by death. That coming of the light is like dawn after the darkness and loss, and dawn is revealing, the shadows do not hide the possibility of giving light to the world in a personal message. Nothing can ever equal the personal message-mother to son, son to father, wife to husband, and love to his own. Death almost gives the personal contact. When these words, mine to you, repeated over and over again, of love that re-energizes and makes useful the heart that is crushed almost into insensibility of its own pain-I call it the next step to faith, and after faith the knowledge of the importance of adding it to the religious fervor for right and beauty and constancy through the changes that the body may experience. The body changes only in its growth, not

VISION 

through any new form of any certain type, but a form that fits its necessities. One must in the earthly life have digestive organs for food in the body with its power to meet changes of atmosphere and growing body. The spirit-body absorbs its strength from its surroundings without the domestic effort to provide food for sustenance, clothing for a body that must withstand all the atmosphere gives; and so on through the days I have been here I have never eaten a peach, nor have I stooped to find an apple that my thought might have taken as a "good bite" in old days-but I have stood near to fruits that were as helpful to me where I am as if they had retained the power to add to the spirit-body as they had in the earthly bodies of people, a perpetuation of the life of fruits and their properly adjusted service to the needs of the next life. I wish some day to say more of these things because the epicure might wonder if lobster salad would not be as acceptable to him in this life as the other, and I would say, "How much of the lobster added to your mental and spiritual unfoldment?" How these things that invite to the tables of hotels are necessary to our unfolding life. Of course we take them because we have been educated to that point, and of course many may insist on things quite like the old life with delicacies, and of course some dear old country mother may still feel her home incomplete without cookies and pies on the pantry shelf-her gift was to the sustaining power for family and friends, not lost after death but supplanted by new and better provision that only spirit-life may supply. It is the unfolding processes of life, life eternal, from cookie jar to 

Alpine height, or only a pathway between them. Change

VISION 209 of sense, of beauty, of love-all these come in the natural procession of life. True also of houses. I often speak of them because the element of the external life is in the daily life of their inmates. I shall see you in a great room that holds our books, a room where a piano standsours-yours and mine, because we each own part of every experience. The grand piano stands open for you to play. There are windows to look through, the sun shining in, and the relaxation of a chair that fits my ease and gives me time for meditation.

They all come. We outgrow this and take on something else, yet any hour we may return through memory to a touch of the old life in the movement forward in the new. Love is the master of all ceremonies. If I walk with you as you are-sprightly in body and actively moving forward, I shall not remember you less sprightly in body or in spirit, for all this will pass to the other side with you. We may sit under the spell of music, but not under stained-glass windows, when we wish to invite our religious desires.

LETTER XXXIII 

June 4, 1936 

HE VOICE. Such radiance in his face.

THE THE LETTER. I am in that beautiful presence of beauty and vibrant love that recreates all the passionate years when the many days of service to the life I led kept me at the high point of honor and desire to feel that there must be some answer to the purpose of all that we felt and knew. As I find the way to all that now

VISION 

illumines whatever was hid in the daily contact with the world, I put out my hand and push forward with the same ardent love that was always like an altar fire in my heart and find your hand right there, stretched toward me with everything I may share with you, and have you share with me. It is at the altar of love that we meet, and in that light your radiant spirit is ever mine to see and discover ways that may reveal the secrets of what we find as we journey toward the stars. 

Love lights the altar and truth breathes its messageand truth and love bear us onward in the flight into higher realms.

To be able to speak through the silence is not the greatest gift, but to keep the tender tone of confident faith that is the beginning of knowledge in the tempest and the storm and confusions of the life of the physical body. Once in a while I find myself so constant and so responsive to the peace of our one-ness, I am like one wrapt about with something that is as tangible as raiment, and I wonder how I may convey that sense of being clothed with the spirit of eternal love to the dusty travellers who are pressing weary feet on the pavements of the world that gives back no glory of our understanding of what comes as day dawns after the night. I have a stray sense of the gifts that bind me to a world in stress, yet I am never consciously feeling the burden as in old days when a face that had evidence of wrong and sin asked for opinions or advice or means to be in a better condition that might give peace. Strange how I am so placidly comforted in these hours of the death-like stillness of the honor and the glory of life among those who hold the seats of honor in the world

VISION 211 of fire and flame, flood and disaster! I have questioned at times my right to that peace when there is need of us, and yet I feel sure that it is the part of infinite wisdom to be aware of the end that must eventually come to these unreliable and conflicting seasons, and that as we hold the peace of love in our daily lives we add to the quality and the purity that is so important an asset in all trials that we know will not destroy us but give us a distinctive part to play in the affairs of the busy world. 

That has sometimes seemed like an unresponsive mindpain, as some even great men, noble in hope and promise, have expressed the idea that there could be no smiling attitude toward life when everything was so darkened by sin and sorrow. One could not think of unsmiling angels, nor of a weeping Fatherhood of mankind, without the utter helplessness of the situation making for misery and pain. So as smiling babies bless the hearts of those who behold them, there is, I am sure, a gift to the world in peace and joy and the glory of confidence that weyou and I may bestow on our world of friends and the strangers who knock at our door. You see I said door, not doors-ours. There is nothing we may do in a personal approach to the insolent makers of conditions that depress and despair. They do not know our language, and to make retort to any appeal only adds to the memory of an insulting governor or ruler. We do the work of dissipating doubt and adding the knowledge of continuous life, not merely continued, like a bright ribbon that weaves itself into a garment of joy that death did not bring a tragic ending to our dream of life-no, not that—but continuous life, with all its virtues of power for all that we desire. Quite true that

VISION 

what we desire is not always evident to our own minds, shining on the possessions we did not know we desired. 

There are so many desires that become a feature in life -almost like the coil of a serpent, or the roots of a tree that bind and hinder; we move on with the impedimenta and never are wholly aware of them until we look down on the highway we have travelled. 

One thing I know-you and I helped each other in our own earnestness to be aware of what and why and how we were moving on in our own procession of life, and whenever I in our correspondence felt that I must wait until more experience, more years, more efforts gave me a better understanding, you, wise, good, and ever reliable, gave me some of your faith and your desire for me to find peace in an answer to my prayer. 

Those words said over and over to me since I died have been a part of your message to me. Sometimes as in a reverie "Now he knows," and sometimes as if directly to me, "Now you know," I have repeated to myself, and what do you think it has given me beyond the peace of the answered prayer?—a constant and unremitting effort to know more, just as a youngster who has been allowed in the drawing-room looks up the stairway, catches a light from the window of an upper room, and longs to run up and see just what it may be like "up there.” I am not a youngster, but I have the youngster spirit. I want to know what the upper chambers may reveal, and whether I may perhaps go up some day and know that all the chambered life above is definitely a part of those who go up the stairway of ascending life. 

Yes, I am able to tell you—and only you think of that! Only you of all those who have seen me slip out of

VISION 213 one realm of life into another-the message I would give; that is the reason why I crowd so much into the hours we have in that small room where light descends on a table for our use. I know that these letters, written as if I were dictating them to a secretary, will go into many places and to many people who would gladly come to one who speaks with the mind of truth and love, and begin to "peek" over balusters to the room above: that starts the upward movement of wings of aspiration and feet that walk upstairs one step at a time. When I see you writing at the same table that I use to see if my words are correctly understood by my helper, I glance at the record you are making of the message-that hand that wrote so many times to me a letter that seemed to give an answer to a question I had only thought of, not asked that hand makes its same gift to me in its record of a message that could never-never-find its way to anyone but you. That hand, I hope, when at last it is quiet and still, without power to write the messages again I hope will hold a lily of remembrance, and that I may find the blessed consciousness that you have come to a realization of our hand-to-hand touch of joy, and that the memories will be outclassed by the definite knowledge of our personal relationship to each other made glorious as we look into each other's eyes with tears of joy. I know that this will be true, for often I have seen others who have come here since I died who have been so ecstatically happy to see you as I see you and feel the dear rest and peace that truth gives. Very often I long to tell you of all these who come, some of the family people, always the father of your children, even your dear mother, and I am obliged to make a

VISION 

choice in the messages that are never quite complete without including them, but never could their messages be complete without including ME, and including me means that I may at personal command make up what I can for the loss of these personal messages with the general statement that what I find true, they must. They seem very well satisfied, as it brings truth and love rather than the box of ointment of precious vintage. I find that the sons you are devoted to are under the care and directive power of those who find them so useful in the departments where they work out problems that are important. They grow. They are, each in his way, a gift to the community which needs men, and the fearlessness and confidence of the traditional traits in the need of men-not puppets-makes them impressive to those whom they call to their standards of righteousness and power. It is part of your service that you are still the mother in all your activities; there is no forgetfulness of the place you hold; and that, too, is a great aid to the father. Sometimes when he almost stands on tiptoe in denunciation of the lawlessness of so-called law, he comes close to me and we talk it over and feel that between us we have a mighty good intermediary, and then "leave it to mother." So you see that our families are united by the union of our interests, and power and consciousness of what may be done for the city where you live. There is a very difficult problem that sometimes involves those we are so fond of, in obligations and associations where we may not say all that was said and done, and use the inflowing thought. Thought, dear, is not the vagrant influence that falls where or

VISION 

215 when it may, but as definite a part of instructive influence as words. [My husband speaking: Certain pictures of my boy

H show him with a pile of books; he pulls them all around him, hand on forehead, pushes them all aside and sits thinking—it's just as if there were question and answer. On his processes out to the end, the answer comes. It is there.]

THE LETTER resumed. Some of this help comes from his father, not all; there are ancient spirits in contact with some very old pioneer spirits who came to this part of the world—an inspiration like Columbus-it seemed to them a land of gold. Later came those who had the idea of making it a land of freedom. Then came the receptive ones like Lincoln as a response to something beyond. [Here a new spirit seemed to join in a rhapsody on the development of America and the “down-pouring of infinite life" which is destined to bring to flower once more the old aspirations.]

THE LETTER resumed. I'm thankful for this message. 

There will never be downfall for the United States of 

America. There may be quarrels and quibbles, bad leaders uprising, but the freedom that all are working for, including ourselves, will be won back. 

I began this message with a feeling of the shortened opportunities for our Thursday mornings, and it seemed as if I must crowd all the blessings by name into what I have felt. I have not done it as it should be done, but I know that through this season just disappearing around the corner of June, we have grown a supplementary power that we may use when you are not here. I shall

VISION endeavor to corner you out of doors somewhere, or in some quiet room in an hour when you are refreshed by change and by another light on problems that arrive; only I want you to be merry-hearted-youth with its flowers at its belt, its sunshine, its soft rains that make music as blown by the wind. With this we shall not miss what we have had here. It will be stepping out into the garden, leaving the letters-Post-office closed for the season! 

I lift your hand with a consecration of my love and promise of even more that will be ours together. 

LETTER XXXIV 

June 11, 1936 [A wonderful summer day]

HE LETTER. Treasure of my treasury. 

THE 

In this coming of the hour of communication there is an exultant joy that always surpasses the expectation as I walk up the little path with you and feel the import of our coming into the spoken-word period of our happy, happy companionship. Rare as is your own personality and always has been through the years, I find, ever increasingly, the unfolding gift of rare possibilities, perceptions of truth that add to these messages which will bring so much to those who read. There is the unusual quality of mind that is like a sieve letting through the chaff and waste material that is never useful in the contacts we have found and leaves free the jewel that in its gleaming gives some faint idea of the holiness and beauty of the thought. I feel at times that the procession of

VISION 217 years brought us in our separate life in the world about us to this wonderful acceptance of the truth we each desired. 

In all the world opinions make a vast amount of treasonable and questionable expressions that have no meaning in the realities of experience. Look at the world around us, yours and mine, perceived by each of us through senses that are befogged by the ever increasing desire to express opinions that may make a powerful effort to control such as come under the influence; all men have a perceptive power of some individualistic life that should be the gift of life if it becomes an inspiration to unfold the larger and better understanding of the Divinity. It is too evident that the force behind opinions in all the relationships of life is the way we may come to the possession of power over a few or many as the case may be, but too often with a will to make use of the capacity to rule by opinions rather than to leave interspaces of consciousness of knowledge to be found in a community of interest where is light and wisdom and truth. Opinions are like shutters that keep out the light which might flood the room if the open windows were given a receiving point of light. In all our concentration on these mighty and important truths about the way we may find the law of the lives we live, there has been so great a love for the revelations invoked that the power to communicate has been increased incredibly by the dual quality of our acceptance of the human love that glorifies every moment of our existence.

I think that there has never been any hold on the selfishness of an individual experience even in our moments of delightful memories and expectant joys that

VISION 

are to be. This is the gift we may add to the world's possessions, and with hearts attuned to loveliness of every unfolding life in nature, the grasses at our feet with the little weeds of beauty lifting red buds of food for birds and insects roundabout but not too important in our gardens-and the wondrous light of stars over the quiet seas, and the highest mountains, and the babies in our arms—with that wealth of appreciation of the works of God about us, we have stepped up out of the depths of the common and beautiful all mixed together and find that the same law is ours to understand after death as before. Less appreciative people do not count their jewels in the dusty highways of life, and it is rare that two people find the language that unites them in all the ordinary experiences that thrill them and bring throbs of delight. So we go on and on; and every inch of space is alive with the extraordinary and very pertinent lives that still mingle in the pathway toward the stars. If we find ourselves-you and I-intertwined with the circumstances of life where politics may reign supreme for a moment or two, between the eternities of pure and useful experiences, our wings are not clipped, for we need them all the more to soar above the meddlesome dust of opinions and find the white-robed angels of peace whispering to us as the soft song of pine trees on a hillside may soothe us to rest with the consciousness of sublime powers just outside the busy streets of a city that finds no rest or joy in its constant efforts to achieve place and power even in domestic quarrels, or statesmanship in halls of justice. 

This is the way of unfolding life-we accept it. Once in a while we splash out in the waters of distress a signal

VISION 219 to ourselves that this is not the whole of life nor the fullness of power, but like the restless feet of growing children the effort is to find the larger experience, and take a man's share of the burdens of those who listlessly drift in the waves of chance, and forget that tides and winds may be mastered and charted and become our servants, and we rise from puppets on the way to the unexpected yet always sought for, to find the glorious endeavors of the eternal life.

I have just made a small effort, small because it is not a great contribution either to science or whatever one may wish to call the opportunities of life. This I saidthis is the law and order of what I call the spiritual universe, and the spiritual transcends all else, and is, I presume, the life of the Infinite intelligence. Lovelove is the light of the world-whichever way we may look for results that are surpassingly beautiful, we find love—and if love be the law of life eternal, why not add it as the first tribute we make to the most glorious gift of the world whence we catch gleams of the truth that guides to those who live under the restrictions of opinions? This word I felt impelled to give to a group of philosophical brethren who felt that wisdom was the key to success. Even philosophers fall for the desire to be wiser than those they would teach. Love is wisdom, and without it wisdom is a vagrant passing through the world superior and wise but useless in the highest forms of life. If I did not feel your hand in mine with that assuring pressure that you are with me in every word I add to the literature of spiritual understanding, I would not be able to go on; the loneliness that pervades the soul in its solitary climb toward eternal heights is not

VISION for me; to walk high above the levels of life in the expectation of finding at last comradeship and divinity of purpose I have no interest in, for the divinity of lovethe saintship of companionship and the exchange of thought and purpose is the roadway through life wherever lived in that blessed knowledge of the need besetting us for the other half of the dual existence. 

About the first thing I do is to look up-it was my habit to look up—not to see if it rains and I must take an umbrella—many people look up just for that purpose-up at the window, down at the street"—is it not? I look up from where I stand, whether in the doorway of our future place of abode or from a window where eternal hills are blue in the morning light. Yes, we have morning and evening, measure of days and seasons; the idea of singing angels that have no abiding place in the clouds above us-the idea that there is no soft evening dew falling on flowers that are refreshed, no early bird song waking me from sleep to the loveliness of dawn-I cannot imagine a heaven without all these attributes of peace and beauty-unfolding hours of rest-one is not omnipotent and able to command all life after death, but the sweet slumber that gives a pause between the day and the night-all these are important. Just as important as the way you take breakfast for refreshing, quite as much as for food that sustains, we take refreshing hours-I spend mine with you.

As if I tapped on your door and said softly, “It is starlight, come with me"-"It is dawn, come with me"literally those hours that your body needs for its upbuilding and your mind that needs the dear pause for poise that may come in the day, these hours are mine

VISION 

221 with you. Never have I failed to find you, and often I have been dependent on this for a clearer vision of what is constantly like looking through a glass in the eye of an optician looking into a watch to see the works. It must be a tiresome task, and I do not like the idea of being pinioned to discover what is wrong with the works, the management of the world, unless to tell the road to those who need it. Both eyes open, all may be revealed, just as at dawn one sees a tree, hears water flowingone touches something to awake. So I touch your hand, look into your face, hear sounds, voices of those who call, and I am able to take my place right there for as long as I am needed.

THE VOICE. He speaks about what the world is muddled over now that we are electing new officials who may lead us up or down.

THE LETTER continues. Never has there been a more decisive moment for the people—the future is at stake.

It does not make so much difference what a man believes about himself, but whether he is balanced, and able to weigh and measure the bonds of the life about him; sometimes kingdom and kings seem a bit wiser than the republic, all shouting one way or another, but in the effort to find the decisive moment rightly used there are legions of wise leaders, constructive in thought and fine in purpose. The world will be better for this outspoken word from those who believe that the time has come for a division of opinions for and against, bringing stability and quality to its United States of 

America. That I join in most heartily, with a love to continue the experiment of a problematical future for all we hold dear. Not so problematical now that leaders are

VISION 

beginning to find the pulse of people beating against the heart-breaks and sorrow of the world. O my Faith, I know that I am able to do something right here where I am, for whatever is truly felt as a personal need over here one may add a word, a thought, a feeling of the need and settle down in a convention or a prayer closet, and find a spot for contact. The prayers, the noises, the gayety that is not jovial and true but excitement gone wild, and the fury of desire dancing through conventions of state-all these awake not high but foolish prancing like horses getting ready for a race instead of men riding their horses to victory.

It gives me a little sense of separation that the hour draws near when I may not be able to put into plain words the love that is so much a part of my every hour now; but will you read over some of the words I have tried to give and add to them your own interpretation of me back of them and feel that if this were a hundred times more emphatic they would still be true. Once in a while 

I pray to God to guide us―keep us together in safetythen I just admonish myself. I know whereof I speak. I know that if I have come through this first experience of seeming separation and have been filled with confidence of the continuation of all I hold dearest, there can be no separation ever again. It is the most beautiful expression of my faith in the eternal law of love. All I pray for is more light-and that means that we share light forever in the life of love.

THE VOICE. He looks toward the little door of the room.

THE LETTER continues. Such a narrow one. To walk in, just with you or following, or after the welcome-

VISION 223 just that small aperture for all of us to come into the eternal experience can we ever forget it? We remember it that we may say it again and again to the woman bereft, clothed in widowhood. We say just "the open door," there you find feast for the soul famished by sorrow. I look at it and seem to pass through a lighted passage into a world wonderfully fine and dear that would be sorrowful for me unless hand were in your mine, or that strong spirit of yours had found a way to find me if I had not found you. So the world is beautiful; all becomes a thing of beauty and joy. If you were not the transmitter of joy to me by being a point of the knowledge I hold through you it would be of little value, like leaves fluttering before a wind. Many silent people walk through the streets-walk out into a larger beauty than this little byway, and are helpless because of pain and loneliness. [I asked him in view of this farewell, whether he wished me to come once more before going away. He answered quaintly and humorously, "Yes, Ma'am!"]

THE LETTER XXXV 

June 18, 1936 

HE VOICE. I wish you could see the smile on his face-like an angel as he bends over the table, yet so completely a man full of strength and joy, and so gentle, a sort of sanctification about him. 

THE LETTER. With the knowledge that we have found together in a book of golden counsel, there lie forever on your heart and mine the memories that return to

VISION bless us even in the scenes and new associations that await our coming. I think that the wonder of all that we have been able to express may be much more to us than to any other group of investigators who sit at the feast of spiritual knowledge and like children see the beauty and taste the dainties and are gratified that so much has been added to their delight, for we have feasted on the far finer acceptance of the meaning of all that has been ours. It is that meaning that has given life to our souls, and with the wisdom and glory of truth I feel that we stand in the white light of beatification of the eternal and may never feel the old desire for the quest of something indefinably longed for as a gift of God, which we have found and accepted. 

Let me for one moment say what is to me the deep secret of this clear and decisive influence in the work we have found good and intelligible and in accord with all the best and finest in life anywhere and everywhere. 

Without hesitation we have come to the house of expression for whatever may be revealed. The love I hold as the bond is as definite as if we stood before the throne of eternal verity and the completeness is overwhelming; the training of mind and body, the ever increasing will to keep step with the unfolding life of those about us, has given impetus, and we move as the years pass on in the full acceptance of the obligations of the life which is ours, to friends, to society, to righteousness and to God. We do not think it a sacrifice, but joy, to carry all these as treasures of experience and growth that wisdom designs for the full joy of life. Often Faith, I have spoken lightly of the queerly incomplete efforts of well-intentioned teachers, preachers, and ordinary

VISION 

225 folk going about with the unsettled problems as if life would explain itself in the unfolding days. It has seemed a stupid, half-awake, almost automatic life that habitually goes on, and I meant only to cry out in the vestibule of their experiences, "Awake! Awake! dreamers of dreams, and those who have heard heavenly tones, seen visions come out from the days of deception and disharmony, get the keynote of life that is wonderfully clear, and change the world by your efforts to make it lovely and beautiful!" And if I seemed to belittle the efforts that are often evident to all who listen and wait for the new day, I knew that I was only toppling over houses of sand built by children's nimble fingers as they play in the sun. It is so easy to use sarcasm for expressions making some contribution to the life of chance instead of choice. I would have choice given and expressed in words of invitation to the perfected gifts of the world. 

Science, and devotion to knowledge and the universal desire to use what science may prove, must not stop at the door of its findings-the care of bodies, the building of temples, the structure that supports the ignorant and unlettered into the life I am speaking from; for that life is a reality long before death flings down its challenge for the heavenly life of pure and accelerated power of all other nobleness and experience. I don't take a retracing step either to over-estimate or belittle the words I have fitted into this vocabulary of the language we both understand and make understandable to those about us. I might speak in another language quite as defifinitely understood by German or other native tongues, but it would be like a test, a greater dominion over the minds of those who help us in this statement of truth;

VISION 

it might add wonder to the gift of the message, but to the German or the Chinese only would it become valuable as from one of a race who had given personal effort to please a fancy rather than to have translated what is given here in the simple form best adapted to our present use. When a person has been blest with a message in the form used here, never varying in desire but wishing to give without depletion, and quickly, the text that so many need, I am wondering if the prayer of an Italian poet given in native tongue could do more good than the "Lord's Prayer" uttered in the language that we usually speak. 

Those tests may show something that delights a type of investigator, but the simplicity and order of the words you and I without effort translate into the truth of companionship-love, light, glory, and God—are enough for us to-day. There have been many evidences of the power to use native tongues in messages given in this room, several languages giving evidence of identity by a spirit have been given, as when an educated Frenchman drops naturally into the use of terms or names, and adds to the traces of identity. That has not given me any hope that by such means the truth of the message is conveyed to the mind and heart of one like you who listens, and another one like me whose mind is set on the infallibility of the truth of communion. On that statement 

I rest my case without the introduction of much that is puzzling, baffling, and consequently not accepted for its true value.

There are many little people, infants in knowledge, suddenly developing the power of painting, sculpture, music, and all the unusual tests of the mental faculties

VISION 

227 that have been unexplained, called sometimes by the seeker inspiration, transmission of the gifts of parentage and not often admitted by those who are satisfied with that explanation as a gift of the union of an infant prodigy with a spirit finding the use of the child or even older person as the definite answer. With the same understanding of two in accord at some point of the mind and spirit we may find in the future much that is transmitted without the least understanding of the method or manner. So great scientists, so teachers, so those who travel into far lands in the quest for gold or the precious things hidden from the eyes of men, may feel the leading of an unknown person. I mark that word "person" as distinctive from the power ascribed to God as a personal adaptation to the case; and it is easy to see the contacts sustained as response to the inner call to the spirit may be revealed; Columbus, sailing an unknown sea with a purpose quite out of the course of his vision, gives a new kingdom to the world. I do not attempt to make records, only to give you an intimation of leadership by those who have died and guide those who respond without question. I have wondered if some day one of yours or mine might suddenly find the gift of inspiration proving itself as of the divine order of the leadership from those gone out and on after death. I could not pray for prayers in the Egyptian tongue or the Italian vernacular, but I would rejoice if a better understanding of statesmanship with truth flung to the breezes, a banner carried by hands we love, could be attained that would give us the joy of comradeship in the world by those who could respond to leadership by the wise, the good, the true, all from the hand of life in its

VISION 

progressive state, and from God as the Creator of humanity in its up-reaching power, and yet not lightly referred to without the understanding of what part each may play in the unfoldment of another. The world has spent itself in an energetic effort to force power in its demonstration of the needs of its own particular plot of life. Power, dear, is only temporary, unless bequeathed by love-love of the person to whom the gift comes.

So, in these almost informal sessions of our beautiful life together to-day-not to-morrow or the life after deathbut to-day, with light thrown on the screen before us, we may feel that we have not failed in our desire to give joy for sorrow, light for darkness-and God-for his 

Satanic majesty. There are those who toss their sins about them like the apparel of the evil one, as if he and 

God stood at arms against each other, one fighting for darkness that sin remain underground, the other for light that truth be revealed. So I take both out of the realm of discussion, and we know that love sits at the foot of every cross and brings its perfect wonderment that any could resist the passion, the plea, the power of personal love. So long mothers have been held as the fragrance of love that turns all the weeds of the children under their care into roses that bloom in the deserts; all this is a part of the divinity within the life of motherhood. Let fathers also stand in that light, and brothers, sisters, and all the little ones who make the world sick with sorrow for their rich, uncultured minds. We may start with the family for which we are responsible, and we may add to our responsibilities the joy of giving to another the blessing we have shared together. So much 

I owe to you that I include in our message the joy of

VISION 229 giving to another the blessing we have shared together. 

I must repeat again and again that the listening ear is as important as the speaking voice, for without the two gifts of the listener and the speaker we have no way to impress the beauty, joy, godliness of the spirit messages. There are some who pin their faith on the power of seership-responsive and definite expressions from spirits and all that follows to the power of prophecy. 

So strange that fortune-telling should be the curious reason for an approach to the spirit-world! Not for us that gift, though gift it may be; the president seeking to know if election will give him power to go on in his own way of experience may seek the knowledge he wishes at the place where prophecy is given as a test of contact. Quite in accordance with prophecy by vision, dreams, or revelations in the old-time ways of such mysterious and gladly received power are the instances of Saul and Paul, the woman of Endor, and the vision of the Master in the Garden when the two who came and talked with Him are taken by some as the divine right of prophecy and a type of spirit communication. Spirit is Right and Light, and spirit in the body of life after death may reveal much that is hidden, but more than prophecy we desire to realize that there is activity in every form of life on to the end that may bring about either the best, or most disastrous power to find the best, if it interferes with the realm of the selfish ones who would dictate terms and destroy faith in the best gifts. No better exhibition of the disastrous power of selfishness has ever been known; some approach to it in small sections of the world and some in disputed rights of the kingly power as you have seen in foreign

VISION lands, and the freedom of our country held in detestation by some who would live and die for the truththat is before us, before me as before you-death never closes eyes that are open to see the cause and ministration to add and detract as the heart determines. Out from the school-room come those who must hold high the hands of such as would be useful in the world, the mere over-valuation of a Social Register of life and of wealth is not enough. Justice, the queen of the world, must be served to further those whose hands are unloosed by the power of the courts of our land, and must find use for every voice raised, every song sung, every flag of freedom that floats over the city we love. (He stops.) That is not merely a prayer for justice, for justice is the child of love, and you and I, my contributor to these words—we know and love both justice and its parent, love.

I have been among some of our leaders over here-do not blush when I say that I find in Abraham Lincoln an example of spirit leadership that had no university training or culture to do with his power-it was the love that was a thread in his garment, and response of the soul to the voice of justice-not alone in the world about him, but in the spirit realms where power was born for his use. While such instances as that untrained man were given us and when such instances as the disgraceful attempt of a person honored by training in law and statesmanship and the order of the universe fails in selfish purpose to attain the presidency he desired, these records could be given as the beauty of the fertile soul of an untrained lover contrasted with the perfected soul of a trained mighty man without love.

VISION 231 Now I make no difference in all existences that are to be, and I may perhaps rightly say all that have been. I am happiest as I recall that never has there been wanton play in our order of life. Free as the wind over garden roses and over dust-heap the same dry winds of your life brought all that could be brought to the understanding of divine things. It is a part of our approach to perfectness of life in all our experiences. The letters you wrote, those I wrote, disclosed ideals, the ideals you held and I hold are all part of that transition of life into longer life, purity with love of purity, joy in completeness of joy and of love that never finds completeness, but is ever flowing onward in the love-life in all the spheres that await our coming. 

God bless you.

THE VOICE. I never see him make a movement toward you but would be a sort of worship. He suddenly bent over your hand as in prayer and thanksgiving, joy in the companionship of body, soul, and spirit, the look of joy in your queenliness. When the foolishness of youth speaks out in your desire and purposes, he understands everything. There is an inner kindness of a true gentleman. Never fear to say what is in your mind.

LETTER XXXVI 

September 24, 1936 

HE LETTER. I am eager to keep this hour just as

THE LETTER Pen which have made anew the light and beauty of life itself. It has been a desire on my part to record my individual experience and expression. I

VISION wish to keep it always free from the many experiences that have come to the rest of the world, and I have the purpose to make our history of what we have found, not troubling myself with the interpretation; for it is very evident to us, I know, that the personal is the important note. If I sometimes wandered into the thought-life of others in the world where thoughts are continually a part of the processes of life, I did not intend it to be so, and I am not able to measure for myself how much the impending doom of many conflicting experiences may add to the furthering of truth, or the desire to express an opinion that may be good, but first and last there is this very real attitude I have toward the method and realization of a companionship that is heightened, glorified, and made useful by the intensity of a personal love; this is enough for me to leave as a light on the problems of the eternal life we have found. To me the invisible becomes visible as I sit here not thinking so much of the words I repeat as of the definiteness of my vision, the invisible made visible, the inaudible made audible, the spirit of life itself flowing into our small space as if we had torn asunder the veil that hides us from each other, in various efforts to take our place in the world and come into the actual, the absolute of life. All that I knew, all that I dared to hope, all that I felt must be revealed sometime, somewhere, was revealed to me in that first consciousness that you and I stood alone on a highway of life and light. I was not swept by an emotional desire to call you all that love finds in words, endearments, and all the clinging notes of beauty—no, no-not emotion. I was aware that emotions awakened to some situations that might give a pleasurable sensa-

VISION 

233 tion of proximity, but the reality of transcendent love was as definite as if I had always known it-in truth I did; for in all my purposes, all my quandaries, I sought you; we were part of a soul-journeying. Business friends, good, good; tenderness and parenthood, good-all knowledge that added to the understanding of problems that face every individual, good-but our souls met within the limit of absolute knowledge, peace, and acceptance of the life that is ours. So in many moments too few for the complete structure which we have not yet been able to talk much about, there came that clear call across the mysteries and efforts that make men and women what they are, the life-consciousness.
