With comments not at all important, but because vision gave me a look behind the curtain and I quite naturally expressed myself, there was never a moment of doubt at what we found that hid us away from experiences such as many find in church, but the desire to be one man who could say what seemed evident or might be used if two sat before life with the completeness of vision given. 

It is mysterious only as we lack understanding-lowhanging clouds do not reveal the heights of experience that an hour may give; it does, of course, make very much difference to you and me what the world doesstanding in the waves of doubt and unbelief and trying to explain does not help much; but here in this atmosphere made holy by vision and the knowledge that this is a moment snatched off the wheel of time we are one in receptivity and made free by our acceptance of it. I have never thought of love as the experience of the young or old or wise or simple or complete or, as it was observed by me in my contacts, something hidden, the

VISION individual not fully conscious of union with people, friends, family; but here is the soul-contact, and it does not make me less of a person to be aware of all the intimacies of the unfolding soul of you. I have no thrill as some feel in approaching some high experience that leaves nothing to guide us, but I have peace, that oneness with the invisible, now seen without the presence of thought which makes it desirable to repeat over and over again, "I love you." I have never before known that thought-life is the life least understood. I knew that those who think out their lives by processes of contact with the thoughts of others do make some heavily entrenched conditions from which they do not emerge into individuality. Now I touch your hand-you are not aware of it. I know I have brought something that only occasionally you could name as sense-response to my thought. I touch your hair; I sit beside you. I think and think much that must be illuminating, but it does not reach the sense realm. Thought-life is very real, but puerile, and to come into what we have is to rise above that oft-repeated expression that thoughts are things -of course they are in a creative sense. In the world of thought I think out a decision and it becomes reality among my friends and co-workers. That has become something evident in the realm of thought; but the supernal joy of understanding your soul and the outflowing words that are dependent on the life of the physical body are not so important, yet in the habits we attach to these experiences one may drop into them again and say "precious love" with all the ardor of a soul-contact that has absolutely no reason for using words when the two are in the same realm of reality.

VISION 

235 When I have whispered to you a line of poetry or a declaration of my dependence on you, I did not intend to add the desire to run away with you like the knights of old; I did not wish to hasten any condition that might depend on such a holding of you to myself, but it had its use and I should (or might) be accused of being less a man as I become spiritualized in the spiritual life. 

That would not be true or helpful or useful, for old loves bind together and hold to all that is beautiful and true.

A step up, out of the realm of thought expression into the consciousness of the Eternal, gives the definite individuality that persists long after great changes have come to the bodily experience, and in these illuminating moments I have found that one man's registry of his full acceptance of the divine in life may help others to realize that life after death is not a continuous performance of bread-making and rearing of institutions that may make or mar the present belief of what is best. I have not been interested in the efforts of anyone to make me a missionary to carry good news or find opinions that politicians might be redeemed from sin or led into belief of spirit-communion which I feel sure they would turn to some accounts of their own; that must come through understanding persistent as it unfolds. 

You have been my staff; and wherever I may have intruded a pertinent remark about situations or people it was because I saw it so. And I come this very moment with all the glad tidings that the world is moving on and may be lifted into what we have found. I am still only able to express hope that the vitalizing influence of the communion may be far more useful than prescrip-

VISION 

tions for better housing or better forms of government. 

Do you catch the meaning I am trying to give you, that this has been to me vital power-the spiritual influx which the world needs? There is the communion of soul that we may have in our hours apart from the struggle that adds to our strength, wisdom, and thirst after righteousness. I have sometimes wished to add words that are testimony, evidence of the personal identity after death. Once in a while one of ours has been in great need of revitalizing power, and a word I may let fall into our given times may start into action a part or purpose that brings returning strength and courage. 

Words make men courageous, but the vitalizing power of the spirit gives them strength and power. One cannot take up every individual case, but you and I have been as one in many cases and will continue to be helpful to those who walk in the shadow, longing for the old words of comfort. 

When you may have felt I was rushing off into some new desire to bring virtue to the sinner or to turn the influence I might express into bringing harmony into the statesmanship life, I feared I troubled you. Please know that no one ever lived as I did and as you do whose associations have ever been along the line of redemption from all that is squalid or inferior, can forget nor want to, but for us there is the divinity of the contacts sustained by and to each other. [Sunbeam says: He is bending over your shoulder as if reading what you write.] 

All lovely. I love to read it over with you. I am so glad you came. Sometimes I fear I have wearied you. Sometimes I feared I seemed unable to give you all I feel, but

VISION 237 mind to mind, thought to thought, and soul to soul we are one. You are mine because you are a part of all these revelations. [Sunbeam says: He wants you to feel his arms around you as the Bible says, “Underneath are the everlasting arms."]

LETTER XXXVII 

October 8, 1936 

HE LETTER. The go on and dear conscious- 

HE LETTER. The days go on and the dear conscious TH become a part of all the life I live now. The quick and useful communication which was established so soon after the new experiences came to us left no long wonder and question between us, and I believe it is the secret of the perfect life that is unbroken by death and useful in making definite statements of personal relationships sustained without the uneven and faltering record that is often a hindrance in the deep and constant affection that has been ours. I have read and re-read the words which you have given the world, and I am satisfied that the personal note in each carries a conviction of the truth we love to express, the other forms of communion following as naturally as our lives before the change came to me with all the precious documents of life, bringing the guide and precept for the higher and finer aspiration. That there are so many vague, almost contradictory messages called out is, I think, because the recorder of them may be insistent on the more personal words often expected to be a message that may convince skeptical or curious people of what they wish to

VISION 

repeat. With you there has been discreet silence that left me quite free from the discussions and wanderings of a diverted thought; and I have never felt that you were in any manner set aside in the work but fully aware of my dependence on you. Not my own thought alone could have given the freedom and joy, but your patience, your wise attitude, and my hand literally in yours taking its strength from you. Perhaps I have not realized fully how much I absorbed of your energy, yet I know you gave it gladly, for the results have been beyond my own hope when I began the work in blessed obedience to the will of love. By and by I shall know from other records laid aside from those who have been held in the state of submissive acceptance how greatly I have been blest, first before the change, then the change brought to me freedom to express all the latent powers that the soul-life gives us. The body is a treasured casket hiding much that is best hidden from those who have no gift of understanding and would make havoc of words and feeling that we are able to keep in our hearts until the day of revelation; and even as you and I find our thoughts crystal-clear, there are still many treasures that wait for the coming of life in its full measure to be revealed as wisdom approaches the hour. When I call you the perfect and complete other part of my life now, it is not like a new idea or even a new discovery of the beauty and warmth of our relationship, it is new only in expression, and I am always sensible of the way we are enfolded in the spirit and soul of consciousness, like a little light falling across the threshold, revealing the depth and beauty of the 

Beyond the light of vision as far as a star may shine

VISION 239 into a garden and reveal what the darkness has not yet made clear, the vision of the sunlight which is beyond the light of stars.

I have been so often just longing for the hours we have had. I realize that the care, the journey, the records are all in the efforts made by you, far beyond any contribution I make, standing quietly in the light giving my thought utterance that is caught by sound and given permanence by your pencil. I always feel a kind of solemnity in these hours, yet often I am as light-hearted as youth when a fine light gives the vision of what may come eventually and between the realization of what these hours mean and the promise that they give, I am full of the joy of achievement. You are to me, as I have heard someone express, hands and feet to do the Master's will. Long after we set our steps to a trail beyond the stars we have named and fancy that we know them by name, if not as friends yet as acquaintances in a new world of opportunities-long after we have caught the ways of those who have lived on other planets, these experiences of ours I hope may become literature read with the meaning of communication between the seen and the unseen, a narrative which will bless those who find the light, the way, and the truth. Often I look out across the troubled waters that sweep humanity against the shores of time—yes, impatiently I watch the foolishness and un-wisdom of those who do not find any evidence of the life abundant and glorious just over the shadowline of all the fuss and fury for less worthy and useful pursuits, and I lift my eyes, not to mountains, not to deep rivers that divide the lines of countries and peoples, but to you. Is my love a human attitude which passes

VISION 

away with the body I used? Oh, no-no-no-no, it is the soul, which endures and takes the new forms that fit the needs of the experiences, and the spirit animates the soul as the immortality which we continue to find as the gift of life forever. The body does not change in its power of expression, but is intensified for the new rôle, and the new rôle may not call for feet that clumsily thread a pathway, but find a will, giving precipitative power as used with the body when we move through thoroughfares and avenues of experience and add to the power to propel ourselves through the world by ships, trains, carriages, horses, all the devices that we have found useful to speed us away to where our minds desire to go; all these airships that span the worlds, all at the behest of the will to move forward at the highest rate of speed, hurtling through the air, driving through storms at sea, one purpose-to get where we wish to go. All that, in time, over here, apparently disappears, for I have seen people coming through spaces that are not visible at the starting-point, yet giving no thought to the traveller on his way except to keep to currents of atmosphere that are as visible as roadways or highways in the physical world. You arrive in your vehicle (he smiles) at the foot of these steps, I see you coming up-up to the door by the will you have to come on where I am, and I am coming to the room by a little highway through space that reaches the door, the same door, a moment before you-up the same stairs, through the door, to the table; all is natural and orderly as one would expect a place sought by pilgrims to be. I might come in at the window, but that would seem a strange way to approach what I want to find. I tell you that I

VISION 

241 have seen no spirit-people dashing through windows or ceilings or up from the earth; always they have been orderly. I never thought of such a thing as taking any underground passages, it would seem as if I had come out of a cemetery to find life. No, I believe that all this talk of spirits appearing in unearthly and unnatural places is a thought apparition, held by one who uses the thought of some other person to bring it into the mind of the friend he wants to reach, by taking the form either outside or inside where it would be seen as something extraneous, and bring a fruitful prophecy. 

I have never heard anyone speak of this, but I well remember the angels on battlefields thought to be guiding either to death or life; it was probably visions, not reality, as some go into a session to use the minds there for some betterment-the vision gave courage. I do not want to leave out that I sit beside you when you are resting or sleeping; that is orderly enough. I would have done that if among the possibilities of transporting myself into your presence with the dear ones.

LETTER XXXVIII 

October 15, 1936 

HE LETTER. The day comes to bless me and to add the desire of heart. precious hour I mine has never yet failed to give me the deeper and finer understanding of life in the sense of acceptance of the gifts that are ours-the experience without the senses that we find important in the earth-life is evidence of all that divine power which becomes ours through the

VISION Divinity we hold deep in our consciousness and call God. It is the solvent of all that life gives, and adds its power for the full realization of all the perplexities that bind us and are not quite understood until the spirit comes into perfection after death has brought release from the numberless limiting gifts of the body. This body seems quite like a prison-house, but soon we discover it to be a shelter from many deepening influences which might rob us of joy. Often when I touch your hand and find the old feeling of having come into physical contact, I marvel that the old dependence on touch and sense of companionship through touch has no power now to bring response from those we love and desire to feel our presence, but there is a thrill surpassing all that old evidence of companionship through response, and it is always evident to me that there is something superior in the contacts through responses that are definitely spiritual. When I am aware that anyone whom

I have known and loved is suddenly conscious of me through an awakened memory, I am grateful for that power that thought creates, and we live again in the past, as all the associations are as much a part of to-day as when first experienced. 

It is like sunset on a night that gave glorious beauty on mountain or open sea; we loved it and longed to keep it, and it is really a part of to-day, to-morrow, and forever. If this is true, then the noblest sentiments and divine experiences of a comradeship and love that sustains and invigorates is never lost but is always a part of life-forever, I believe. 

Day after day I think of all you have been to me, and the way your soul has become part of every throb of my

VISION 243 spiritual life even up to this moment. Sometimes I wonder how we have come to this understanding of the Infinite even through the finite life, and I am aware that God is the essence of our life that unfolds daily and gives the understanding of Him in everything with the supreme love, the unfolding gift, bringing tidings of eternal life; it is so wonderful and remarkable in its blending with our other more common experiences that find emotion and joy and peace, and always hope, as attendants of the hours. And now that we are able to give our thoughts language, it helps us to set in order our contacts and communications. I could not get along without you. You are as much a part of my experiences and my armor as my feet, my hands, my eyes, or my prayers. Yes, just that. I have often wondered why we do not understand that all our contacts may be blended into a greater love; it took death to give me the light, for even the humble experiences that inspired to service became a part of the normal life with the earth world. 

Men who sinned and found the relativity of their sin to the sins of others with less experience or hope, became to me a moment of enlightenment and purpose to lift them away from the burdens that such sins impose, and that remains with me to-day. I wish I might speak to those who are careless, unbelieving, and selfish, and add to the responsibilities they assume without love to soften all that is hard and wrong. I would preach just what I have found, that even if no definite word is spoken, the desire to atone lifts one into a realm of tenderness akin to love. There have been for me, since you and I are able to find ourselves together with an energy expressive of our spiritualized life and love, new dem-

VISION 

onstrations of the power of the mind-attitude. I do not now refer to the spiritualization of thought, but the clear, definite expression of the mind in its attitude toward the terrible situations that appall us and turn us away from our confidence or desire for those who seem but a fragment of tattered morality and civilization working in the midst of the affairs of life. To look deeply into the actions and have no conception of what a perverted mind may invent for the pain and horror of living people is not an easy matter, for our conceptions are the result of training, teaching, and inheritance. So in some of our conclaves (he smiles) among the statesmen and the sturdy pioneers of our world which we assume as part of our inheritance to-day we have taken up much that seems almost impossible to accept as part of the unfolding principle of a higher destiny. That warlike spirit that knows no honor but what is brought by blood and the desecration of all the best instincts of mankind is a high type of wickedness against which, it is so asserted, we may help by standing still with the love that binds us together and to God. All this I have in mind whenever I sit with you glancing at the dreams of the world in its efforts to attain what it deems best for the future. I am not sorrowful in the sense of doubt or distrust, but in the teachings that are yet to be discarded that love may be accepted as the gift of God. By this I mean as we find it, that there is the personal acceptance of love that is the life of the world, that personal acceptance that leads us to an encompassing and definite power holding us in arms of tenderness and love. Poets still sing their songs of the Eternal goodness, a lovely faith to express but so hard to under-

VISION 

245 stand in mere words. Perhaps to sing of love and faith in God makes possible so much that its absence would bring doubt, pain, and sorrow; at any rate, they are still singing of faith, and it is still fruitful in the soil which gave it a place to find new growth from an unexpected source. I think of the squirrel trusting the oak tree glorified by the productive soil, rain, and sun, bursting into life through the acorn or walnut tree. You and I have had the experience of finding that the natural expression of faith has found lodgment in the weary heart and brain, adding what we wish to give, that confidence that is never lost with the power of renewing life. I first found that you were accepting what I had to say—it was as if you had already been part of the inspiring life that comes after the body. I told you once that I had a body-it was like a mirror when age came to my face with wrinkles and gray hair, finding here and there a sign that it was writing on my body an epitaph that would become the memory of what had been and become.

A word about this body; it fits the atmosphere in which I live and move. I have feet and hands and eyes, and many other growing powers add to my capacity to move from place to place and to find that contact may move someone who has never had a spiritual body. 

When you see me as you come through the door that opens for everyone, your hand will find its way to my hand and the one who is now your eyes see before you speaking words reproduced that you may find them to read and find confidence in them. My love grows. It was complete for experiences in the old way, not words of poetic inspiration as if I might have longed to say,

VISION "Oh, my love, you are my light and joy”—not in those words did I ever address you, but just as soon as I found we had a new binding of heart and love they came as naturally as the song of birds in spring when the fullthroated warbler sends out a joyous note bringing life to all. I walked with your mother and my mother through an Egyptian country not long ago; you were familiar through a visit to that country with much that is now in the background of your present life. They were not aware of things that you saw at a glance, put away in your mind to recall at any time or hour; and to me they expressed the desire that many friends have, to travel, and keep for their joy in memory the temples, towers, or whatever might be observed in the enlarging quality of life and mind through memory. I said, "This is the only gift I have to-day in my effort to come to my Faith and give her memories of the life we had when in the same sphere." Often I had thought that the scientific man may prove that memory remains when messages come recording life on earth, but it does not make any impression in me or excite any desire within me to know when I last saw one or other friend saying good-bye at boat or plane, in order to prove me to be myself; but to bring to one I love that interest or desire means everything. So I help our mothers to understand what we their children now feel about the messages that carry out into the world what is only acceptable when it brings gold to their feet or honor to their career. That is one of the reasons I persist in going over all that I treasure as still my own. "We only keep what we give away" is not true, for we keep treasured all that made life beautiful. I like to tell you about the homes that I

VISION 

247 see and know that we, too, are to have homes as we need them for the development of the diviner life. If I say table it does not necessarily mean a duplicate, it means something to develop-not old places sanctified by love in families, and articles that become the usefulness of life. Death does not bring a gipsy life, it does not mean idleness-never forget that. Life is fairly understood by gipsies as a trail from place to place, but I have found in the home I call my own, where I am planning for ours when you come, a place where our minds may centre on certain problems of civilization and bring out of it the fullness of life. In my thoughts of you the first is always of that love that binds. If one has too great a love for a career, and the other longing for quiet and peace and study, it is not binding-often separating our own children with us often going away finding new experiences but keeping love through the years. Then comes an "in-law," into the circle, bringing with him or her new ideals, new hopes and new plans, but there is, through love, acceptance of the new without laying aside the dear things of the past. 

As I have approached those dear to me I have found in my daughter something I did not know was there; it begins the unfolding life of spiritual desire and it will grow, I know, bringing a relation of father and child that endures and has drawn us together; and with the son I find inspiration blossoming in his work. With you

I never seem to have changed any ideal or hope, but have seen it become the divine principle of all that life holds. It is true of all my family that I had contact beautiful and real, but there can be but one who comes into the soul-life and sanctifies all by interest and desire.

VISION 

I have so much more that I wish to write; the disappointments of some dear ones of mine who might feel a longing for what you have from me, I understand. They could not have what you have-not that you take away; you would give all possible, but they have not the capacity to receive. It is what you give me that makes it possible to write what is worth while to the world. It has always been your heart to receive, your mind to understand, your desire to become one with me in the journey toward larger life.

There is peace and a sense of duty paid to God in worship on Sunday, but the hymn of praise, the tone of the great organ pealing out its music through the aisles, the statuary, and all becoming a part of the cathedral standing in the midst of life as a monument of worship and house of prayer, impels one to utterance and service that is forever appealing to men to know the Father in Heaven. Quaker church on green hillside has its place, and no one would wish to close cathedral doors in London, New York, Philadelphia, or Boston, nor wish away the beauty and joy and the expenditure of energy, money, and time to keep alive city worship in beauty and love. So we in this small room express our worship to each other and the Heavenly Father and find joy in accepting the knowledge of life after death. 

We add this as our tribute to truth. If for any reason we were obliged to discontinue our wonderful experiences there would be sorrow on my part and yours-personal sorrow, not the sorrow of relinquishment of service, but that silence that fell when death came, before we found the voice that gives us encouragement for further happiness together.

VISION 249 This is not good-bye, for I think that there is one more day here before we go to the new place. [Mrs. 

Soule's change of house.]

LETTER XXXIX 

October 28, 1936 [ON account of a decision to change Mrs. Soule's sittings to another place, avoiding the high hill and other inconveniences, there seemed to-day to be a feeling of slight disturbance. H. P. came first to the sitting, evidently somewhat anxious. He said, “I will give only one word which I wish to add to one of the messages, always mine; I join you as we move on, for these words are important." He broke off, and Sunbeam burst in, eulogizing young H. R. then began, but so confusedly that I could not make sense at first. He recurred to the idea of memory, of which he spoke recently.] 

THE LETTER. I find, without any effort to remember, the light that was there but was not stressed until the picture of what happened long ago is placed as the background that time reveals. Sometimes I think that you, precious comrade of the years, knew and made use of it as I do. Again flowing across memory I see the knowledge as if it were entirely new, and I knew that revelations of the past are suddenly yours as they are mine: your sudden appreciation of the leadings and the helpfulness of what you did not know at the time was at last yours. Like crossing a bridge, not realizing you had done so until you turned your head and asked quite as if you did not know, "Did I cross that bridge over a

VISION 

river? Did I find my way through such experiences as are visible to me now?" And I touch your hand and say, "Yes, we are both a part of each other's experiences, but we were not able to realize that we were in that moment's experiences because of human limitations." 

So much has been revealed of care and guardianship in many influences that shaped not only our destinies but other unfolding life-a step here, a step there, the hand of a guide whose influence is affection, rounding out the day of some important step in the life that depends on associations. Without these associations there might be missteps and sorrow. So those who have given help and care without words but with responsibilities that make for the higher type of activities and add the wondrous power of love to the unfolding life find at last that love stands as the superior gift over every other step in the world. That is the way it is suddenly part of an experience increasing and glorifying each year of life and association.

I think it is but the evidence of love with wisdom and growth. If we had lived and died and stood in the same relationship to each other, there might be doubt as to the length of time that it would be dependable power, but love is the divine gift and never fails. One may wonder if the full complement of the gift is bestowed by experience, by contemplation, by prayer, or mere acceptance of the unfolding of the soul. It is difficult to separate, for there are some very definite experiences which seem as if pushed into contact. I believe in the growth of the soul, the unfolding process, the same process as in a planet, and because it is the law of life, even of the millions of stars that become more powerful and add

VISION 251 to power and complexity, we are not able to cast it off, but bind it to others of like quality but limited until the higher unfolding leads each individual into new realms of life. That has been a part of our life together with the same love of beauty, of life, of power, of all that brings new and beautiful experiences like the breaking away of hindrances and limitations, and the freedom that brings the diviner life and constant growth. Since you have been here we are more completely absorbed in the processes of the freeing of the soul from the life that is still bound to outgrown possibilities, and find a new, definite experience of creative power in the world when we move out of the daily toil of testing and experiment, passing on to the highway of the mind and of circumstance and taking new degrees of power without releasing that of decision but finally coming into such animation, desire, and activity as befits one who takes new responsibilities with delight. 

It is not always possible to measure until we look back around the circle of our own experiences. These are the circles of light. Anyone who suddenly turns around, takes a look at some of the appliances of the straightline record and then moves straight ahead, may reach the Alps, but that is only in one direction. Life should have the power to see all around and bring the sun shining from all sides. I did not stay in that attitude that would keep me moving farther away from the associations and experiences of activity among those I had been interested in, but in various heights I have been able to find the contact high, low, intermediate, or any contact, just like waves of the sea, always with the power to find on the upper surface one or more-and

VISION the same throughout the liquid universe of space. It never surprised me. I took it just as I used to go upstairs in an elevator, down in a lower type of office to examine old records still standing complete that I might read them, and I found always near wherever I might find my way, someone whom I might lead or see what the best influence might be to help. Sometimes I have taken you, when sleep released you from demands. We do not struggle to breathe or move to change the attitude, but often move out through a doorway into an upper air, just as real as to step up on the roof, away from the building, and find always atmosphere and foundation as real to us as the floor above where someone has added height to the building. This is very natural, just as if we dream something without any reason, strange dreams of height or any place that holds us sufficiently safe. And so far, wherever I have come to a point that misses the Beyond, I have reached for your hand to make sure that you and I are going in the same direction. Life moves outward, with those who keep consciousness, whether dead or alive, to return again with memory, all that leads to the hemisphere of life, but not to any strange seeming of unreality. Many people are now experiencing the sense of moving out, but many of them do not realize that it is anything but a dream or imagination. These experiences I often want you to remember, to make you sure that when the change comes we may find the reality of life; bodily life and friendly love. This reality of a bodily life may for a time be better to forget because for those who have so much to do, it is not possible to leave the question and doubt, only accepting the new and wonderful knowledge

VISION 

253 that sooner or later comes to the awakened spirit. Death to the bodily life does not always awaken. Strangely enough, I found people sleeping, unaware that the life was anything but a period of rest and adjustment. I did not believe them at first. I thought there must be something deficient in the transportation, just as we see the cocoon of a worm, and have no idea that time will bring out a butterfly and make beauty from the ugly demonstration of the unfolding life of another type. One may jump over a stone wall and brush off a caterpillar's nest and leave it without real development. It is not difficult to imagine it suddenly coming to its wings. Some people may be like that, not you or I, for in that bodily existence is the mind that slowly awakes to the beauty of the outer life.

I was wondering, as I faced the possibility of a change in the place of our messages, whether it would make any difference to us. They have told me that I shall not mind it at all, that it is not the table, the hillside, the room, the way you come, but that when we go into the next place of experience there will be no difficulty. I believe this is true, for I have never found it difficult to approach you. I see you sometimes sitting quietly thinking; sometimes I see you with eyes closed, and for a moment I think you are sleeping, but I always know the difference, the peace of rest, or sitting up with your eyes closed, mind peaceful, thinking out some of our experiences. I cannot help feeling that these are far different from the average dreaming; some of it may be that we have lived long and gained understanding, especially since this message-bearing. It means so much to me to have you know what I am thinking about you, and my

VISION 

dependence upon you. If we were stopped for a long time by illness or some separation in a new life, I should miss it as if I looked in at the window, and then in desperation began to struggle through to you, hoping that you would catch something. Now that I have got so much through trying to make it intelligible, with new types of prayer leading forth to experiences literal and true-the place you have, people you have found; you have come miles beyond the average person, but you have brought to your own all you have had. 

I do not dread the new place of meeting. I have been with some of the friends who have been making some contacts to familiarize themselves with the light and the ways of making any new or useful contacts that may help us. Love is the creative power for all gifts—there is nothing impossible with that power, and you will find that deep within your own power will come some new and useful experiences of your own; and while I should be grateful to express in some sound or some vision coming to you, I would not take away from what we have in order to make something entirely new. Step by step we shall go forward.

LETTER XL 

November 11, 1936 

[Armistice Day] 

[THIS sitting was in Mrs. Soule's new abode in Cambridge.] 

HE LETTER. I welcome you to the little sanctum up the little stairway where we find the influence that binds us to the sacred past and the happy future. I see the clear thought and am eager to make all the past a stepping-stone for the better understanding of what this means to the world veiled in the misunderstanding of doubt and hunger for the revelation. When I recall my own wonder, sometimes dark with doubt, it gives me so much gratitude to you, precious and dear as you always were and continued to be after the silence fell upon us. Many times I sit quietly for hours pondering over the mystery of our doubts now that all is so plain and simple and beautiful-all right now-in God's good hour the light shines for us.

There is a peculiar sense of all that our messages mean to many of the dead who have never been blest by the fragrant beauty of memory binding them to the living; like strange clouds on the horizon they move with no new and wondrous revelation and are neither human nor divine in their rapture over what you and I have found by the blessedness of a friendship so unusual; it is quite beyond conception in the minds of many who would welcome it if someone guided them to light, joy, beauty, and all that makes life answerable.

I have wondered how I should feel when I entered a

VISION 

new room of communication—and here I am, just exactly as I felt in that first room—not the small one where we last met, but the first one when the morning broke and darkness was no more. And then the precious hours in the little one-and now this, where everything seems so airy and light; and I think there will be much that I may add to what has already been written by you. In all our letters there has been the personal note which I never want set aside for any wonderful explanation of what others have done or failed to do. They are, of course, personal opinions that have been found as truth-givers in these experiences, and I wish to encourage all who might be helped to a faith in the still larger life that is bound to arrive as wisdom comes. I am not the least disturbed by the feeling some may hold that this is a wicked illusion that will pass away, for I have not seen some of the beautiful souls (I refer to spirits as 

I use that word)—like literati, who have many of them held themselves always as those to whom the secrets of the gods were whispered into the consciousness of life; have I not longed to say to you, "These are the high notes of the life that follows life on the lower forms of experiences"? And yet each time I find myself saying, "I would love to whisper some of these divine affluences to you." I put my finger to my lips and say inwardly, "No-no, not to tell her, but to see her smile and hear her rapturous note of joy as she walks with me into the experiences, some of which I have only dreamed of, but a few that were like the crumbs from the Master's table giving me knowledge that there was the feast of soul and reason for rejoicing farther on." If I could be any more aware of heaven it could only be to have your

VISION 257 hand in mine, your eyes shining radiant with the new influx of life, not only of beauty-earth has its beauty for eyes and ears and the visions that come and go, giving us so much to remember—the hills, the stars, the vista always fresh in the morning and glorious at night; all these awake emotions we rarely speak of, hidden in the soul until memory re-awakes the scene, and then we lapse again into silence, and know what we are unable to express.

It may seem that I am looking forward all the time, but that is not true, for I am remembering-remembering—all the time, and prophecy to me is the crown of memory high on my head where I do not see it, but know it is there. No mirror reflects what is coming, but deep in the heart the knowledge rests as a dove in my breast. You do not borrow it from me, but it is an emotion set into action by the words I give you through my little friend ["That's me!" said Sunbeam], and I feel that this is the first sitting, so lovely even though "Light" [a name for Mrs. Soule] was somewhat discomfited to have you come before order had been established. 

What do we care, as long as we are together? The union is what we get. It's almost like having every one of the family just outside, looking in to see that all is going well; they love to have you where you are receiving something from on high.

There are many everyday occurrences which if we were in the older form of life we would speak of-advice about what to comment upon-but I cannot bring myself to the point of using much of these hours to discuss or even refer to most momentous problems of the world. 

If I ever got to talking of the situation on earth I should

VISION 

probably waste much time and energy that can be better used helping those who need the peace and knowledge of continued life and opportunity—giving courage to wade through wars and wickedness toward the highway of life—that is the result of life in more perfected forms. 

I have had now and then to make vigorous statements among groups of spirit-men like me who retain interest in good and right thinking and acting, and we have decided to keep the high tone of thinking among our dear ones made possible-a kind of silent messengerpower in the world, so we have silent moments at certain times of the day or night when we practice what our rector would call silent prayer-service. It has been said that the silent prayers in the home or the temple, even if singly uttered, have a far deeper influence in the world than all the conflicting influences of evil, as the good is eternal and the evil dies of its own demolition. 

THE LETTER XLI 

November 18, 1936

HE LETTER. Here in the little space reserved for the communication I have a strange sense of quiet repose with the feeling of belonging only to you in a new and beautiful relationship that does not come when the questions and problems that confront every hour are before us. It is like withdrawing for the hours of the night, the peace of night with the refreshing of all that rest may give in the opportunity to lay aside the burdens that press upon our consciousness. Nothing disturbs me as I enter and know that I have all there is of

VISION 259 your attention and effort to take my words, serious or otherwise, and then read them over again with sometimes a very new light upon them. How much I depend on you, your faithful hand, to trace the thoughts that are mine; I cannot tell you how I am blest by your faithful work-for it is work, I know. I have had my apprenticeship in taking notes and making conclusions for my own benefit, and when I see you reading over the words I have picked out of a vocabulary with the purpose to make my thoughts clear, I wish I might say more that give you the consciousness of our relationship and yet leave you the knowledge that there are never the small misunderstandings between those who are never quite free from the feelings that men and women hold in hearts not spiritually awakened. There has never been in your true heart any of the common experiences of jealousy or the holding of rights because of family attachments, however dear-never will be, never will be, for on each plane of experience is a light shining like a star illumining the tiniest bit of flower and the mightiest tree under the wings of vision that reveal, restore, and give to each the value of its contacts with those who are able to find the highest, the ordinary, and the smallest expression of life in every form as the gift of God-each in its own divine whisperings of love and the inflowing of the Heavenly Father. [THE VOICE. His face grew white with a faint light as of moonlight as he said "the gift of God."]

When I first began to speak of what your life meant to me I knew it was the continuation of a most unusual affinitizing of our desires, our high affirmations of the beautiful, the true, the good, and the holy; we did not

VISION take those words into our correspondence, but the ideal was underneath all we expressed to each other; there was no deception or underhand motive, no feeling of anything except the joy of the contact that was held as a wonderful gift never approached in the grace and beauty of a soul experience. It gave life to the lowest ebb and flow of our highest emotions without any knowledge that we were one in our climb toward the perfected friendship, but when death took me away from the activities of the earthly career—that was what I called itmy career-I saw at a glance that I was bereft. I tried to find some way to tell you that I felt as if I had dropped part of my most loved opportunity to express as I had never expressed to any other person-my family, my parents, those I had formed, those who were devoted to me—and I was like one famished-famished-for some way to keep right on. The day came, and from that hour when I knew you would help me to give my answer to my own prayer about that solemn-and what I considered final-word on this matter of life after death, I was glorified, and have never since sunk into that state of anxiety and questioning which possessed me so long. 

Your old faith was so difficult for me to accept. I had it and I did not have it; one day I had hope, another day I was despondent; but from the first word which fell on the ears of the little Indian, I did not fall ever again into despondency or doubt. I think I always believed something would knock the blinders off my eyes -and of course it was you-you always tried to keep my faith through yours-your own faith in God. Where did you get it? It was better than your words, even though stated as a faith. What I longed for was what

VISION 261 yet I you have now through my constant iteration, and did not seek it; I had no one who could appreciate my impatient desire for revelation. Yet here we are together; and every day—not only the days you come for my messages, but every day-I grow a little more tender, a little more insistent on speaking the direct word to you; and standing with those guides of yours-at first

I did not realize that they were leaders-where they came from, who they might have been I did not question -they were good, they were strong, they were sure. 

There they were, with many of those who had gone beall eager for you to have surety that life after fore you, death was for all. It is not salvation—not that; I see many alive, busy, but not much use in the world following death, until they stand above the mere machinery of movement, of folly, and of pleasure in the unusual manner of those who have been lifted by the desire for fine and true service—just like those who are awakened to cries of pain and the desire to bring peace-all this to me is love; a spiritual love that listens in the dark in sorrow and pain for the cry of the soul in its desire to know God.

There is a great deal that is good, quite true, quite ordinary, but just as I used to feel the beauty of twilight after sunset, it had nothing to do with the river I fished, nothing to do with the stars that came out living planets, but I took it all in, it was part of all I was doing; tramping home after a day of fishing, catching small things on the ground or in trees-they come back to accompany me as I remember those days of so much joy in the open. You have it, too, my dear. I see you looking across the street to the river, seeing lights flashing, and

VISION you

I know that everything leaves an impression on your mind. Your mind has a bit of the photograph quality, it sees and registers and recalls. It may be June when catch a glimpse of a rainbow; your mind retains it, it stays, and in January you may look at a different world; then suddenly comes the photograph of the rainbow you saw in June. That's what I do, I have a whole gallery of fish (he smiles) and I see the beauty of those damp bodies. Do you ever wonder if I want to fish now? 

I don't see them. I rarely think of them. I do think of hills that I love, of trees I watched in a furious storm, lashing themselves back and forth in the wind; I think of them and love them. There is something I want to say just now. I think I have never spoken of a garden all my own, with places sheltered from any wind. I sit there and say, "When she comes we'll sit here and here, and see these forms not destroyed by man for houses or fuel." 

I have very lately been invited to go to some very old countries to see how they are propagated and carried on in a new universe for new races. I'll tell you about it when I have found out more. Whether they disappear I don't know. I would not think of the destruction of their beauty.

Here is something I want to say about our children. 

Sometimes I have felt that my daughter should have a larger interest in life which would give her the desire to know about life after death, not merely for the comfort of knowing where her father is, what he is doing, but a wider interest that she might find in a great truth that so few people are really able to comprehend. Just to be sure that we shall meet sometime is not enough. Unless

VISION 

263 she is able to make a contact with me she will not have the blessedness that you have. She is sensitive, able to feel, and I believe could hear, but until it is a dignified and helpful consciousness it would not add to her strength and hope. I should like to see her doing something equal to yours. My love may be strong enough to convince her through you that there is something more than mystery, just as your H. often gets impressions almost like dipping into a sea of knowledge and uses it. 

THE LETTER XLII 

November 25(), 1936 

HE LETTER. Welcome, blessing of my heart! The day fills me with exultant joy, though I am in the midst of tumult and distress, the world bobbing about in the atmosphere of torrents and the worry and fret so hard to overcome. Here I find a little harbor of purity and peace and love, giving and receiving the knowledge of eternal uplift and promise of the vanished years that leave a record helpful in memory and precious to hold to new faiths which appear as stepping-stones to higher and diviner life. I cannot help rejoicing when I find this does not end-this communication, I mean-for when there comes a time of perhaps losing the power to write my letter to you, I have for a breath or two wondered just what would become of me were I to find no way to give you my letter. That is most important of allknowledge would come to you in the passing years as to others who have never had experiences like ours, but the radiance and glory of life is so dependent on com-

VISION 

munication that I dare not take it as an expression that I could hold always as possible. So we take our gift and are happy even when "there is no school to-day." (He smiles.) It was so lovely for the helpers in spirit-life who are ever eager to adjust all that is possible to circumstances to send our little Indian to ask me if you might help. "Oh, of course," I said—and you responded, and made all right for those in need. That gave me a new light on the way we may work together, and I hold it as a part of the gift. The little girl whose mother was so distressed as to her way of coming here, said to her Aunt Anna that she felt like a bird which had lost its song; and that made me long to tell you how you gave the song to her again. All this should not have come to the child, but it serves to show us how dependent love is on the song that may reach the heart for which it is sung. It adds lustre to our jewel, and it will be good for those who do not know the value of spirit communication, and that those who die do not forget but ever yearn for the voice that carries hope and knowledge. 

When we—you and I—who have always carried so much interest in the affairs of humans who suffer and struggle and are in need-when we have made life somewhat more than a holiday, we are very glad and satisfied with the next step in life when death reveals the stairway that leads on and out of the fog and the eternal sunlight pierces the dark, sending sparkling lights that lead us onward. I do not pretend to know all there was to know when for so many years I was baffled and full of questions that gave no answer to my prayers; but you must have had a divinity within you that, like that dove in the ark, flew out across the deep waters but,

VISION 265 thank God, flew back again bringing the symbol to old 

Noah; and while it did not land me safely in a new world, it gave me love for your faith and courage to add to your confidence the evidence of my living soul. And here we are in our communings in a little spot no larger than is necessary for us to find space to sit at a small table to give the love and beauty of a larger life that does not crowd GOD into a corner and demand an explanation of all that life crowds into our minds. Perhaps that is what I was unconsciously doing, but I have also, perhaps, brought about the vision of unfolding knowledge without demanding too often or too humanly the answer in complete and perfect language that I might understand. The sky, the verdure, the stars, the orderly stars that range themselves each night in serried ranks and move by law, ought to be the answer if we were not depressed by the weight of ungodliness and the effort to attain godliness in the world. Now that I did find it possible to send so definite a message to help in a problem, I call it another feather in my cap, and see possibilities before unguessed that will help men like our boys and women who have problems all the way; this may not express to you as I am doing now that they will use methods to make their problems subservient to this law we have discovered, but they will do so because they will recall what you and I have done. Isn't that a stepping-stone across the world to the desired place of helpfulness? I say so. Limitations are largely of human experience; sometimes very unsatisfactory, but they pass away just as we have stood by and seen our little folks reaching and striving for perfect examinations, for perfect expression in words, failing, stumbling,

VISION but trying again as years bring new power and strength, and at last success. Now we may look for our helpfulness in any organization, whether a religious assembly, a political discussion, an artistic colony, a musical conference that keeps light and shade in the lightest song of love and joy or deepest tones of misery; what we add will mean more to our own performances in life than we had before been able to perceive. It makes a broad world in which we have a full part in the play of the drama; all this goes on.

I have been surprised that all the horrors of ancient warfare, the efforts for conquest, that have been a part of my own vision of world affairs I am not disturbed over, for I know that possibly much wrong spilled into the consciousness of those who must obey lords and masters may get it out of the heads of rulers to longer disturb the peace and destroy the safety of nations. It must end some day—the idea that men who know the futility of war and destruction should never give the idea that we must always have war in order to have business that will sustain life. No-no-no, and so we see oncoming storms and protect those who are not able to protect themselves, uninformed people who do the bidding of men, even the slaves. We have seen some war scenes ourselves; nothing much came out of it, after all, except long rows of white crosses over which mothers bend, and a few hectic deeds that were costly and too soon forgotten-not worth the price of a world so soon forgetting and smashing into kingdoms that were quite able to care for themselves. I did not think I would talk just like this, yet in a way it lets you see what I see, that although we are not in a heaven whose pearly gates are

VISION 267 opened that we may enter and choristers in white robes sing forever in happy unconsciousness of the needs they used to understand, every day I find some centres of service where I may ask and receive answers as to my best work with my friend who works with me. The answer I love best is always, "Keep right on loving, for love is the strongest power in the world, and the more you love your dearest the more power flows from you and is creative of the beautiful experience that leaves no room for evil or hate or laxity." In great big, almost scintillating lights, I see the love of the individual is the opening door for the love of other individuals as every mother with a baby finds every other baby an individual responsibility as far as the family or neighbor and the enlarging interest of city or country and nation extend with time and opportunity. 

I am very happy to take the "little lad," who walks with me many hours and is so companionable, and now a part of our group. G. P., who is an instructor and leader, tells me to tell you that he did not die in vain; his youth has purpose, has desire, and his friend R. H. became like you and me in adding to the knowledge of these experiences. [THE VOICE. He dropped his arm around your shoulder.] Never a step without my care and my desire to do so much with you, my love expanding, if possible, into everything in life: poetry, art, all these things are like flowers on altars of love. Each night I look out at the stars with you, and say, "Right here, come walk with me in the hours of sleep to the near lands between the stars and any city where you may be, whether New York, Boston, Philadelphia, Albany, or Washington—always together.”

VISION LETTER XLIII 

December 17, 1936 

HE LETTER. You come with the loveliness of the

TH thought and desire to have me spare you not, and yet I must ever be aware of the draft on energy which does not affect me and must be some burden to the body, carrier of life and energy, I use. In this I am aware of the season of gifts and the many demands that come with the loving thought of giving. I said as I came up the little stairway, "I give myself to you." Yet it is not a gift in terms of what makes up happiness for the children of the earthly kingdom, but the joy of finding myself able to express somewhat of the life-giving principle, which sustains thought when years of the life after death have claimed the body. It is this (putting one hand over the other and pressing it down), hand on hand as if belonging to the same body, never to be separate or apart except to press more closely to the other. I could not avoid the Christmas spirit as I went forward with the onrush of those on this side where I am, in the same desire held long ago when life was fragrant with the thought of giving to those one loved. I was not as eager as they were, for I was with the one who must take what I may give and bind it with the gift of truth to the world in its need and its purpose to re-create the blessed assurance of life that does not die at set of sun, but comes into the clearer light of the new life that we have found so good-so good. I would not take away any effort to make life lovely while it is lived in the bodily world if it does not take away too much from the spirit-

VISION 

269 ual significance of the Event and submerge it in the commercial life that distorts and destroys the beauty of everything it veils. It is the beautiful understanding of the reality of love that is so evident. I have been thinking of the way people over here return to the old habits that were so important when life was a part of dates and commemorations; they look back on calendars and live again the days, and that gave me an idea that you might like to know if Christmas is carried on over here. Yes, by some, but also other saints and personages have left a memory that is cherished as are the saints and heroes in far lands; they are remembered by those who cherished these memories long ago. It is 

Christmas in Christendom, but just as remarkable and well remembered are the soldiers who fought and the martyrs who died for their own bit of soil; and it is only as minds outgrow the idea that it is a worthy form of devotion, that we find the higher and diviner realization of the spiritual life. I always turn as if you were beside me and put out my hand to make myself realize that you will walk with me through these scenes and that I shall smile at you no longer in wonderment and surprise, but with the knowledge we hold now and shall find revealed later, that all are on the way; they may leave at any moment the shadow of unreality, a belief that has led to acceptance of the upward path, and come where we happen to have come in our wanderings through life's experiences and love's ardor. All this I frequently see as a panorama, and it is very wonderful to me to see them, like pilgrims, walking up a path that is made brilliant by the light of the higher life. They drop out occasionally and look about as on a new and

VISION 

wonderful creation just made clear to them. It reminds me of a journey in a new country, where suddenly the sunlight falls on an alpine height and there is perceived a man striding away from some of those he was with, to find the path to a peak that, like a vision of untold beauty, invites and gives strength to reach it. And I think one always feels that behind such a height rising out of sight may be another who is climbing the altitudes of the spiritual countries. Yes, we may fancy that bridging any chasm that looks to beginnings of new worlds, there are bridges that clasp each to the other in a perfectly safe and approachable contact to the more distant zone. I have seen that love is ever expressed by the lifting of hands in adoration of the Maker of the world.

Now from this little picture of how we find ourselves moving outward-upward it is but outward it is-I leave it for a more personal touch of all I feel for you. I think it may take away all the imaginings that some people here have, supposing them to be the sublime, and bring to us the natural resourcefulness as the knowledge which a pilgrim, a plain man born a lover, seeks to bring to all who love life in the definite way in which we move from height to height and yet remain a man, a woman, a teacher, and a friend to all mankind. If

Jesus gave himself to the world as a gift of knowledge that may add to belief in life eternal whether or not we see the immortality which is so difficult to realize, we may praise Him for the purpose to bring the children of the Infinite One into the contacts that lead to a belief in the Fatherhood of God. I hope that I may never strive to express in mathematical terms the Three in

VISION 

271 One and One in Three; we are all more than one with a thousand others who have mind and heart and soul confined in the measurements of life in its variety of experience; yet in all this one is a part of another in a coming together at all points of perfection in desire and love and knowledge. Once in a while when I am sitting close by you, hand in hand, with all the precious influences of memory crowding in upon me, I am thrilled and happy, precisely as when I read from your hand some helpful and believed truth you had offered. Just why we slipped away from some very normal and natural experiences I never knew until I remembered that it brought us together in a mightier and more nearly perfected relationship than is quite possible under ordinary circumstances and situation. The cares of parenthood are beautiful and impressive; they are, however, cares that bind parents into common solicitude and the other bonds of parentage, and while we may feel often the desire for daily companionship one may not realize that the loss of it gives something quite remarkably like soul companionship. I think that explains our moving toward these experiences which have been so wonderful, so much so that not one in numberless people finds in spirit communication what we have found. There is a movement among some of the psychic research people to build up a new form of spirit communication. 

That is as it should be, and it should keep out curious people who do not want to be bothered to take up the problems for themselves or companions and go hunting for the answer to prayers they breathe to the dead. Is it not a tragedy that a mother must still keep on giving receipts for family parties and the cookies she used to

VISION 

make when the highest and finest truth may be instilled into young minds and the reality of death be given through any message that might be written or spoken? 

I thought that several times when I knew that so many of my friends would not take any trouble to know what 

I tell you, but would rush to the ends of the world if I said, "There go you and find a pot of gold buried long ago." And I have known that much that has kept the spirits who would give to those they would help some of the reality of the life after death as you have done has been put aside to ask, “Shall I go to New York or 

New Haven?" It seemed so utterly useless to ask such a question when the heavens were opened to give back the heavenly harmony and re-create life, whether on a far prairie in a little house or in the city influences that are often betrayed into using power for something not at all necessary to life. At last, when you were having much the same thought about the ideal communication, I saw what you did, that some of these communications brought zest to go often and at last catch the sacred influence of those who stood within the ranks as helpers of communication-something found-mother and child, husband and wife, friend with friend-many, many aching hearts relieved of the fear of what might happen when silence fell. These may become stepping-stones to the truth that the people who die are just people and will be fed husks if they have nothing else to feed on as they come through the desert of life-education which about many things is absolutely soul-less. It is really a question of the manners where one has to live-good enough if that is the way they use the key to happiness,

VISION 

273 yet there is something that we believe may help to swing open the door that Jesus may enter in every day and add to the glory of life wherever lived. The church is full of such people. It is the leaders we must reach; they desire to know what we could tell them, for even a child can bring back a pearl, even a weak boy can point out a rainbow.

All this with all my heart I long more than ever to express, to give to those who would hear, this gift we have found. One moment I grow so earnestly into this purpose as to give a message to one in need, yet I do not want to be always talking about the most important thing I know. I wanted to be simple and true, with various forms expressed in my letters to you. I have become aware, strangely enough, of that tragic story of the Englishman who falls in love with one who is married and gives up his kingdom with no realization that there is something that might be of use to her in the sin (to any good person a sin) of stealing another's wife, and crying out in the anguish that is his. I don't think he'd cry for sympathy or understanding to-day if the father of your son whom I love and cherish stood here smiling and saying softly, "Don't forget to tell her that we are to be together at Christmas with the happy family that Christmas gives us as in true love.” And 

Christmas over, the chimes having sounded and the weary, happy-makers (not merry) may sit side by side and know that all are bound together for higher service in the world-sitting in starlight, watching the flash of beauty in the night sky. So those who may have suffered, not through sin, but overdoing in service to the world,

VISION 

may come into the pure atmosphere of the after life. I cannot see why love, enthusiasm, and power enriched by it should fail. I think it goes on forever.

All the little folks here-the little lad and the little girl and little Roger-all I could name-they send you their love as an accepted member of a group tried and useful, boys and girls growing into the gift of power by constant expression to those they love. They all send their love and I add mine just as strong as it may grow, each day stronger.

THE LETTER XLIV 

December 31, 1936 

HE LETTER. We are here with the last day of the year, which gives the opportunity for the word of love and the experiences that give us closer bonds of the spiritual life.

Love is a word like many others that are used to give a sort of general description of the forms of interest in people, situations, and whatever appeals to an emotion. 

I sometimes feel that we might find a word that should be used in the exalted and wondrous sense of the spirit that surpasses all other feelings that come and go, flame and fade, and is woven into memories, at last finding the exaltation of spiritual and divine life. So much comes into these bindings of body, intellect, and unfolding, revealing life, that it is difficult at times to separate feelings from emotions set in action by the beauty and desire for something quite beyond the words we use interchangeably, and as worship may mean de-

VISION 

275 votion to some high and lofty beauty of early dawn or starlit spaces of the vesper hour, and to feel the inexpressible worship of the Divinity that eludes yet is always present in roses that bloom or dews that fallthat is the onward moment of understanding of all that is to be, and even great poets bend in worship in the thought of the evening star that is always a permanence of Divinity in action. All this in meditation comes with a new sense of the Ineffable and Divine within and about us. I never find myself looking afar off across waters that divide and yet reunite us to worlds that are yet undiscovered, that I do not feel the worship of the 

Creator; I may not see Him, He is formless, yet there

He is with all the beauty of all the worlds in a formed and definite orderliness that is beyond conception. There is much of that quality in human affection beyond our understanding yet impossible to separate us from God; and so we go on-literally. I have several points of contact with some beautiful and wonderfully impressive places that are like shrines, where I take you into the confidence of my soul aspiration; and as if you had been there with me in the body, I know the companionship I am aware of. I worship and adore the expectant life almost as if it had come to us. Sometimes in your light dreams, when weary with the effort to think out the many words I have used in my letters to you, I take you just as literally as if I held your hand, with no idea at all of helping you, not for your need of physical help over a strong path, but for that feeling of hand in hand, forever we go; and I may not say the blundering words that I give you for memory to aid in holding consciousness of all the feeling I would express, but I am in these

VISION 

moments alone with you, and the one sense of presence fills me and abides with you when you wake. If it were not possible to hold this abiding consciousness of presence there would not be the saving grace of spiritual companionship which is ours. Many heartbroken people who have come to this consciousness before death have no such perception of limitless life as we have. 

From the days before I knew you, from the babyhood that came creeping up the steps of life, I must have recognized something akin to eternal love which would come into form and feature, voice and power, knowledge and intimacy, and yet the name was not there. To-day 

I call you Faith. I call you the expression of faithfulness and tenderness and fearlessness. Many more names fall out of my mind into my awareness of their fitness for you, the quality and the quantity of so many wonderful experiences that have come to us since we kept the faith in all that pertained to a higher life not always recognized by men. Maybe it lies too deep to come like a fountain of life with the display of its power, but we found it in full measure at moments, not naming it, not even realizing it, but through the constant desire to lose nothing of truth or beauty in the world of our service in life. Neither you nor I called it love; it was not because we did not realize that it was more than a quality of deep affection that touched us every day, not in the order of declaration and fidelity to the person, but as the motive that kept up faith to truth and bade us to move forward, sometimes in a fury of desire on my part to know so much that seemed incomprehensible to me and was definitely accepted by you as a possibility that

VISION 

277 we might not be able to understand but still accept as a problem that time would solve—just as you thought after my death, "Now he knows, now he knows"and I asked myself what more did I really know than what I had doubted before death came. You gave me that problem to solve for myself. You gave me the responsibility of telling what I had found; and while it led me out of the shadow of the world that whirls in space with atmospheres, changes, and limitations, into the free air of the higher altitudes of life, I have made some things clear to my own mind. And you, Faith, you have been obliged in the midst of these changing lights of the atmosphere of life to take what I have told you and accept it as you would a letter from one who had stepped out into clearer air, accepting what he sees and adding it to your ideals and the hopes that I tell you will be yours. All this is not just a matter of telling you what is to be, because you are mine in the perfected days of life after death, not the satisfaction of hearing that you and I are not doomed to separation, but taken into the full beauty of continuance in all that makes for the divine experience we wish for. [Here came a digression about fishing.] 

Some days I am near singing, myself, voicing life's joy in a tune-really a tune! Who that has heard the pines whispering together under the stars, and finally bursting into harmony, waves dashing against a rocky highland, singing softly, or breaking into loud anthems of joy as it leaped again; who that has heard these things that are just the music of the natural world, with the headlight, the water rushing toward it, the

VISION 

rocks, the branches, all these are the music of the natural world. Before you know it, Faith, you will find someone to add his music to your own!

I have talked with some of your family who have been seers-not those who see the future but who know what effect will follow another as each grain of sand is a part of sands behind. That's what I mean by a father not displaying knowledge but always aware of the effect of one person on another. They are going on with this gift of seership, adding by their thought to those who accept it. They are a part of a group of seers; it is religious because it finds causes and reason to bring out the best. 

We are now beginning to understand that the urge and push from this side can affect thought. So sons and daughters become more responsive, and if this thought is taken in that just ordinary talk, the essence of truth comes to be accepted. You and I have begun to chart out a path to the higher life. 

So we say farewell to the year and to-morrow brings with the new year a new impulse and stronger love. 

I am glad that you came. I was just hungry for you, dear Faith, and this gives me a new impetus, wherever 

I am, with instant response to all that comes, and to lay it on your table. Love enfolding, service constant, fear never shadowing the light that we know comes from time and makes eternity.

VISION 279 

LETTER XLV 

January 7, 1937 [I PUT this question to him: "I have wondered how I ought to feel about that experience in New York several months ago, when you and my husband and several of my family spoke with me in audible words." 

He answered: "You were in a static of deep receptivity which made it possible for us to come to a definite understanding to which we shall add later; and there are some very important matters that I wish to bring up to-day and if I may do this I will take up the other later."]

TH HE LETTER. Again and again I have wished to tell you of some of our experiences which we may joyfully give to our correspondence for the better understanding of the things that have come to mind often and need the light of experience for those to whom it is impossible to explain in terms of the new association, so luminous to us and so dark to those who think of reunion after death as a continuous marriage-service. 

When one marries in the accepted conditions of life together on earth, there has always been the sense of virtue and the desire to leave behind all the old associations that make the old intimacies of life unpardonable; this is the absurd position taken by many who object to second marriage after the death of a romantic episode that very nearly made a new one acceptable. To us there is no misapprehension of love and its full play of power in our life of the present and all that is to be; it is not as if one closed all doors of sweet association

VISION 

and put between the present and the past the acceptance of all that is additional beauty and love and service to take into marriage the full complement, all good and true and useful. As days and years come into the fruitful period of larger service, finer devotion and godliness to all, we should move on every step of the way, not destroying tender recollections by the foolish belief that one rises on the destruction of loveliness and beauty -that is, to pay tribute to the present which in many cases is succeeded time after time until the perfect note is found and one adds to strength and power, destroying nothing, but accepting all as stepping-stones to the 

Throne, finding wisdom and light and glory while adding to the finest and divinest of law, love, and joy. It is not the tossing aside of the joy of motherhood to find other children to be mothered and to give all that is important for the unfolding of the life in need; this is as natural as to find one's heart and soul enlarged and glorified by any service in any form. So in our glorious experiences of these blessed hours we have accepted the blessings that have come and I touch your hand and find new strength, new joy, and yet am ever mindful of my past as something ennobling and beautiful to all who come into the circle of relationship; but as I rise to the new gift of vision and the newly awakened desire to rise again into the strength of spiritual vision that had never been more than an ineffectual desire kept away until the brightness of the life with you left nothing to be desired or set aside as impossible or imperfect, I have been pondering over those who never looked beyond the family life, and considered any slight estrangement of desire unworthy and untrue, and were not prepared

VISION 

281 for the full surrender of the knowledge that is ours to the greater service. There have never been any doubts in my mind, nor are there any now, as to the serenity of an obligation to the divine life which is dual, and springs into full activity when the light reveals the truth. I have assumed at times the light-hearted, boyish extravagance that is an expression of the joy that anoints me as I come into your atmosphere; it is never any sort of relinquishment of the place in my life in every experience good or less good, but faithful to what it has brought me and a desire ever to hold, in its rightful admission to all other experiences, the development that is growth. 

I have long wanted to tell you this, just as I knew you wished it to be so.
