No Robinson Crusoe Island far apart from the life known before, for you and me, but with that waving of joyous anticipation to expectant friends we move on, and husband, wife, child, or friend takes what belongs to the realm of life they know; and in the silence that falls on blessings of tenderest care and with the upward climb to divinity which must eventually bind two in one with fingers of love, and voices of music, and wisdom shining, all are carried on-carried on; each thought of tenderness to another, whatever realm of life they occupy, becomes the swift mounting of the soul into larger and glorified experiences. 

To say that your children, boys, men, working out problems for themselves are dearer or less important than my own, would not be correct; they have their place in my affection and care whenever it is in my power to give it; just as you care for those that were my responsibility, and wish to include your prayer and desire; they are yours and mine in the degree of their

VISION need, and with the admiration and love they hold, and are held by, forever, they and theirs will grow with usthe most needed, the most served, by either of us; families become more than families as they are united in interest and love and care, and with all this there is delight, for neither you nor I wish to release from the consideration or our purposes one little finger or one weary old hand-they are part of the new kingdom death until they need us no more; but whenever this problem of teaching this truth comes for you or for myself I am aware that we have come to another step where the far vision is perceived. Sometimes the rejection as dangerous teaching of problems that are entertained too early to be understood may have to have a curtain drawn-and I think it is just as all problems must be given in the form of the stair on which they base conclusions; license is not a consideration, but truth is the essence of divinity and its acceptance. I do not mean to be so eager to tell all those I have known before light came, how things seemed so vastly important before the door opened and I knew what you meant to me in those rare experiences that have brought me something that nothing can ever take away.

To add to the desires of men the form of possession of the bodily life in the bodily kingdom is another matter; that, too, is a matter to let alone until the recognition of the difference between body and spirit is a part of the teaching from my side of life. There may be jealous wives or husbands who have locked doors between those who claim such ownership through a marriage license; they often base their claims on the "rights” given them by law, and love is only the word used to

VISION 283 make law the master of their rights. Law is in its last acceptance the law of God, and we may not cry for what we do not understand. Working out of this spiritual problem we may find some whose sobs soften our desire for the light for them, but do not work too hard to feed an infant on the husks of life; the milk of human kindness will do more than arguments which do not hold the answer to their prayers. I know that we are not the only triumphant victors of the knowledge that we always desired. I have once or twice referred to those super-characters who have not found what we have, but have held to the legacy of truth and wisdom and found the answer, after death gave them the light we have; and some who were abundantly blest by a companionship that was never recognized as the song that rises to the height of understanding until there came a note that answered to the note of the other in the same terms; and others who wander on, trying this and that and the other, at last finding in art or science or music or any form of great service to the world a panacea for loneliness, but not an answer to the actuality of the truth we have found.

All this I have seen, sometime before I came back to you to give you the wonder of it all. And often I have seen a little dove of a lady cooing alone in the nest of her happiness, trusting in the love she held in her memory of the past and the hope of the future, that the action of the past will be bound to the future and all will be a redemption of faith in love as the beautiful and true. When I have caught a gleam of flashing wing as it moved on alone, and its desire gave patience, have I not longed to breathe the secret of the high life in the

VISION spiritual world that she might know what we know! It is not mine to give that service to those who do not need it, but grow into holiness by the faith and constancy of love. But when one comes seeking as they come to the little room, or some other, I whisper to the messengers who have built around the circle of influence here, "Give! Give all you can to help can to help on the way.” One thing

I have noticed in the service in this room or another where they work is a note that I think is not always heard or given, and that is that the "Light," as they call her, cares more for the truth to become the saviour and not the succor for the body or materialistic hope; that's the difference between those who may give advice on all the denominations of fortune-telling, and so forth. 

A new type of minister is what our addition to all religious movements may be called. As far back as in the days of Jesus of Nazareth, the psychic gifts in Greece, and in the witches stirring up boiling cauldrons that visions of the future might tell of poisons stored for the destruction of those who drank-all these forms and visions and spoken words have played a sad part in the world.

That's all for that explanation. It came after many hours of meditation, as to whether I should have you let this business alone. Of course I don't want you to let it alone-I want you to go on-one in a thousand who dares to tell the story of truth as given to you. I remember how I opened my eyes to the truth as it came floating down like manna from heaven to you, the substance of life unfolded in small and glorious bits of the gold that lies hidden in the chalice of a rose.

There is to be something later that will help to bring this to the world. When it will be offered I can't say.

VISION 285 Some word as a demonstration of the heavenly kingdom ought to come in songs and prayer; it is needed everywhere.

LETTER XLVI 

January 14, 1937 [ALLUSION to a visit made by me to Mrs. Soule. I had taken some roses to her which had been transported into the room of our communications.] 

HE LETTER. The roses have blest me all the hours

THE 

that I have spent here since you were a visitor to the Lady, and I have been so grateful to have caught a glimpse of Sunbeam quite apart from her message-bearing work. Now that I have been a part of the normal life where people live in homes, I have found a new and quite remarkable duality of personality that is not the division of a gift of language and power, but like two in a family with common knowledge of a number of people who constitute the life of one devoted to a specific work. 

Now I may add to much that has become vividly a part of what seems very natural and very helpful and human. 

But I desire to go forward with the acceptance of opportunity to add to the life we are living together. Even though you do not see me, the consciousness of my presence is as real as if I were writing from a city that is livable and definite, and making for me that wonder of wonders-that the dead are neither dead nor dying, but quite real and happy in the life we are finding gloriously real, and which I express in my own language, and hear it repeated for me into your willing ears. What should 

I have done without the voice that never mocks my

VISION statements but is quite ready to speak out as something outside the life she normally lives; with all this coming day by day, never faltering in the moment of a new place of meeting, not at all like a dream, but like a fine, exquisite companionship of spirit that binds us and keeps us in the contact that strengthens and revives the old ideals of life to the realities of activities when the awakened mind weaves them into precious harmonies that are ever a call to this service. It is so compelling that it will find lodgment in many hearts and minds, speeding us toward the spiritual life and experiences. I do not spend much time in thinking of the sins that frustrate our efforts; they have always been, as far as I know, a part of the strength we find in receding from a useless conflict; but there I may stand and use the influence of decision and expression to lift up problems that are too often found to need a vitalizing word of strength and assurance and understanding. I still find words and evidence of the usefulness of the statements and the strengthening of will; this does not blind my eyes to errors that need to be made plain for those who are wickedly kept from the reality of what goes on where I now have found an abiding place. I do not go out to fight the devil, but if the devil comes in where I am thinking out what I may say to you, that the message may give friends who receive it some ideas that clash with old ideas and beliefs, I simply look up and dismiss him with all the gracious manner I may assume, and he leaves, but I stay at my task. Is that the meaning of "resist the devil and he will fly from you"? Anyway, that works with me. If we make ourselves sure that it is a devil and that we do not have to pray God to put him

VISION 

287 in chains for our comfort, we may depend on our own minds to take care of such, and there will be less contest and more peace. I think that was often a point of my religious faith in the carrying forth of the light and hope and beauty in the world. As I put my thought into this new service I am always confident that you share responsibilities that would outlive much of the viciousness in the world. I may exclaim in surprise that so many spend so much time trying to think up punishments that fit the crime and then fear to add them as a part of sustaining law. I have been to some of the friends who still serve in the teaching that through light shining on faces with the desire to bring the Kingdom of Heaven to those who have never felt the kindness and love that is creative they may be helped into the light, and as I hear some of the passionately earnest workers speak as softly as a mother to an erring child, I am convinced that to many hardened minds, ignorant of what makes a man a man, and repellent of any effort to talk softly or to ask that anybody may come to their aid in time of need, these workers would quite outrun that desire to make a sinner pay the price of sin. So now there is the gift of love which may bring the Christ spirit to enemies who could destroy and grow more wicked under the pressure of a defence that man makes for himself against society. All this is a problem that touches everywhere where selfishness lifts its ugly head and demands what it wants whether necessary or belonging to him. 

Now I come into that relationship which makes our life to-day quite as heavenly as if we walked through the streets of gold and gates of pearl that have been so often called into the problem of reward to the good and faith-

VISION ful servant. I do not care for streets of gold or gates of pearl. To watch the dawn of a new day with your truthful eyes catching every gleam of a rising sun-to feel the pressure of your hand in mine as we walk through a little street where the autumn sunshine has refined the maples into gold and tints of red that gleam in beauty, is far better for my wants than any long-shining highway that might lead us to an unexpected golden way to reach the heaven of our Father, the Creator of worlds without end. Very steady and unchanging; but you and I see the gold in trees and shining rivers, on mountains lifting their peaks far out of sight, and know that God is there; quite as expressive in love and beauty as in bars of gold and jewels that mock us when we are athirst or when dread of the coming hour is met with amethysts and pearls in the search for the sustaining needs of life. I have yet to sit down at a table over here to find the laden board that the family provides for those who hunger and thirst—that is, I have never eaten food like the food at home or abroad, but I have felt well fed looking out on a field of fruit that is more like the continuous beauty of a garden in bloom. Whether this kind of sustenance is continued on and on into years of persisting life, I do not know. What I do know is that I am satisfied-something so rich and beautiful comes to me that I rise and walk and feel that sense of vigor that I have felt many times after taking a walk before breakfast, and feeling no desire to eat even a trout that I might find waiting for me. It may be that beauty sustains strength, it may be that the love of beauty that reminds of early life gives a thrill that now and again we seek as a precious memory of loving care.

VISION 289 

Do not feel that I belittle the family life of bread and butter, whatever the mother fed us on, not that I ever try to find out whether the same fare is offered-it is not to me; I do not take this to make any argument as to what comes as sustaining power after death, but some people, like the Indians or the Chinese or the Swedenborgians, go on taking what is buried with them for sustenance on the journey, and some a gun to shoot new favors offered in the next world. There are signs of the literal provision of food and rest and nourishment. It may be so; it does not call to me. If it helps to make the next life more real, that is the first step for them to come into the knowledge that I find. I am sure that one whiff of those roses over there would make me desert a breakfast table. Now that we share the knowledge we have found, I am doing all I can to give the word to you. I feel that we are making progress toward spiritual desires and acceptance of spiritual life. We must wait until something is offered besides the high spirit-altitudes of crowns, glory, and angel choirs. 

Now each day, even each hour, shows me no thraldom, no desire to be released from service, nor do I feel that there is a state of punishment horrible to believe in as a part of the system of justice and right reward and punishment. I have never seen it. I have seen men walking alone, head bowed, hearing, as in a dream, a harsh word, a cry of pain, a sadness portrayed in slow footsteps, with no light or beauty; these are on the way to light and beauty, life and love. Let that light in; it is the saving grace of knowledge, and that is what we are seeking, the saving grace that brings light and knowledge. It is, at this moment, one of the hard tasks to

VISION 

think of what one man may have done to the world in desire to inflict punishment when human design rides at the wheel. Yet with this anguish in mind that comes from delusion and craft, we may find that the next step, such as many long to inflict, is useless; it gives to the one who suffers a desire for retaliation, and that is never stepping heavenward. It holds to false ideas; no thought of salvation from wrong committed helps to redeem sinners from results. When sent back even after paying the price of prison and pain, and striving to be decent, some tongue lashes out a further punishment, often driving to further wickedness. I and you, you as I, do as I did, and as you will think it over and often step into the position that has brought that about-not society always, or lack of home training, not always someone not sure of his position, but who sins because of mistaken judgments. That is it—because we are reformers; it isn't because we are indifferent to what is going on, it is because at the bottom of our hearts there is a love for each other-That is it, that we always come to at last-what may be, not in knightly manner, perfect knowledge, ever increasing statesmanship—all these have been in the minds of great criminals. There have been such criminal activities among those who know law or have information about law. 

But here we are, you with me, not only in this little room. Do you know, when you were listening the other day, I had a desire to speak in my very own voice; in spite of myself I would reach across and say, "Here I am, my faithful one," out of the mediumship, and would give all to express “here we are." And just as I was about to try to see if I could make you know I was there,

VISION 291 in comes that little Indian speaking to you just as nor mally as could be. A new experience. I had thought this room in the house was the important one for communication. I still think that it keeps its place like a little lighted sanctum where people may feel it easy to express what is in their minds; and I hope many times we may have those visits apart from here that we may see how normally those messages may come.

LETTER XLVII 

January 21, 1937 [I GAVE him a message from "the other Frances," saying that she always felt him near. After a second he said, "Yes, and with love," and immediatley began his letter.]

THE 

HE LETTER. I have been thinking of you in the same constant manner of those who are united in more than the expression of words or letters or any communication that may fall into the daily walks of life. To say that there are thousands of intimate ways that make themselves the continuous line of light throughout the ages may sound almost too much to understand, and so it is, in continuous ways and manners of the merely bodily or intellectually perceived thought—the constantly increasing manner of spiritual understanding.

But it is to me as I perceive it like a rare jewel that, hidden away in a box where light does not reveal the full beauty, becomes suddenly wonderfully like an animated quality revealed by the flashlight of a new experience, or a dawn that gives out the light that is and

VISION 

292 the beauty of a dawn that is near. So often I draw so near to you and find there the sparkling and brilliance of the light about you; again I look, and the shadow of the time, time in the night or day, or the body of others near you, does not allow that spiritual glory that 

I find with you when standing in radiant love and constantly revealing the quality that is so beautiful to me.

Now that is a new experience which is very largely a part of the knowledge "what is love"-all thingsknowledge, beauty, beauty of form or face, understanding of qualities naturally sympathetic, response, longing to have you near-all are evidence of the love I hold for you and each of us holds for the other without speaking of it or qualifying it as the one beautiful link between two who grow nearer day by day. It is a growth that may have come into beauty which is realized at the first touch of hands, the first gleam of interest; it is the basis of love at first sight, but not the full and complete experience, and I am not able to think that the completeness ever comes; it goes on growing in fairer fields of expression even when bodies are bound together in ties of companionship and duties; the very experiences which we enjoyed when separated by the life that took us apart in many ways: life that gave us new and higher understanding of each other, until to-day-I say this advisedly—we are practically one-no difference in our upreachings, no difference in our love for those dear in all relations, accepting them not as obligations but as trusts and loves that become dearer to each other.

THE VOICE. Suddenly he puts out his hand toward the little heart on the table that we gave to you. He

VISION 293 says, "Give it to her (his daughter). As if I sent my heart to keep her company."

THE LETTER continues. Now that there is so much of true understanding of the full spiritual life coming into our lives with so many of the intellectual desires changed by experience into spiritual perfection, you must feel it as gardens feel the dew in the cool of the evening and are refreshed, as roses send out their fragrance when the warm sunshine comes pouring into their hearts releasing all that the heart holds for just that expression of something deeper, something finer, something wonderful, in the interplay of warmth of sun and all that we know as beauty and love.

Whenever I sit in a council chamber-now I mean not the old experiences of taking account of what has been done and what may be done I always call attention to the growth and unfolding of communication. 

You might have waited until death brought you to my side; you might have caught the beauty of understanding as something for the heavenly life that follows the life of the body; you might have delayed the coming of the treasure, and I would have made every effort to bring by sign or in some way to make you conscious of my desire to come to you-and many of our friendly preachers, teachers, and instructors in the days of bodily service and intellectual understanding do just that thing, and keep the most precious gift of life in a little ante-chamber through which they pass after death.

So strange, when all the teaching and all the blessings are set aside and are lost in the finest experience in life; they make such sacrifice, not that life is improved or

VISION 

enriched by what the years bring to them of the beginnings of constancy and growth-one does not set aside the love and growth of a mother with children that she may be stronger in the house-bound to life and the Heavenly Father as life adds its dangers, its temptations and experiences, always sweetened by the knowledge that the child grows in stature, and at the same time with knowledge comes the occult combination of growth in body and spirit which makes for history to prove the truth of contributory graces. This will be, as we move forward, shown to us as all over the earth men and women grow away from lives of childlessness in the desire for freedom to dance away life and the contacts that are almost futilely pursued to find what they deem the life of joy. The very wars that steal away the babies and train them to fight each other suddenly remind us of the babies they have not found in the emptiness of lesser life and ugliness of selfish pursuits. 

I am not trying to teach you, you who have so much of the mother spirit; I am stating a fact-only the need of those who fight that the country they hold dear for themselves makes it obligatory to have children born often, that the surprise may be in the hands of their particular nation. Now that we find uses for our own (he smiles) youngsters that are far beyond training camps in the wars of men against men, art, science, truth, come forward for the later expression, making better headway than mere politeness. All this has come now to the attention of thinkers and those who grasp the fine knowledge of more than the bodily world, and give heed to the vision of wisdom and prayer. For the young women who also have found so much to do, even

VISION 295 to taking up arms beside lovers and brothers, they find the training for war an obligation if they catch the idea that the sex has strength and knowledge, quick eye and ear and desire to win what they are taught is acceptable to the gods. Soon they find that the increasing service of the house-mother is a loss to the finest and best in the world. This gives me something that I have not spoken of before, I believe, about the extension of the war-spirit after death. It does not pass away. When coming into the elementary work of taking all-men, women, and children, into this dull service that fights for land and power-power is the motive that is degenerating; if spiritual power is exulting there are powers that are degenerating. I have not said much about the taking of life in this life of the world from those who are cruel, sinful, and ignorant; but I do not see any good in putting to death those who value life so little in experiences they do not care for. Life is persistent, and is the protection of the people who are still in danger-thus the hatred still lives, for if there is death to one and the effort to carry on is still a pertinent question, there is the lowest form of life after death, always a failure in coming to the life without the teaching and power of the highest. Quite true it is that the feeling of frenzy that must be in many homes and people is at a loss to know how to eradicate the form of punishment that does not correct an evil, and of that we should take heed in teaching such as the higher ideals give to the world as the only way left, and let the shadows flee away in the higher identity and service of those who are working for eternity. I, who know so much of the world's ways of dealing with the lower strata of

VISION life and the reality of a definite interest in those who are unfortunate, still have the question always before me-love must have the voice in all the reforms, and love is for the soul that listens in the end to the voices of those who make an appeal for justice to their chance in life. Teach work and service. I have not been among those wonderful teachers who are moral and do not have to write but live it. I do not see to-day how we may reinstate the wicked who have lost the sensibility of interest in anything that does not give them what they will not work for. The sin of the world as I see it is selfishness, and that blackens all who yield to its influence, whether in the glorious kingdoms of life or in the capitals of wealth and power, in the laws of the world, in its understanding of life, in purity, or in the houses where sins are allowed to live without molestation; it will come, I believe, when the principle of "love your neighbor" comes to be the recognized power in New York or London, Egypt, or the little towns where ignorance of many things leaves freedom for peace and contentment. 

I keep these apart from my messages; they are so much a part of the life we have had. From the post of stars it may be dark, but every step upward by anyone anywhere is an upstep for a world bound together by times of need, the common interest of all. It grows glorious as life appears, and in a little garden may come something that at once brings the Heavenly Father into view; and wherever you go there goes the influence of good sense not starting out to preach some gospel. 

You may smile just as if I took a message of joy and presence as the message of the Eternal in word of the 

Heavenly Father. Never till I found myself walking into

VISION 

297 this new, wonderful Eternity in its beginning did I feel sure that if one is saved we all must be. 

About the little heart; it was childish to you; it was pleasant, but to her it's like the evidence of a presence. 

So with my love to her and you and my constant joy in her growing interest-she brings to that interest all her experiences. She is with you in all this work. I leave you now. [The little heart was a tiny sachet, very small, very new, very pretty. Sunbeam had given it to me when I arrived. He took it for his beloved daughter, separated from him. I sent it to her in Italy.]

LETTER XLVIII 

January 28, 1937

HE LETTER (he was already writing). Here I am be- Tfore you. 

Like a breath from the upper regions of life you come, and I begin again a message that binds us in the purpose of life to be as faithful to the Light as sun to the world that awaits the spring. Often as I drop a message en route I say, "This is a feather from a bird in flight, but no one may find the cryptic meaning but one who has kinship with the bird, winging its way through the avenues of approach, unseen, unnoticed, apparently carried forward until the perfect contact is discovered. The feather may give an idea of this life, after death has silenced the song, but there is the beginning of a new altitude reached, as onward, onward, the flight is pursued." Now as all the evidence of death and destruction

VISION 

is so much a definite part of knowledge, without any apparent reason for such havoc among men on the way through life, I feel that my heart would tuck you in with my experiences and find the beauty and loveliness of the companioning years, to bring the happiness which is bound in and through them, becoming a part of every breathless moment when consciousness is the guide to the heavenly life we yearn for. All these sad days when sweeping emotions come whirling through disaster and distress, we you and I-are constantly assured that there are experiences which send us upward to the undisturbed and growing processes of the soul in flight to reach out and let fall, without loss in the speeding, the evidence where all the dying, dead, saddened, and disstressed may find the experience that leads as eagerly as waters flood the place of earthly rest and companionship. I do not dwell on the tragedies, for they give desires that suspend the way-marks of the years and lead to the upreaching life that never ends. To us such experiences become an obligation and an opportunity to give what comes from the dear ones lost to vision, and yet are a continued and helpful guidance toward the 

Eternal, never fading in expression. 

Always I am aware of what you are to me in such days of doubt and un-wisdom. I was always as conscious of your helpful soul-companionship as at this moment I am; the words were not mine to utter, and yet I always knew the precious blending of our lives even when the consciousness of approach was not given, but the moment the body was released from its cares and responsibilities I cried out—I see! as Paul called to the vision of Jesus, and made his way out of the darkness to Light.

VISION 

299 Revealing a new personality within the old body, he began to preach and lead forward. Same words, same methods, but lightened by the truth that from that hour made him the leader of increasing groups. Now you are the leader of groups who have found the spoken words that have been put into the printed page and are now becoming a matter of knowledge to those who listen and heed. Not for our importance in the transmission can any of us claim the divine right to lead, but through the experience given as from one who loved truth better than the exact proportion which he may not have perceived it to be, he now clasps hands with you in our still joyous acceptance of whatever beam of light is afforded our search. You may not know it until you are able to read the record in the high light of wisdom that sees in review the beauty and the joy, wiping away all tears and finding love the strength and delight that gives knowledge to all experiences, with words that do not need explanation but acceptance. Some most wonderful people on earth accept much that they do not understand, but it seems good, so they take it as words of light, enduring and encompassing what they do not subscribe to, yet often repeat as if they did. But when I call you "Faithful," and you say in your heart "Comforter," we have used terms that do not need to be accepted otherwise than on trust till we have found the very terms in the spirit of life that continues after the body has changed-not its thought but its raimentthat needed only the light to shine upon it to bring it into visibility and then into the beauty of the meaning of the twilight of life and dawn of the next day. All these are terms that may be taken as a message from the

VISION Father-that to sleep is not to die, but to wake after sleep is to accept the new life as the unbroken life on earth that energizes again and again to consciousness of sun and stars and all that make up the outstanding experiences of each day. Tears may fall on the waxen features of one who has stepped from the little life to the less encumbered life of those who are conscious of what has happened. It is consciousness that gives us the best that can come each day; the sun may be shadowy and held from one's consciousness to understand that the morrow will come with power never destroyed, but useful in the better hours of warmth and light. To-day may reveal in that light mysteries that have held the mind in bondage through many years, and every day that I think of myself as the bird without loss of consciousness of any memory, or experience of any bodily harm, I have dropped a feather from my wing and still fly outward, onward, upward, whispering, "I am I, and you are you," and our consciousness of each other-not mine of alone nor yours of me alone, but the accepted consciousness-is ours, to overcome the shedding of tears from a lonely heart in the loss of companionship. I would make every shroud a step in the eternal life that comes from the Creator with bodies that have to be laid aside because usefulness for them is no longer desired. We do change—not in form or feature, not into any type of life; types are not interchangeable, as far as I am able to see (he smiles) by more eyes or limbs, but with stronger form, intensified and spiritualized by the atmosphere that bears them up. Not because one has many eyes, or one intensified, is there a remarkable change in appearance. Your mother comes as mother 99 you

VISION 301 to you who have always held her in the thought of a mother, and when you see her you will put your childface close to her face with joy at having a mother gone so long yet so close and dear to you. I refer to that because one's mother is always true to her children in resemblance, attitude, and love. It is useful to have babies and the mothers they have lost come together to let the world know the outrage of stealing-by some old method of unbelief-mother from child, and vice versa; that does not mean mere physical discernment, it is of the soul, a thing apart; and even when it has been a long time, or in the case of the unfit, he may awake after a time and at the approach of knowledge he may give a very definite instance of what real mothers have furnished. There is a wonderful experience that came to one who refused the birthright of his own child and finding it, found the Heavenly Father-found God, in a form of love which possibly came from watching a baby in its mother's arms.

All this comes sometimes even in those who work out a bare living between rocks and stones hardly expressed in the rare capture of the emerging of mother and child into life; this is not the usual spiritual way of accepting. 

It is mine at this moment; you and I share experiences in children and ourselves. This expecting life in sixty, seventy, or one hundred years-to give all these is to give about all that it takes so many years even to grasp. 

We need the life after death to carry on wisdom, and with knowledge, otherwise life would be vain and much left for some explanation somewhere. I do not strip life of its advantages when death brings slumber of a night from which we awaken. I mean this as absolute truth, to

VISION find (and here I trespass) a preacher sending those who die straight to God. It is what men have been slaughtered for, but the Heavenly Father is Creator of all that lives. To-day I felt I would take you with me away into the "feathers." I have dropped away from all this terrible tragedy of loss and separation now going on. How may we accept it as part of good and evil unless we take up the burden and express the actual demonstration of thought-life in these experiences of care and brotherhood and love for those in need and such agony? You and I will share the "feathers" as they move onward and upward; and from you who take it as a blessed sacrament of love and care. This morning we lift faces to hearts that shine with love that I may send floating through the atmosphere to you. You join me with gratitude for every word I have made it possible to gather, my author of love and peace, my companion in these experiences which we share. I long to whisper love to my children; what they need is their father's words, their father's arms. All doors would open if I knocked, would open wide if they heard my voice, but they have not yet acclaimed me as you do—communicating with one who brings communication in spoken words or meditation or thought or willingness to hear and accept. 

This is from the depths of my own experience before and after death. And if I have any prayer to send to the world it is that some word of yours or mine may help them to find God in His heaven.

That's all of this letter. I had much to say about companionship, but it will find its way.

LETTER XLIX 

February 4, 1937 [Allusion to floods and disorder in motor industry] HE LETTER. Here we are together again in this precious little room where we are shut away from the conditions that disturb the calm and useful evidence of peace beyond the strife that un-wisdom adds to the situation geographical and terrible to look upon even when we are conscious of the aftermath of death and destruction. It is so beautiful to find that nothing can steal from us the knowledge that even in the processes of death by such awful onslaught in the world, it has help for those who do not know that they are face to face with love that is ever stronger than in the days when need is not so great-children taken into fond embrace, carried forward into light and quiet peace before fear of the terrific flood becomes powerful. All this is not agonizing but comforting for one who knows that the next realization will be care and love and joy; men again holding fondly to the glory of reunion. I had always felt that death should be a peaceful and lovely moment of reunion, but I soon discovered that the body did not realize in its proper sense that death had any effect on the moment of the ended earthly life; it is usually like the dawn or a sunset that one sees shining through the tears of those who watch and wait from both sides: the mother who waits with smiles and outstretched arms, and the dear ones who close the eyes with tears that could not see the peace but only the shadow of separation falling heavily on the present

VISION peace. It is not always the feeling of release from bondage that one may feel when slipping out of pain or disability-one may rise on an elbow and take a look around the room, and feel happy to be able to get that far up from the couch of illness. That was the way I lifted myself from the bed and looked about with my old quick glance at the familiar life around me and felt, "Well, that's over, now what next?" and like a gleam of light there was love, shining and real—not one, but several faces long held in fond memory, now actively smiling; and I knew I had found Life, with not a second, it seemed, of unconsciousness. Not everyone dies like that; but I had longed to know what it would be like, whether a long-drawn-out process of unfolding consciousness or sleep-sleep that so many people expect to come to, and often speak of death as the "blessed sleep of death ere I awake." Nothing like that for me or for you. I hope to share the first joy over reunion. Past relationships do count, in a way, about the order in which they come, and I often whisper to some who want to give you the first embrace that comes with recognition, "Do you not think we deserve to take a place right away for all we are giving to those who have no other way to get the understanding of one who helps so much to add to spiritual consciousness?" Anyway, I should not make a protest over any demonstration of affection for you, for I know my hand will be closed tightly over yours, and I will share in any demonstration on our precious day of sitting together in a garden where roses bloom and birds are singing and there is that sweeping joy that is like nothing else anywhere experienced. Then we may shut away everyone for a moment

VISION 305 and breathe in the better understanding of the spiritual life which is abundant and ever a part of the processes of life beyond the world we first knew. In the hours that I prophesy you will feel youth and love animating every memory, every chance to express what you have known and found good in all its activities, and now can stand up, so tall with the height of your own perceptions-not as I picture them to you, for I know you will probably say, "Oh, it is so much better than I thought it would be!" I say, too, in that light to-day that you live with, you give to them evidence of thought and love; you are living so much better than ever in the days of dream and hope and larger service.

The other day when I left before you did, I knew what was going on, and I wanted you to have all the joy that would be yours when you heard the words that the father and I were eager to have expressed, not only that we might hear, also, but to be proud and "toplofty" over the boy [Harry, Jr.]. We, you and I, are very fortunate in our children, and there is more or less gratitude to you for the way you take interest in all of them quite as beautiful as if they were all the same brood. I could be grateful, indeed, for the way my personal responsibilities have been kept in mind by you in all that has been given you to bring light, comfort, happiness, and joy; and I am adding to my responsibilities over here by doing all I may for yours. There is much to come now in the process of adding to the knowledge and peace of all; we do not stand still, nor shirk our effort to add to the acceptance of our care, and we find a new receptivity whenever any of them is ill or sails far away, or whether one stands up to full stature-

VISION then some of us stand near, and find that thoughts and ideas are perceptible as they are expressed almost as if we were telling them. 

THE VOICE. Suddenly I see him touch the hand of your mother so softly as if happy. He writes.

THE LETTER Continues. In the group of good men there are always some who have wished for the truth of definite communication, and they have been blest by some words that were so clear and plain and true that they often sit quietly and repeat to themselves, "This is something that if true is worth while to be spoken of freely, and not with some special definition of a strange gift." Once in a while I find a rector or teacher who may have passed swiftly over supposed evidence without effort to accept or denounce it. Thinking over these evidences you had accepted and before quite aware that they also inclined to accept and teach it, they have really repeated the words by themselves (and something like it seems reasonable), "Suppose it is true and I fail to accept what is evidently truth to her, am I not failing in my opportunity?” And it sinks in the feeling comes that duty is duty, and truth that is hidden because of unbelief should be revealed, that all may have the blessing in the presence of death and the hereafter. It helps me to know that the words have come through your perfect work in taking them down that they may not be scattered or lost; and we share the joy that comes ever to those who preach and may speak to their parishioners of those who are ever near, tender and loving, telling them not to sit away at a private hearing in a heaven not revealed to them, but to know that life lives on, deathless and heedless of unbelief and dark-

VISION 307 ness. So we are members of an actively interested group of parishioners, and I hope that there are those who will add to their creed something like this: "Death is not destructive, it is an open door; we may see those we love, always ready to bless us with love never lost." It is good for a motherless child who weeps to know that the mother is eager to tell the story of love holding them together, that is so important; that some man may know that their little ones playing together look up as if they see someone-we should encourage thatone fears to encourage, fears some mistake or too much imagination. Not so much danger in that saying, "Oh! they have gone to heaven." It is an additional evidence of the father's goodness to tell the children that he is always ready to help-it is quite reasonable to tell the children that and accept the father's presence; they won't run into danger or foolish things instead of a high heaven of which they are not quite sure. All that religion if tinged with a child's helpful imagination is a good thing to have in the world-good for the father to die with that feeling to help. That is an additional reason for faith in our Heavenly Father to see a child defy the pain and sorrow of separation—no one can take the place. Death does not lessen the responsibilities of the father's spirit for those he is forced to leave. It may be the part of the clarion voice of duty to make aunt, uncle, any member of the family accept a responsibility for the spirit father, but a spirit father feels far more keenly the responsibility that is only needed in its rightful place. I am happy to know that my family accepts me as doing my work, not as an absentee father, but a spirit father who feels far more keenly the respon-

VISION sibilities than the absent one who puts restraining hand to avert catastrophe, moral or physical, now and again. 

The spirit father is a spiritual father thinking of his responsibility, his loving care and devotion to those near to him. These children of ours, knowing much or little of the faith you have given me and the father of your sons and daughters, have a right not to demand but to accept what comes to them through their love for us. It grows; it remains forever. With all this, who has given me a voice, a fine destiny for life in the Beyond? I never think of it as the gift of God without you. 

I never dream of it as something I discovered. I think of it as the sweetest gift; the memory of a golden past that found its need when you came to the place of knowledge—not a temple, not a great cathedral, not a stirring grand event, not that, but your face bending over a paper on which you wrote without question or surprise you believed the message which was given by a woman in her sleep with nothing but a paper and pencil and a voice and your intimate relationship, with words appropriate.

February 11, 1937

HE LETTER. All the emotions of life rise like little THE 

leaves of joy clapping hands of joy under blue skies as you come into this dear contact that is so wonderfully revivifying to all the finest and best in human life. 

In these days of desire for the joy that is really life I bend over the table and spell out a message that gives us promise of the Forever that is ours.

VISION 309 I am often amazed at the interest I take in all the madness of the days at our elbow, for there is the existence of splendid possibilities to give to the world burning in our hearts, and yet it is pushed aside by whims and assailable conditions for the unimportant; and then 

I come to a period of understanding that the lower serves as a stepping-stone for something higher up; and 

I talk it over with the loved ones who have served and saved us in the past, and I am glad that we have found so much to light us on our way, and as we are able to give to each other a fine example of what simple words may come for our peace and let it flow into the hearts of those who stumble on in our desire and confidence that grows like a tree in the new-found experience, I know that we are a partner in the service of the world. 

We certainly hear of a funeral just as soon as death has ended the life-work of our statesmen, our leaders, and our helpful group that moves forward hour by hour.

The communications that are thrust into daily life even at the table or the conference may give some idea of the pressure of time and time that seems to trespass on the quiet hours; yet it is important to keep up with the leaders, whether good or bad. I often close a door and let no sound break my happy contact with you in your work among those whom you love and who love you, and that is because I desire you to take this message; we, here, are not supposed to know anything in which we are not interested as you are, but in a way, after a fashion, even in the life that does not bind us to some effort in the earthly sphere, to close our ears as we do our eyes even on street cars or railway trains, and live quite apart from the news, and conferences between

VISION those sitting near us in form.

That is the way I take up the power of concentration on my neighbor's method of speaking so loud that everyone may know what is going to be eaten at noon or has been breakfasted on. It is the same process that separates us from those whose very aspirations may be a dividing line as definite as high from low. It could be and often is a quality that we use while busy with some very definite work we assume, and if one gathers the understanding of independent life even in a place of worship or service, music or art, it is the possession of power to protect one from the tawdry or sad or baneful, and if we should make effort even on some of the common things we see without seeing it would be quite like the new freedom that comes with the released life of the spiritual. I know that this truth is recognized by many people, as in a crowd a face reminds one of an absent friend who went away by death long ago; it is so much a part of memory that the face one sees brings another vein of thought and leads to past years as if they were the present. This when used is a real and beautiful gift and soon becomes like the mind in double harness.

Now that there is a general upheaval of power to be and do and listen to the ways that improvements may be helping in the adjustments of life, I find that our thoughts when read over and over again have wings, and fly out into the world like butterflies or songs of birds to bless and strengthen those who have never held such truths as we are giving expressed as anything but just talk, or misunderstanding of the messages we hold as sparkling rivers of light. Perhaps some of these new converts to the usefulness of prayer, never having had

VISION 311 any conception of men making new and wonderful discoveries of prayer, may add to faith in God as the one and only one to answer the prayer they have uttered. 

Closed eyes may shut away the vision of those who intervene in the process, but perhaps the prayers are answered. Prayers are revealed as an answer usually felt to be from the heart of God. Now and then there is a desire to know if the messengers who are used to carry the power are spirit-people or some investment of heavenly power that goes on thought-waves to be used as God desires. I answer that all methods are used to answer prayer; sometimes a prayer is so true and strong and good that by its very presence or atmosphere it produces a result, no matter if it be a child loved and lost by death who, in its own desire to stay near the loved one, carries an answer of faith and ennobled service, or whether from the hierarchy of heaven comes the power to bend low and whisper into a place of need a new belief in the help of words that may echo in many places at the same time and revive the fainting hearts of those in battle, or in illness that is often made strong by confidence and faith. Am I one who has accepted as the truth that there are to be believed in faith-healing all the other new-fangled beliefs in sciences that are only half accepted? No, I am not. Many of them should be set aside as good only as far as they work, but in many groups of earnest seekers after help, no matter which way it is given (only that it is given) it counts, just as a parent holds fast to faith in the Heavenly

Father, and finds vibrant life struggling back to the little one where many another gives up in despair. 

Preaching truth and its acceptance in a case where it

VISION has been an available asset in life is the way we find ourselves in many experiences beyond any faith we may have confessed, and I would not dare to breathe any lack of faith since I have seen so much more of the work performed by it than I ever did by my own effort to believe what seemed impossible. That is the way I have grown with my hand in yours and my words falling into your heart, just as I did try to accept your faith as mine, yet could not silence my doubts, for I had not seen what I now see, the religion of faith that I have now accepted; I have accepted it from you, not in the old way of being written out in your own angel words that fell often into a question or an argument by me. 

But you did not pass me as evidence of this experience -I passed you!-and so we each have the actual experience that united us and gives the added understanding of life after death as something that will abide forever with us—a book of good cheer, a book of good counsel which gives growth and understanding to all the ways of souls in their upward climb toward the full measure of our capacity. You were always very reasonable to me in your method of reasoning out truth, in so many instances, but you seemed to have something out of the ordinary. I am telling you this just as I would if we were sitting together in a garden alight with beauty and your appreciation of it-you looking up at a tree or bird or any living thing and with a new light in appreciation. In a way we always responded to that light, that beauty of mountain and waterfall. All these were exceptionally personal to you from the smallest to the highest in sympathy and understanding. You had your individual way of expressing it until it was like a light

VISION 313 on a high hill. Your power is a persuasive power, an unbelievable gift which is not usual with accomplished people or with those who bind their energies to one sort of expression. Your feeling was part of your own system and manner of living; you spiritualized it, and it became as natural as your voice. It was always wonderful for me to know. I could not explain it except by saying, "She is different"; others, too, said, "She is different." I gave myself the compliment that I knew it first;—and each day is like a little flower coming up, the miracle of the bulb coming up here a flower in the garden of your life, always remembered as coming when most needed, when winter is over and cold has left its world of beauty to go on growing through the years. This year I shall say this to you one thousand times, probably, "Here comes the lily of the valley, the leaves unfolding on the hill in the garden of the world.” It's very nice and good to send thought-messages, but I love words, to understand how they intensify and glorify language and we find that, as naturally as the song of joy through the air from a bird, speech will come out as if it could not be kept in. I have often put in some unuttered plans that might keep certain expressions only for those interested, but I have found true what I have said to one of our own, that they shared with me the love speaking to several at the same moment, knowing their experience of sorrow, sin, knowledge, not just one gift but all of these. 

Again I refer to what I heard as you came near my body, heard better than for many years before, "Now he knows." That was the key to my earnest desire to prove that you are you and I am I. We have lost nothing from that hour, but gained every day. I hope I shall never

VISION forget to use some precious word of my recognition of gratitude. If I should wait because I needed some word from you, it would be enough to say "Amen." 

I like to send to all my friends my love. It is not possible to name each, and when I speak of all these things to my people or yours, those on this side come with words of love knowing my confidence that we hold both, and others who look in who have the truth and serve in many ways we know not of. 

I want to stay with you and bless you again and again, with perhaps some little hymn. 

You are a leader; you lead me, as you always did.

HE LETTER. Often I sit long hours putting these

threads together as a definite expression of human experiences, yet forever linking the past, present, and future as one life divine showing through the masked experiences that I always found it difficult to call the evidence of the Almightiness of the Creator. VISION 315 comes in the same language that has been used through all human contacts. It is the very essence of knowledge, it is familiar and so inviting that one accepts it without delay in the confidence of the renewed experience, the fine discovery that life is always demonstrable through language that forms itself into assurance of life beyond and again beyond, showing the Vision. From the moment and even before your voice fell as a heavenly message that I might accept the word as a truth which death had revealed, I never felt that it would be as literal as it has been; I had not believed, but I hoped. It is the unfolding of our natural and literal association after my body was still, and I know that we were helped by our desire to find the answer to my prayer through your faith in the life that could not give the answer until the words came through the mists of unanswered experience and desire. Like the dawn it came smiling through the new experience. Then I began to put into words my ardent desire to say to you "It is true!”for there is vision and the love of those who went into the silence long ago; I saw those we had revered, and to hear your voice was sweeter than you can imagine, for it was not a thing occasionally thought of and considered important to know, but a continued desire that I might be aware of all our friends around us who had shaped our lives to the gospel of truth and faith. There they stood, not far away, in a glorified kingdom of life, but quite like a doorway, as they waited to say, "This is the outward experience of the soul which is clothed with intelligence and never forgets its union with the dear ones." Then something came sifting down into that new experience and I at once took my faith from your

VISION 

faith and, holding your hand safe in my own, I said, "This is the love of perfected desire." Never since that day when eyes were dimmed in the presence of that enfranchisement have I been out of contact with you. 

There was something we neither of us had with anyone else in the life of human contact-children we had, brothers, sisters, and the communion of life with those who took almost for granted the vital hope they held of a revival from death into life sometime after a judgment day, and the finding of loved ones redeemed and restored. All this I pondered over and over again, as if I half expected that men and women should be born with the knowledge and vision of those who were in the next world. I wondered that a man of good sense and knowledge should have to wait for experience like a child that sees through eyes that grow into usefulness of vision, and I sometimes wonder now if I really did expect to be given the knowledge that only death may give through experience or communion as we have found it. There are many who have talked with me about the way we wait for what death holds for us, and I often say, "Yes, yes, yes—why wait?" It is because opinion and ideas make it difficult to accept such as you, unless it comes down from the pulpit through the aisles of the church and passes us the golden Grail.

Since then I have a table, and you use a pencil, and one very little helper listens, looks, and sometimes sees perfectly my hand on yours as you write and get my message. Human love, the very wisdom of taking the message that grows into legibility to be read and not through a High Priest, but through a person having had experience and desire that lets him go on without hesitation

VISION 317 because he hears his own thought turned into the message of love, the Conqueror. And when you are not able to take them by yourself, you have the note-book at hand and drink deep of the nectar of the inflowing life from the spirit-world. I am glad that it is not an exception to truth, but that we are trained by perception, and I believe that it is because we are able to add truth here, truth there, truth in our experiences, and continuing faith we shall later find much more to help redeem the world from the ignorance that is the price we pay in the silence that we let fall about us. There are so many little ones who look up to a starless sky, no tender mother, no fine care-taking father guiding, pressing forward hand in hand with those who go into the silence quickly, without words, and give testimony of care by the way out of difficulty, and footsteps that make no sound, yet are always by the side of their loved ones. I have wondered about some most unusual experience of yours; by the same token you have been saved on occasion; there is the undiminished joy that suddenly comes like a wind from ethereal heights and wards off the hand that would destroy. Then I know the mothers of the world would rise against any belief that they could forget the babies of their younger life. All these are human experiences that I find, having a work after death. Parenthood is not the work of those tender years to be then resigned to the care of teachers and cousins and aunts; they do something, but fathers have pride and joy in carrying on; there is a power almost like godliness that was missed, sometimes as creative in its desire to carry on the gift of fatherhood in the understanding that divinely springs up through pride and love, but is trans-

VISION formed into the glory of fatherhood. Sometimes when 

I have wanted to send a specific message at Christmas or in Lent or on a birthday, I am quite as eager as if I did it, but I am keeping our method of the humanity in the messages I send, and the loveliness of the angel who takes them and gives them to the world. I want it to be a human communication, and it is not neglect when I make no mention by name of our children; as I talk of the father of your children and the mother of mine, I am as pleased to give some evidence that may be read far off as the years give light and joy. We love open doors, you and I; as we pass on in life doors open, later we remember how lovely it was to find a door swinging open. That's what I want our messages to become, not only to our little ones, but to others who pray often and long to the Heavenly Father that they may find their own in Heaven safe with Him. I see so many who are afraid or ashamed to accept the gift. 

So here we are. The Indian, my friend [THE Voice. 

I am his friend, he's mine!], the table prepared, and messages flowing through. Sometimes your father talks with me about all this misunderstanding of law. He says, finger lifted, "It is a good time for angels to do some work just now." Your father is a wonderful man. 

When there is anything that by my thought I am able to get into the mind of any who are trained to think right, and will not sell the opportunity for a place in a group working for selfishness, I shall be there, for as we gather the ideals of communication and power we are able to transpose it into the language we love as service to our fellow men; love to those close by, love that gives power, energy, experience, and expression,

VISION 

319 not to fathers and mothers alone, but there are martyred ones who gave life for larger life to those they did not even know but only saw. By what proceeded from life on earth they became staunch and ennobled in the grander service which holds steadily to those who lost some opportunities but are now learning to serve. 

Now I look forward to the lovely springtime days warming the earth, with a precious sun coming out on trees, bringing beauty, fruit, friendliness, and strength, making a precious season for you and me. I do know that I shall be able to speak each time I see your faith lifted to the waters, the hills, all beauteous things. Often 

I think of the night, as if hearing hushed songs of birds -they leave us quite breathless.

THE VOICE. Now he puts several pink sheets of paper, long and narrow, on the table. Someone is going to write you something like appreciation, a word of joy because of your "temerity."

THE LETTER continues. Now with the joy that has been mine this morning I give the message to the dearest one. Blessings fall on your footsteps and radiant love make plain the full heart of gratitude for all that you have done. Now, until another day, good-bye-not to go away but just to say, "Good-bye!"

LETTER LII 

February 25, 1937 

HE LETTER. There are blessings all about us which

Tare full of influences often accepted as the values that flow into gardens and from the atmosphere, as

VISION 

unregarded as dew or shadowy nights which revive and sustain us as a part of Nature's plan for the upbuilding of life that sustains the bodies of men. Quite as naturally we have verdure and beauty that we appreciate but do not offer prayers for the blessing.

I thought of this as I could see the effort that shrubs and young things made in response to the effort to bloom out of the order of months and add to our wonder that they are pushing forward to make us believe that the universal, unseen Father is giving them a right to do so. And I thought how quickly we explain without any precise reason to thank the unseen procession of Nature and to add thanks for our satisfaction that we did not do it and have only to be happy and accept what Nature does. Then I looked about me and I actually saw people on this side of life working away on little places where there seemed some idea that these efforts helped to bring into expression something like fruits and flowers by warm streams and little brooks and small ponds where the rain had fallen or the snow melted, sending out that wonder of all the world when the leaves begin to swell in the atmosphere of the wealth of the world responding to waters that do not flow directly to their roots, but send a message down below and open up the actual help that atmosphere gives. 

How much of this is due to humans who retain all these additional beauties which they do not create but find time to be grateful for?

All this has much to do with those in the next step in life. There is a very responsive and responsible group of those who take what God gives through a grass blade and so on through the better and more suscep

VISION 

321 tible life of those who add to God what they may to bring strength and beauty and increasing life; as far as 

I am able to determine by working with what God gives with "all their might and main" (he smiles) but it is civilization and plenty and love and unfolding life. There are those barren parts of the world where men do not take any responsibility but just soak in the larger beauty of hills and rivers that stand wildly until the awakened soul makes use of them for thoroughfares and rarified atmosphere. There is much for the children of earth to understand and shake hands with the gods of the seasons. It turns our thoughts to the wonder of things we do not even prospect in our earliest reasonings, it gives me the idea that we may yet forsake one law we have cherished as the most important for the world, and find a new kingdom that bids us look and see what we might accomplish for love of God in a new and definite help for the kingdom of Heaven to be established on earth. 

This is not a sermon, but a vision that is often unknown, unseen, unhelped by the law of the clergy and all the mental unfoldings that spend ages creating laws to keep the ribald in check like those that not only keep hands off your garden but do not urge protection. It means keeping your own property from the intrusion of the wild men of earth. I have often thought of the fine example of the divine quality of protection for persons and places dearest of all to me, and as we add to our words they may give ideas to those who have not understood that to such as had lands of their own and become partners with God there comes a spiritualized understanding to those who at last feel the tugging in the problems of something more than property, higher

VISION than universal experiences with things, titles, even educational opportunities and the brotherhood of life as established. I think I was not particularly interested in those groups of people who desired to take out a little realm among souls of like interest, and settle down like the old Brookside Farm company, where there would be kindred souls. Did they forget that growth always erects new highways for minds as well as matter, and did they not see that every unique place would find some running ahead where something was discovered?

Discovery is the life of the universe. Consider the Quakers, how they stand still, only quaking at what they are familiar with, opening in cornfields things that grow underground and beans that grow on poles; they might find things as wonderful as an oak that goes on for centuries and no one takes much care of it until it is realized that it may be good for something even if ground into meal.

I have been on a little outing, watching for signs of those fruits and flowers when they first invade the country. I always loved the coming of spring-I find so many tiny little leafy things suddenly grown up tall and helpful like those lilies that hold the showers that are not especially helpful to other growing things but always leave a drop or two in the heart of a bell-shaped beauty like the drop in the bucket when the days are dry; and I love to see those wild yellow lilies growing near a little brook or some bit of garden spot where Grandma comes out to water one she found in pastureland and it grows expansively and is the delight even of the children or the old folks-just water did that! 

That leads me to a house I was once familiar with-

VISION 323 not in New York, not Philadelphia, but a real country house; here and there something grown for the family fare and not all for that, for there are, near by, those spots of beauty that almost everyone loves—and can you realize that that house is here?-with the same desire to keep the old beauty and find it an inspiration to add to some level that had not been cultivated by those who still love it all just for old remembrance. 

Many of my papers and books are not now needed as they were years ago. I worked a long time to put in order whatever I thought would be useful to those who followed me; there they are, put away, tied up and made useful if needed, but not often are they untied and read. 

I would have been loath to destroy them, yet I knew that two things might happen-somebody curious to see how I looked at a prospect, and another come along and glance at it—into the wastebasket it goes! So much that we tie up, put into an envelope, tie the pink tape, place where to be found easily. I have applied all—no, not all-but some of ideals for order and preservamy tion of them to my life over here. I am not especially interested in turnips, but at the same time I find one getting the first idea of the way in which his vegetable garden may be helped by his care, and I say, “You don't expect to eat them, do you, now that you are not depending on them for nourishment?" And the querulous answer was, "No, but I like to see how they expect to go on forever when nobody wants them any more."

He did not know-he only thought that things grow after they are useless, like feet that run no more and eyes that look beyond the common things of life. That's to tell you that some common things are useful after

VISION death; heads come into their own after they have been put away as useless. I had intended to speak of that old house-it had several things that I hope you and I may some day find, and talk over the queer way the old rocking-chair rocks away on an old porch and brings back many a heart-throb of earlier days.

One awful situation in the world is to have priest or prophet frighten people. Fear does not belong to the 

Kingdom of Heaven—no chain may ever hold one away from the gifts of the Heavenly Father. No, we are, you and I and some friends I frequently talk with, going on a new crusade against fear. Something divine comes in its place-love of all that is beautiful; and finding it leads to more beauty: a daily joy, like violets blooming.

There are too many who turn out children at a tender age to the care of a teacher who often does not give the best that is in him, tenderness and love are often absent. 

Now I want to say, "Why does the old yellowish brown house with rambling rooms that give a chance for new discoveries, various articles long discarded because unused, why is it one of the precious memories I have of seeing now and then things I did not knowan old churn bringing back the thought of a curd that might be passed to a child recalling the hand that gave it with a smile? [I could not keep up at this point.] [He resumes.] One of our pleasure trips will be on the highway of the beginnings of life. In all these things I look forward to you as in my happiness. We may weave into our stories or histories of spirit-life after death some sweet old-fashioned experiences.

LETTER LIII

HE LETTER. So much has been said that when I

TH appropriate some sentiment very definitely true which I might use to a friend, a relative, or a companion of past experience, I draw a line through it, knowing that these words would not fit you or many of those whom I think of in connection with the appropriate sentiment; just as I would never think of calling my daughter by the same type of name-affectionate, but not just as I would think of a son. I was thinking of the name I call you by, which is sacred as I write it or speak it. In other words, I would never use a name like "Faithful" or others of that type, for they are peculiarly right for you; this finding of the perfect expression of a mental appreciation or spiritual fervor or diviner word belongs to my feeling toward you and your acceptance of it. I have been with some groups of people who seriously consider new names among their devotional attendants and friendly associations, as one may marry several times during human existence and apparently have interchangeable experiences with the one who comes into home association even with children born of another, and suffer no inconvenient memory to disturb the harmony of a marriage that adds to without affecting the situation. Not that, between you and me; it is entirely apart from every other association so entirely and definitely ours and complete in itself that if every one blest by your love and care stood side by side with us there would never be any sense of sharing those divine

VISION interests and happily adjusted associations, but as perfectly adapted to our companionship as if of a differing order; I believe it is that—it is that deeper sense of something that needs no explanation to any one as it is a step forward in the revelations that come in a new experience. It is in no way a taking up of love, but a light that shines as nothing ever did before, and includes much that has been lovely and good, yet has not reached in any degree the perfect union of mind, body, and soul. After that I can see no reason why other changes could ever add to what is already a part of all the emotions and the moving on as a complete union including all that has been right, useful, and growth of mind, body, and soul, with the still complex life of relationships unfolding and blessing us. We were friends through life and death, and that friendliness of soul was as evident before death as after, in our desire to know truth wherever it found us looking toward the light; that was our search, and these are the revelations that shine as footsteps of the planets in the worlds about us. I have not yet been seeking to find the numbers of the planetary systems that even the freshman at college feels about his Alma Mater, and I do not wish to go beyond my depth in mysteries until I may turn to you and say, "Shall we venture?" For just as 

Venus and other stars in the planetary system are visible from earth, so there are many more just outside the boundaries of our human vision. So I glance up and take a look at them, knowing that this becomes a highway that our minds may not grasp all at once. Now to make this little story of the onward life I know, if I have been able to catch the glory of splendid equipment and

VISION 327 companionship after one is moving hand in hand with the perfect mating of desire and purpose, I feel the wisdom of all the beautiful accomplishment of language, making possible an imaginary journey to longer and more beautiful relationships. That may come. And as we enlarge our relationships with those weary dreamers who wish to have a picture of Eternity, we may add now and then something of what may be, and say, "But let us take what we have, and find that joy as a part of the revelations." That is why when I see a little lady lifted from the desire to retain human loveliness, as that lady who wishes to know how far she has come after finding me, her only helper, after death, and taking walks with me, and she talks with you and is blest by this service we are trying to explain to those in need —well, that to me is much better than just putting up marble tombs to signify that your dear is feeling peace.

Many would give all to know what you or I, near the truth, have found as an answer to prayer. I find that it means that many turn to the lower forms of the animal life, and would send to their horses and dogs all they enjoy of food and drink—that would prove that we are coming up in our understanding of eternal life, not eating, drinking, or even worshipping some great beauty. 

I think that every time I turn to you and think of the real beauty of your soul-the things you wish for, the things you wish to do, the light you carry in your soul, the song you would sing when darkness becomes light to bewildered people who had been living on the sawhorse combination of working oneself along for a sensation-all that I see. You stand up and speak out, unafraid because knowledge has brought you joy. To think

VISION may take you over difficulties does not give what experience holds in the heart as life eternal, life forever.

THE VOICE. He puts down a white paper on which is written a vital truth in a line from Shakespeare, “Who steals my purse steals trash."

THE LETTER Continues. From generations long ago we find words made into indelible, ineffaceable truth which never dies nor fades nor passes away from the deeper consciousness. So we build up civilization and find the reality bound up in one soul and then passed to another. 

That is how your people and mine have suddenly perceived some truth by an act of ours which gives them light as in the translated Bible. Why not also give them what may be in human thought that holds the treasure and pass it on to the dead and the living? This is a part of the new knowledge that comes from experience. If we did not enter into their desire to know more than we do, we might still be eating raw birds, but by and by somebody comes with something fresh from the river gleaming like sunlight and one rises from savagery. 

Now I am not more eager than before except to be quite sure that the growing communications may add their bit to good manners, gracious like the betterment to give to younger ones just what we are enjoying; even the youngest may show quietness and pleasure, then someone teaches them to fight and "steal the purse, and then comes the desire to kill out hate by love and engage the world in an effort to bring men nearer to the kingdom of God and not into the hell of warfare. 99 THE VOICE.
