HE LETTER. Here we are very happy to see the

THE 

snow as a surprise move that adds to our happiness when we thought the winter was over, and like those early days we once more find the beauty of the seasons.

There are so many memories that hold us in the excitement of the different seasons that we always feel the sentiment of falling snow and start all over again.

Over here there is no consciousness of overcoming methods of progress that have to do with melting streams of winter, and I have never been in Arctic scenes bringing danger. Taken as an experience I much prefer the flowers and pathways that open up a way to the lighter and more beautiful effects than the heaviness of a cold country. It is the unfolding process that builds the gardens and highways that invite and interest us. That is in passing, for I was surprised to see snowflakes again,

VISION 

but all is well when we are able to find the little door that opens into the little room. 

It was a surprise to find that illness had kept us apart on our day. I discovered that there had been some difficulty-one who entered here wishing to continue without understanding that the lengthened time could cause illness, kept the "Light" [Mrs. Soule] away. I went seeking to find if anything could be done to hold in contact one who was imposed upon. Just here, I want you to realize that never under any circumstances would I hold you from your actual needs for change and contact, even if it gave me added words to remember and enjoy. Have I ever said selfishness is the enemy of the world? I think the world has grown careless of the rights of those in it, and with all this egotistical effort to impress the opinions that we may feel so important, we must find out how far such depravity may be used to carry out the will of one in power. Right here, in this part of the world, there is an increasing effort to make others come to beliefs and opinions that would be strikingly wrong to carry out to any extent the leader's wish. 

We know that; I need not emphasize it, for you or those around you who are broad and wise and kind, and unless we can find a way to answer these critical problems much will be lost and much pain come to those in the world who do not need any more the plans to make them loyal and true. I am not able to wait until all the distress going on to-day has come into the world like a tyrant whipping up the course of the ship. I sometimes say, "Where are the leaders? Letting this dominating personality run to the limit of rights."We go on. We may not live to exact from the taskmaster some portion of

LETTER LV 

March 18, 1937

HE VOICE. He has been here for a long time. He seemed to be wandering around.

THE LETTER. All this week I have been thinking I 

would have given everything I had to call you back that last day I gave you a message. I felt that I had greatly disturbed you by my words about the situation in America. I knew I was doing it, but to me it seemed a very terrible situation to have so much going on in the world which was blasphemous and difficult to set aside and know that men and women of finest type are subjected to such effort on the part of men who should be leading people into beauty and high fervor for the best that can come, yet in the midst of the display of wilfulness and a situation that might bring something as unreliable as we had ever known in America, without wisdom or consciousness of right and noble purpose to bring to those who work for the best. And I know that with a group of women ready to serve, never to resign their part in the world, we might still expect something divine to sweep through all classes of mind and find a way to keep right and order, and men at work. And for a while I kept repeating to myself, "Is the law no longer a part of the moral life in America?" and then felt quieter and thought I, too, might be able to send some note

VISION of hope that would be part of your work. I sat down and thought where were all the days of insecurity that we are bound to by our faith of good and triumph of right?

And I am sure that many of the wise men who brought us away from contacts not strong enough to hold us in obedience to the law, must be set aside as not coming into our reckoning in the laws of God and of honor. So 

I think to-day I will not touch the problem so alarming, but stand as if no such thing exists as dishonor. That is why I feel that all our thought of going onward in the laws of life with only law, the law for the nation, and world peace, is vain.

Why should all these fanatical ideas come to the young to cut out those who are able to stand steady in discussion in every household, and daring to speak of the unworthy associations that give nothing but what they consider the way to hold land in their keeping with no order of the high type of thought that we always had coming after changes that were asked for and are now demanded? Just the same power that has been ours to cling to and carry on with determination to win is ours to-day. I will not try to discover anything that may give the argument to fight out to the conclusion of their demands. Ever, in my place of service, I have held that the law, justice, and power would save us from lower forms of selfishness and untruth, and it has not discouraged me nor given me the realization that it could be otherwise. I am almost speechless even without feeling that we may be under the stress of a lower form of living, but one must speak that words may be heard, life enlarged and glorified by sustaining all that is honest and true. I went at last to some of the pioneer workers who

VISION 

333 have accepted in the vision of high and lofty purpose the way of looking into the reasons for changing this or that and bringing to the open those who had only miserable makeshifts for truth, goodness, and the divine law through every power to act. I found that perfect truth was the one solvent for the curses all around us in church, school, home, and street, and already we have established means, without naming president or coworkers to carry out our will.

When I first got through to you it was the happiest moment I ever had. Death itself stood away until I found that even to a presence without words could be brought the possibility to be creative in ideas and methods. Then as I saw it build so much that death had destroyed I became enthusiastic in making sympathy come into homes, and one can see what is going on carried by thousands of children. The dead are not always the recent dead; they go away, find their own, reuniting they come into the society of those long gone, still eager to do much for those they have loved. I would increase this; once the attention is attracted to those gone on who wish to serve, we begin at once to find here and there one who is sympathetic to our interest, or find some new way to carry a burden. It is of the spirit; different form, sympathetic, true, a service like that lifts the world in its agony out of suffering and finds a new ray of light always calling attention to something a spirit may do. 

Someone from this side finds a way to every spirit and mortal who may work together and bring us to a point of lifting the burden again. The Will is the strength, and when the burden is gone life becomes a rare and beautiful gift. You, that in so many beautiful poems

VISION given by some great artist like Browning, every corner as with Pippa adds to joy and peace and faith, only by her joyous song. We who read poetry and listen for words to give to the suffering the joy of the new life must look to such as Pippa. That was not a song of death—it was gay to all, a new life, a new happiness. Still we allow those who have no song, nothing except to speak of what they wish, to take from their fellow men. This we all know is so ordinary, so right, we can only pray and hope. The day of demand is here and it cannot be set aside. You need it for your little ones, for those who are not able to demand what they need. It must be a new kind of service; stop and think of the thousands of little folks that die daily-it's not begun. If we could get hold of this and put it on the ground of civilization, I think everyone would see this-the little boys in the streetshow to bring in a certain amount of stamina among those who have gone to prevent starting and hiding and spreading in different ways when food and lodging and bed are nearly impossible to find. All this could be done soon if only the greedy, selfish, sinful could be made to see that what they do is as if they robbed a bank. 

Heavenly vision and the need should do all this without taking more than you have to add, bringing as in the old days when mothers in the country made each week preparations for those who had nothing. In his own neighborhood such were thought of. To have this a part of the life of those who die I think never appeared to me as a duty but always a pleasure if I had something to give. Coming through death I have lately seen what it might bring by the thought of someone who had died, love gave the prompting to look and see a new realm of

VISION 335 living. Now when this comes the love prompting such action would make much difference. I will leave that now. You and I know that there are days when either of us might be very busy and happy, not even thinking of wishing for someone to bring us food, but there comes a desire to provide equally for someone near, even only in hospital; we would add by the very incentive to do something good. Love can unite us all in a wonderful exhilaration. 

I think one of the sweetest things you have done is the helping of a man like Mr. H. to communications. 

THE LETTER LVI 

March 25, 1937 

HE LETTER. I am now experiencing the highest joy for any human to fall heir to, as I am able to make my thought come to legibility for our understanding of life that never ceases. What we know as death is swiftly transposed into a higher note and so continues without loss of memory or vision that gives understanding of the notes that constantly lend new power, peace, and glory to what has been.

If you had been within this room when the little 

Louise sent her message to her mother and added a love word to her Aunt Anna and to you, and included the 

O. P., you would have been happy for days. I was asked incidentally if any suggestion of mine might be sent to that dear lady for her strength and better health; and that brought to my attention the idea of trying to give back energy lost by accident or over-exertion by those on

VISION 

the physical side of life. I had never been asked that before. I knew the great body of healers that Boston holds in its care, and I think I would not wish to take up any healing that would subscribe to statements not quite true. I could not say that before Louise or her mother, but I said for her gentle sake that I would see what I might be able to transfer from a personality as I would take from a jar of lilies something to energize the thought and love of beauty into a form of thought-wave, just as we all look at a garden or a gift of roses and are lifted into an atmosphere of beauty and love and strength. I have known of the various forms of faith-cures in downpourings of new ideals from one heart to another, and if I had faith in prayer-and I have I would use that for Aunt Anna or you or any other of our dear ones. I know I would question any who had been able to do as they would for a sick baby and give whatever could be given to bring peace and joy and strength through confidence in the transfer of health as they would of better air or any form of inpouring of all one has, as they do with the outpouring of the emotional life that is the real life. I say this as a word to be given to Aunt Anna, that the old philosopher cares very much for the little lad and will do or send whatever he may to her and her dear ones. Right here I say by words to you that since that idea of mine to add to the strength of any worth-while efforts, I found one day before one was to come here, that a hand laid lightly on the forehead of the "Light" [Mrs. 

Soule] whose eyes were partly blinded, without any particular word used, the eyes opened and the partial blindness had disappeared. And so I have come several times and suggested to the Indian that she call for something

VISION 337 laid lightly over to rest from all—and she cried out, “I can see!" She had been seeing several everywhere she looked-she saw words, she could find no printing in writing like that anywhere. She says, "It's not my eyes, I can read and write." But that gave me the idea that there were waves that cure by removing some obstacle of vision, and if that were true perhaps the same methods that Jesus used might have been faith in the power of spirit, not to deny but to accept and use truth. 

Now I am facing the Easter service with you. I love those mountain services of faith in the arisen Christ as never before, in various places where day is greeted by many people in the open with a thrill of joy and cherishing those true services of faith—and never had it occurred to me that any criticism of preacher or priest by me has been anything but a criticism of the shortcoming of the truth that they hold as a gift of God and teaching by one superior to the ordinary prayer. It makes so much light on my own path as I see you. Beside you all the hours of the Easter season I shall be finding you wherever you are and learning, and leaning on your knowledge of the experiences we have together. I am very happy in the idea that I shall not miss a word that you hear and that I may send to one whose words particularly touch mind and heart. Hearts are responding always to words spoken in commandments and songs and prayers that express love to Jesus, and when the mind stands up to its reasoning powers and is able to find the use of the new understanding of Jesus to wish the truth accepted because it makes the splendor and dignity of the Easter message. As I place my lilies on the altar of that finding, I place my hand in yours in a

338' VISION solemn covenant that always we step up each year with a finer understanding of what it means. We place our lilies with heartful, grateful appreciation that we have found the royal road to the eternal life with no Last Judgment Day to leave us on the place assigned us. It is our choice; it is unfoldment. First we had the revealing life, now it is the unfolding life we hold. I think I may still speak slightingly of having run a race and then finding ourselves sitting down to rest. I believe as 

I pronounce these words that life is endless. Not the same moments, activities, or triumphs over and over in the infinite life; we know how silly it was to think of the same music, the same precision, and the same place, but to arrange and find new combinations in still higher, more glorious, most beautiful harmonies we know or expect still, is to go forward God-ward eternally. This sounds solemn; it is very solemn, but not difficult-only obey a command for action. We are not making activity, but a movement, always something to be desired, finer, higher methods of giving the best we have, at the moment we serve. I do love, just as always, the rivers, the seas, the waters; the ceaseless activity down to the little ripples of a small stream which add much to my joy-nothing especially served by their activity, but inactivity is not to our liking.

I want to speak of some of our own occasionally. 

My fulfilled happiness will be when you are here with me; but we would not hasten the day, for I know that the joy is in experience, and I cannot feel that any double promotion would be best. I think when the time comes for you to come over here I shall know it and be there. Perhaps not the first-mothers have an appealing

VISION 

339 look. I hope we shall know right away—no tragedy, you go to sleep, open your eyes and I am there. And I say, "Shall we walk away now where we have been so many times at night when you slept, and sit in that place and wonder how it came so easily?" Others who love you will be there. We are in the same plane of life.

LETTER LVII 

April 1, 1937 

THE LETTER. The very natural way that I accept the time I may give my message to you is as beautiful as if we had found the way to a new language; it preserves us from the methods that entangle me in the wonder, and gives the freedom of the world that is just as beautiful as we would expect in a spiritual and normal relationship. From the very first I have desired to use all that is possible to express the truth as it comes to my power, and keep the fine and useful beauty of friendship that never fades but grows into the reality of all that is good and true. There are no secrets to bring complications perplexing to the combinations of old friendships; we are led to the close and perpetually unfolding light binding us together, yet with the broader understanding of the spirit that makes for godliness and the depths of the eternal life. While intellectual perceptions groped for knowledge of the determining powers which gave confidence, they found no way to prove what is not disclosed until death comes, removing the

VISION 

covering bandage and revealing the vision. No word that faith may give is ever complete without experience, and the battle between lack of knowledge and what is believed by faith is ever quite like the acceptance of actual experience between the dead and the living. I have accepted this work as a wonderful way to express my unfolding consciousness of the methods I may use to tear down the barriers built between the dead and the living. One might find that through unbelief and prejudice there might arise the constant question whether or not we change our methods of finding out the precise truth of something difficult to accept, the words and methods we have found useful and accepted as we accept the lilies of the field, and odors that bless us yet are not recognized as language of the growing things after death. 

One may listen to the song of a bird, yet not catch the way the sound is transmitted, not seen, nor even heard. 

Every day the water flowing onward to the sea is heard continuously if one stands in the path, but in the sweet tone we find the loveliness that comes from the water to the ears, which brings consciousness of the way sound is constantly given to us. When words are added, there is language and the knowledge of the human mind that we hold in reverence, blessing, and in our consciousness of humanity talking out the evidence of thought and love. There is no confusion and rarely mistakes that destroy confidence in the experience.

All this leads us to still higher understanding that nothing can take away. In my desire to place responsibility for learning and use of language I hope it will always be my continued effort to bring some experience -to you and to me-that shall help us to place these

VISION 341 messages in the churches, where we should help those who wish to give the evidence of human contacts after death and never let the evidence fail. This is accomplished more perfectly in peace and quiet and alone, without the loss of energy that comes in a company or a group of people all eager to say what they think and feel that has been my purpose. The scientific investigator will pound open every door and over and over again give the same words spoken on an excursion or railway train or some sacred moment that reveals memory. All well and useful and good, but time and difficulty were found to be a hindrance that tested patience and took the opportunity to bring every letter, every thought to task for the evidence desired. 

I have much of all this to write for your aid when time admits, but in a crowded hour all too short, we lose the constantly unfolding revelations of presence and love and belief from the mothers and fathers, relations and friends who are kept away for the needs of those who find an open door by chance and make every effort to be patient, for later they may have the same chance to add their evidence and their message of love. 

There has never been any extended time that your father has been out of contact with you or any of the dear ones who never lose the care of parenthood, nor would they find the same peace and sanity in the numerous contacts of all kinds of human relationships; but the dead are the people who sustain the care and love and in a moment of need keep safe and sure the contact; the whole company of love and family are within call. I go where I catch the call from those I left without the power to do much that is important for my own.

VISION 

The father of your children, while not accepting what you would give or I would add, has never surrendered to the waves of wrong or the same dangers of pain and sorrow, and we find the precious contacts that reach and save, and are ever in the same holy relationship of parenthood and all it implies. I am planning to give you some of my experiences of long ago, and give the evidence of the unknown presence. I had but felt it to be some superior power, and wondered how it could be, but it was. These letters are to give the creators of life the care that belongs to him or her, and in the place of the service of life to loved ones there is unspoken joy and help; when you and I joined in the gift of our service we found much to be grateful for.

There is great trouble in the world to-day. Sin has apparently crept into the high places of life, and the young and uncared for have fallen by the way; death has taken them from the human care of their own. In this plan to destroy which we see as the contact of right and wrong there is—mark the word—terrific danger to the careless, untrained, and those who wish to rule; we must rise in our interpretation of the need of love that is powerful and creative; and to the group of women whose husbands have slipped away we add the knowledge that they are still a part of the love and life that was theirs. It is rather difficult for me to say the words 

I wish to say; none of them is fit to rule, nor suspect the dangers to those who have not been trained, and yet this should all the more be taken up as a very serious matter in classes and constantly with knowledge of the truth about these super-sensitive people who destroy

VISION 343 other people to bring to the world crimes that must be understood and set aside.

Whenever I see a place where you may take part in giving knowledge of the usefulness of contact I long to speak to you each day, and by that we may be able to add more and more through good and true ideals of that precious contact which we may sustain and give 

God the Heavenly Father back to the children of men.

A little later some wonderful experiences may be given in evidence and may everyone have the blessing of contact!

LETTER LVIII 

April 8, 1937

THE LETTER. Every hour spent with these expressed feelings increases the power and joy. I should never feel hurried or impatient, and I feel that I impede my own unfolding if I do not go quickly and with the confidence that it falls into the mind, and is included in the acceptance of the body of truth retained in the capacity you have of feeling so much that is included. I realize that it is quite impossible to leave unexpressed the sense of the bodily contact that is mine. I see you here, and

I never feel that any person shares the interview with us; all our loves are included, but not in any measure a hindrance as people would be if words of theirs were added. You from the first have caught that sense of bringing the gift of eternal life, which may be given to those who can understand; silly or curious minds may not even catch the glory of the everlasting truth, but

VISION 

those who see the gleam of shining stars through tall pine trees are able to find the deep meaning of all that rises-rises above the tiny dew-drops that are at our feet and moisten the roots that must be nourished and given the needed currents of the little things that add growth. Once in a while as I come in with my thought advancing to the point of contact I have a wistful feeling that this would never end-then I realize that extension of the gift of communication does not cease, but is being added unto in many new and vibrant ways; then I am taller, see farther, feel deeper-not like a boy grown up, but like a more distinctive gift of knowing what has never been seen before. Your voice does not ring out as from a deeper melody of truth, but as a diviner height of beauty, tender, sweet, and vibrant. I think, perhaps, my earlier infirmity makes hearing so much dearer, when I do not have to look up, questioning, but catch all that is there; and I wonder if the noisy world with its mighty engines and its excess of speed does not drown those tenderest notes of life which, even whispered softly, find the spot where sounds are deepened, not strengthened. So much we hold as the added power in sound of song or love when once in a long time we find it in the breaking bubble on the sands at our feet and feel that it is sweeter than those throbbing tones of cataract or wild wind. Perhaps it is the mood, the mind with which we receive the tone that sounds through the world unceasingly. I love the quiet, reverent twilight hours: no sound that speaks of power, but there is something as tender as the touch of a baby hand on the face in slumber, without knowledge that it is seeking the food that it would have if awake, and conscious and

VISION 

345 tender as the kiss of love. You have known that, half waking, half sleeping, yet deeply aware of the angel touch of love in its first emotion, that never dies. When 

I come to you after sleep has set your soul awing, moving away through chairs and pictures, from resting couch, and with that soft conscious holding of hands as we move outward in any direction, the freedom of movement takes us to a little garden where everything is vibrant, thrilling, life safe and secure. I wonder if when one day we do not return and those who love us whisper, "She is gone, she is dead," if we shall be able to do for one who longed to know, how we find the beautiful experiences you and I have had since I have been able to tell you all that has come so naturally into the common spiritual life that awaits communication. 

I know that there is nothing like that sudden sense of separation that I had thought must be the only way to spiritual freedom, or that affiliation with all who had gone before we even realized that earth life was over. 

Did I say “over”? I did—but it is never over until we wish it to be; it is never a burden of companionship that holds to cares and duties, but always an available comradeship of life on every sphere. I have seen friends of yours and mine of the earlier years going on in very beautiful life without demands or any expressed purpose to leave or stay, but the most natural and simple and perfect understanding of adjustment. I can see that all these demanding notes have much to do with memories that were never quite completed, and one day when I am able I hope to make one happy in the feeling that there will be no intrusion on companionship where there is love, and that the appreciation of the fact that all the

VISION differing experiences are sacred and dear, and would ever bind or hold or make the only experience beautiful and true. When I first knew that there would be no severance of our affection-our union-I felt that thrill of the mastery over selfish domination, and have never doubted at all that we would come to a point of confidence through what might come one thousand years from now.

I one day went to a very early experience in my own child life with one who had been fond of me, like any grandmother, and I found those dear influences of older and wiser unfolding life—that was quite as distinctive as mine for my children, you for yours, and no tormenting questions. After a while I saw them grow into maturity. I would not have little children always asking about everything. I felt that some hours must be given to discovery.

To-day I purposely try to get away from problems of the old world from the beginning of time. I do not wish to tell the President what I think he should do. One must have the sense to see and receive, and for one I should in his place take little children, rounding them out for the opportunity of life. I think that illness which carries one to death with the imposed cares and duties is far better than the accident of taking power, and something more than adding to gold, beauty, even, of ordinary days that pass without finding God, GOD.

Experience makes life a combination of so many ways to bring the consciousness of God to the world. I am thinking of some plans that you and I will use when we are both here together with so many who need the touch of understanding, everlasting life put into form, expe-

VISION 

347 rience, and growth; we will turn all the hours' experiences into stepping-stones to the highest and best. 

Still the old problem; but if we are never to find separation as a disaster that sends us out again into another world, why should we think about that until after the new, mightier power has come? Right here, with you in a room holding nothing cherished but the memory of experience, simply through doors of Eternity comes a part of wherever we are. I know many spirit-people who are pounding on closed doors of the life they are in to find something unusual-some gift of surprising experience to hold in check the less important life and find the perfected experience. My heart is too full-one day I look up and call the Creator to let us and those we love move forward a step at a time, and those worlds through which we pass become stepping-stones to still greater wonder and beauty. 

When I hear you think, when your voice responds across the earth to a voice you love-from Egypt or any far-off place, any of the cities that rise and fallwhen you are able to know the voice you hear as the one you always find available, the realization of being a part of unfolding ages will give back youth, loveliness, and love, then some consciousness of God comes flowing back to you.

I am only serious about the little ones who go down in fire and flame; those I would hold steady to grow into the loveliness of heaven. Are we spirits ever troubled by fire and flame? Yes, indeed, you couldn't expect me to stand and see tragedies happening every day, not always sin, but those who walk with eyes closed going on into the care of love. I find so much that is wonderful in

VISION 

the animals who do not talk or work, but live-horses beautiful in affection, love of people, so many companionable in the animal kingdom. It is the most wonderful experience to know what they think—in deeper feeling that we have known. There are great animals of a past day, and beautiful fish that I know I shall not fish to feed myself but take the graceful beauty out of the water to admire.

I have been much interested in some evidence of the forms of spirit-people being produced without fraud or too much interest in a particular form, and in a group of ancient people I have seen they were producing in their own interest something that would leave the image long enough to be reproduced by a human in paint or marble given as the after life of many creatures. I would not wish to spend much time doing this reproduced extension of knowledge that they did not die. I will talk with you about this later. I think some of our scientific people gone long ago might have left such work as no good to anyone; nothing to do with evidence of what life after death is in forms of those who worked to build life and forgetting the study of them as man, developing minds superior, striving for the best way to find evidence of eternal, not passing, life. I am not so much interested yet in that, but I grow intensely anxious to have you with me in some new discovery. 

Your mother, standing now as I touch her hand on this little table, with evidence of her love and care and desire that all her babies and our babies may be with her with minds unfolding, is grateful to you for helping them to come here. 

The gift of death.

LETTER LIX 

April 8, 1937 HE LETTER. I find that even with all these meetings. there are many times when, as I think of my message to you, it is far different from the words I think to my family and to the friends I often come in contact with, as I see them in the usual way of happy and healthful meetings in the nearer contacts of earthly association, binding ourselves in plans for work that must be carried on in the idealistic life that most thinking people are so earnestly seeking to implant in the lives of the Christian world. There are as many approaches to the varied thoughts of those who do think as between children and grown people, good and badfor all sorts of men meet in the intercourses of thought, they are not labelled and set in form by the worthiness they may wish for, it becomes a part of one's own aspiration or degradation of life. I think that is a very definite advance over the power to do only what is desired, and between the jumbled mass of humanity taken from one place to another, looking out of windows that hide the beauty of sky-line and all the natural beauty of unfolding growth, in discontent and longing for the freedom which money, care, and friendship may giveit is the finest situation to know that there is a power of will which saves or destroys, it is a new, wonderful gift; one may accept it and realize knowledge is the swift power that carries us up the high roadway over heights we cannot climb without that assistance giving very definite knowledge of something that increases with use

VISION 

and takes us immediately away from sins that are not to our taste and desire. You understand.

Well, if I am able to prove even in a small way that the will gives us strength when weakness falls on us or when those we love need arms of strength and knowledge there is the divine gift of inflowing love and safety from sin and death and the blessedness of all that binds to the Eternal. The other day, when finding a man of very erratic power, I asked one who loves problems about seeking to awaken a new use of strength that might be wonderful if rightly applied. To my surprise this helper said, “Not all who have power come at once to the possession of wisdom, and I have wondered if the Universalist belief that there is eternal life for all were right. Some who apparently do not wish it may find a world that is better suited for that under-world of sin than any of the natural and beautiful impulses that have come into action through new desire and may bring the angel within into desire and purpose."

That we will take up later and perhaps may find that the flower of light may bear beauty that had just sprung into being; for in our association with all that has been a part of growth we have things as difficult to accept as anything except to help awaken. I have always known this, as you have, but the Christian sublimity of life when once we accept it is as the yeast that sets to rise the unfolding of the best to make life better and braver and the exalting of all that is ideal, even if not yet discovered. But I still wonder. There is a power of will which saves or destroys; it is a new and wonderful gift which increases with use; it is the divine gift of inflowing love saving from sin and death, and binding to all

VISION 

351 that is eternal. All this we will take up later, perhaps. 

But I still wonder how long it takes to bring the Godlife into some hearts that I have found. We have to wait. The light of star, the granite of the mighty hills shining in sunlight all contribute beauty that I have recognized only as dividing lines of the spaces that separate light and darkness and bring out for our thought all that makes them important in unfolding. There is now in my study (I am studying) so much that keeps me wondering about those who have brought about so much of unfaith because their power for destruction is as mighty as storms, earthquakes, torrents and floods and carelessness that add to death and separation. 

Yet it works on with those old, old masters of the earth, and finally crashes through the torrents, floods, tumblings of death and destruction, and brings this great expression of life that comes out of death and adds knowledge to give back faith and confidence in the Eternal. There are some who have a strange philosophy of bringing ourselves into Godhead, a new supreme power that brings out of all the evil, joy, and understanding, not hopes, not ideals, but beliefs that spring up like flowers that bring back the love of the creative influence.

All this adds to my desire to have all around me and about me when you come to me in that confidence, confidence in God, the peace that is not destroyed by loss of faith in men or in their desire for disaster that adds to their power. All these days when I watch and wait for this hour that brings us to the very surface of knowledge, I wish that my gift of speech or communication could be carried to some who are bound by a disastrous

VISION 

belief in the power of war that takes away the gifts of unfolding, lost when we realize that such loss is never a stepping-stone to God; that those who are part of systems that steal away all that should be purest and best as atmosphere about children, homes, duties, and real conquests, which are ever at the gateways of life and death. One question arises again and again, "Have they taken away the love of God in His world?"

So this is my sermon. I would stand before them asking how it gives larger life, purer ambitions, finding understanding of the Divine. They must answer. 

I have found-oh, how I love to say this to youthat the limitations of vision that show only one at a time or a few at a distance imperfectly or those who have only recently disappeared from life as I and others disappear at death, gives us the vision of ranges of far hills rising one above the other, each finding place and power and receptivity of the thought of others, so, to those I have lived with, guided by my own desire to promote knowledge, truth comes finding its way into every life as experience adds its knowledge. But it is after the separation, when voices are still and when expression and after years have brought new wonderful contacts, that I still find that those who went long ago and those who went yesterday or a week ago are visible, do not have to be sought for or idealized, but are there, just a little beyond the place where we stand, and are visible, easily intensified in love and knowledge and leading as needed. So all those dear ones that you knew, loved, and lost in the shadow of death, who often stand waiting and hoping for all the old affection restored in the dear relationships that were lost in shadow-father,

VISION 353 the family, brothers, sisters, and friends-all these were never lost, not the goodness that they have hidden in the sanctity of love that binds, but the love itself. I speak like a lover to this-one stands in his rightful place, always available, so beautiful they are—oursthe holding of one nearest your heart in understanding love, moving with it, having definite plans of acceptance and hopes. That's my place; I shall never go away, neither will they. He who takes to himself the one he loves always feels the rightful place. In this life of limitations the shutting away of some who are taken by death is a great sorrow, but there they are! each in added love and gifts of beauty. I have a home; I have in it the treasure of my soul. I'm not so serious that this should be a burden, windows through which I see other associations a part of all that are beautiful. I wait each day to bring the divinity of love, not its desire, but stepping into the chambers of eternal life, gift of all that is right and good, going into its fullness. So there I sit with you and all the younger ones and I see my own.

I stand with the sense of protection and find each day another flower coming up out of the sky, broader sunlight, and that is moving onward to the Eternal. I am often asked if, in this vision I have of relatives, all are as dear. No-no-dear in different ways, as you all, others in the family life, are always accepted as part of the life of all who are united. Now this is not a sermon; it is a truth, all this that I add. 

I do not dread your going away. When you go it will be all right. I hope that I may be able to send you a message direct that will give you the proof that I have been where you are, and seen you and added a word of affection.
